<meta>This page begins the preface to the 1649 English edition of Jacob Boehme’s Epistles. It serves as an introduction to the reader, explaining the value of these collected letters as a guide to spiritual practice and the limitations of written words alone.</meta>

<sig>A 2</sig>

# THE PREFACE
## TO
# THE READER.

AS there is no book or treatise that this author has written in which the footsteps and signs of *Divine Light* and *knowledge* are not imprinted and discernible—and which may be of special use and benefit to the impartial Christian reader—so likewise these his epistles <note>original: "Epistles" — letters written to individuals or groups.</note>, written at various times and occasions to several friends, and thus gathered and collected together, may rightly be considered as one book. This collection is of no small benefit and direction to the reader, lover, and practitioner of that divine light and knowledge which his writings contain and present. 

<margin>☛</margin> Indeed, the bare *letter* <note>original: "Letter" — this refers to the literal written words as opposed to the living spiritual essence of a message.</note> of any man’s writings, even if written from a divine gift and a real manifestation of light within the authors themselves, cannot *breathe* the *spirit* of *wisdom* into us. For the sound of understanding cannot be written down <note>original: "Characterized" — represented by written characters or symbols.</note> or painted on paper, any more than the notes and symbols of music can give the actual sound and tune to the ear. Rather, they are a guide for how the skillful *musician* shall play on the instru- <meta>The text continues on the next page with the word "instrument."</meta>

<summary>The preface introduces Jacob Boehme's collected letters, asserting that while they are filled with divine light, the written word serves only as a guide and cannot replace the living spirit of wisdom.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Epistles, Divine Light, Spiritual Wisdom, Letter vs. Spirit, Musical Metaphor, 17th-century mysticism</keywords>

<header>The Preface to the Reader.</header>

ment; so also our mind is like an organ or instrument, but it sounds only according to the tune and note of that Spirit that possesses and activates it. We convert and assimilate <note>original: "assimulate" — to take in and incorporate information or experiences.</note> all things according to the spirit and will that is ruling and predominant within us. In that spirit, the mind, thoughts, and senses are enkindled and inflamed. For if we have a merely astral <note>original: "Astrall" — in Boehme's terminology, this refers to the "spirit of the world" or the natural/celestial influences on the human psyche, as opposed to the Divine Spirit.</note>, worldly, carnal, self-conceited, contemptuous, caviling <note>original: "Cavilling" — making petty or unnecessary objections.</note>, Pharisaical <note>original: "Pharisaicall" — hypocritically self-righteous or obsessively legalistic.</note>, and hypocritical spirit, we will form and frame our notions, judgments, and criticisms accordingly, perverting everything to a wrong sense and *use*. 

But if the Divine Spirit of love, light, meekness, humility, self-denial, sincerity, and holy desires dwells, rules, and acts within us, then our understanding is accordingly holy, *divine, and real*. For *to the pure, all things are pure; but to the impure, all things are impure; for their very minds and consciences are defiled* <note>original: "To the pure, all things are pure..." — a reference to Titus 1:15.</note>.

<margin>☞</margin> Just as this author did not receive his learning, gifts, and high endowments from the schools of this world, but from the school of divine wisdom and the illumination of the Holy Spirit, so too the meaning and purpose <note>original: "drift"</note> of his writings will not be understood by those who are merely trained and skilled in the literal and historical schools of this world. These people follow the canons, rules, and axioms of their own "wise" Pharisaical reason; they condemn, judge, and rashly criticize anyone who does not speak in their specific phrase, style, or dialect <note>original: "Dialect" — here meaning a specialized vocabulary or way of speaking.</note>. For

<summary>This page argues that a person’s spiritual state determines how they interpret truth, comparing the mind to an instrument played by either a worldly or divine spirit. It cautions that Jacob Boehme’s work cannot be understood through traditional academic reasoning alone, as his insights came from divine illumination rather than worldly schooling.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual perception, Jacob Boehme, divine wisdom, literalism, Pharisaical reason, internal spirit, Titus 1:15, illumination</keywords>

--- Page 7 ---
<meta>This page continues the preface from the previous page, finishing the thought that academic learning without spiritual connection is insufficient. Catchword: unto.</meta>

<header>The Preface to the Reader.</header>

<warning>Page contains significant bleed-through from the reverse side; shadow of a thumb is visible on the right margin.</warning>

<margin>☞</margin> If we had everything that the prophets, Christ, and his Apostles ever spoke and taught, along with the knowledge of all divinely illuminated men in their various gifts recorded in writings, and if we were to read them for many years; yet, if we did not desire to become one with that same spirit of divine knowledge, and endeavor to have it become innate and essential *within us*—as a living expression of divine love and righteousness—these writings would be nothing more than dark and historical accounts to us. Furthermore, the frame and structure of knowledge which we would build for ourselves upon that foundation by our own artificial reason would be merely an insubstantial <note>original: "vapouring" — boastful or lacking substance, like a mist.</note> notion; a blind, *pedantic, and prideful display* <note>original: "Luciferian ostentation" — a display of arrogance or vanity, referencing the pride of Lucifer.</note> of *Pharisaic knowledge that would not edify, but puff up* <note>original: "not edifie, but puffe up" — a reference to 1 Corinthians 8:1: "Knowledge puffeth up, but charity edifieth."</note>. 

<margin>☞</margin> From this self-conceited, verbal, high-flown, and contrived knowledge—which wise, "letter-learned" reason, lacking the illumination of the Holy Spirit, has gathered in various forms through its own imaginary understandings and interpretations of the writings of holy men—many mental idols, opinions, contentions, rifts, and divisions have arisen in the Christian world. These have almost entirely destroyed all brotherly love. But may the God of love—through the outflow and breaking forth of His divine light and Holy Spirit within us—so expel and drive these divisions into the abyss of darkness from which they come, that in the various dispensations of His divine gifts and manifestations of light

<summary>This page argues that true spiritual understanding requires an internal connection with the Divine Spirit rather than mere academic study or "letter-learned" reason. The author suggests that reliance on human intellect alone leads to pride, vanity, and the sectarian divisions that have destroyed Christian unity.</summary>

<keywords>divine illumination, Holy Spirit, Pharisaic knowledge, pride, sectarianism, brotherly love, reason vs. spirit</keywords>

--- Page 8 ---
<meta>This page continues the prayer that God's grace might break through to humanity, leading to mutual understanding and love. Catchword: They.</meta>

# The Preface to the Reader.

...unto us, so that *we may rightly understand and serve one another in love.* We pray that the great Babylonian building <note>original: "Babylonish building" — a reference to the Tower of Babel (Genesis 11), symbolizing human confusion and the prideful attempt to reach heaven through human effort.</note> of our own tottering imaginations and reckless opinions may *fall*. These are the structures we have tried to build up for ourselves through the clever inventions and flattering interpretations <note>original: "glozing glosses" — deceptive or overly smooth explanations that hide the truth.</note> of our own empty, plotting reason—making many various guesses about how God should be worshipped and served. We hope this falls so that we may all come to speak the *one holy language of Christian love to each other.* Until now, we have been so divided that, instead of building each other up in the holy faith of Christ, we have bruised, battered, and beaten each other down in the spiritual pride and hypocrisy of *Antichrist.*

<margin>☛</margin> There are some who are so preoccupied with a strong conceit of their own "enlightenment" and a love for their own self-will that they reject anything that does not carry the stamp and signature of their approved patrons, schools, and institutions. To them, such things are nothing but darkness and <term>nifling</term> <note>original: "nifling" — insignificant, trifling, or worthless.</note> shadows. They are so possessed by groundless prejudice, nitpicking <note>original: "cavilling"</note> superstition, and empty suspicion that they reject such writings as not even worth reading. They imagine <note>original: "weening"</note> that they have a correctly *orthodox* judgment, perfected in all points of *philosophy and theology* <note>original: "Divinity"</note>. But these people cannot see how much they resemble the blind, self-important *Scribes and Pharisees*—those *prideful, self-styled wise men* <note>original: "Luciferian wiselings"</note> who thought no one could teach them anything because they were skilled in the literal text <note>original: "the Letter"</note> of the Scripture. But Christ told them that—

<summary>The author calls for the destruction of prideful, human-made religious systems in favor of a universal "language of love" and warns against the intellectual arrogance of those who rely solely on academic institutions and literal scripture.</summary>
<keywords>Tower of Babel, Christian love, Antichrist, spiritual pride, Scribes and Pharisees, orthodoxy, religious division, interpretation</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of different types of readers and religious factions who fail to understand the truth due to their own prejudices. Catchword: opinion.</meta>

# The Preface to the Reader.

*They erred, not knowing the Scriptures, etc.*

Others again, the mysterious Antichrist in Babel <note>original: "Babel" — a reference to Babylon, representing spiritual confusion and false religion.</note>, through a selfish illumination <note>original: "illumination" — a claim of divine insight or enlightenment.</note> and blind persuasion, has so deeply captured and imprisoned—indeed, bewildered and bewitched—people in his mystery of iniquity and hypocrisy, that they not only take it upon themselves to despise <note>original: "contemne"</note> and condemn all that seem to contradict their received principles and opinions, but from the workings of the unclean Pharisaical spirit within them, *they will draw strange conclusions and perverted meanings from the words and works of others* to make them hateful and disgusting. These people are like toads that turn everything they eat into poisonous nourishment, which they then spit out again upon anyone who even touches them.

<margin>☚</margin>
There are also others who are very greedy and eager to read books to better and improve their knowledge; however, their simple, well-meaning minds are so darkened and possessed by the principles, mental idols, and *opinions of their approved masters*, and they have so chained, devoted, and *sacrificed* their consciences to them in blindfolded hypocrisy, that nothing can make any *true* impression upon them except for that which speaks for, pleads for, and maintains their cause, their way, or their sect, to which they have given themselves over. They will hold and believe those things simply because they choose to, or because such and such people—of whom they have a good opinion—[opinion...]

<summary>This page describes two types of problematic readers: those who maliciously twist others' words to make them seem hateful, and those who are so devoted to their own religious leaders and sects that they cannot accept any new or differing truths.</summary>

<keywords>Antichrist, Babel, Pharisaical, prejudice, religious sects, hypocrisy, mental idols, spiritual blindness</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's critique of prejudiced readers and introduces the intended audience: the humble and sincere seeker. It concludes the previous sentence regarding people who believe things only because of the opinions of others.</meta>

# The Preface to the Reader.

...opinion says so; or because they have already received and *maintained it*, rather than from any true, impartial consideration that it is right, or from any effective, living, essential knowledge and real spiritual Being of it in *their own souls, etc.* But for those who think they know enough already and are so full of their own opinions <note>original: "well conceited of their owne selves"</note>, this Author has written nothing. He writes instead for the seeking, hungry, and desiring soul that—through unfeigned repentance, conversion, and the turning inward <note>original: "introversion" — a spiritual term for directing one's attention away from the external world and toward the presence of God within.</note> of its will, mind, and thoughts to God—*strives for the real practice of Christianity* and the leading of an upright life <note>original: "conversation" — in the 17th century, this referred to one's general conduct and way of living among others, not just speech.</note> in all humility, meekness, simplicity, patience, forbearance, righteousness, and Christian love towards all people, without a selfish, narrow disrespect or a Pharisaical <note>A reference to the Pharisees of the Bible, used here to describe someone who is hypocritically self-righteous or judgmental.</note> scorning and condemning of *others*.

The true way and means for a person to free 
<margin>☞</margin>himself from all blind arguments, disputes, doubts, errors, and controversies in religion—and to get out of that tedious maze and wearying labyrinth of troubling thoughts, ways, and opinions concerning God, Christ, Faith, and Election <note>the theological doctrine concerning how and why individuals are chosen by God for salvation</note>; the Ordinances <note>referring to established religious rites or ceremonies, such as baptism or communion</note>, or the way of worship in which the world loses itself <note>original: "doth trace it selfe"</note>—is faithfully set down and declared according to the foundation of the *Scriptures* and the *true experience of this Author*. The attainment of this light will give real satisfaction and assurance to the soul, so that it shall not need to say, "Is it true?" or "What arguments are there for it?" or "How 

<meta>catchword: can</meta>

<summary>The author explains that this book is not for the self-satisfied or judgmental, but for sincere seekers who want to practice true Christian virtues. He promises to show a path out of the confusing "labyrinth" of religious controversy through personal spiritual experience and Scripture.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual introversion, repentance, religious controversy, Election, Ordinances, Pharisaical, Christian conduct, spiritual experience</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument from the previous section, emphasizing that true spiritual knowledge is not found through external debate or human logic, but through the internal witness of the Holy Spirit and the "new birth" within the soul.</meta>

# The Preface to the Reader.

...can it be proved, etc.? But it shall find the real **Sign** and **Seal** of truth within itself, as the **Scripture** declares in 1 John 2:20, 27: *That the anointing <note>original: "unction"</note> from the Holy One teaches all things;*
<margin>☛</margin>
so that no one needs to teach those in whom it abides. But this knowledge—this precious **Pearl** of light, this darling of wisdom, this 
<margin>✿</margin>
**Garland** and **Crown** of **Virgin Sophia** <note>In mystical theology, Sophia represents Divine Wisdom.</note>, which surpasses all the beauty and treasure, all the **pomp, power,** and pleasure of *this* **world**; this **universal touchstone** <note>A stone used to test the purity of gold or silver; here, a metaphor for a standard of truth.</note> to test and find out the qualities and virtues of all things; this **heavenly tincture** <note>A term from alchemy referring to a spiritual essence that can transform the soul.</note>; this *true* **Philosopher’s Stone**; this **highest good** <note>original: "Summum bonum"</note>, which no human tongue can express—is only obtained in the **new birth**. By this new birth, the light and life of **God** is renewed in us; the essential **Word** of **divine love**, **Christ** himself, is begotten and formed in us. The *whole* **Scripture** and the **writings** of **divinely illuminated men** <note>original: "illuminate Men" — those who have received spiritual enlightenment.</note> direct us to the reading and experimental <note>Based on direct experience rather than just theory.</note> understanding of this **Word** of **life, light,** and **love.** All words, works, and **books** that do not proceed from *that* **Word**, and do not lead us back to *that* **Word**, are either merely **astral** <note>Related to the stars or the natural, worldly mind as opposed to the divine spirit.</note>, **outward**, and **transitory**; or they are the fantastical **idols of the mind** <note>original: "Idola mentis"</note>, the shadows of fancy, and learned **Pharisaicall reason**. This reason, through its deceitful words of subtlety and human wisdom, entices people to *believe strong delusions* and follow after lies in **hypocrisy**.

In our own **book**, which is the *image of God*...

<sig>a</sig>
<meta>catchword: in</meta>

<summary>The author argues that true spiritual truth is confirmed internally by the Holy Spirit's anointing rather than external proof. He describes this divine wisdom as a "Philosopher's Stone" found only through spiritual rebirth in Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Virgin Sophia, Holy Spirit, new birth, Philosopher's Stone, divine love, Pharisaical reason, spiritual illumination</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the argument that true spiritual enlightenment is internal, emphasizing that the human soul contains the potential for understanding all mysteries through the "new birth" and the indwelling of Christ, rather than through academic study alone.</meta>

# The Preface to the Reader.

...within us, time and eternity and all mysteries are nestled and contained. They can truly be read in our own souls through the enlightenment of the Divine Spirit; for our mind is a true mystical *mirror* and looking-glass of divine and natural mysteries. We shall
<margin>3</margin>
<margin>☞</margin> receive more real knowledge from one effective, innate, and essential glimpse or ray of light arising from the new birth *within us* than from reading many hundreds of authors. By reading those authors, we scrape together an abundance of crafted works and concepts, framing for ourselves a *Babel* <note>A reference to the Tower of Babel; here, it means a confused structure of human pride and language.</note> of knowledge in our theories <note>original: "Notion"</note> and imagination <note>original: "Fanse"—fancy</note>. For in the true light, we receive the pledge and deposit <note>original: "earnest"</note> of *that Spirit* which *searches all things, even the depths of God*. Let no one think that this is <unclear>not</unclear> impossible; for in a Christian who is born again in Christ, Christ—the light of life and the essence of love—dwells within. In *him are all the treasures of wisdom and knowledge*; and he expresses this wisdom in the humble, surrendered <note>original: "resigned"</note> soul, in the manner and measure that pleases him and is most necessary for the current time and age.

These *epistles* are not filled with fine, flattering phrases or pleasing concepts of human art. Nor are they filled with learned quotations from ancient authors or the witty insights of sharp reason, dressed up in *scholarly pomp* and proud words to tickle and delight the reader's imagination. Neither do they taste of a sectarian spirit of hypocrisy...

<summary>The author argues that true divine knowledge is found within the soul through the Holy Spirit and the "new birth," rather than through the accumulation of academic learning. These epistles are presented as sincere expressions of spiritual truth that avoid the prideful "scholarly pomp" of human intellect.</summary>
<keywords>New Birth, Divine Spirit, Babel of Knowledge, Internal Light, Epistles, Mysticism, Resignation, Scholarly Pomp</keywords>

[...reader with] affectation; arrogance and *pedantic* <note>original: "Pedantick"; here meaning an obsessive focus on minor details or formal rules to show off knowledge.</note> presumption, to make himself that which in reality he is not, to get a great name, and to distract and captivate men’s minds with strange, high-flown concepts <note>original: "conceits"</note>. But he has written (according to the divine gift which he received) of the greatest and deepest *mysteries* concerning God and Nature. He has expressed them in such suitable and significant terms and phrases as are best suited to convey them, in their own native and proper idea and meaning, to the understanding and capacity of others. Although *few* will understand them according to the depth of *his meaning* <note>original: "sence"—sense</note>, every one may receive *benefit* according to his capacity—provided that his own image-based imagination <note>original: "fansie"—fancy</note> and the arrogant pride <note>original: "over-weening conceit"</note> of his own light does not bias and hinder him. Without a doubt, his writings are left to posterity as a precious talent <note>A reference to the Parable of the Talents (Matthew 25), implying a spiritual resource that must be used and grown.</note> to be improved. 

We should not only gather a talkative, historical, and literal theory <note>original: "Notion"</note> of the mysteries, but in all simplicity and sincerity, we must practice the way of regeneration in the Spirit of Christ. [...so that it] may be restored to its original purity, being <note>The text likely implies being "freed" or "cleansed" from these distractions.</note> from those idols of fancy and vain reason that do darken and perplex it. Through this, we may come to find out from where the inward radical <term>being</term> <note>original: *Ens*; a Latin term for "essence" or "entity."</note>, working essence, true subsistence, and full existence of everything proceeds. We also learn for what purpose everything has such an essence, life, power, virtue <note>original: "vertue"; here meaning inherent power, efficacy, or medicinal property.</note>, form, and color; and then where it goes and what it shall be hereafter in eternity. By this, we may come to see how it is that all things proceed from **God**, subsist in **God**, and again return to **God**; and in this, we obtain the right knowledge of ourselves and of **God** in nature. 

From this center arises the true knowledge of the *three principles*, and also of the *threefold* or *tri-une life* in **man**. By these, the deepest, darkest, and hardest **questions** and queries <note>original: "Quere's"</note> that can arise within the mind of man—or come within the reach of any imagination or thought—may be rightly understood and determined. In a word, courteous and Christian reader, <margin>☞</margin> these epistles <note>letters</note> will serve as an introduction and accurate information to show you who this author was, where he obtained his great knowledge, and upon what foundation and center it is built. Whether you are learned or unlearned, rich or poor, master or servant, parent or child—no matter your calling, profession, physical temperament <note>original: "complexion"; in the 17th century, this referred to the balance of the four bodily humors.</note>, astrological influence <note>original: "constellation"</note>, or disposition—you may find such knowledge here that may better and *improve* you correctly in Christianity.

Therefore, be like the hardworking bee that seeks honey at every flower; where she does not find it, she does not leave her sharp sting. Similarly, if you find something that you do not like, let it be. Do not leave a taunting scoff, a stinging venom of scorn, or a rash judgment behind you. Instead, *pray* that *God* would give you his *Holy Spirit* to lead you into all truth. # A
## <margin>* Or Letter of Divine Wisdom.</margin> * Theosophical <note>original: "Theosophicall"; referring to the study of divine wisdom or the direct knowledge of God</note> LETTER,
### WHEREIN
->The life of a true CHRISTIAN is described.<-

---

->Showing what a Christian is, and how he comes to be a Christian; and likewise what a titular, nominal, or historical Christian <note>These terms refer to people who are Christian in name only or who believe only the historical facts of the Bible without inner spiritual transformation</note> is, and how the faith and life of each differ.<-

---

> *The fountain of Jesus Christ's heart be our life-giving <note>original: "quick-ning"</note> consolation, renovation, and eternal life.*

---

1. **B**ELOVED and much respected friend in Christ. Out of an obligation like that of members in a body <note>referring to the biblical concept of believers being individual members of the one "Body of Christ," as one branch on a tree is connected to the others</note> and with a desire to work together, I wish for you the open wellspring of grace which God in Christ Jesus has manifested in our humanity. I pray that it may richly spring up in you, and that the Divine Sun may thereby cast the influence of its love-beams into the soul. May it also stir up and open the great magnetic hunger <note>original: "Magnetick hunger"; a term used to describe a powerful, spiritual pull or attraction toward God</note> of the soul (which is the true divine mouth) for Christ’s flesh and blood, along with your bodily welfare.

2. Since I have often understood from your dear friend, **D. K.**, and have also observed during my time with you, that you—through the "drawing of the Father" <note>A reference to the Gospel of John 6:44: "No man can come to me, except the Father which hath sent me draw him"</note>—bear a unique thirst and an earnest, sincere longing after His life, which He, out of His highest love, has manifested...

in <term>Christ Jesus</term>; therefore, I would not (at the request of Mr. D. and also of yourself) omit a member-like and mutual obligation to visit and salute you with a short <term>Epistle</term> <note>original: "Epistle"; a formal letter</note>, and so to recreate, enliven <note>original: "quicken"</note>, and refresh myself somewhat with you in the same fountain of the life of <term>Jesus Christ</term>. <margin>☛</margin> 3. And hereupon, Sir, I will declare to you, out of my small gifts and knowledge: *What a Christian is, and why he is called a Christian*; namely, that he alone is a Christian who has become capable of this high title within himself. He has resigned himself—with his inward ground, mind, and will—to the free grace in <term>Christ Jesus</term>. In the will of his soul, he has become like a young child that only longs for its mother's breasts, sincerely pants after the mother, and sucks the breasts of the mother from which it lives.

4. Even so, that man alone is a Christian whose soul and mind enter again into the first Mother, from whom the life of man has its origin; *namely* <note>original: "Viz." (Videlicet)</note>, into the <term>Eternal Word</term>. This Word has manifested itself with the true milk of salvation in our humanity (which is blind regarding God) and sucks this milk of the Mother into his hungry soul. From this, the new spiritual man arises, and thereby the fiery [dark and dry] soul—proceeding from the <term>Father’s property</term> <note>In the author's theology, this refers to the soul's raw, energetic origin in God the Father's "fire," which must be softened by the "light" of the Son</note>—reaches and obtains the place of <term>God's love</term>. In that place, the <term>Father begets</term> his beloved <term>Son</term>, and there alone the <term>Temple of the Holy Spirit</term>, which dwells in us, is found. There also the spiritual mouth [or believing desire] of the soul, which eats <term>Christ's flesh</term> and drinks his <term>blood</term>, is understood, or experienced and essentially enjoyed.

<margin>☛</margin> 5. For he alone is a Christian in whom <term>Christ</term> dwells, lives, and has his being; in whom <term>Christ</term>, regarding the internal ground of his soul, has arisen and been made alive in the heavenly <term>Essence</term> <note>original: "Essence"; the spiritual substance of the "New Man"</note> which disappeared and departed in <term>Adam</term>. Indeed, I say, he alone is a Christian who has put on <term>Christ's Victory</term> (that is to say, <term>Christ's Incarnation</term>, humanity, sufferings, death, and resurrection, against the anger of <term>God</term>, and also <term>Hell</term>, the <term>Devil</term>, <term>Death</term>, and <term>Sin</term>) in his eternal ground. 6. For in <term>Christ</term> alone we are received as children of <term>God</term> and heirs with <term>Christ</term>. This does not happen through an outward, accidental, or strange appearance of some specific claiming of grace, I say, nor through a—

...[strange] merit of grace [or application of promises] credited from the outside [or received in a historical understanding <note>original: "Historicall apprehension"; a belief based only on facts and history rather than personal spiritual experience</note> of being justified and acquitted by another, like criminals], but through a child-like, regenerating, innate, in-dwelling, member-like, and essential grace. 7. He is far from being a Christian who only comforts himself with the passion, death, and satisfaction of Christ, and applies and credits it to himself as a pardon or gift of favor, yet remains an unregenerated, wild, [worldly, and sensual] beast. Such a "Christian" is every ungodly person; for everyone would gladly be saved through a gift of favor. Even the Devil would very willingly be an angel again by grace received and applied from the outside.

8. But to turn and become like a child, and be born anew of God's grace-water of love and the Holy Spirit—that does not please him. Even so, it does not please the titular Christian <note>original: "Titular Christian"; one who has the title of "Christian" but lacks the internal reality</note>, who will wrap himself in the mantle of Christ's grace [and claim his merits for himself by a historical claim to a promise] and yet will not enter into adoption <note>original: "Adoption"; the spiritual process of being made a child of God</note> and the new birth—even though Christ says that he cannot otherwise see the Kingdom of God.

9. For what is born of the flesh is flesh and cannot inherit the Kingdom of God (*John 3*). To be fleshly-minded is enmity against God, but to be spiritually-minded is life and peace. And only he who is born of God hears God’s Word, for only the spirit of grace in Christ hears God’s Word.

10. For no man has ever seen God; the Son alone, who is in the immeasurable bosom of the Father, declares the Word and Will of God within us. Thus, we hear and understand his will and good pleasure within ourselves and are willing to follow it, yet we are often kept back by the outward, sinful flesh. Therefore, the operation or effect of that same divine power [purpose and godly resolution in our mind] does not always manifest in the outward form <note>original: "Figure"; the external behavior or physical body</note>, yet it enters the inward form in the inward spiritual world, concerning which ***St. Paul*** says, *"Our conversation <note>original: "conversation"; in this context, it means "citizenship" or "conduct of life"</note> is in Heaven."* <margin>☞</margin>

11. Concerning this, all the saints of God, and especially ***St. Paul***, have complained: that they had an earnest, sincere will and served God with the mind of the internal ground <note>original: "internall Ground"; the deepest center of the soul where it meets God</note>, but with the flesh, they served the law of sin. So it is that the flesh lusts against the spirit, and this lust and evil desire <note>original: "concupiscence"</note> is daily drowned and mortified in the death of Christ by the inward ground [or the center of light rege-

[...rege]nerated in the dark abyss of the soul; but this occurs only in those in whom Christ has arisen from death. Thus, "there is no condemnation to those who are in Christ Jesus, who walk not after the flesh, but after the spirit," etc. For the physical body <note>original: "bestiall body"; referring to the animal-like, mortal nature of humans</note> belongs to the earth, but the spiritual body belongs to God. But whoever does not have this spiritual body is dead while he lives, and neither hears nor perceives anything of the Spirit of God; it is "foolishness to him," as the Scripture says.

<margin>☛</margin>
12. Therefore, this point is not sufficiently understood or clarified by merely teaching and maintaining an external receiving and application of grace and the forgiveness of sins. The pardon of sin and our reception into adoption as children of grace consists in the justifying blood and death of Christ, when Christ's heavenly blood infused us <note>original: "tinctured"; a term from alchemy meaning to permeate and transform the inner quality of a substance</note>. With the highest love of the Deity in the name of *Jesus* <note>original: "Jesu"</note>, He overcame the anger of God in our soul and inner divine foundation—which proceeds from the Essence of Eternity—and turned it back into divine humility, meekness, and obedience. By this, the broken, torn, and divided balance <note>original: "temperature"; the specific ratio or harmony of spiritual qualities</note> of our human nature and spirit of obedience and goodwill entered again into the harmony and unity of those qualities. [That is, into the light, love, and life of Paradise—that *True Balance* where variety comes together and agrees in unity.]

<margin>☛</margin>
13. At that very moment, the Father's wrath (which had been awakened in the qualities of our life and had made itself the lord and master of our soul and body, making us dead to the Kingdom of Heaven and "children of wrath") was turned back into the harmonious love, likeness, and unity of God. Our own human will died in the death of Christ, turning away from its self-centeredness <note>original: "selfnesse"</note> and selfish desiring. The original human will (which God breathed from His Spirit into *Adam*) sprang forth anew through the victory of God’s sweetness and love in Christ's heavenly blood. Then the Devil and Hell, which held humanity captive, were put to shame and made a laughingstock. This was symbolized by the dry *Rod of Aaron*, which budded in a single night and bore sweet almonds <note>Numbers 17:8; a biblical sign of God's chosen priesthood and the power of life arising from death</note>.

<margin>☛</margin>
14. Now, just as sin came from one man and spread to everyone, so also the sweet grace and victory in Christ spread, pressed, and pierced from one to all. This death and God's anger were shattered in that one *Adamic* soul in Christ, and the possibility for grace was opened through the breaking apart of death. Through this open gate, the soul's will may re-enter its first Mother (the source from which it originally came), which is the adoption or rebirth of a new life and will. There, it may obtain the sweet blood of Jesus Christ, which in Christ broke open the gate of death and changed the anger...

of God in our humanity, in himself, into love, where the poor captive soul drinks from God's fountain and eases and sweetens its fire-breath. 15. Now, just as the revealing of death had to occur in the person of Christ himself—within our soul and humanity—so that the eternity in Christ (with which he came from heaven and was also in heaven, as in John 3) overcame time (that is, the life and will of time) and changed time and its will into the eternal will of the Deity. All this must be accomplished in our received humanity. Likewise, our soul's desire must receive into itself that same eternal will in Christ (where time and eternity stand in equal agreement and harmony) and, through the power of that will, immerse or cast itself into the adoption of free grace in Christ. This allows that same inward Paradisiacal foundation which died in Adam to spring forth again in the will of Christ's obedience, through his heavenly and human blood assumed from us.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. The atonement and expiation must be made manifest and experienced <note>original: "experimentall"; in the 17th century, this meant personally experienced or known through practice, not just theoretical</note> within ourselves through the atonement Christ once made. Indeed, the atonement and reconciliation were fully finished and accomplished in Christ's blood and death. However, what was once wrought in Christ must also work in me; it must also happen in me now through Christ's shedding of his blood. Christ also pours his heavenly blood into the desire of faith in my poor soul and transforms <note>original: "tinctureth"; an alchemical term for permeating and changing the inner nature of a substance</note> the anger of God within it, so that the first Adamic image of God may appear again and begin to see, hear, feel, taste, and smell.

<margin>☞</margin>
17. For that same image—the true Paradisiacal image—which died in Adam from the heavenly world's essence, does not dwell in the four elements <note>The four elements are earth, air, fire, and water, making up the physical world</note>. Its essence and life do not reside in this world but in heaven (which is manifest in Christ within us). That is, it lives in that one pure, holy Element from which the four elements sprang at the beginning of time. This same inward, new spiritual man eats Christ's flesh and blood; for he exists and lives in Christ. Christ is his trunk <note>original: "stock"; the main stem of a plant into which a graft is inserted</note>, and he is a branch on that trunk.

18. For every spirit eats from that which gave it its origin. The animal [sensual] mortal soul eats from the spirit of this world—from the stars and elements, and from the kingdom of the world. But the true eternal soul (which was breathed out of the Eternal Word into man as divine life) also eats from its mother—that is, from the holy, essential Word of God.

<summary>Boehme explains that Christ's historical atonement must be personally experienced within the soul to restore the "Adamic image" lost at the Fall. He distinguishes between the mortal soul, which is nourished by the physical world, and the eternal soul, which is nourished by the Word of God.</summary>
<keywords>regeneration, atonement, mysticism, Adamic image, four elements, blood of Christ, internal transformation, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s "First Epistle," explaining the internal process of salvation. Boehme argues that Christ must be spiritually born within the individual, rather than being an external legalistic "imputation" of righteousness.</meta>

<page-num>6</page-num>
<header>The First Epistle</header>
<meta>Catchword: ven,</meta>

19. Since it was impossible for the soul—after its own departure and separation from God, in its excluded condition, nature <note>original: "property"; here meaning the essential characteristics or qualities of the soul</note>, and spirits—to eat of the **Divine Word** and live in the holy element of love and humility, this same Word of life (being the soul's true Mother) came forth to the abandoned soul. It entered into the valley of misery and the prison of hell, and infused its heavenly essence into our human essence to serve as a body for the soul. <margin>☛</margin> With this, the Word embraced and clothed our poor captive soul and opened its heavenly mouth again with the tincture <note>original: "tincture"; in alchemy and Boehme’s theology, this refers to a spiritualizing or transformative essence</note> of love, which was previously dead in the anger of God. Now, the poor soul can once again eat heavenly *Manna*. This eating was tested in Christ’s person within the humanity he received from us during the temptation of Christ in the wilderness. There, Adam—acting through Christ—did again eat *Manna* from Paradise for forty days.

20. Therefore, I say that no one becomes a true Christian through a mere show or a specific form of grace that is simply assigned or "imputed" from the outside. If someone is truly a Christian, his sins are not forgiven simply by someone speaking a word, or by him claiming a specific form or promise of absolution for himself from the outside. A Lord or Prince in this world might freely grant a criminal his life through an external favor, but this accomplishes nothing before God.

21. There is no grace or favor by which we reach adoption <margin>☞</margin> except only in the blood and death of Christ. God has foreordained and appointed him alone to be a throne of grace or a propitiation <note>original: "propitiation"; an offering that turns away anger or brings reconciliation</note> in his own love, which he has infused into him in the *Sweet Name Jesus* <note>original: "Jesu"</note> out of *Jehovah*. He is the only sacrifice that God accepts to reconcile his anger.

22. Now, if this sacrifice or free offering of grace is to do me <margin>☞</margin> any good, then it must be worked and brought to pass within me. The Father must beget or impress his Son into the desire of my faith, so that the hunger of my faith may lay hold of and comprehend him. If the hunger of my soul’s faith does comprehend him in his promised Word, then I "put him on" throughout the entire process of justification within my inward ground <note>original: "ground"; the deepest, fundamental center of the human spirit</note>. Immediately, the mortification <note>original: "mortification"; the "killing off" of the sinful nature</note> and destruction of anger, the Devil, Death, and Hell begins and moves forward through the power of Christ’s death in me.

23. For I can do nothing; I am dead to myself, but Christ <margin>☚</margin> does it in me. When he arises in me, then I am dead to my own self in regard to the "true man" <note>the spiritual self</note>, and he is my life. In that I live, I live for him and not for my selfhood <note>original: "selfehood"; the ego or the self-centered will separated from God</note>. Grace mortifies my own will and sets itself up as Lord in the place of my selfness <note>original: "selfenesse"</note>, so that I may be an instrument of God, who does with me whatever he pleases.

24. And then I live in two kingdoms: namely <note>original: "Viz."</note>, with my newborn spirit of life, or spiritual man, in Paradise—in the Kingdom of Heaven—

<summary>Boehme describes salvation as an internal transformation where Christ's "Word of life" enters the human soul to defeat its inherent "anger." He emphasizes that true Christianity is not an external legal status but a living process where the individual's "selfhood" dies so that Christ can live within them.</summary>

He argues that true Christianity involves sharing in Christ’s suffering to overcome the internal "anger of God."</meta>

<page-num>7</page-num>
## The First Epistle.

...even in the inward spiritual world, and with the outward mortal man, in the vanity of time, in the kingdom of this world, in the dominion of the stars and elements, in the contrariety and malignant discord of the properties where the yoke of sin still lives; this Christ takes upon himself in the inward kingdom of the divine world and helps my soul to bear it.

<margin>☛</margin>
25. For the yoke of this world is Christ’s burden, which he must bear until he shall again deliver up the kingdom to his Father, which his Father has given him. For he said, *All power in heaven and earth is given to me by my Father*; so also this burden or charge is laid upon him, that he should bear God’s anger, hell, death, and all evil in us, as *Isaiah* says: *He took on him our diseases and carried our sorrows*; but we esteemed him stricken, smitten by God, and afflicted. <note>original: "Isaiah 53:4"</note>

<margin>☛</margin>
26. And this is why a Christian must be a bearer of the cross; for as soon as Christ is born in him, the assaulting of hell and the anger of God in the eternal nature begins. Then, the hell within man is destroyed, and the Serpent <note>the biblical serpent, representing the devil or original sin</note> is trodden underfoot. Because of this, great unrest, persecution, and reproach from the Devil and the corrupt world arise against the outward sinful man. Even then, the outward sinful man must allow himself to be condemned and judged to damnation by the "children of anger" and by God’s severe justice in that anger—seeing that another man <note>the "new man," or the regenerated spiritual self</note> lives in him, who is not like or conformable to the outward mortal man. And so, God’s justice executes its judgment in anger upon the "house of sin," and all the ministers of God’s wrath help in that execution.

27. And even then, Christ helps to bear the yoke, and the person is sacrificed in Christ’s process <note>original: "processe"; here referring to the sequence of Christ’s life, suffering, and death as a model for the believer’s own transformation</note>, contempt, and scorn—in his suffering and death—to the justice of God in his anger, and becomes conformable to Christ’s image.

28. The Holy Scripture testifies in all places that we are justified from sins by faith in Christ, and not by the works of our own merit, but by the blood and death of Christ. This is indeed taught by many, but rightly understood by few of those who teach it to us.

29. We are indeed taught an <term>imputed grace</term> <note>the theological doctrine that Christ's righteousness is legally credited to a believer, rather than being an internal change</note>, but as for what faith is, how it is begotten, and what its essence, real being, and substance are—and how it takes hold of the merit of Christ along with grace—here the greatest part of teachers are mute and blind. They depend on an <term>historical faith</term> <note>James 2; a faith based merely on accepting historical facts or scripture as true, without internal spiritual transformation</note> which is only a bare knowledge or literal conjecture. With this, the "man of sin" <note>the unregenerate, sinful nature</note> tickles and comforts himself; through imagination and blind persuasion, he flatters and soothes himself, calling himself a Christian, even though he has not yet become either capable or worthy of

<meta>The text continues on the next page with: "this"</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains that true faith is not merely a "historical" belief or a legalistic "imputed grace," but an internal process where Christ helps the believer bear the "yoke" of God's anger and the world's persecution to destroy the sinful nature.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s discussion of the "new man" born in Christ. He distinguishes between those who are merely Christians in name and those who have undergone a genuine internal transformation through the surrender of their will to God.</meta>

<page-num>8</page-num>

<header>The First Epistle.</header>

...this so high a title, but is only a Christian in name and title, externally clothed with Christ’s purple mantle <note>original: "Purpur-Mantle"; referring to the purple robe of mockery placed on Christ, symbolizing a superficial faith that hides an unchanged heart.</note>; of whom the Prophet speaks, saying, *They draw near to me with their lips, but their heart is far from me*: And Christ said, *Not all that say Lord, Lord, shall enter into the Kingdom of Heaven, but they that do the will of my Father in Heaven.*

30. Now Christ alone is the will of the Father in whom acceptance, grace, and adoption consist; and no one can do the loving will of the Father except for that throne of grace alone—Christ himself. As the holy Scripture declares, no man can call God "Lord" without the Holy Spirit in him.

31. For we do not know what and how to pray before God as we ought, but he, even the Holy Spirit in Christ, makes intercession for us with unutterable sighs before God within ourselves, as it pleases God. We cannot attain anything by our own willing and knowing; he is too deeply hidden from us, for it lies not in any man’s knowing, willing, running, or searching, but in God’s mercy.

<margin>☛</margin>
32. Now there is no mercy except in Christ, and if I am to reach that mercy, then I must reach Christ within me. Are my sins to be destroyed in me? Then Christ must do it in me with his blood and death, with his victory over Hell. *Am I to believe? Then the spirit, desire, and will of Christ must believe in my desire and will, for I cannot believe [on my own].*

33. *But he receives my will when it is surrendered to him, and includes it in his own will, bringing it through his victory into God; and there he intercedes for the will of my soul within his own will before God. And so I am received as a child of grace in his will of love.*

34. For the Father has revealed his love in Christ, and Christ reveals that same love in my will when it is surrendered to him. Christ draws my will into himself, clothes it with his blood and death, and <term>tinctures</term> <note>Boehme uses the alchemical term "tincture" to describe the spiritual transformation where Christ’s divine nature permeates and changes the quality of the human soul, much like a dye or an alchemical elixir.</note> it with the highest power of the Divine, and so it is changed into an angelic image and receives a Divine life.

35. And immediately that same life begins to hunger after its body—which body is the degenerated <term>fiery soul</term> <note>In Boehme's system, the soul is a "fire" that has become harsh and dark through sin; it must be "tempered" or enlightened by the love of Christ to find peace.</note> into which the will in Christ has entered—so that the new life in Christ now also <term>tinctures</term> the soul. By this, the soul (in the spirit and property of this [<gloss>new</gloss>] will) obtains a true Divine hunger and is made to long after and desire Divine grace. She <note>The soul was traditionally referred to using feminine pronouns.</note> begins to behold and consider in the spirit of this Divine will in Christ what she is, and how she, in her properties, inclinations, and dispositions, has departed from God and lies captive in the wrath of God. Then she acknowledges her abominations and also her deformity in the presence of God’s Angels, and finds that she has nothing with which...

<meta>catchword: she</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains that true faith is not merely a title but an internal transformation where Christ’s will replaces the human will. He describes the soul's "hunger" for God and its subsequent recognition of its own fallen, "fiery" state when illuminated by Christ's spirit.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s description of the soul's transformation. Having surrendered its own self-will to Christ, the soul now begins to experience a spiritual rebirth and a direct, internal connection to the divine nature.</meta>

<page-num>9</page-num>

<header>The First Epistle.</header>

she is able to protect herself; for she sees that she stands in the jaws of death and hell, encompassed by evil spirits which continually shoot the strong influence of their evil desires into her to destroy and corrupt her.

36. And then she dives into that same newborn spirit of the will and wholly immerses herself into humility itself. Thus, the spirit of Christ takes hold of her and brings her into the spirit of this new will, so that the soul essentially and experimentally feels and finds him. At this point, a divine glimpse and beam of joy arises in the soul—a new eye—in which the dark, fiery soul conceives the <term>Being</term> <note>original Latin: "Ens"; a philosophical term referring to the core substance or essence of a thing.</note> and essence of the divine light within herself. Thereupon, she hungers and thirsts after the grace of God, enters into an effective repentance and sorrow, and mourns the evil she has committed.

37. And in this hunger and thirst, she receives Christ’s flesh and blood; for the spirit of the new will (which in the beginning entered into the grace of Christ, and which Christ received into himself) now becomes substantial and essential through the <term>magnetic attraction</term> <note>original: "magnetick impressure"; the spiritual force by which the soul draws the divine nature into itself.</note>, hunger, and desire of the soul.

38. And this essential reality is called <term>Wisdom</term> <note>original Greek: "Sophia"; in this context, it represents the divine presence or the "body" of Christ that dwells within the believer.</note>, being the essential wisdom or the body of Christ; and in this, faith in the Holy Spirit consists. *In this, Christ and the soul believe on the same foundation.*

<margin>☞</margin>
39. For true faith does not consist in thoughts or in merely assenting to the history: *namely*, that a man <insert>imagines in his mind</insert> that Christ died for his sins, <insert>and by a historical understanding or blind persuasion of a particular election clings to Christ's merits and righteousness, without the inborn righteousness of Christ and the life of God essentially working in him and birthing him as a new creature</insert>. For faith is a receiving of the promised grace in Christ; it receives Christ into itself. It draws him into its hunger, along with his heavenly flesh and blood and the grace which God offers in Christ.

40. Christ feeds the soul with the essence of <term>Wisdom</term> <note>original: "Sophia"</note>, *namely*, with his own flesh and blood, according to his saying: *Whoever does not eat of the flesh of the Son of Man has no life in him, but whoever eats the same remains in him.*

41. And in this, the <term>Covenants</term> <note>original: "Testaments"; referring to the spiritual promise and the sacraments of Christ.</note> of Christ and also the right Christian faith consist. For an unsubstantial <note>meaning: unessential, speculative, or merely verbal</note> faith is like a glimmering fire smothered in smoke or moisture; it would willingly burn but has no proper <term>fuel</term> <note>original: "Ens"</note> for it. But when the right substance is given to it, then that little spark of fire increases, from which a beautiful, shining light arises. Then it is manifest that such a fire and beautiful light lay hidden in the wood, which was not known before.

<summary>This page explains that true faith is not just intellectual belief but a substantial internal process where the soul "eats" Christ's spiritual essence to be reborn. This transformation turns the soul's "dark fire" into a "shining light" through humility and divine grace.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Boehme's explanation of how the human soul is transformed by divine light. He uses the metaphor of a germinating seed and the sun's influence on the earth to describe the process of spiritual rebirth.</meta>

<page-num>10</page-num>
<header>The First Epistle.</header>

<margin>
* Wick of a candle, or smoking flax. <note>A reference to Isaiah 42:3; a metaphor for a flickering or weak faith that God will not extinguish.</note>

* That which lies hidden, and yet exists in *actuality* <note>original Latin: *Esse*</note>, as well as in *potential* <note>original Latin: *Potentia*</note>.
</margin>

42. And this should be understood in the same way for a child of God: while the poor soul is wrapped up in the anger of God, it is like a glimmering <term>wick</term> <note>original: "Wiek"</note> that would willingly burn but cannot because of the vanity of sins and the anger of God. But when the soul—being a little spark of divine fire—receives into itself God’s Love-<term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ens"; the spiritual substance or "being" of God.</note>, *that is*, Christ’s flesh and blood, then that spark grows into a great fire and light. This light shines and sends forth its brilliance through beautiful virtues and good works; it lives with great patience under the vanity of this world, and yet it grows like a beautiful flower out of the wild earth.

43. We have a comparison for this in the Sun and the Earth. Namely, if the Sun did not shine upon the Earth, no fruit could grow. But when the Sun shines upon the Earth and penetrates into the <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ens"</note> of the Earth, then the essence of the Earth receives the virtue or powerful influence of the Sun into itself. Because of this, a great hunger arises in the essence of the Earth for the virtue of the Sun. This hunger impresses itself upon and feeds upon the Sun’s virtue and influence. By this hunger of the Earth's essence, which eagerly reaches after the <term>essence</term> of the Sun, a herb is drawn out of the earth with a stalk. In this stalk, the essence and virtue of the Sun also ascends during the growth and flourishing of the herb. Thus the Sun, with his beams of light, becomes substantial within the essence of the earth in the stalk and root. We see how, through the power of the Sun and stars in the spirit of the world, another body arises out of the stalk that is different from the root in the earth. We also see how the stalk puts forth a bud into a beautiful flower, and afterwards into fruit; and we see how the Sun, from time to time, ripens and sweetens that same fruit.

44. And so it is with man: the ground of the soul is the divine field. When it receives the divine sunshine into itself, a divine plant springs forth. This is the new birth of which Christ speaks. Now, this plant must be nourished from above by celestial influence and drawn up in growth by the divine Sun and the divine water. It must be preserved by the divine <term>constellation</term> <note>original: "constellation"; here referring to the spiritual alignment or governing power of God's virtues.</note>, *that is*, the divine virtue or power of God, until it becomes a divine body of a divine and angelic figure, like the body on the stalk.

45. And just as the body on the stalk must stand in rain, wind, and storms, in heat and cold, and allow the Sun to ripen it, so must a Christian stand in this thorny world—in the awakened anger of God, in the kingdom of the Devil, and among many wicked people. He must allow himself to be beaten with scorn and contempt, and yet he must turn his hope and confidence away from all creatures

<meta>catchword: only</meta>

<summary>Boehme compares the spiritual growth of a Christian to a plant's growth, explaining how the soul's "essence" (Ens) must absorb divine light to grow from a weak spark into a fruit-bearing divine body. He emphasizes that this growth occurs despite the "storms" of a hostile, sinful world.</summary>

<page-num>11</page-num>

only and alone upon the Divine Sun, and allow it to ripen him and bring him forth as heavenly fruit.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Which is only empty talk <note>original: "lip-labour"</note>.</margin>
46. It is not temples made of stone houses, human ordinances, or outward forms of *mere lip-service* <note>original: "Word worship"</note> that give birth to [or regenerate] a person into a newness of life. Instead, it is the Divine Sun in the divine constellation of the powers of the Word of God <note>The "Word" or *Logos* represents God’s creative and life-giving power.</note> within the temple of Jesus Christ—inside the person himself—that gives him birth out of the fountain of life, Jesus Christ. Thus, he becomes a branch on the vine of Christ and brings forth good grapes, which the Divine Sun ripens so that God's children (being his loving fellow-members) may eat them. In this way, they also grow and flourish in and with him; these "grapes" are good doctrine, a holy life, and good works.

47. A person must progress to the practice, effective performance, and productivity of Christianity; otherwise, the new birth is not yet visible in him, nor is the noble branch yet born. No amount of flattery or soothing, comforting oneself with promises or evidence from Scripture, or boasting of one's faith, is of any use at all if that faith does not make him a child who conforms to God in essence and will—a faith that brings forth divine fruit.

48. Everything that men now strive, dispute, contend, and fight about—thereby destroying and laying waste to countries and people—is only a mere husk without fruit [a form without power, a pursuit of wealth <term>Mammon</term> <note>original: "Mammon"; a biblical term for wealth, often personified as a false god of greed.</note> without Christian mercy]. It belongs to the fiery world for separation [and shall be decided in the judgment of the Lord]. There is no true understanding in any party; they all contend only about the name and the will of God, yet no party will actually do His will. They care for nothing but their own glory, advancements, and the pleasures of the flesh; if they were true Christians, they would have no strife or contention.

49. A good tree bears good fruit for everyone. Even though it must sometimes allow the wind to break off its branches and fruit, and the Sun to wither and dry them up—and even when they are ripe, to be devoured by swine or trodden underfoot—despite all this, it still endeavors to bring forth more good fruit.

<margin>☞</margin>
50. And thus a true Christian in Christ can desire nothing else but what Christ desires in him. And though he must allow his good fruit (which springs and grows out of the internal man) to be often trampled upon and spoiled by the evil flesh and blood, and also by the "Devil's wind" [the wicked censure and false interpretation of the hypocritical <term>Luciferian Serpent</term> <note>A reference to the pride and deceit associated with Lucifer/Satan.</note>] and the wickedness of the world; yet the tree of the new plant in the life of Christ still stands and springs forth through the outward mortal man, despite <term>maugre</term> <note>original: "maugre"; an archaic word meaning "in spite of."</note> all opposition, irresistibly. It is just as eternity springs through time and gives life and power to time, and as the day...

<summary>Boehme argues that true Christianity is found in internal spiritual fruitfulness and the "new birth" within the soul, rather than in outward ceremonies or religious conflict. He criticizes those who fight over doctrine while failing to live out God's will through mercy and good works.</summary>

--- Page 30 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's explanation of the internal spiritual transformation, comparing the arrival of divine light in the soul to daybreak overcoming the night.</meta>

<page-num>13</page-num>
<header>The First Epistle.</header>

## The First Epistle.

50. ...arises and breaks forth through the night, and changes the night into day, and yet the night remains there still in itself, and yet it is not known or discerned in the day. So likewise the Divine day does spring and bud forth in us through our eternal night; and changes the night—specifically, God’s Anger, Hell, Death, Anguish, and eternal destruction—into the Divine Day of joy and consolation. This happens even though the dark night, with the <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for "being" or "essence," here referring to the fallen nature inherited from the "Serpent" in Eden</note> of the Serpent and the poison in flesh and blood, rages and struggles against it.

<margin>☞</margin>
51. Therefore, beloved Sir and Christian Brother, we have more need to endeavor for the growth and increase of the precious <term>Coral</term> <note>original: "Corall"; likely a metaphor for the "pearl of great price" or the growth of the Christ-spirit within the soul</note>, and to learn how we may attain it, than to run after the unprofitable chatterings and speculative fictions of a fake holiness. In that state, one brother despises, reproaches, rejects, and proclaims the other a heretic—even giving him over to the Devil—all for the sake of an <term>Opinion</term> <note>original: "Opinion"; Boehme uses this to describe intellectual religious dogma that lacks living spiritual power</note> which he has made for himself.

52. I tell and declare to you, by the knowledge which God has given me: it is a mere deceit of the Devil, who brings us poor men into <term>Opinions</term> to make us despise and reproach one another. He leads us to cry down and hold one another as <term>Heretics</term> and <term>Fantasticks</term> <note>original: "Fantasticks"; a term for those perceived as following "fantasies" or unconventional spiritual ideas</note> if someone does not bind his conscience to our mental <term>Idol</term> or opinion. Thus, we strive and wrangle about the "husk" [the outer form of religion], and in the meantime lose love and faith, and fail to attain the new birth.

<margin>☞</margin>
53. Our whole religion is but a child-like work: namely, that we wholly forsake and disclaim our own knowing, willing, running, disputing [and the manufactured conclusions of blind, hypocritical Reason, which bewitches the natural mind into the shape and frame of its own wisdom]. Instead, we must sincerely and fully resolve within ourselves to enter into, and persevere in, the way which brings us back to our own native country which we lost [in Adam, when he, with his mind and full will, went out of Paradise into the spirit of this transitory world]. We must return to our <term>Mother</term> <note>original: "Mother"; a reference to Divine Wisdom, or "Sophia," who Boehme believes is the spiritual source of the soul</note>, who in the beginning conceived us and brought us forth out of herself.

<margin>☞</margin>
54. Now if we will do this, then we must not come to her in our own self-will and way—in pride and ostentation, in applauding ourselves, or in contempt of her children, our fellow-Christians and fellow-members. For we are the lost <term>Son</term> <note>original: "Sonne"; referring to the parable of the Prodigal Son</note> who has become a <term>Swineherd</term> and has shamefully misspent our <term>Patrimony</term> <note>original: "Patrimony"; inheritance</note> with the fattened <term>Hogs</term> of the Devil and the <term>World</term>. We must enter into ourselves and carefully consider ourselves and our <term>Father’s house</term>. We <margin>* Which loves all our brothers through our enemies <note>Boehme teaches that true divine love is impartial and extends even to enemies.</note>.</margin> must set before us the mirror of the <term>Law</term> and <term>Gospel</term> and see how far we have departed from God's righteousness, and also from [sincere, impartial] * brotherly love, and well examine our heart to see what it is inclined toward.

<summary>Boehme argues that true Christianity is a simple, child-like return to God's love, warning that religious infighting over "opinions" and dogmas is a trap of the Devil that prevents spiritual rebirth. He compares the fallen human state to the Prodigal Son and calls for a humble examination of the heart rather than intellectual pride.</summary>

<keywords>new birth, sectarianism, Prodigal Son, divine light, internal transformation, Divine Wisdom, Sophia, religious tolerance</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Boehme's internal anatomy of the soul, identifying specific vices—pride, greed, envy, and anger—as symbolic "beasts" that inhabit the human psyche in its fallen state.</meta>

<page-num>13</page-num>
<header>The First Epistle.</header>

55. Now, when we do this, we shall find in ourselves many hundred evil beasts, which we have set up in God’s place, and which we honor and love more than God. Even then, we shall see what hideous, horrible beasts were made manifest in *Adam* by false lust or <term>imagination</term> <note>original: "imagination"; in Boehme’s mystical theology, this refers to the soul’s power to "image" or shape its own internal reality and desires, which can lead it toward either God or self-centeredness.</note>; and why God said to *Adam*, *The Seed of the Woman shall break the Serpent’s head*—that is to say, these monstrous beasts.

56. First, we shall see in our desire the proud *Lucifer*, who has departed from divine and brotherly humility and treats the members of his body with contempt. He has set himself up to be a god and lord over them, and in him there is no divine love to love either God or his brother.

<margin>Or, everything.</margin>
57. Secondly, we shall find a beast in our <term>properties</term> <note>original: "propertyes"; the internal qualities, drives, or characteristics that make up a person's nature.</note> resembling the covetous, greedy swine, which wants to take everything for itself and alone devour and possess all. It desires more than it needs, so that the proud *Lucifer* might be able to boast and vaunt himself—pretending that he is a god over \* *substance*, and that he can domineer, having power and might over his fellow branches. We shall see how this proud *Lucifer* has broken himself off from the Tree of Life and the mutual growth and increase of love, and wants to be a tree unto himself. Therefore, he is a withered, dry branch in the eyes of God.

58. Thirdly, we shall find the poisonous, envious serpent in our properties, which tears and rages like a poison. I mean envy, which wishes no man as well as itself; it stings, rages, and rides in other men’s hearts and slanders them with words. It only applauds its own haughty *Lucifer* and calls its own falsehood <insert>and cunning hypocrisy</insert> an "angel of God."

59. Fourthly, we shall find in our properties the fiery dragon sitting in hellish fire—I mean *Anger*. If covetousness and envy cannot obtain what they want, anger will lay violent hands upon it and take it by force. It is so raging mad that it destroys its own life out of pure malice and iniquity, and breaks into pieces in fiery malice. It is a very dry branch on the tree, fit only for the fire.

60. Fifthly, we shall find many hundred evil beasts in our desire, which *Pride* loves and honors more than God. Covetousness draws them to itself as a treasure, with which *Pride* boasts and vaunts as if they were gods. By doing this, a man withdraws sustenance and comfort from his brother’s life, so that the brother is forced to spend his life in misery, trouble, and perplexity because of this violence and extortion.

61. Now, when a man beholds himself in this mirror of falsehood and perceives these evil beasts, then let him truly

<meta>catchword: Imagine</meta>

<summary>Boehme provides a mystical anatomy of the fallen human soul, personifying vices like pride, greed, envy, and anger as "beasts" (the Luciferian pride, the greedy swine, the envious serpent, and the fiery dragon) that have replaced the divine image within.</summary>
<keywords>Lucifer, Adam, internal beasts, pride, greed, envy, anger, Tree of Life, spiritual anatomy, psychology, covetousness, hypocrisy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Boehme's internal anatomy of the human soul, moving from specific vices to the cosmic origin of these "beasts" in the fall of Adam and the influence of the Serpent.</meta>

<header>The First Epistle.</header>

imagine to himself that he is deeply guilty of them, and thereby consider the grievous fall of *Adam*, and think that all these evil desires and vain imaginations take their rise and origin wholly in him, from the Monster of the Serpent, through the influence and insinuation of the Devil into our first parents.

<margin>☞</margin>
62. For all the properties of the desires existed in one harmony and steadfast unity in *Adam*; and one loved the other, and they were in mutual agreement. But such false desires have arisen in man through the envy of the Devil, who stirred up the false lust and imagination in *Adam* and *Eve* to experience inequality [in the property and spirit of the mixed world] and to taste what good and evil was; to feel heat and cold, and to try the multitude and variety of the properties. So now these desires attract, long after, and desire that which is like themselves; and every desire of these properties is a separate hunger of life in man, which has broken itself off from the harmonious unity. It opposes the love, likeness, and mutual society of its fellow branches, or brothers, and covetously desires to draw their life and maintenance to itself, to make itself a lord over them, and wants to be a self-seeker <note>original: "Selfeist"; one who acts out of self-interest or ego rather than communal or divine unity.</note>.

63. All of this is contrary to the Divine will and foundation, and is a perjury against God; yes, it even opposes the course and order of nature. We see this by the trees and plants of the earth; how they all stand and grow lovingly one by another, and rejoice in one Mother; and how one branch on the tree imparts its sap and power to the others and they mutually serve each other.

64. For in such a lovely equality and harmony the life of man (*John 1*) was inspired out of the eternal Word into the human image, being made out of the *limus* <note>original: "Limus"; Latin for mud, slime, or clay, referring to the "dust of the ground" from which Adam was formed in Genesis 2:7.</note> of the earth. So all the properties of life stood in an equal proportion in the <term>temperature</term> <note>original: "temperature"; in Boehme’s thought, this refers to a state of perfect balance where no single quality or desire dominates the others.</note>; in one love, and they mutually loved each other.

65. But when the Devil mixed his poison and false desire into it, the properties of life were divided into many desires. From this, strife, discord, sickness, infirmities, the burdensome coarseness, and mortal frailty of the body have arisen through the false desire and the introduction of the bestial properties. Because of this, the Image of God (which was from the heavenly essence) disappeared; concerning which God said to them, *In the day that you eat of the tree of the knowledge of good and evil, you shall die the death*: That is, you shall die to the Kingdom of God, as it also came to pass.

<margin>* Or, Consider within ourselves.</margin>
66. And we must really *imagine to ourselves that this bestial, false desire in man is the Monster of the Serpent, and an enmity against God and the Kingdom of Heaven, and that we therein are only the children of Hell and of the anger of God, and cannot

<summary>Boehme explains how the fall of Adam shattered the original harmony of human desires, turning man into a self-seeking "monster" driven by competing "bestial" properties rather than divine unity. He contrasts this fallen state with the natural cooperation seen in trees and plants, which still reflect the original order of creation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s explanation of the "beastly" desires within the soul, arguing that these internal vices belong to the realm of "Hell" or "darkness" and must be replaced by a new spiritual birth through Christ.</meta>
