--- Page 55 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme’s explanation of his spiritual journey following the publication of his first work, *Aurora*. He describes his initial reluctance to write again after facing persecution, and how his spiritual "internal man" eventually overmastered his rational "external man," leading to further revelations.</meta>

<page-num>37</page-num>
<header>The Third Epistle.</header>

...that the Spirit of God showed and foretold to me so much beforehand; so that I know what His counsel is in His way. Therefore, I willingly and patiently yielded myself under the Cross <note>original: "Crosse"; here referring to a period of spiritual suffering or trials</note> and committed my cause to God, often entreating Him that if it did not come entirely from His own counsel, to take it from me, and not let me know or understand anything of that kind, or to proceed in that way.

<margin>☞</margin>
7. I also intended (after the Persecution <note>Referring to the opposition Boehme faced from local church authorities in Görlitz after his first book, *Aurora*, began to circulate.</note>) not to write anymore, but only to keep myself quiet in obedience to my God, and to let the Devil roar over me with his scorn, revilement, and derision. Many a hard combat was fought against him, and I cannot well tell or declare what I endured; but it happened to me as with a grain of seed sown into the earth, which against all reason springs up anew in all storms and tempests. Whereas in the winter everything seems dead, and reason says, "now all is gone," so the precious grain of mustard-seed <note>A biblical metaphor for small beginnings that lead to great spiritual growth (Matthew 13:31-32).</note> sprouted again despite all dispraise, contempt, disdain, and derision, like a lily. It returned with a hundred-fold increase, and also with deeper and more unique knowledge, and came forth again in a fiery prompting <note>original: "instigation"</note> or forcible driving.

8. But my *external* man <note>The physical or worldly self.</note> would write no more; it was somewhat discouraged and timid, until it reached the point where the *internal* man <note>The spiritual or divine self.</note> captivated and overpowered the external. Even then, the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Great Mystery" / *Mysterium Magnum*; a central concept in Boehme's theosophy referring to the hidden source of all things.</note> *appeared*. Then I understood God's counsel and threw myself upon His will. I would not invent or fake anything out of human reason, nor would I give any more place to reason; instead, I resigned my will to God's will, so that my reason might be as if it were dead—so that He (the Spirit of God) might do and work what and how He pleased. I willed nothing through my own reason, so that it might be His will and deed alone.

<margin>☞</margin>
9. And when this was done, the internal man was armed and received a very faithful guide, to whom I wholly yielded my reason. I did not study or invent anything, nor did I allow reason to dictate what I should write—except only that which the Spirit showed me, as if in a great *depth* and a full *chaos* in the *Mystery* <note>Boehme uses "Chaos" here in a specialized sense, meaning a formless state of potential from which all things arise.</note>. Yet this was without my full comprehension, for the creature is not like God, who performs and comprehends all things at *once* in His wisdom.

10. And then there was a purpose in me again to write something, and in the space of nine months *three books* were made. One was concerning the three principles of the *Divine Essence* <note>This refers to Boehme's work *The Three Principles of the Divine Essence* (1619).</note>—that is, the Being of all beings—in which the Great *Mystery* has somewhat opened and revealed itself. In that book, many excellent things are contained that are much deeper than in this first work [namely, the *Aurora*].

<summary>Jacob Boehme describes how, after a period of silence following persecution, he was supernaturally compelled to write again, leading to the creation of three new books in nine months. He emphasizes that these writings were not products of his own rational mind but were dictated by the Spirit of God from the depths of the "Great Mystery."</summary>

### The Third Epistle.

(which your Honor has sent here for me to examine) and it has about a hundred sheets of paper.

11. After this, one was produced containing sixty sheets, which treats the threefold life of man <note>Boehme refers to his book *The Threefold Life of Man* (1620), which discusses the three realms of existence: the divine world of light, the dark world of God’s wrath, and the visible material world.</note> and also the whole of Creation. It is a great open gate to the Mystery <term>In Boehme’s writing, the "Mystery" (often *Mysterium Magnum*) refers to the hidden, unmanifested nature of God from which all creation proceeds.</term>, and it is truly a wonder that surpasses and goes beyond the reach of all human reason. I myself, in my own reason, wonder and marvel at what God intends to do—that He uses such a humble <note>original: "meane"</note> instrument for such weighty matters. For in that work, the Mysteries are revealed and laid out—the very matters about which the world has contended and fought ever since the heavy fall of Adam. Yet until now, such a fundamental ground has not been brought to light; nevertheless, it shall not be understood by the world, but by the children of God, as it is already made manifest and known.

12. And then thirdly, forty questions were sent to me by a learned and understanding man who is also a lover of the Mystery and a great friend of it. He encouraged me to answer them according to these gifts and spirit. These are indeed very high questions, and they contain within them the great depths and secrets of the origin of the soul <note>These answers became Boehme's book *Forty Questions on the Soul* (1620), written for Balthasar Walther, a director of a chemical laboratory who traveled widely seeking spiritual knowledge.</note> and all the secrets or mysteries of the Mystery. Upon these questions, an answer has been brought forth in which the world might well rejoice, if the anger, iniquity, and malice of the Devil did not hinder it; yet the counsel of God must stand.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. Now, because I perceive that your noble mind and heart have a singular hunger and thirst for such Mysteries, and that you disregard the world which despises them, I acknowledge God's counsel in this. It is my solemn duty to share these things with you, for bread belongs to the children and they are worthy of it, but the "pearl must not be cast before swine" <note>A reference to Matthew 7:6, suggesting that sacred knowledge should only be shared with those who will respect and understand it.</note>. My spirit and mind show me clearly enough that your Honor does not search for such things out of curiosity, but by the prompting and guidance of the Spirit, which often leads Peter to Cornelius <note>A biblical reference to Acts 10, where God directs the Apostle Peter to visit the Roman centurion Cornelius, showing that God's message is for all who seek it sincerely.</note> so that he may tell and declare to him the words of eternal life.

14. And though I am a stranger and very simple, your desire and will embolden me to write to your Honor, albeit with a simple hand <insert>in a plain and coarse phrase and style</insert> (for God's gifts are not bound to any human arts). I am the more bold with you because I perceive that your noble heart appears so low and humble as to send for me, who am but a lowly and rejected <note>original: "meane and abject"</note> person. But seeing it is so, I also assuredly hope that the Spirit of God shall open the doors and gates of the Mysteries for the soul, and grant a right understanding to grasp and know His wonderful gifts, which I heartily wish for your Honor.

He commends the recipient's humble and sincere desire for these "Mysteries," comparing their meeting to the biblical encounter between Peter and Cornelius.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Boehme's explanation of his writing process, moving from a listing of his works to a defense of their divine inspiration. He explains that his words come from the Spirit rather than his own intellect and offers the recipient of the letter a glimpse into the spiritual "Mystery" and the "Philosopher's Stone" found within his teachings.</meta>

<page-num>39</page-num>

### The Third Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin> 15. My writings will seem somewhat strange to you, for in some places the zeal is vehement or earnest, especially against **Belial** <note>In the Bible, a term for the personification of wickedness or the Devil.</note> and the **Antichrist**, who is known by God in His anger [or who has come into remembrance in His wrath]. Therefore, I say that I could not, nor dared not, write otherwise than as it was given and dictated to me. I have continually written as the Spirit dictated and did not give way to reason [or to the wisdom of the natural and astral spirit <term>The "astral spirit" refers to the part of the human soul influenced by the stars and the physical universe, rather than by divine light.</term>]. I also do not acknowledge it as a work of my own reason, which was too weak; but it is the work of the Spirit, who has shown what He intends to do, and what shall come to pass, and also what is already done. For He proceeds forth out of the Abyss into the Byss <term>In Boehme’s philosophy, the "Abyss" (or *Ungrund*) is the bottomless, unmanifested void of God, while the "Byss" (*Grund*) is the foundation or manifested ground of being.</term> and searches through all things. He tries the heart and reins <term>Archaic: "reins" refers to the kidneys, formerly believed to be the seat of a person's deepest emotions and hidden thoughts.</term>, and tests the thoughts of men. Moreover, He hereby signifies and declares the Last Judgment, that He will try and examine every Being through the fire. I could not, nor might I, write at all (even in that fiery inspiration) unless I set it down exactly as the Spirit represented it. Therefore, I have made it as a *memorial* to myself; I have no further intention with it.

<margin>☞</margin> 16. But because you desire to read it, I will send it, and I pray you to return it back again, for I will keep it for a *memorial*. I am certain (so long as your noble mind gives God the praise, reads it diligently, and takes this path to heart with a desire to understand) that the Lord will open to you the door of His love in the Mystery and crown you with the diadem of His wisdom. This is more precious than the created heaven and this world, for the precious *Philosopher's Stone* <note>In alchemy, the Philosopher's Stone was a legendary substance that could turn base metals into gold or grant eternal life; here, Boehme uses it as a metaphor for the ultimate spiritual truth and divine essence.</note>—the foundation of all mysteries and secrets—lies within it. This same diadem [or garland of wisdom] is set with this stone. This [diadem and crown of light in the Holy Spirit] the soul puts on as a garment, being a new body in and for the Kingdom of God. In this garment, the soul is the child of God and is able to stand in the fire of God's anger without any hurt or grief. It can overcome the Devil, Death, and this world, and can also rule over the stars, the poisonous influences of the constellations, and this outward life—which is otherwise impossible for human reason. For it gives that knowledge of things which no art [or literal insight <note>original: "acumen"</note> from external reason] is able to search out or dive into. It sees through heaven and earth, and it reaps where it has not sown. It does not ask the question, "Is it true or not?" It has the sign of truth and righteousness within itself. It has all virtues lying in hope. There is no fear of God's anger in it; it provides a very joyful hope and confirms and assures it, proving the soul to be the child of God.

<summary>Boehme claims his writings are dictated by the Holy Spirit rather than human reason and describes the spiritual "Philosopher's Stone" or divine wisdom that grants a soul victory over death and the stars.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Boehme's defense of his writings, transitioning from the spiritual source of his inspiration to the physical circumstances of how his work was shared and eventually persecuted. He uses the metaphor of a "garland" to describe the divine wisdom he has attained.</meta>

<page-num>49</page-num>

<margin>☛</margin>
17. This garland is a Virgin, a chaste purity, and divine Beauty; a joy of the life, it comforts and rejoices the mind in affliction. It goes along with man into death, but it has no death or dying in it; it lives from Eternity, it is a guide into Heaven, and it is the joy of the Angels. Its taste is more precious and pleasant than all the joys of this World; and he who once obtains it esteems it higher than all the goods and riches of this World. It cannot be paralleled, except only with the Deity, but it lies hidden in a dark valley. The World does not know it; the Devil blows against it like a storm of wind and often so covers and disguises it that Reason does not recognize it. But it springs forth in its time like a fair lily with manifold fruits; it is sown in tears and it grows in tribulation and affliction, but it is reaped with great joy. It is contemned <note>original: "contemned"; viewed with contempt or despised.</note> and despised by reason, but he who obtains it holds it as his best treasure.

<margin>☛</margin>
18. Such a garland is set upon him who seeks after it with earnestness and wholly resigns himself to it. However, his own "self-reason" in flesh and blood does not obtain it, as my writings fully testify; for what is written there, the Author has known by experience. There is no strange hand or spirit foisted in <note>original: "foysted in"; introduced surreptitiously or deceitfully.</note>. I do not write this for my own vainglory (my boasting is only in God) but as a rule and direction for the children of God, so that they may know what reward God gives to those who put their trust and confidence in Him and disregard the dispraise and contempt of the World.

19. I also wonder how you and many more in <term>Silesia</term> <note>A historical region in Central Europe, now mostly in Poland, where Boehme lived and worked.</note> have obtained my writings; for I have no acquaintance with any of them. I am so private regarding their publication that the citizens here around me know nothing of them, except only for the first part, which was <term>per-force</term> <note>original: "Per-force"; taken by force or necessity.</note> taken from me. By means of a person in the <term>Mystery of Babel</term> <note>Boehme’s term for the confused and corrupt state of the institutional church, which he compared to the biblical Tower of Babel.</note> (who persecuted it out of envy), it was proclaimed among them as <term>heresy</term>. Yet they never read it, nor was it ever examined as was appropriate.

20. Indeed, I never asked any man's advice about it, nor ever committed it to the censure and judgment of man to this very hour, but commended it to God. Yet by this, I know and acknowledge the way of God; and likewise, I understand that it is not known only in <term>Silesia</term>, but also in other countries, without my prior knowledge. I must even say that he who has so persecuted it has thereby published it; for my intent was to keep it with me as long as I lived, and I wrote it for myself only.

<margin>* The three Principles, and the three-fold Life.</margin>
21. But what God purposed in His counsel is now manifest; and it shall yet appear more clearly when the * two last books shall be read, at which I myself in the external man <note>The "external man" refers to the physical body and the rational mind that interacts with the material world.</note> do excee- <note>The text breaks here; the catchword on the following page completes the word as "exceedingly."</note>

<summary>Boehme describes divine wisdom as a spiritual "garland" and explains that his writings, though intended to be private, were spread and published through the very persecution meant to suppress them.</summary>

<page-num>41</page-num>
<header>The Third Epistle.</header>
<sig>G</sig>

<margin>☛</margin>
...exceedingly wonder, and marvel at what God intends and will do. I acknowledge that I am altogether unworthy and ignorant, and yet the greatest and deepest mysteries are revealed to the internal man <note>The "internal man" refers to the spiritual or inner self, as opposed to the "outward man" or physical body.</note>. I give these to you and other lovers of God to consider with humility; for in truth, I cannot say at all that this is the work of my own understanding or reason. Instead, I acknowledge it to be a *wonder* in which God will reveal great things. My reason looks into this and continually marvels at it.

22. I never studied these mysteries in all my life and knew nothing of them, for I am a layman <note>original: "Lay-man"; a person without formal academic or theological training.</note>. Yet, I must bring such things to light that all the high schools or universities have not been able to do. Compared to them, I am but a child and have none of their arts or wisdom. I must write from an entirely different school; and what is even greater than all this is that *the language of nature is made known to me*, so that I can understand the greatest mysteries in my own *mother tongue* <note>Boehme wrote in his native German, arguing that divine truth did not require the "sacred" languages of Latin, Greek, or Hebrew.</note>.

<margin>☛</margin>
23. I cannot say that I have learned or mastered it, but as long as the hand of God stays upon me, I understand it. But if it hides itself, then I do not even recognize my own labor and become a stranger to the work of my own hands. By this, I can see how it is an altogether impossible thing to search out and understand the mysteries of God without God’s Spirit. Therefore, I ascribe and attribute nothing to myself; it is not my work, and I do not desire any human applause or honor for it.

<margin>☛</margin>
24. I am only a simple, humble instrument. God works and makes what He pleases. Whatever God wills, I will also; and whatever He does not will, I do not will either. If it is His will for me to know anything, then I will know it; but if He does not will it, then I do not either. *I will be nothing and dead to myself, so that He may live and work in me as He pleases. I have cast myself wholly into Him, so that I may be safe and secure from the Devil.*

25. Although I must leave my outward body and life to the disposal of the world and allow the Devil to rage against me, I will not trust the Devil or the world with my internal man. Neither will I do (according to the inward man) what the world wants me to do. Although my outward man is bound and <margin>☛</margin> obligated to the world—and in its obligation and allegiance must obey the laws and ordinances of the world and do what its outward *duty* <note>original: "Obligation"; referring to legal, social, or civic responsibilities.</note> requires—my internal man shall only be obedient to God, and not to the world. For the internal man is not in the world, but has made himself dead to it, so that God might live in him and be both the will and the deed within him. Though I cannot say that it is possible to live like this [ <insert>in perfection</insert> ], yet my will is

<page-num>42</page-num>

...is so directed and inclined; and this neither the world nor the Devil shall break, even if my outward life should faint and perish. And though reason often flatly contradicts it, and temptation appears in heaps to the terror and sadness of the external life (where the spirit also hides itself, as if all were dead and gone), yet it always brings forth new fruits, and that in abundance.

26. I have related this to you at length so that you may know and recognize what kind of man I am, what the beginning and cause of my writing is, and from what art and spirit it was produced or generated. I also want you to know its purpose: namely, to keep it as a memorial for myself. However, because I perceive honest, religious hearts to thirst after it, I will not conceal it from them but [impart it] in a brotherly and Christian way. I commend and commit it to God, so that he may work and do what he pleases in them; this is what we are bound and obligated to do for one another.

27. Lastly, I entreat you to conceal my name among the learned, for I know that a humble layman <note>original: "Lay-man"; a person without formal academic or theological training.</note> is considered only ridiculous and contemptible by men *learned in scholastic art* <note>original: "Scholastique Art"; referring to the formal, academic theology and philosophy taught in universities of the period.</note>. Although God has his children among them too, I do not care to have my name attached to it or authorized by me. 
<margin>☛</margin>
For the praise belongs to God, who is the giver. I do not seek to make a great and glorious name for myself through this; Christ is my reward, my name, and my glory. I hope to have the glory of it in the life to come before angels and men, and to rejoice in it with the saints in Christ, as my writings sufficiently testify.

28. Regarding the book *Aurora*, which your Honor has sent me to examine, I have read some of it and find that it is indeed my work and well copied. Some syllables are left out for the sake of brevity, yet the sense and meaning are not diminished by that. I am well satisfied despite this, as (so far as I have read in haste) I have found no additions. However, the great <term>mysteries</term> <note>In Boehme's context, "mysteries" refers to deep spiritual truths about the nature of God and the universe that are hidden from the rational mind.</note> are contained within it very deeply. They were understood and grasped by the author, but it was not very easy for reason to comprehend them at first. Although it was known well enough in the depths, the author was not accustomed to it; when heavenly joy met him, he only followed the spirit's guidance. But the wild nature is not immediately regenerated [or made into a new creature].
<margin>☛</margin>
Indeed, if a kernel is sown, a tree grows. But if the virtue is great [if the power of the resolution is strong, and the practice is sincere and constant], the tree grows up all the sooner and is recognized more quickly.

29. In the other three books you shall find the mysteries <note>original: "Mysteris"</note> more clearly, and so on throughout—the further you go, the deeper they are. Each book from the first is grounded *ten times deeper*; so that the fourth is a very clear mirror <note>original: "mirrour"</note>, in which the Great Mystery <note>The *Mysterium Magnum*, a central concept in Boehme's work representing the source of all manifestation.</note> is sufficiently and visibly seen and understood, yet only by its children. Reason shall remain blind, for the spirit of God does not dwell in the outward principle <term>principle</term> <note>Boehme uses "principle" to describe the fundamental realms or states of being (Darkness, Light, and the Material World).</note>, but in the inward; and it proceeds forth from the inward into the outward [principle of this world], yet the outward does not comprehend him.

<margin>* The Book of the Three Principles.</margin>
30. But Sir, I must tell you that the book *Aurora* <note>Boehme's first book, *Aurora, or the Day-Spring*, written in 1612.</note> was not finished, for the Devil intended to make a bonfire <note>original: "Bone-fire"</note> of it, because he saw that the day would break forth in it. But for all that, the day has even overtaken the *Aurora* [or morning], so that it is already clear day. There are about thirty sheets still missing from it; but because the storm <note>Referring to the religious persecution and censorship Boehme faced from the local pastor, Gregory Richter, which forced him to stop writing for several years.</note> broke them off, it was not finished. In the meantime, it has become daylight, and the morning is extinguished; since 그시절 (that time), the work has been to bring forth the day. Yet it shall remain as an eternal remembrance because the defect was restored and supplied in the *Second [Book]; the fault and blame for the defect is to be attributed to the Enemy.

<margin>☞</margin>
31. But I will blame no one, except the falsehood and iniquity of the Devil, who is an enemy to all good. He even confuses and entangles kings; how then shall a poor, humble man <note>original: "meane Man"</note>, being employed in such a work, be so quickly acknowledged and known? Especially if it is firmly stated that he is a layman and also unlearned. Those very wise and skilled in the arts will be offended at the plainness and simplicity of such a thing. When one hears a person speak of such wonders and deep mysteries in such a humble and simple way [without scholastic pomp of words and artificial terms and phrases of logic and rhetoric], then he thinks it is a rhapsody <term>rhapsody</term> <note>original: "Rapsodie"; here meaning a confused, disconnected, or nonsensical collection of ideas.</note> [or some confused heap of notions packed together, an enthusiastic, fantastic hotch-potch of whimsies, or a bundle of nonsense]. For he does not understand the gifts of God and is also unable to see into the heart of another. Therefore, I will disturb no one, and I desire no one to trouble himself about it; but I confess that it is God’s providence, otherwise this book would have still remained hidden in a corner.

32. But thus it was published without my knowledge, consent, or will—and that by the persecutors themselves—which I acknowledge to be by the providence and appointment of God. For I had no copy of it for myself, nor did I ever know those who have it; I do not have it myself, and yet it has been copied out; and

<page-num>44</page-num>

brought to my sight and hands four times already, so that I see others have published it. I consider it a wonderful work that the grain grows against the will of the enemies; for that which is sown by God, no one can prevent or hinder [from growing].

<margin>☞</margin>
33. But regarding what you and others also misunderstand in my book *Aurora* (which appears to be wrong to the understanding and which also needs some clarification and explanation), you shall find these points sufficiently cleared at length in my third and fourth books. In those, there is an open gate to the Mysteries of all Beings. There is nothing in Nature that could not be fundamentally searched out and grounded in this way. It shows and opens the *Stone of the Wise Men* <note>original: "Stone of the wise men"; the Philosopher's Stone, an alchemical symbol for the ultimate substance that could transform base metals into gold or achieve spiritual perfection.</note> to all the secrets and mysteries within both the Divine and earthly Mystery. Through this knowledge and understanding, all the metals of the Earth may be brought to the highest degree or perfection—yet only by the children of the *Divine Magic* <note>original: "divine Magia"</note>, who have the *revelation* [or experimental science] of it.

34. I see it clearly enough, but I have no *manual operation* <note>original: "manuall operation"; practical, physical skill or alchemical laboratory work</note>, urge, or art for it. I only set forth an open Mystery, for which God shall stir up laborers of His own. Let no one seek the physical work from me, or think they can get the knowledge and operation of the Philosopher's Stone [or universal tincture] from me. Although it is clearly known and could be explained even more clearly, I have broken my own will and will write nothing except what is given to me. This way it is not my own work, lest I should be *imprisoned in the Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or the wrathful, destructive spirit that can entrap the soul and lead to spiritual ruin.</note>.

35. And if you wish to have anything copied from these writings now sent to you, it is necessary that the transcriber be an educated and insightful man. Many syllables are not fully written, nor do they have correct grammar or spelling <note>original: "Grammaticall autography"</note>. In many words, some letters may be missing, and sometimes a capital letter stands for a whole word. This is because "Art" <note>Human scholarly skill or formal academic rules</note> did not write this, nor was there any time to consider how to set it down precisely according to the standard use of letters. Instead, everything was ordered according to the direction of the Spirit, which often moved in haste. Consequently, the writer's hand (because he was not accustomed to it) often shook. Although I could have written in a more accurate, fair, and clear manner, the reason I did not is that the burning fire often forced me forward with *speed*, and the hand and pen had to hasten directly after it. For it comes and goes like a sudden shower; whatever it touches, it hits. If it were possible to comprehend—

<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of the depth of his spiritual insights and his decision to publish multiple books to avoid confusion and properly convey the complex "Divine Chaos" he perceives.</meta>

<page-num>45</page-num>
<header>The Third Epistle.</header>

comprehend and write down everything [which my mind beholds in the Divine Chaos <note>original: "Divine CHAOS"; Boehme's term for the infinite source of all things, the unmanifest state of God before creation.</note>], it would then be three times longer and even more deeply grounded.

36. But it cannot be, and therefore more than one book has been made; there is more than one philosophical discourse, each progressively deeper. This is so that what could not be contained in one might be found in another. It would be well if at last, out of *all* of them, only one might be made and all the others set aside [or laid aside]; for the multiplicity and plurality of books causes strife, contradiction, hostility, and wrong understandings. This happens because of the hasty ideas and guesses of the *Reader*, who does not know how to test and distinguish the spirit that uses such wonderful phrasing. Often, Reason assumes that the text contradicts itself, and yet in the depth of meaning, there is no contradiction at all.

<margin>☛</margin>
37. Out of this misunderstanding [or the false interpretations of human logic and literal, outward scholarship applied to the writings of holy men], the *Great Babel* <note>original: "Great Babel"; a reference to the biblical Tower of Babel, symbolizing religious confusion, sectarianism, and the "language of the world" as opposed to the spirit.</note> on earth has been brought forth. Here, men argue for nothing but words, but they ignore the spirit of understanding in the Mystery. But its end and its number have been determined and committed to the *Turba* <note>original: "Turba"; a term used by Boehme to describe the spirit of "disturbance" or the awakening of God's wrath that breaks down corrupted systems.</note>; for the beginning has found its Limit, and there is no longer any withholding or staying [of the wrath of God upon Babel]. It cannot be crushed by any power or force of arms.

<margin>* The Forty Questions.</margin>
38. I do not speak of or from myself, but from that which the spirit shows, which no man can resist; for it stands in its *omnipotence* <note>original: "Omnipotence"; all-powerfulness.</note> and does not depend on our opinions or will. This is explained very deeply in the *fourth book* of these writings, which is strongly grounded in the light of nature <note>original: "light of nature"; the natural understanding or wisdom inherent in the created world.</note> and can be demonstrated in all things.

39. Furthermore, I want you to understand that in these writings which are now sent to you, the Author sometimes speaks of himself as *We* and sometimes as *I*. You should understand that by the word *We*, the spirit is speaking (being in the plural) in two persons; and in the word *I*, the Author refers to himself. I give you this as direction and information to prevent any wrong understanding or suspicion.

40. And with this, I send you the *Fourth Part*, which consists of the Forty Questions; in them, you may see yourself reflected. At the next opportunity, I will send you the *Second* and *Third Parts* if you wish to have them. I ask that you return this to me at the next opportunity, for I will send it to the person who framed and sent the questions to me. And so, I commend and commit you to the love of God.

<summary>Jakob Boehme explains that his multiple volumes are necessary to capture his vast spiritual insights and warns that literalist misunderstandings lead to religious strife ("Babel"). He also clarifies his use of the pronouns "I" and "We" to distinguish between his own voice and the spirit's voice.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Boehme, Divine Chaos, Babel, Turba, The Forty Questions, Light of Nature, spiritual authorship, manuscript circulation, religious strife</keywords>

<page-num>26</page-num>
<header>The Third Epistle.</header>

I heartily wish that God would illuminate your **NOBLE** heart and give you the right understanding of the *author's* sense and meaning regarding the internal principle <note>original: "internall Principle"; Boehme’s term for the inner spiritual reality or the divine essence within the soul.</note>, along with all temporal and eternal welfare.

<meta>This page begins the "Fourth Epistle," a letter from Jacob Boehme to Paul Keym. It serves as an introduction to a larger discussion on eschatology (the study of the end times), the concept of a spiritual millennium, and the state of the church.</meta>

<page-num>47</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle.</header>

## A Letter to
# PAUL KEYM:
### BEING
## An answer to him concerning
#### *The End Times.* <note>original: "Our Last Times"; refers to the theological study of the end of the world and the final days of humanity.</note>
---
Wherein he discusses *the first resurrection of the dead* and *the thousand-year Sabbath*. <note>original: "thousand yeares Sabbath"; a reference to the Millennium, a period of a thousand years mentioned in the Book of Revelation when Christ is said to reign on earth.</note>
---
### ALSO,
Of the fall of **BABEL** <note>original: "BABEL"; in Boehme’s writing, this symbolises the "confused" and corrupt state of institutionalized churches.</note>, and of the new building in **ZION**. <note>original: "ZION"; represents the true spiritual community or the renewed state of the soul.</note>
---

->I.<-

> Light, salvation, and eternal power, flowing from the fountain of the heart of Jesus Christ, be our quickening <note>original: "quickning"; meaning life-giving or reviving.</note> consolation.

<margin>2.</margin>
Worthy and much-esteemed sir, and good friend in the illumination of the Holy Spirit; and in the love of our Lord Jesus Christ, beloved brother: I received from Mr. C. E. the letter you sent me, dated about the 20th of July, together with two small treatises attached. In them, I understand that you have received and read some of my small manuscripts concerning the wisdom of God, and as you affirm, they bring you joy. Furthermore, you have a great desire and longing for them, as you are engaged in a similar exercise in the wisdom of God.

<meta>catchword: 3. Which</meta>

<summary>In this opening of the Fourth Epistle, Jacob Boehme greets Paul Keym and acknowledges receiving Keym’s letters and treatises, expressing mutual joy in their shared study of divine wisdom.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Paul Keym, End Times, Resurrection, Millennium, Babel, Zion, spiritual illumination</keywords>

--- Page 66 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Fourth Epistle" to Paul Keym. Boehme is expressing his joy that Keym is also seeking spiritual "rebirth" and the true understanding of God, contrasting this with the "Babel" of institutionalized, superficial religion.</meta>

<page-num>48</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle.</header>

<margin>☞</margin>
3. This likewise brings me joy to see that even now the time is at hand when the right divine understanding and true knowledge of God are springing forth again in *Zion*; that the ruined *Jerusalem* shall be rebuilt, and that humanity’s true image—which vanished and departed in *Adam*—is showing itself again in *Zion* with a truly human voice. God is pouring out His spirit into us, so that the precious pearl is once again known, sought, and found through the power and light of the Holy Spirit.

4. Through this, we clearly see and understand the blindness we have lived in for a long time in *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; Boehme’s term for the "confusion" of corrupt institutional churches and worldly systems.</note>, going astray in worldly and evil ways. We have forsaken the true *Jerusalem*, shamefully wasted our inheritance, and lightly valued our angelic trophy or crown (that is <note>original: "Viz."; short for the Latin "videlicet," meaning "namely" or "that is to say."</note>, the beautiful image), wallowing instead in the filth of the Devil. Under a pretense of divine obedience, we have played with the serpent and walked in purely erroneous ways. The divine light now sets this before our eyes and urges us to return like the prodigal son <note>original: "lost Sonne"; referring to the parable in Luke 15.</note> and enter into the true *Zion*.

5. This must not be done with historical assumptions, opinions, or blind persuasion, as if we already understood it perfectly. That is not *Zion*, but *Babel*, which confesses God with the mouth and makes devout speeches to Him with the lips, but in the heart clings to the great Whore of Babylon <note>original: "Babylonicall Whore"; a symbolic figure of spiritual corruption from the Book of Revelation.</note>—to the dragon of self-love, pride, greed, and pleasure—and yet tries to present herself as if she were a virgin. No, this is not the Virgin in *Zion*; it must be a matter of deep sincerity.

<margin>☞</margin>
6. We must be born of God in *Zion* and both know and do His will. God’s spirit must bear witness to our spirit that we are God's children—not only in our speech through knowledge and theories, but in the heart through actual deeds. This is not achieved in a holy-looking way that lacks power [in the formalistic ways of worshipping words and rounds of lip-service, in which the captive conscience places the power of godliness]. The Devil mocks this and does not care for it. Instead, we must put on the helmet of righteousness and love, as well as chastity and purity, if we intend to wage battle against the prince of this world <note>original: "Prince of this World"; a title for Satan (John 12:31).</note>. He does not care a bit for any outward show [or for the long and loud-mouthed cries of blind devotion]. It must be power that overcomes him, and that power must shine forth in goodness and the holy fruits of Christianity. Only then may we fight for the noble prize, the crown of life.

<margin>☜</margin>
7. For we have a powerful warrior against us; he attacks us in body and soul and quickly casts us down. There is no other way to overcome him except with power in humility, which alone is

<meta>The text cuts off here at the catchword "able," which will begin the next page.</meta>

<summary>Boehme describes a spiritual awakening where the "true image" of humanity is restored through the Holy Spirit, urging believers to move beyond the superficial "lip-service" of corrupt religious institutions (Babel) toward a sincere, lived faith in Zion.</summary>
<keywords>Zion, Babel, Jerusalem, Adam, spiritual rebirth, Holy Spirit, Whore of Babylon, repentance, spiritual warfare, humility</keywords>

--- Page 67 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme’s fourth letter to Paul Keym. Boehme is warning that simply recognizing the corruption of "Babel" (the worldly church) is insufficient; one must actively live a life of spiritual integrity and possess true, experiential knowledge of God before attempting to teach or "rebuild" spiritual truths.</meta>

<page-num>49</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle</header>

...able to quench his poisonous fires with which he fights outside of us and within us, against our noble image.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. Therefore, beloved sir and brother in Christ, since you apply yourself to divine wisdom and labor in it, it is right and necessary that we encourage one another to be vigilant in withstanding the Devil. We must continually keep before our eyes the path we ought to walk and also proceed along it; otherwise, we accomplish nothing. If we know that the world is blind in <term>Babel</term> <note>original: "Babel"; Boehme's term for the "confusion" of corrupt institutional churches and worldly systems.</note> and goes astray, then we must be the first to effectively depart from <term>Babel</term>, so that the world may see we are in earnest.

<margin>☞</margin>
9. It is not enough that we expose and reveal <term>Babel</term> while still being found acting as <term>Babel</term> does. For if we do so, we testify that God reveals His light to us so that we see, yet we choose to do nothing but the works of darkness. That very light which enlightens our understanding shall witness against us—showing that the Lord has called us and shown us the way, but we would not walk in it.

10. It is *well* that we expose <term>Babel</term>, but we must *take care* regarding the spirit, mind, and type of knowledge with which we do so. It is good to be zealous, but the heart must be upright toward God; otherwise, we run without being sent, and in our journey, we are not known or acknowledged by God. By acting this way, the Devil mocks us and leads us into the side-paths of error.
<margin>☞</margin>

11. Besides, the Holy Scripture declares that our works and words shall follow us. Therefore, we must seriously consider with what spirit and knowledge we approach the high <term>mysteries</term> <note>original: "Mysteries"; profound spiritual truths hidden from the natural mind.</note>. For whoever pulls down something evil must set up something better in its place; otherwise, he is none of God's builders, nor does he labor in <term>Christ's vineyard</term> <note>original: "Christs Vineyard"; a biblical metaphor for the spiritual work of the church or the kingdom of God.</note>. It is not good to tear down unless a person knows how to reconstruct the building in a better frame and form.

<margin>☞</margin>
12. For God alone is the <term>Master Builder</term> of the world; we are only servants. We must take great care how we labor if we wish to receive a reward, ensuring that we have learned His work in His school and do not run without being sent when we are not yet capable of His service. Otherwise, we shall be found to be <term>unprofitable servants</term> <note>original: "unprofitable Servants"; a reference to the parable in Matthew 25:30.</note>. I say this with good affection and in all faithfulness, to instruct and direct one another in what we ought to do, so that our labor may be accepted by God.

13. For the obscure <term>mysteries</term> can be known in no other way at all except through the <term>Holy Spirit</term> <note>original: "holy Ghost"</note>. We cannot draw conclusions about hidden things unless we have them in actual knowledge and find through experience, in the <term>illumination of God</term> <note>original: "illumination of God"; the divine light or spiritual insight granted to the soul.</note>, that what we assert is the truth and the will of God, and that it also agrees with His Word; and
<margin>☞</margin>

<summary>Boehme cautions that exposing the "Babel" of religious corruption is useless unless the reformer lives an upright life and builds something better in its place. He emphasizes that spiritual work must be guided by the Holy Spirit and personal experience of God's light, rather than intellectual pride.</summary>

--- Page 68 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's explanation of divine knowledge. He argues that true understanding comes from the "light of Nature" and divine illumination rather than academic study or institutional authority. He continues his sharp critique of the corrupt clergy of his day, whom he identifies with the biblical "Babel."</meta>

<page-num>30</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle</header>
<lang>English</lang>

grounded in the light of Nature; for without the light of Nature, there is no understanding of Divine Mysteries.

14. The great building of God is manifest in the light of Nature; and therefore he whom God’s light illuminates may search out and know all things—although knowledge does not come in one and the same way or measure for everyone. God’s wonders and works are boundless, infinite, and immense; they are revealed to every person according to their gifts, and he to whom the light shines has pure joy in God’s works.

15. Furthermore, that which is old and passed away more than a thousand years ago is as near and as easily known in the light as that which is done today. For a thousand years before God are scarcely as much as a minute or the twinkling of an eye is before us; therefore, all things are near and manifest to His spirit, both that which is past and that which is to come.

16. And if we see in His light, then we must declare His wonders, and manifest and praise His glorious Name, and *not bury* our <term>talent</term> <note>original: "Talent"; a reference to the Parable of the Talents in Matthew 25, meaning one's God-given gifts or abilities.</note> in the earth. We must deliver it to our Master with *increase*; He will require an account from us of how we have traded with it. Without knowledge or certain illumination from God, no man should presume to judge, or be a doctor or master in the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Great Mystery" (Mysterium Magnum); a central term in Boehme’s philosophy referring to the manifestation of the spiritual world within the physical.</note>. Such a role is not committed to or commanded of him; instead, he must labor to attain the true light, and then he goes rightly to work in the school of God.

<margin>* Their livelihoods.</margin>
17. For there are many "masters" to be found who presume to judge in the Mystery, and yet they are not known or sent by God. Therefore, their *school* is called <term>Babel</term> <note>original: "Babel"; meaning "confusion," used by Boehme to describe the corrupt institutional church.</note>, the "Mother of Whoredom" upon Earth. They flatter both sides; they play the hypocrite with God while also serving the Devil. They call themselves the shepherds and pastors of *Jesus Christ*; they run, yet they are not sent, much less does God acknowledge them. Whatever they do, they do for their own honor and for the sake of their *bellies* <note>original: "Belleys"; implying they only care about their own material comfort and income.</note>; they would not run at all if they did not profit from their course of spiritual whoredom and hypocrisy.

18. They have turned the righteous and exceedingly precious Mystery of God into a mystery of their own corruption and pleasure. Therefore, the Spirit calls it "Babel"—a confusion—where men practice a hypocritical service and worship of God. They acknowledge Him with their tongues but deny Him in their power; they dissemble and flatter God with their lips, but in their hearts, they embrace and love the <term>Dragon</term> <note>original: "Dragon"; a reference to the beast in the Book of Revelation representing evil or worldly power.</note>.

19. We must not be like these people if we wish to obtain the Divine Mystery and be capable of receiving the light. Instead, we must fully entrust our path to God and resign ourselves to Him, so that God’s light may shine in...

<summary>Boehme explains that divine knowledge is accessible through the "light of Nature" and warns that those who teach without true spiritual illumination are part of the "Babel" of corrupt, self-serving religious institutions.</summary>
<keywords>Light of Nature, Divine Mysteries, Babel, Parable of the Talents, Great Mystery, hypocrisy, spiritual corruption, illumination</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the Fourth Epistle, where the author responds to a correspondent who has been studying the end times and the corruption of "Babel." He argues that true divine knowledge requires becoming like a "child" of God rather than a stranger to His work.</meta>

<page-num>57</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle.</header>

in us, so that he may be our intelligence—knowing, willing, and also doing. We must become his children if we want to speak of his Being and walk or labor in it; for he does not entrust his work to a stranger who has not learned his work or the mystery of his wonders in nature and grace.

<margin>☞</margin>
20. I have read through your books and found your great diligence and much labor in them, in that you have gathered together the texts of the Holy Scripture in great abundance. I also understand that you are in good earnest about it and that you would like to clearly prove and explain by them the dark terms and passages of the Scripture concerning the Last Times, as well as the first resurrection of the dead and the thousand-year <term>Sabbath</term> <note>original: "thousand yeares Sabbath"; referring to the millennial reign of Christ (the Millennium) described in Revelation 20.</note>. Likewise, you would reveal and describe the ruin of <term>Babel</term> <note>original: "Babel"; here used symbolically for the corrupt state of the church and society, referencing the confusion of Babylon.</note> and the new building in <term>Zion</term> <note>original: "Zyon"; symbolically the true, spiritual church or the kingdom of God.</note> of which the Scripture speaks in many places.

21. First, concerning Babel: how it has grown up and how it shall again be destroyed is sufficiently clear. The *Destroyer* is already active and is now about the work; he has long since made a beginning, yet the world will not see or take any notice of it.

<margin>☞</margin>
22. Men cry *Murder!* <note>original: "Mordio"; a German exclamation of alarm or distress, often meaning "murder!" or "help!"</note> [murder, confusion, and destruction to their adversaries], and yet there is no external enemy; rather, it is the <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Boehme to describe a disruptive or destructive spiritual force that arises when things are out of divine order.</note> alone which has grown up in the midst of Babel in her wickedness and unrighteousness. It has reached its limit and destroys only that which for a long time has been worthless, useless, and selfish—things which should have been rejected at all times. For where God should have been honored and loved—and our neighbor also as a person loves themselves—there men have set up in God's place abominable and beastly greed, deceit, falsehood, and wicked craft under a hypocritical show and pretense of holiness. They have minded and loved falsehood instead of God, and so have made of the Mystery an abominable, vicious Babel full of reproaches, revilings, and contentions. There, with sweet speeches and enticing words of human wisdom [with feigned explanations and interpretations of Scripture], they have blindfolded our eyes and bound our consciences, leading us captive in a very deceitful way to the glory and magnificence of the great <term>Whore</term> <note>original: "Whore"; a biblical reference to the "Whore of Babylon" from the Book of Revelation, representing spiritual corruption and false religious systems.</note>. Thus she has fattened her adulterous offspring and dominated our bodies, souls, goods, and estates.

23. This bastard is now at odds with himself about the great prey and spoil, and discovers his own wickedness and great shame, so that we may see what "good" was ever in her. For the great wickedness she has committed plagues her; it is no external thing, by which it may be seen that her prostitution has been

<page-num>24</page-num>

have been many; and that the Devil has beset and caught us in many nets; and that one spiritual corruption [or the mystery of hypocrisy and iniquity] runs in opposition to another. They are malicious, biting, devouring, destroying, and flaying each other in a hostile manner.

<margin>☛</margin>
24. For the great pain has come upon her <note>referring to "Babel," the personification of the corrupt church</note>, and she shall now bring forth the great iniquity with which she has become fully pregnant. Therefore she cries out because of her labor and the woe that has fallen upon her. She speaks of the child which she shall bring forth: namely, murder, greed, and tyranny. She uncovers her attractive features and shows what she is in her heart; now, for those who refuse to recognize her, there is no remedy.

25. The Book of *Revelation* says, "*Go out from her, my people, so that you may not be partakers of her plagues*"; for she has filled her cup full with the abominations of her spiritual corruption in the anger of God. She shall drink from that same cup and be forced to burst herself by it.

26. And this is what I say of *Babel*: that she is a whore and shall suddenly break in pieces and be destroyed. No stranger shall do it; the spirit of her own mouth strangles her; her own <term>Turba</term> <note>original: "Turba"; Boehme’s term for the spirit of divine wrath or the source of destruction within a being that has turned away from God’s love.</note> destroys her. She cries for vengeance and murder against heresy, yet she does not do it for God's sake, but for her *adulterous offspring* and her own greed. For if it were for God, she would follow His commandment and His will of *love*, where *Christ* says: "*Love one another, for by this men shall know that you are my disciples.*"

<margin>☛</margin>
27. The kingdom of God does not consist of war and insults, or in an external show during pleasant times. The children of God are not found in these things, but in love, patience, hope, and faith under the cross of *Jesus Christ*. Through this, the church of God grows into the <term>Sacred Ternary</term> <note>original: "Sacred Ternary"; referring to the divine Trinity or the three-fold nature of the spiritual realm.</note>—to a heavenly, paradisiacal essence—and the new angelic man, hidden within the old, springs forth in God. This is my certain knowledge, briefly summarized, regarding this topic; you may see more of it in my writings.

28. Secondly, concerning *Zion*, I speak and declare according to my knowledge, just as the spirit shows it to me: there shall surely come an end and a removal of the *deceit* [or the mystery of iniquity by which men are blinded]. *Zion* shall be found only by the children of *faith*—not in a general sense, as if there would be no wicked men left.

<margin>Note.</margin>
29. For the oppressor shall be the cause of *Zion* being born; when men see how *Babel* is a whore, then many children shall be found

<margin>☛</margin>found in Zion, and seek the Lord; but the Oppressor shall dog them, and denounce <note>original: "cry them downe"</note> them as Heretics; they shall also be persecuted and put to death, and where one is killed, ten—even a hundred—shall rise up in his place <note>original: "roome"</note>.

30. But the General *Zion* appears first in greatest misery; when *Babel* <note>Boehme's term for the corrupt and divided institutional church</note> comes to ruin, then it shall stand desolate and miserable; and the Children of *Zion* shall then say, "How has the Lord forsaken us? Come, we beseech you, let us seek His face; let us cease from strife and war. Have we not, alas, made our country desolate? Is not all store and *provision* wasted and spent? Are we not brothers? Why do we fight? We will now enter into love and unity, and seek the Lord, and fight no more and destroy ourselves; we will be content. Are we not all pilgrims and strangers here, seeking our native country?"

<margin>☛</margin>31. In this time, a *Zion* shall truly <note>original: "verily"</note> be found, and the Heaven shall drop down its dew, and the Earth yield her fatness; yet not so that wickedness should be completely done away, for it shall continue until the end, of which Christ says, *Do you think that when the Son of man comes, that there will be faith upon the Earth?* <note>Luke 18:8</note>. And though the Children of *Zion* shall have a fiery deliverance <note>a spiritual purification or survival through trials</note>—so that they shall remain, despite the will of the Devil—insomuch that God will work great things, as in the time of the Apostles, yet it does not endure until the end. For as it was in the days of *Noah*, when he entered into the Ark, so shall the coming of the Son of man be, as it is written.

32. But I acknowledge and I know that the Holy Ghost shall be in the hearts of the faithful in *Zion*; for *Zion* shall not be from without <note>external or institutional</note>, but in the new man. It is already born; he that would seek it, let him but seek himself, and depart from the old *Adam* <note>the fallen, sinful nature of humanity</note> into a new life; and he shall find whether Jesus is born in him.

<margin>☛</margin>33. If he does not find it, let him enter into himself and seriously consider himself; and so he shall find *Babel* and her workings in him. These he must destroy and enter into God’s Covenant; and then *Zion* will be revealed in him, and he shall be born with Christ in *Bethlehem of Judah* <note>original: "Bethleem Jehuda"</note> in the dark stable, not in *Jerusalem*, as reason would gladly <note>original: "faine"</note> have it. Reason thinks Christ should be born in the old donkey <note>original: "Asse"; likely referring to the physical body or the animal soul</note>; the old donkey must become a servant, and serve the new man in *Zion*.

34. But as for the idea that in *four hundred years* there shall be a purely golden age, I know nothing of it; it is not revealed to me. Also, the *limit of the world's end is not revealed to me*. I cannot speak of any four hundred years, for the Lord has not commanded me to teach it; I commend it to God's might and leave it for those to whom God would grant the knowledge of it. Seeing, therefore, I have not as...

<summary>Boehme describes the emergence of "Zion" as an internal spiritual rebirth occurring amidst the worldly desolation of "Babel." He emphasizes that true faith is found within the "new man" rather than in external structures, and he declines to predict a specific timeline for a "golden age" or the end of the world.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Boehme’s discussion of the spiritual rebirth of "Zion" (the true spiritual church) and the end times. He shifts here to a humble admission of the limits of his own understanding regarding the "thousand-year Sabbath" mentioned in the Book of Revelation.</meta>

<page-num>54</page-num>

...yet I have not yet fully grasped it, but I remain satisfied in His <unclear>grace</unclear>. I despise no man, however, if he has the knowledge and command to teach in such a way.

<margin>☛</margin>
35. The fourth Book of *Esdras* <note>original: "Esdras" — referring to the apocryphal 2 Esdras, which contains apocalyptic visions</note> is not sufficient, as I understand it, to give a positive assurance on this matter. Yet I wait for my Savior Jesus Christ and rejoice that I may find my Lord. When I possess Him, then I hope—after the death of my "old Adam" <note>the fallen, sinful nature of humanity</note>—to fully refresh myself in the quiet rest of Zion. I will wait in my God, expecting whatever He will do with me in His Zion and mine. For if I only have Him, then I am in and with Him in the Eternal Sabbath, where no strife or contention from the ungodly can reach me in my *New man* <note>the spiritually reborn self</note>. In the meantime, I rejoice at this, even while in this miserable *Vale of Tabernacles* <note>a biblical reference to the temporary, mortal state of human life on earth</note>.

36. The first resurrection of the dead to the thousand-year Sabbath (which is mentioned in the Book of *Revelation*) has not been sufficiently revealed to me, specifically how it should be understood. Scripture does not mention it elsewhere, and Christ and His Apostles give no hint of it in other places, except for *John* in his *Revelation*. Whether these shall be a thousand *solar years* <note>actual calendar years</note>, or how the time should be calculated, I leave to my God, since I do not have full assurance. I leave it to those to whom God shall grant a right understanding, until God is pleased to open my eyes concerning these mysteries. <margin>☜</margin>

37. For these are secrets, and it does not belong to man to draw conclusions about them without the command and light of God. But if anyone has knowledge and illumination regarding this from God, I would be ready and willing to learn—if I could see the basis for it in the "light of nature" <note>Boehme's term for understanding God through the observation of the natural world and its inherent laws</note>.

38. But since I should not hide my knowledge of it—so far as I grasp it in the light of nature—I will therefore set down some suppositions or noteworthy opinions that are on my mind. I do not state these as absolute facts, but offer them for consideration, for good and wholesome instructions may be drawn from them. It is also profitable for man *to search in this way*. I will do so in all sincere uprightness to see if we might move somewhat closer to the truth; perhaps there may be some to whom God will grant such a gift, who will be stirred up by this to write more clearly.

<margin>☛</margin>
39. First, consider whether it is certain that the world must continue for *seven thousand years*, with one thousand of those being a pure Sabbath. This is suggested because God created everything in six days and began His rest on the sixth day toward evening (which is why the Jews begin their Sabbath on Friday evening). Yet *Elias* <note>referring to the "House of Elias," a Jewish tradition found in the Talmud stating that the world would last 6,000 years</note> also says that the world should stand for only *six thousand years*. Furthermore, Christ declares that the days of tribulation shall be shortened for the sake of the elect, otherwise no one would be saved. You apply this to the fall of *Babel* <note>the corrupt church</note> and to the time of *Zion* <note>the spiritual church</note>, but it seems as if *Christ* spoke of the...

<summary>Boehme discusses the uncertainty of apocalyptic timelines, specifically the "thousand-year Sabbath," expressing humility about his own lack of direct revelation on the matter while offering some cautious interpretations based on the "light of nature."</summary>

...the fall of the *Jews* and the end of the world, and it foreshadows an evil end.

40. Also, Christ says that at the time of His coming to judgment, it shall be as it was in the days of *Noah*, where men married and were given in marriage. Now we know very well (as the Scripture testifies) what kind of wicked world existed in the days of *Noah*, such that the Deluge had to come and destroy them. (*This would suggest and signify a very poor <note>original: "meane" — meaning low-quality or spiritually unremarkable</note> Sabbath.*)

41. And even if a person were to interpret the words of Christ concerning His coming in another way, that would not be sufficient to prove it; especially since the disciples of Christ always represent the end as being near, and *Paul* says that the end should come after the *Antichrist* is revealed.

42. But the idea that the resurrection of the dead and the Last Judgment should be understood in two parts—namely, that the righteous shall arise for the thousand-year Sabbath, and some ungodly people among them, and that *Gog* and *Magog* shall fight against the saints at the end of those thousand years—it seems to run quite contrary to the light of nature <note>the inherent logic or "common sense" of the created world</note>.

<margin>☞</margin>
43. For first, I cannot understand how the first resurrection could take place, seeing that the saints shall have their works follow them, according to the words of the Spirit. Besides, we know very well that all our works are sown into the Great Mystery <note>original: "Great Myſtery" — Boehme's term for the divine essence or the spiritual "database" where all actions are recorded</note>; they are first brought forth into the four elements, and so pass into the Mystery, and are reserved for the judgment of God. There, all things shall be tested by fire, and that which is false shall consume in the fire; and the figures <note>the outward forms or results of actions</note> shall fall into the center of nature—*namely, the dark eternity*.

44. But if people's works shall follow them in the first resurrection, as you affirm, then God must truly move the Mystery (which is Himself), and that signifies the *Last Judgment*.

<margin>☞</margin>
45. For God has moved Himself only twice since eternity: once in the creation of the world, and secondly in the incarnation of Christ according to His heart. The first motion belongs to the Father of all beings, and the second to the Son according to God’s heart. Now, the third motion of the Holy Spirit is yet to be accomplished, both in love and anger, according to all the *Three Principles* <note>Boehme’s cosmological system involving the Dark World (Father), the Light World (Son), and the Outer World (Holy Spirit/Nature)</note>. In this motion, everything that has been corrupted shall again be restored by the Holy Spirit, and each thing given to its rightful owner.

<margin>☞</margin>
46. How then can the dead arise with their works without the motion of the Holy Spirit in both love and anger? For the restoration of life consists only in Him. Moreover, I do not know how the first resurrection should take place—whether it should

<page-num>86</page-num>
<header>The Fourth Epistle</header>

...come to pass in the twofold man <note>original: "twofold man" — Boehme's concept of the human being possessing both a temporary, earthly nature and an eternal, spiritual nature</note> (which cannot be understood any other way), that is, in good and evil. But what perfect Sabbath can we keep in such a state? Was not *Adam* unable to stand in that condition?

47. Now, if only the new man were to arise, then he would not be within the four elements of this world. Furthermore, the new body in Christ needs no resurrection; it lives eternally without any want, necessity, or death in Christ, and it only waits for when God shall move the Mystery <note>original: "Myſtery" — the *Mysterium Magnum*, or the hidden divine foundation from which all things are manifested</note>, at which time he shall *then put on the crown* of his wonders and works.

<margin>☞</margin>
48 *The manner of the resurrection is this*: the Mystery shall restore whatever it has swallowed up. A person's works shall be placed upon them, and with those works, they shall pass through the fire, where it will be tested whether those works will endure the fire or not.

49. Now I cannot understand how this would agree with dwelling upon the earth. For if it were to happen in a paradisiacal manner—that man should arise with his "wonders"—then it could not be done without the movement of the Great Mystery. Since your writings say that some wicked men shall also arise, this shows that the Mystery must be moved; and at that movement, the "inflammation" (or the Last Judgment of fire) must necessarily occur. If the Mystery were to be moved, it would not only move [awaken and raise up] some people, and that in only one "source" <note>original: "ſource" — here meaning a specific quality or state of being, such as heaven or hell</note>, seeing as some ungodly people shall also arise.

50. Besides, you say that they shall all die at the end of the six thousand years; then there must be a dwelling upon or an inhabiting of the earth, where the ungodly who arise would again marry and build. According to your opinion, there would only be "some" of them, but according to the Scripture, they shall be as "the sand upon the seashore." Otherwise, where would Gog and Magog come from, or how could they fight against the children of Paradise? For in the paradisiacal children, there is no strife. <margin>☞</margin>

<margin>☞</margin>
51. Also, it would not be necessary for them to die at the *end* of the six thousand years if they were to arise in the twofold body as we are now. But if they should arise in the new body, then no ungodly person could either see or touch them—just as we now *do not see Paradise*. The new body is such that no ungodly man can fight against it.

52. What would they fight for? Are the saints in Paradise? Then they make no use of the external elements, but only of the internal element in which all four elements are combined in unity. Therefore, they have nothing to strive for, but they are separate in their source.

53. But if the ungodly should die and then also arise again in the four elements, this seems much more strange; but if they should arise...

# OF THE RESURRECTION
<sig>I</sig>
<meta>catchword: radical</meta>

<margin>☞</margin> <unclear>... the four Elements could not contain it, but the Abyss said still they would be separate as light and darkness;</unclear> what pleasure or satisfaction should God have to bring the Saints again into the conflict and influence of the <term>four elements</term> <note>the material building blocks of our world: earth, air, fire, and water</note>, to which they have been dead for so long? And yet should they then begin to fight with the wicked? It would be much more fit and agreeable for those who here have suffered nothing for Christ’s sake; that is, for those who here upon the Earth have not lost their lives for Christ’s sake.

**54.** And though you would say that they should not fight, but the Lord for them, what pleasure could God take to raise up the Saints and to set them again in the presence of the ungodly? Or should not the joy in <term>Abraham’s bosom</term> <note>original: "Abrahams bosome" — a biblical term for the place of rest for the righteous dead</note> be much greater than this life in the four elements, from which natural strife and contention arise? But if they should dwell in Paradise without the four elements, then no strife or ungodly man can reach them.

**55.** Besides, to what end should the ungodly be upon the Earth if there shall be such a <term>Sabbath</term> <note>the promised thousand-year period of rest and holiness</note>? Their source is not in the four elements but in the <term>Abyss</term> <note>original: "Abysse" — the bottomless pit or the dark world of chaos</note>, where their soul goes when the body dies.

**56.** Besides, should none but those dwell in the Sabbath who have died for Christ's sake (of which truly there cannot be such a number as is set down in the Book of <term>Revelation</term>) so that they should be sufficient to possess the Earth? And should the ungodly also dwell upon the Earth and hold their "Hellish Sabbath"? This runs directly against the light of Nature <note>reason and divine understanding as revealed through the natural world</note>.

**57.** Moreover, Christ says, *That they shall marry, and be given in marriage, as in the days of Noah:* Also *two shall be grinding in one mill, and two sleeping in one bed, and the one shall be taken, and the other left; when the last day shall come.*

<margin>☞</margin> **58.** Besides, Christ also says that when he shall come to judge the world, *all generations and kindreds shall see him and tremble before him;* and the wicked shall wail and lament, and say to the wise virgins, "give us of your oil." All this denotes a universal expectation of the <term>Last Judgment</term>.

**59.** For if at the <term>last trumpet</term> <note>original: "last Trumpe" — the signal for the final resurrection of the dead</note>, two shall be lying in one bed (namely, one holy, the other ungodly), this shows no prior difference or separation. And if the Saints are mixed with the ungodly, then truly there must needs be a poor Sabbath.

<margin>☞</margin> **60.** When we look upon the words of Christ and his Apostles, they will not in the least manner agree to that idea; and though there is mention made of a thousand years in the Book of <term>Revelation</term>, yet the meaning is hidden from us. We do not know when they may begin, or whether they have already begun; if the <term>first resurrection</term> <note>the spiritual awakening or the first stage of the rising of the dead mentioned in Revelation 20</note> be Pa—

# The Fourth Book.

radically; then it may be done without our knowledge.

61. They shall not dwell among us, nor shall they marry; for we die once to the states of **male** and **female**, and we shall not rise as **male** and **female**, but we shall live in **Paradise** in the form of **angels** (Matthew 22:30).

62. Besides, at the appearance of **Christ's** coming, the wicked shall beg the wise for the **oil of faith**; and you write that the fire of God—being the wrathful and hellish source—shall be in them, and that they shall be tormented here upon the **Earth** in the **four elements** <note>original: "foure Elements" — the material building blocks of the world: earth, air, fire, and water</note> by the anger of God. However, the anger of God is not fully manifest in the four elements, for within them good and evil are mixed together.

<margin>❦</margin>
63. But how shall someone who is once dead to the good, and cannot have even a single good thought, beg the saints for **faith** and comfort? Rather, it indicates that when **Christ** comes to judge the world, they shall all still dwell together in the flesh, within the four elements, where one shall be received and the other rejected. The sins of the wicked shall then come into view at the appearance of the severe countenance of God in the fiery zeal of the <term>first principle</term> <note>In Boehme's philosophy, the "first principle" is the dark, fiery, and wrathful foundation of nature, which becomes "hell" when separated from God's light.</note>, so that the wicked person shall be terrified and would then gladly begin to be honest.

64. And although you mention that they shall only awake and not arise, this is to be understood regarding the uncorrupted. Now you say that they shall dwell upon the **Earth** in the four elements and the saints in **Paradise**; when this happens, there will be no more strife or controversy, for they are eternally separated.

65. But if the saints dwell upon the **Earth** in **Paradise**, as **Adam** did before the Fall, and the ungodly are set against them, then they are in as much danger as **Adam** was—that they might again eat of the forbidden fruit and die once more.

<margin>☛</margin>
66. But if they are to be hidden from the ungodly for a thousand years, and also hidden from the four elements, why then should they first be revealed in the four elements at the end, so that <term>Gog and Magog</term> <note>Biblical figures representing the enemies of God who gather for a final battle in the Book of Revelation.</note> could enter into battle with the **children of Paradise**? It agrees with neither **Scripture** nor **reason**.

67. The first epistle of Saint **Paul** to the **Corinthians**, **chapter 15**, does indeed teach about **Christ's** resurrection and also **our own**, yet it does not speak of a third. Instead, it speaks first of **Christ's**, and then of **ours**; for he says there, *“That Christ is the first fruits”*, and then those *“who belong to Christ”*. This is the general **resurrection**. And though he says, *“Then comes the end”*, by "the end" he does not signify another resurrection; rather, the end *is* our resurrection. This is a much better understanding than suggesting that God meant another resurrection by the word "end."
