...or time; for just after our resurrection comes the end of the world.

68. The dead shall first appear before the judgment before the end of this world and the <term>four elements</term> <note>original: "foure Elements" — the traditional building blocks of the physical world: earth, air, fire, and water.</note> arrives; for the end is the igniting of the fire, and the final conclusion.

69. Also, the apostles of Christ and all teachers from God have always represented the end as near at hand; for *John* himself says in his epistle, *that we are in the end* <note>original: "That we are in the End" — likely referring to 1 John 2:18, which describes the "last hour."</note>. He speaks indeed of the last hour; but if the wicked were assured that they still had four hundred years until the end, how they would seek after riches for their children!

70. Besides, we are to look into the end, for this world is confined and determined within the beginning of the creation; and then we look to the end where the creation ceased. All of this was finished in six days, and in such a time the mystery of God’s kingdom shall be finished; and a thousand years are before him as one day.

<margin>☞</margin>
71. Concerning the <term>seventh day of rest</term> <note>A reference to the "Sabbath Millennium," the belief that human history will last 6,000 years followed by a 1,000-year "Sabbath" of peace on Earth.</note>, whether or not the world shall yet be at rest for a thousand years: this is hidden from mankind. We cannot certainly determine it; we must leave it to His power. I have no knowledge of it, seeing that the Scripture does not give clear evidence as to when the thousand years begin, what years they are, or what they relate to. Therefore, I leave it alone as it is and will not hinder anyone who has a certain knowledge or a command to teach it; I give you this to consider, meaning well toward you.

72. What I might further answer concerning this matter, you can find sufficiently in my writings. Although I could set down a long answer about it, I did not think it appropriate, seeing as this knowledge has not been given to me. Thereupon I let it alone, for I know that I must give an account of my works; and I send your two books back to you by the bearer of this letter, and I thank you for them.

73. Concerning the end or the limit of <term>Babel's</term> downfall <note>original: "Babels downefall" — In Boehme's writing, "Babel" symbolizes the corrupt, confused state of the institutional church and human systems of power.</note> (namely, that *Babel* should be wholly destroyed about the year 1630, according to your calculation—and although many others are of the same mind), this likewise is not sufficiently manifest to me.

<margin>☞</margin>
74. To me indeed it is given to know that the time is *near*, and even now at hand, but the year and day I do not know. Thereupon I leave it to God’s counsel and to those to whom God shall reveal it. I cannot conclude anything without certain knowledge; otherwise, I would be found a liar before God.

75. But I wait for my Savior Jesus Christ and will see what He will do. If He wills that I shall know it, then I will know it; if not, then I will not know it. I have committed my will, knowledge, un-

<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s personal reflection on his spiritual journey and the origins of his writings. He transitions from discussing the timing of the end times to explaining his own lack of formal education and the accidental publication of his first work, *Aurora*.</meta>

# THE FOURTH EPISTLE.

...understanding, and desire for Him; He shall be my knowing, my willing, and also my doing; for without Him, there is mere danger and uncertainty.

76. A person can hardly grasp what is right before their eyes, much less that which is hidden and mystical, unless God is their light. I give you this answer out of good affection for you to consider, even though I am a simple, humble <note>original: "meane"</note> man, not educated in any worldly art [or not raised in any <term>scholastic learning</term> <note>The formal, academic education of the 16th and 17th centuries, focused on logic, Latin, and traditional theology.</note>].

<margin>☞</margin>
77. But what I have is the gift of God. I did not obtain it through any art or study, but from the light of grace, which was all I sought. Although my beginning was simple because of my childlike understanding, God has since that time, through His light, worked something in me and opened my childlike eyes.

78. Regarding the book ***Aurora*** (which is my first), it needs to be better explained in many places. At that time, full understanding was not yet born in me. Like a sudden shower that hits whatever it falls upon, so it was with that <term>fiery inspiration</term> <note>original: "fiery instigation"—a sudden, intense spiritual insight or urge to write.</note>.

79. Although I had no intention at all that anyone should read it—writing only of the wonders of God shown to me as a personal memorial—it went abroad without my consent. It was taken from me by force <note>original: "per-force"</note> and published without my knowledge. I had intended to keep it with me as long as I lived and had no desire to be known for it among such high-ranking persons, as has now happened.

80. But the Most High (in whose hands and power all things reside) had another purpose for it, as is now clear. I am informed that it is known in many cities and countries, which both surprises and does not surprise me. For the Lord performs His work marvelously, beyond and above all human reason, even if He should employ a shepherd in the work. Although worldly art and outward reason will give Him no room or place, His purpose must stand against all the ragings of the Devil.

<margin>☞</margin>
81. And although I have not gained many days of pleasure from this, I must not resist His will. I have written only according to the form as it was given to me, not following other masters or writings.

82. Besides, my intent was only for myself. Even though the Spirit showed me how things would turn out, my heart desired nothing but to commit the matter to Him, to do with it as He pleased.

83. I have not run out with it (since I was not called) to make myself known to anyone; for I can also say in truth that those who knew me best knew the least about it. But whatever I have shown to anyone, that same

# The Third Epistle.

<note>The following text begins with the conclusion of paragraph 83 from the previous page.</note>

... done upon earnest and persistent desire.

<margin>☞</margin>
84. And then, further, I want you to understand—since you have my writings in hand to read—that you should not look upon them as coming from a great **Master**. You will not find formal **Art** <note>original: "Art"—referring to academic skill, rhetorical polish, or formal scholastic training.</note> in them, but rather the great earnestness of a zealous mind that thirsts after God. In that thirst, the mind has received great things, as the **enlightened** <note>original: "illuminate"</note> mind will clearly see. Without this light, no one can rightly know or understand them, as the **Reader** will surely find.

85. And yet, it could not have been written more clearly or made readier for understanding. I believe that it is clear and plain enough despite such a depth; but if there were anything that seems too obscure or difficult, I could represent it in a simpler and plainer manner if it were pointed out to me.

86. There are other books besides this one, written concerning the wisdom of God, containing very deep meaning and understanding. They treat the great depths of the wonders of God, but I do not have them at hand at the moment.

87. The reason I have not given you a long answer regarding my judgment of your book—specifically concerning the "thousand-year Sabbath" and the "four hundred years in **Zion**" (which you try to prove with many passages of Holy Scripture)—is because I do not fundamentally and certainly know whether those **Texts** can be applied to such a meaning.

88. For there are many sayings in Scripture which seem to suggest only one **General Resurrection** of the dead. These are very clear, especially in the words of Christ in the four Evangelists, which I hold to be the most certain.

89. It is the same with **Zion**: wickedness shall continue until the end. Even though a **Zion** <note>In Boehme’s work, "Zion" often represents the true, spiritual church or the state of a soul in union with God.</note> shall exist, it will not be entirely **Universal**. **Babylon** <note>original: "Babel"—representing the confused, worldly, and corrupt institutional church.</note> shall go to ruin and take another form, yet not everyone who is called a "child in Zion" will truly be a child of God.

<margin>☞</margin>
90. Also, I have no revelation <note>original: "knowledge"</note> of the thousand-year Sabbath; I do not know enough to support it with Scripture, for we find one passage seeming to contradict another. People may interpret the Scripture as they are inclined, but since I have no command from God regarding it, I let it alone and leave everyone to answer for their own opinion. I tell you this sincerely out of good affection, and I remain your faithful friend in the love of Christ.

91. On your forty-second and forty-third pages, where you write of the **Mystery of the Souls departed** [or separated], you cast doubt and suspicion on the opinion of ***Theophrastus*** <note>original: "Theophrastus"—referring to Theophrastus von Hohenheim, better known as Paracelsus (1493–1541), a famous physician and mystic whose work influenced Boehme.</note> and others, as if they had not written correctly about the Mystery. It would be better if...

# The Fourth Epistle.

...had been left out, since you say you have not understood those opinions; and it seems just so. You shall find sufficient and extensive information in my *Book of the Forty Questions* [specifically around the thirtieth question] concerning the Last Judgment, and also in other questions, if the same be read and rightly understood.

<margin>☞</margin>
92. There is no need for any further searching; it is clear enough there what the *Mystery* <term>original: "Mystery"—referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, the underlying spiritual reality from which all things originate</term> is that comprises body and soul. It also explains the condition of separated souls, both in their expectation of the Last Judgment and, in the meantime, in respect to their habitation, source <note>original: "source"—often used by Boehme to describe the internal quality, well-spring, or active state of being</note>, life, and difference. I had thought that it was so deeply and highly grounded that the mind of man should be satisfied enough with it. And if you have not, and cannot, set forth anything more fundamental, then it rightly remains in its own place. The thousand-year *Sabbath* <note>The Millenarian belief in a literal 1,000-year reign of Christ on earth.</note> and the four hundred years in *Zion* will only find fault with everything and bring it into suspicion; and though many objections might be made, they would be of no service or value.

<margin>☞</margin>
93. Moreover, the revelation of the thousand-year *Sabbath* is not of much importance or concern to the world, since we do not have sufficient grounds for it. It should rightly rest in the *Divine Omnipotence*; for we have enough in the *Sabbath* of the new birth. For that soul which obtains this *Sabbath* [of *Regeneration*] will, after the death of the earthly body, have *Sabbath* enough in *Paradise*. We may very well leave and commend the other to the *Divine Omnipotence*, and wait on God to see what he will do with us when we shall be in him and he in us.

94. For I suppose there should be a better *Sabbath* in God than in this world; and if man should dwell upon the earth in *Paradise*, then God must restore that which entered into the *Mystery* through his curse, as is to be seen in the *Forty Questions*.

95. But your supposition that the righteous shall not be brought with their works before the judgment is contrary to the words of *Christ*, who says that all things shall be proved through the *Fire*.

<margin>☞</margin>
96. I do not say they shall come *into* the judgment, for the judgment is in the wicked—understand this as the judgment of *Anger* <note>original: "Anger"—referring to the divine wrath or the dark, fiery principle of nature in Boehme's cosmology</note>, of which the *Scripture* says of the righteous (or as *Christ* says), "He that believes on me does not come into judgment." But understand by this the source or pain of the judgment. His words do hold forth that they shall all come together before the judgment and every one hear his sentence: "*The ungodly depart from here, and the righteous come here, etc.*"

97. Also, everyone shall stand forth with his own works in the *Mystery*; and they themselves shall be judged according to their works;

<meta>catchword: now</meta>

<summary>Boehme argues that spiritual regeneration is more important than speculating about a literal thousand-year earthly Sabbath. He clarifies that while the righteous do not suffer the "judgment of anger," they still appear at the Last Judgment to be vindicated by their works.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Forty Questions, Last Judgment, Mystery, Thousand-Year Sabbath, Zion, Regeneration, Paradise, Divine Anger, Works, Eschatology</keywords>

--- Page 81 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's explanation of the final judgment and the state of human works after death. He argues that works are not immediately separated from the soul at death but must eventually be tested and refined by the "fire of God."</meta>

...how then shall their works in this world, having been performed in both good and evil, be tested and separated in the fire of God? How shall they then, being unseparated, follow the saints in the resurrection to the Sabbath and keep the Sabbath there? But if they follow them, then they must be tested and separated in the fire; and then they shall no longer have any need to come before the judgment. But if they were to keep a Sabbath without their works, then they would not be perfect.

98. If we wish to speak of *Paradise* and understand it, then we must have clear eyes to see into it. For the internal Paradisiacal world and the external world exist one within another. We have only turned ourselves out of the internal into the external, and so we work in two worlds.

<margin>☛</margin>
99. Death cannot separate our works; the fire of God must do it. For they remain in one Mystery <term>original: "Mystery" (Mysterium Magnum)—the hidden spiritual essence and source of all things</term> until the judgment of God. At the hour of the resurrection, everyone shall come forth in his own Mystery, and he shall see his works before him and feel them within him.

100. It is not meant that they shall answer for themselves with words, for the Kingdom of God consists and proceeds in Power. And although the ungodly shall cry out "Woe!" upon their abominations and their seducers, yet every man's work shall be summoned in **POWER**, which shall either rejoice or torment him.

101. Now, the old body of this world is the Mystery of this world, and the new body is the Mystery of the Divine light-world. The soul is the Mystery of God the Father, and the earth with the elements also contains both Mysteries, which shall be moved through the Principle of the Father.

<margin>☛</margin>
102. And then the doors of the Mysteries shall be set open, and each shall give and set forth the figures which it has swallowed up. For the principle of the soul must stand before the judgment with both Mysteries.

<margin>☛</margin>
103. Happy are they who shall have the body of Christ in the Mystery of the wrath <note>original: "wrath"—the fire of God's anger</note>. They shall have the soul's fire (the Principle of the Father) surrounded with the light-world and illuminated with the Divine Majesty. They shall feel no pain or hurt and shall pass imperceptibly through the fire. There, the outward (or third) principle shall be tested, and all earthliness or falsehood shall remain in the fire. But the works shall be renovated <note>original: "renovated"—renewed or restored to their original purity</note> in the fire and freed from their earthly source <note>original: "source" (German: *Quelle*)—the internal quality, well-spring, or active state of being</note> and soil. Then the earthly Mystery remains in the fire and serves as fuel for the fire from which the light arises, and the righteous lose nothing.

<summary>Boehme describes how human works are preserved within a spiritual "Mystery" until the Last Judgment, where they are purified by God's fire. He emphasizes that the internal spiritual world and external physical world coexist, and the soul must eventually stand before God with its works transformed by divine power.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Last Judgment, Paradise, Mystery, Resurrection, spiritual fire, purification, works, light-world</keywords>

--- Page 82 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's explanation of how human works are processed after death. He distinguishes between works of love, which remain in the light, and malicious works, which fall into the "dark world." He also references his other writings to help the reader understand the "Great Mystery" of existence.</meta>

<margin>☛</margin>
104. For the works of Love, which emerged in the new body, pass through the power of the soul and through the fire, and remain in the divine Image within the source of the light. But those works belonging to the third Principle <note>In Boehme's philosophy, the "third principle" refers to the physical, material world.</note>—that is, of this world—remain in the source of the soul.

105. But whatever was done and acted in a completely evil and malicious manner in the third Principle, and was not renewed in this world through earnest repentance and reconciliation with one's brother—that falls into the *center of nature*, which is the root of the *dark world*.

106. The works of the ungodly shall not be able to remain in the fire; for the fire swallows them down into the dark center—namely <note>original: "Viz." (videlicet), meaning 'that is to say' or 'specifically.'</note> the origin of nature where the devils dwell. The fire of their souls (which is the Father’s principle) also goes there. That soul-fire shall have no fuel to make it burn properly; instead, it shall be like a quenched, dark, and painful source-fire, existing only as an anguish that desperately wants to produce fire. This is called *God's wrath* (and is a state, not a principle), a death or a dying source.

107. For the Father’s principle, in which the right and true soul exists, is a flaming fire that gives light. Within that light is the precious Image of God. That light tempers and sweetens the burning fire with the essence of *Love*, so that it is a pleasant delight and the source of *nature* and *life*.

<margin>☛</margin>
108. Therefore, I tell you not to think it strange or misunderstand when I or anyone else (whether it is Theophrastus <note>Theophrastus Paracelsus (1493–1541), a famous physician and mystic whose ideas influenced Boehme's spiritual philosophy.</note> or whoever) write that man shall stand before the Judgment *with the body which he had here*. I see very well that you have not yet understood my writings in the *Book of the Threefold Life* or the *Book of the Incarnation of Jesus Christ*. The latter book discusses Christ's suffering, death, and resurrection, and how we must enter into His death to rise out of it. You will find it explained and expanded there quite clearly. Since you do not have those books yet, please be patient; you will likely get to read them soon. Then you will be freed from your confusion and this deep searching.

109. For those books lay a foundation much deeper than your current understanding. If you read them correctly, you will truly find what the Mystery is, what the magical *Bysse* <note>original: "Bysse" (from the Greek "bythos"), referring to the spiritual "ground" or depth of being.</note> and *Abyss* <note>original: "Abysse," referring to the "Unground" or the bottomless void of the divine nature.</note> are, and what the Being of all beings is. There is no need to consult with one person or another; he who understands the Great Mystery—from which all beings have proceeded and still proceed—does not weigh himself down with such roundabout complications.

110. You have undertaken a very hard labor that does nothing but confuse, drain, and consume your life; it is entirely unnecessary,

<summary>Boehme explains that after death, a person's works are sorted: works of love remain in the divine light, while unrepentant evil works sink into the "dark world" of God's wrath. He encourages the reader to study his other works to better understand the deep mysteries of existence and the nature of the resurrection body.</summary>
<keywords>third principle, dark world, God's wrath, soul-fire, Image of God, Theophrastus Paracelsus, Great Mystery, Bysse and Abyss, Jacob Boehme, spiritual works</keywords>

--- Page 83 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's mystical exploration of the "Great Mystery" and how spiritual truth is hidden from those who are unworthy or unprepared. He emphasizes that true knowledge comes through spiritual rebirth rather than intellectual searching or astrological influence.</meta>

<margin>☛</margin>
110. ... Whoever finds it and knows the great Mystery finds all things within it; these require no literal demonstration. God, Christ, and eternity with all its wonders lie within it; the *Holy Spirit* is the key to it. If you are in the "new birth" as you claim, then there is no need for such difficult seeking and hard labor. Seek only Christ in the manger, in the dark stable; when you find him, then you shall indeed find where he sits at the right hand of God.

111. Searching alone does nothing. The *Philosopher's Stone* <note>original: "Philosophers Stone." In alchemy, a legendary substance that turns base metals into gold; for Boehme, it symbolizes the spiritual transformation of the soul through Christ.</note> is a very dark, unappreciated stone of a gray color, but within it lies the highest *Tincture* <note>A spiritual essence or "medicine" believed by mystics to transform the soul or physical matter.</note>. If you would search out the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note>, then consider only the earth with its metals, and so you may well find what the magical or Cabalistic <note>Referring to the Kabbalah, a Jewish mystical tradition often adapted by Christian mystics like Boehme to explain the hidden nature of God.</note> foundation is.

<margin>☛</margin>
112. The deep and mystical numbers—which otherwise no man is able to fathom or find out—all lie within the Mystery. But he who finds it does not search for the numbers; he takes gold for earth and acts like one who has a costly treasure lying in an obscure place. The manger and swaddling clothes of Christ are more acceptable to him than the whole world with its figures (or external pomp and glory). He hides the *numbers themselves*, for the outward kingdom must accomplish its wonders.

<margin>* Magi.</margin>
113. Why should the earthly Mystery be unveiled before its time? Inquire of the *Magi* <note>original: "Magists," referring to those who have mastered spiritual magic or divine wisdom.</note>, who have understood the heavenly and earthly *Magic* <note>original: "Magia"</note>. Why have they kept the *Tincture* secret and not revealed it? There is no other cause at all but that the world is not worthy of it; likewise, it is not worthy of the *numbers of the Mystery*.

<margin>MY Note.</margin>
114. Therefore, God has hidden them from us so that the earthly Mystery might accomplish and fulfill all its wonders on us, and that all the vials of God’s anger might be poured out upon us. How can a man undertake to reveal such secret things *without the consent of the Mystery*? Indeed, he tampers with the outside of the Mystery, but if he is to enter in, he must have the will of the Mystery.

115. The outward instigation to manifest and reveal the Mystery proceeds from the *stars*. For they would gladly be freed from vanity, and they drive the *magical children* powerfully toward manifestation. Therefore, we must test and examine the instigation: whether it proceeds from God’s light, from God’s spirit, or from the dominion and government of the stars.

<margin>❦</margin>
116. For God’s spirit speaks plainly of his Mystery; he only declares the *Turba* <note>A Latin term used by Boehme to describe the "turmoil" or "wrath" that disrupts the harmony of nature.</note> and leaves the *numbers* alone. He has once signed and sealed the Mystery with the power of the first principle in the *Seven Forms of Nature* <note>Boehme's system of seven qualities or "source-spirits" that constitute all existence.</note> toward the wonders of God. And again, he has signed it in the love in the humanity of Christ, with the *seven golden candlesticks and lights*. And with that he continues...

<summary>Boehme explains that the "Great Mystery" of God is found through humility and spiritual rebirth rather than intellectual pursuit, comparing it to the alchemical Philosopher's Stone. He warns that secret knowledge is hidden from the unworthy and that one must discern whether their drive for knowledge comes from the stars or from God's Spirit.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Mysterium Magnum, Philosopher's Stone, Tincture, Magi, Turba, Seven Forms of Nature, Alchemy, Kabbalah, Spiritual Rebirth</keywords>

--- Page 84 ---
<meta>This page continues the conclusion of Jacob Boehme's fourth epistle. He is explaining the constraints of divine revelation and why certain "numbers" or spiritual truths remain hidden until their appointed time.</meta>

...until the Judgment; each number manifests itself in its own age. No creature has the power to manifest it, for he who possesses it does not dare; otherwise, he transgresses the magical order and becomes an object of loathing to the Mystery.

<margin>Note ☞</margin>
117. And therefore the Prophets, and also Christ himself, have spoken entirely in parables, after a magical manner. Even to this day, no one who is capable of understanding the Mystery dares to speak otherwise, unless there is a specific purpose of God for that "Number" <note>original: "Number." In Boehme’s thought, this refers to a divine timing or a specific spiritual calculation/order within the cosmic timeline.</note> to be plainly revealed. Consider *Daniel*, who clearly indicated the time of Christ with its own number; he was commanded to do so. I tell you this sincerely and in all faithfulness, out of true Christian love toward you—not out of contempt, but from my knowledge and gifts, since you requested it of me. I have given you a short hint of what you are to do in this matter, and I entreat you to look upon it in a brotherly way.

118. Yet, whatever I am able to serve you with, despite my few gifts, if you desire more, it shall be done with goodwill—provided I perceive you are in earnest and that it will serve the honor of God and the welfare of mankind. And so I commend you into the love of ***Jesus Christ.***

->Dated at Görlitz <note>original: "Gerlits," the town in Saxony where Boehme lived.</note>, August 14th,<-
->in the **Year of our Lord** <note>original: "ANNO DOM:"</note><-
->**1620.**<-

<summary>Boehme explains that divine mysteries and prophetic "numbers" are revealed only according to God's timing and "magical order," concluding his letter with a sincere offer of further guidance to the recipient.</summary>

--- Page 85 ---
<meta>This page begins the fifth letter of Jacob Boehme, addressed to Paul Keym. It follows the conclusion of the fourth letter, where Boehme explained that divine mysteries and the timing of prophecy are revealed only according to God's will and spiritual order.</meta>

<page-num>67</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>
<sig>K 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: 2. Christ</meta>

# ANOTHER
# LETTER
# TO
# PAUL KEYM:

---

## Concerning the way to true Knowledge and the Regeneration <note>The process of being spiritually "born again" or renewed in Christ.</note> in CHRIST:

---

### Likewise concerning the thousand-year <note>original: "thousand yeares"; this refers to "Chiliasm" or the belief in a literal millennium of peace on earth before the final judgment.</note> Sabbath; and how the *Mysteries* in the *Revelation* are to be understood.

---

->***Our Salvation in Christ Jesus.***<-

---

<vocab>Paul Keym, Regeneration, thousand-year Sabbath, Mysteries, Revelation, Christ Jesus</vocab>

<margin>1.</margin> WORTHY and much respected Sir, and beloved Brother in Christ: I have received your last letter, and in it I have once more understood and well observed your zealous, inflamed mind in your intended labor and hard study. I have seen your anxious, earnest desire for the light of true knowledge, and thirdly, your great thirst for the fountain and well-spring of Christ, in which the mind is refreshed, quickened <note>original: "quickned," meaning made alive or given new life.</note>, comforted, satisfied, and appeased. Considering that I am a servant to my brothers and no less than a debtor to them in the love of Christ, I shall, in that same love, show and impart to you what I know and what has been given to me, as your desire requires it.

<summary>Jacob Boehme begins a letter to Paul Keym, acknowledging Keym's intense spiritual yearning for knowledge and the "new birth" in Christ. Boehme expresses his duty as a fellow Christian to share the insights he has received regarding divine mysteries and the biblical book of Revelation.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Paul Keym, Regeneration, thousand-year Sabbath, Revelation, spiritual knowledge, new birth, Christian love</keywords>

--- Page 86 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's letter to Paul Keym, expanding on the necessity of spiritual union with Christ to achieve true divine knowledge, contrasting this with the limitations of natural reason.</meta>

<page-num>68</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Birth.</header>

<margin>☛</margin>
2. Christ said, "*I am the vine, you are the branches; he who abides in me, and I in him, shall bring forth much fruit; for without me you can do nothing*"; also, he who abides in me and [has] my words [abiding] in him, he brings forth much fruit. Herein lies the whole foundation, and it is the only root or spring to the fountain from which the *Divine understanding* flows. There is no other foundation for true and real knowledge in the wisdom of God; no other seeking, studying, or searching is of any use.

3. For every spirit searches only its own depth—[for it reaches no further than its own natural capacity or instinct] and apprehends that in which it kindles itself. And though it searches within its own kindling, yet it finds nothing more than a type or representation of things, like a shadow or dream; it is not able to behold the *Being* <note>original: "Being," referring to the essential reality or substance of God (*Wesen* in German).</note> itself. For if it would see the *Being*, then it must be in the *Being*, and the *Being* in it, so that it may be capable of the *Being* and see truly within the *Being* itself.

4. Now then, seeing that we are dead in *Adam* to the Divine essence and have become blind and estranged, we have no power in ourselves; we know nothing of God in our reason except the history—that there is a God. We neither feel His power nor see His light unless we return and become like children, who know nothing but are guided and ruled. And just as a child looks upon its mother and longs for her, and she also cherishes and brings it up, so must the external reason be blinded, beaten down, and quite quashed <note>original: "quasht," meaning suppressed or completely subdued.</note>.

5. And the desire must resign and cast itself into the grace and love of God, and not regard the opposition and contradiction of the outward reason, which says, "It is nothing like that; God is far off; you must search, meditate, and represent Him only to yourself by your own understanding; you must seek after His will, how He has revealed Himself; *so He will be known, and no otherwise*." Thus the external, historical, Astral reason <note>Boehme uses "Astral reason" to describe human intellect governed by the natural world and the stars, rather than by the Holy Spirit.</note> judges; and it rules the whole world except for a very small number of God's children.

<margin>* How He has revealed Himself in His Word.</margin>
6. Christ said, "*You must abide in me; for without me you can do nothing; you can neither know nor search out anything truly and fundamentally concerning God.*" For "*he who comes to me, I will in no way cast out; in me you shall bring forth much fruit.*" Now, every branch grows on its own tree and has the sap, power, influence, virtue <note>original: "vertue," meaning inherent power or life-force.</note>, and property of the tree, and bears fruit according to the quality, kind, and property of the tree.

<margin>☛</margin>
7. Thus likewise, he who desires to be taught by God and to have Divine knowledge must stand in the tree into which God has grafted us through the *Regeneration* <note>The spiritual rebirth or "new birth" in Christ.</note>; he must have the sap and virtue of the...

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<summary>Boehme emphasizes that true divine knowledge comes only through a spiritual union with Christ, the "Vine," rather than through human intellect or "Astral reason." He argues that one must become like a child, subduing the ego and reason to be grafted into the divine life.</summary>
<keywords>Vine and Branches, Divine understanding, Astral reason, Adam, Regeneration, mystical union, humility, spiritual rebirth</keywords>

--- Page 87 ---
<meta>This page continues the fifth epistle, focusing on the process of spiritual rebirth through the "New man" and the struggle against the earthly nature of the "Old man," using the biblical figure of Nicodemus and the Fall of Adam as primary examples.</meta>

<page-num>69</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

...the tree; otherwise, he produces strange, unsavory, wild fruit that does not have the taste and flavor of the good tree. We must become like a child who understands nothing, but only knows its mother and longs for her.

<margin>☛</margin>
8. We must drink the new milk of *Christ’s Incarnation*, so that we may be made partakers of his flesh and spirit. His virtue and sap must be our virtue and sap; we must become *God’s Children* through *Divine Eating and Drinking*.

9. *Nicodemus* said, "*How can a man be born again in his old age?*" Yes, good *Nicodemus*, and good external, earthly Reason; how could it be that *Adam*, who was a perfect image of God, perished in his perfection and became earthly? Did it not happen through *Imagination* <note>original: "Imagination," in Boehme's philosophy, this refers to the creative power of the soul's desire to shape its own reality.</note>, because he directed his desire, longing, and lust into the outward astral <note>original: "Astrall," referring to the influence of the stars and planets upon the human psyche and body.</note>, elemental, and earthly kingdom [and set his mind upon the dominion of this world]? Thereupon, through his desire, lust, and imagination, he was immediately impregnated and became earthly. Thus he fell into the sleep of the external *Magic* <note>original: "Magia," the underlying spiritual power or wisdom that gives form to the physical world.</note>, and the *New Birth* happens in the same way.

<margin>☛</margin>
10. Through *Imagination* and an earnest, serious desire, we are again impregnated by the Deity and receive the New body within the Old. The New does not mix with the Old, just as gold in a coarse, rough stone is a completely different thing and has a different *Tincture* <note>original: "Tincture," an alchemical term for the essential spiritual quality or "dye" of a substance.</note> and *spirit* than the rough matter of the stone. So it is with the New man in the Old: the rough stone knows nothing of the gold, and likewise the earthly *Adam* knows nothing of the *Divine*, heavenly *Adam*. Therefore, there is strife in man, and man is at odds with himself.

11. The earthly *Adam* wants to see, feel, and taste, but he receives only a ray, a symbolic shadow, and a flickering reflection from the internal Man. While he may sometimes taste something of the Divine Man, it is not in essence. But just as the light of the sun disperses or swallows up the gloomy darkness so that it appears as if there were no more darkness at all—and yet the darkness is really hidden within the light, appearing again when the sun's light withdraws—

<margin>☛</margin>
12. So it is that the New man often swallows up the Old in Divine Power, so that the Old man assumes he has grasped the Deity. However, he is not capable of that essence; rather, the Spirit of God from the New man passes through the Old. But when that Spirit enters again into its Mystery <note>original: "Mystery," referring to the *Ungrund* or the hidden, divine source beyond physical manifestation.</note>, then the Old man does not know what happened to him. He seeks ways to come to God and searches for God's purpose and will, yet he finds nothing but human invention, fiction, and opinion. He is very zealous in his opinions and does not know what he is doing; he find—

<meta>eth</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains the process of spiritual rebirth through the "imagination" or focused desire of the soul, contrasting the divine "New man" with the earthly "Old man." He uses the metaphor of gold hidden within a rough stone to describe how the divine nature exists within the human person without mixing with the earthly nature.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, New Birth, Nicodemus, Adam, Imagination, Tincture, Mystery, Magia, spiritual alchemy, dual nature of man</keywords>

--- Page 88 ---
<meta>This page continues the fifth epistle, expanding on the spiritual nature of the "New man" and the relationship between the internal heavenly world and the external physical world.</meta>

<page-num>70</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle</header>

...does not touch the root; for it is not capable or worthy of it; and this shows that it must die and perish.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. But the **New man** <note>original: "New man," referring to the regenerated spiritual self within the believer.</note>, which—through an earnest, serious will and purpose—arises through **Imagination** <note>original: "Imagination," which in Boehme’s thought refers to the soul's creative desire or the power to form a spiritual reality.</note> [or the effective operation of true Faith], remains steadfast in the rest of Christ. This occurs even in the Tree (which God the Father, by His motion when He moved Himself the second time according to His heart, grafted into the human soul). It springs forth in the life of God and grows and flourishes in the power, virtue, and sap of the **Divine Essentiality** <note>original: "Divine Essentiality," the spiritual substance or "being-ness" of God.</note>, in God's Love. This man receives divine knowledge and skill, not according to the measure of the external will—what the external man wants to know and search out—but according to the measure of the **internal Heaven**. The internal Heaven kindles [and enlightens] the external, so that the understanding or intellectual faculty of the soul comprehends and understands the external.

14. For God, who is a spirit and also a Being, has manifested Himself through the **external world** in a likeness <note>original: "similitude"</note>, so that the spirit might see itself in the **Being essentially** <note>original: "Being essentially," referring to God's fundamental existence.</note>. Not only that, but so that the creature likewise might contemplate and behold the being of God in the outward form <note>original: "Figure"</note> and know it.

15. For no creature is able to see the Being of God outside of itself; the spirit sees God in the essence and luster of His Majesty, and likewise within itself and its own fellow creatures like itself. For God is Himself the **spirit of all Beings** (meaning heavenly beings), so that when we see the divine creature, we see an image or likeness that proceeded from God's being. And when we see the will and working of that creature, then we see the will and working of God.

16. In this way also is the **New man born of God**. Whatever it wills and does is God's will and work; its knowing is God's knowing, for we know nothing of God without God's spirit.

17. The external cannot see the internal; but if the internal draws the external into itself by a glimpse [or influence of light in its own idea or contemplation], then the external apprehends the mirror or resemblance of the internal for instruction and direction. This shows that the external world takes its rise and origin from the internal, and that *our works shall follow us into the Mystery*; and by the separation of God's judgment and the fire of the Principle <note>original: "Principle," referring to the fundamental spiritual forces that govern existence.</note>, they shall be set into the eternal world.

<margin>☞</margin>
18. To this end God has created angels and men—namely, for His wondrous deeds—so that the wisdom of the **Divine Power** might appear, and that God might behold Himself in the resemblances and ideas of His creatures, having joy in Himself with the beings created out of His own wisdom.

<summary>This page describes the "New man" as a spiritual being grafted into the soul by God, capable of perceiving divine truths that the external mind cannot reach. It explains that the physical world is a mirror of the internal spiritual world, created so that God may behold His own wisdom and joy through His creatures.</summary>
<keywords>New man, Divine Essentiality, internal Heaven, spiritual rebirth, Imagination, divine knowledge, creation, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 89 ---
<meta>This page continues the Fifth Epistle, where Jacob Boehme explains the relationship between the soul's desire and the comprehension of spiritual mysteries. He continues the discussion of the "external world" and the "internal heaven" from the previous page.</meta>

<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>
<page-num>78</page-num>

# The Fifth Epistle.

19. Loving brother, do not be offended that I speak bluntly <note>original: "roundly"</note> to you. You complain that you are not always able to reach, understand, and retain the Divine Mysteries; moreover, you say that you often get a glimpse of them, but that my writings are *hard* and difficult for you to understand. I will, therefore, show you—according to the power and ability I have received from God—how the nature of your hidden Mystery stands, which you are currently unable to understand.

<margin>☞</margin>
20. Your intention and will is to keep [the light of the Mystery] in a continuous, steady understanding. This is the desire of the *external* world within you; it would like to be capable of containing the Deity and be freed from vanity. But the spirit of the external world must remain in continual labor and earnest *seeking*; for through its seeking, it finds the wonders of its own *Magia* <term>Magia: In Boehme's thought, this refers to a spiritual "magic" or the power of the will and desire to form and create reality.</term>—namely, the type and resemblance of the internal world.

21. For God does not always move Himself, but the longing and earnest labor of the creature moves the *Mystery*, so that the image or idea of the Divine Wisdom may be sought and found. Therefore, Christ commands us to *seek and knock*, and also promises to give us the pearl or jewel in that seeking.

22. The external world is also from God and of God, and man is created into the external world for this purpose: that he might bring the external forms <note>original: "Figures"</note> into the internal; that he might bring the end back into the beginning.

<margin>☞</margin>
23. The more a person longs for God, and the more he *pants* and runs after Him, the more he comes out of the end and into the beginning—not only for God’s wonder, but for his own edification. For the branch <note>original: "twig"</note> of the tree continually thirsts after the sap and power <note>original: "vertue"</note> of the tree; it labors in desire for the tree and draws its sap and influence into itself, and thus grows into a great branch. In this way, the anxious hunger and earnest longing in the *human mystery* draws the Kingdom of God into itself, of which Christ said, *The Kingdom of Heaven suffers violence, and the violent take it by force to themselves.* <note>Referencing Matthew 11:12.</note>

24. A being or essence that is not attractive <note>In the sense of "drawing or pulling inward," like a magnet.</note> cannot grow or acquire a body for itself, but instead it starves and wastes away. We see this in how the fire of a candle draws or attracts the wax <note>original: "fat"</note> into itself and consumes it, yet from that consumption, it provides a shining light. So it is with man: he is shut up and enclosed (with his original Divine essence) in the darkness of death, but God has again opened that essence to the soul in Christ.

25. Now the poor, imprisoned soul is this very hungry *magical* fire, which again attracts to itself the revealed Divine essence from the *Incarnation* of Christ; and so it feeds on God’s being.

<summary>Boehme explains that the soul's difficulty in grasping divine mysteries is a natural part of spiritual growth, comparing the soul's longing to a fire that must "attract" and consume fuel to produce light.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Divine Mysteries, Magia, spiritual longing, Incarnation, spiritual growth, internal vs external world</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the Fifth Epistle, where Jacob Boehme explains the relationship between the soul's desire and the comprehension of spiritual mysteries. Having established that the "external world" is a reflection of the internal, he now explains how the soul's attachment to the earthly image leads to suffering and the "bearing of the cross."</meta>

<lang>en</lang>
<page-num>72</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

...ing, and takes it into itself; and from this [spiritual and essential] eating, consuming, or digesting, it gives forth a body of light which is both like the Deity and capable of containing it. Thus, the poor soul becomes clothed with a body of light, like the fire in a candle, and in this body of light it finds rest. But in the darkness of this world [in its earthly carcass and clothing of clay, where the curse of God and all evil inclinations and false desires cling], it has anguish and trouble.

26. But now, since it is the case that the soul has put on the earthly image along with Adam, it must therefore bear it. Just as the fire of a candle must take its burning light from the dark lump of fat: if the soul had remained in God’s being with Adam and had not put on the earthly image, it would not have needed to bear it. But now it is bound to bear it.

27. For Saint Paul says: *“To whomever you give yourselves as servants in obedience, his servants you are—whether to sin, leading to death, or to the obedience of God, leading to righteousness.”* <note>original: Romans 6:16</note> Now, seeing that the soul has put on the earthly image—which produces nothing but fruit for death—and has yielded and devoted itself as a servant to sin, it has now become the sinful servant of death.

28. Why has it fallen in love with a strange master that dominates it? Had it only remained a child and not lusted after the Tree of Knowledge of Good and Evil, it would not have needed to be in subjection to both governments <note>In Boehme's cosmology, the "two governments" are the realm of God's love/light and the realm of His wrath/darkness.</note>. But since it desired to be like God in both love and anger, according to both the <term>Principles of Eternity</term>, it must now bear that image and undergo the force and sway of both. Thus, it must endure the "fire-burning" [the curse and anger of God ignited in the divided properties of nature] until the day of separation.

<margin>☞</margin>
29. Therefore, this is called "bearing the cross." For when the <term>Magical fire</term> <note>original: "Magicall fire" — Boehme uses "Magic" to describe the spiritual power of the will or desire to manifest reality.</note> arises, it creates a <term>Cross-like-birth</term> <note>A term Boehme uses to describe the intersection and conflict of spiritual forces (like the four elements or qualities) as they struggle to find balance.</note>; one form of nature presses and pierces entirely through the other. That is, one is contrary to the other, like sweet against sour, sharp against bitter, and the fire against them all.

<margin>* A Being/Thing. <note>original: "O, Thing." Boehme often uses the term "Being" or "Substance" to refer to the essential reality of a thing.</note></margin>
30. And if the soul had let the body of light be its only lord and master, and had not focused its imagination on the <term>external kingdom of this world</term> (that is, on the spirit of the greater world in the stars and elements) nor lusted after earthly fruit, then the wrath [or the working power of darkness in the curse of God, which is the departure of his love from a * Being] would have been, as it were, swallowed up in it [and would not have been manifest or apparent]. There would not have been any sense, perception, or feeling of it. But since the soul has departed from the gentleness <note>original: "meeknesse"</note> of the light and gone out of the love of God, it now feels the wrath, or burning anger, of the eternal nature.

<meta>catchword: 31. And</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains how the soul, by following Adam's example and lusting after earthly things, has become trapped in a "clothing of clay" and must suffer the conflict between light and dark forces, a process he calls "bearing the cross." He argues that if the soul stayed focused on the light of God, the "burning anger" of nature would be unnoticed.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the numbered points from the previous page, detailing the internal struggle between the soul's desire for God and the "wrath" of its natural, fiery origin. <page-num>73</page-num>
<header>The Second Epistle.</header>

31. And therefore it must work, labor, and strive to obtain the light again; this is why the life of man exists in such anguish, in painful seeking, in continual abstinence, and repentance. It earnestly desires the Divine Rest, and yet is held back by the wrath of Nature. <note>original: "wrath of Nature" refers to the harsh, conflicting forces of the material and psychological world that Boehme believes must be tempered by Divine Light.</note>

32. The more the life desires to flee from the wrath [or fierceness of Nature], the stronger and more vehement the struggle grows within that life—aside from what the Devil stirs up and brings into his *Nest* [being the center of the soul] through his poisonous incantations, magical imaginations, representations, and suggestions. He continually presents the magical image of the poisonous serpent before the soul, so that the soul might constantly focus its imagination upon it and inflame itself with that same poison; this happens daily. Thus, the fire of the soul becomes an evil, poisonous, burning, sulfurous fire.

33. Yet if the soul departs from the serpent-like image of the Devil and rejects the evil earthly Tree [where the serpent has cunningly coiled himself]—which *Tree* is pride, covetousness, envy, anger, and falsehood—and does not long for it, but makes itself as if dead to this figure, as if it knew nothing of it; and if it casts away the very lust and imagination itself, and desires only the love of God, submitting itself wholly to God’s will and working, so that He alone may be its willing, working, and doing; then the Divine light begins to shine within it. It obtains an eye of "right seeing," so that it is able to behold its own natural form and features, by which it steps into plain, straightforward, and meek humility.

<margin>☛</margin>
34. It wills nothing, and it also desires nothing, but submits and casts itself into the bosom of its Mother <note>original: "Mother" here refers to the Divine Wisdom or the Holy Spirit.</note>; like a child that desires nothing but its mother, leaning toward her and longing only for her. It does not highly value any clever skill, subtle reason, or great knowledge; and though it may know much, yet it is not puffed up or elevated in its own conceit by its knowledge. Instead, it leaves and submits the knowing, willing, and working entirely to its Mother's spirit, so that the Spirit might be both the will and the work within it.

35. I speak according to my knowledge: the Devil, in the power of God's anger, continually attacks and opposes this precious sprout of the soul [that noble twig of Divine light and love springing forth from the Tree of Life, *Christ within us*]. He casts vain suggestions, false desires, and earthly imaginations toward the root of nature—that is, toward the forms of the "fire-life" in the first principle <note>original: "first principle" refers to the underlying energetic source of life which can become "fiery" or "wrathful" if not united with the "second principle" of Divine Light.</note>—to kindle them in their own natural working properties, which are self-pride, covetousness, envy, anger, falsehood,

<summary>Boehme describes the soul's intense struggle to escape the "wrath of nature" and the Devil's temptations by practicing total resignation to God. He argues that by rejecting the "earthly tree" of vices and becoming like a humble child, the soul allows the Divine Light to transform its internal fire into love.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual warfare, resignation, Divine Light, wrath of nature, the Serpent, Tree of Life, Christ within, humility, first principle</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the numbered points from the previous page, detailing the internal struggle between the soul's desire for God and the "wrath" of its natural, fiery origin. <page-num>74</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

hypocrisy, lust, and so on] and would continually by all means completely destroy the precious sprout [or noble twig of grace]. He continually shoots his evil, poisonous rays into the soul's <term>magical fire</term> <note>original: "Magicall fire" refers to Boehme's concept of the soul's fundamental energy or will, which can either burn with the fire of God's love or the "dark fire" of self-centeredness.</note> with evil lusts, intense desires, and thoughts. He provides strange matter or fuel for the soul's fire to burn or feed upon, so that it might by no means reach a shining light; he extinguishes, suppresses, and hinders it, so that his kingdom might not be known.

36. But on the other side, the noble twig defends itself and wants nothing to do with the fierce, dark, and wrathful source; it arises and springs forth like a plant out of the wild earth, yet the Devil strives continually against it.

<margin>☛</margin>
37. Therefore, my dearly beloved friend, there is such strife and contention in man; and because of this, he sees the *Divine* light as in a mirror, and sometimes he gets a perfect glimpse of it. For as long as the twig of the soul can defend itself against the poison of the Devil, so long does it have the shining light.

38. For when the <term>magical fire</term> of the soul receives the *Divine* essence (that is, the divine body, *Christ's* flesh), then the Holy Spirit clearly arises and flashes forth in the soul as a victory, just as He goes forth from *God* the Father through the word or mouth of the Son (that is, from the Heart of the sacred <term>Ternary</term> <note>original: "Ternary" refers to the Holy Trinity.</note>) out of the *Divine* essence. And thus He goes or proceeds forth out of the being or essence of the noble lily-twig, which springs forth and grows out of the fire of the soul. This lily-twig is the true *Image of God*, for it is the new-born or regenerated spirit of the soul, the spirit of God's will—the triumphant chariot of the Holy Spirit, in which He rides into the sacred Trinity into the *Angelic World*.

39. And with this twig, or Image [of *God* renewed in us] as was mentioned before, we are in *Christ* outside of this world, in the *Angelic World*, of which the <term>old Adam</term> <note>original: "old Adam" refers to human nature in its fallen, unredeemed state, dominated by earthly and selfish desires.</note> has no understanding or perception; also, he does not know it, just as the rough stone does not know the gold that nonetheless grows within it.

### *The Gate of the True Knowledge of the Threefold Life.*

<margin>☛</margin>
40. Man is the true likeness or Image of *God*, as the precious man *Moses* testifies. He is not only an earthly image (for the sake of which *God* would not have become human and put forth, united, and married His heart and spirit [in deepest love] to it after the Fall), but he is originally out of the Being of all beings—out of all three worlds. Namely, out of the innermost nature-world, which is also the most outward, and is called the *dark world*, from which the principle of the *fiery nature* takes its rise, as is declared at large in my *Book of the Threefold Life.*

<meta>catchword: 41. And</meta>

<summary>This page describes the spiritual battle within the soul, where the Devil attempts to quench the "noble twig of grace" with lust and hypocrisy. It explains that through Christ's essence, the soul can be regenerated into a "lily-twig" or "Image of God," existing in the Angelic World even while on earth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the numbered points describing the soul's relationship with the "internal" and "external" worlds. It explains the necessity of the "new birth" and the soul's role as a laborer in God's vineyard, gathering the "wonders" of nature back into the divine light.</meta>

<page-num>75</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

# The Fifth Epistle.

41. And secondly, he is outside of the light, or *Angelical World*, and outside of the true *Being of God*. Thirdly, he is outside of this external world of the sun, stars, and elements—a complete image of God, yet separated from the *Being of all Beings*.

42. His first image existed in *Paradise*, in the *Angelical World*. But he lusted after the *external world* (that is, after the *astral* and *elemental world*), which has swallowed up and covered the precious image of the internal heaven. Now, the external world rules in that likeness as if it were its own property.

43. Therefore *Christ* said: *You must be born again, or else you cannot see the Kingdom of God* <note>John 3:3</note>. This is why the Word or Heart of God entered into the *human essence*: so that we, through our soul, might be able—by the power of the Word or Heart of God—to beget and bring forth a new sprout or image from our soul, just like the first one.

44. Therefore, the old *carcass* <note>original: "Carcasse"; referring to the fallen, physical body</note> must rot, decay, and perish; for it is unfit for the *Kingdom of God*. It carries nothing but its own mystery back to its first beginning—namely, its wonders and works. Understand this as the essence of the "First Principle," which is immortal and incorruptible, being the <term>magical fire</term> of the soul.

45. And not only this, but he must bring and unite the *end* with the *beginning*. For the external world was generated out of the internal and created into a tangible being. The wonders of this world belong to the Beginning, and they were known from eternity in the wisdom of God—that is, in the <term>Divine Magia</term> <note>original: "Divine Magia"; from the Latin *magia*, meaning the supernatural power or wisdom through which God brings ideas into being.</note>. This was not in terms of physical being or essence, but in the mirror of the Virgin-like wisdom of God, from which the *eternal nature* always arises [or takes its origin] from eternity.

<margin>Or, bring forth.</margin>
46. To this end, the poor soul stands in the prison of the astral and elemental kingdom, so that it might be a *laborer*. Its task is to reunite the wonders of the external nature with the world of light and bring them back to the beginning. Although the soul must now be bruised, oppressed, and endure much, it is the *servant* in God’s vineyard. It prepares the precious wine that is drunk in the Kingdom of God. The soul is the sole cause of understanding; the soul's desire works within the Mystery, manifesting and bringing the hidden wonders of God to light. We see this plainly in how man searches out and reveals the wonders of nature.

47. Therefore, we must not be amazed or strangely troubled when the noble image is hidden and we cannot receive any refreshment or comfort. We must know that the poor soul is then being put to work in the vineyard, so that it may produce fruit to be set upon *God’s table*.

48. It is then given a twig or branch of the wild vine,

<summary>Boehme explains that man's soul is currently trapped in the physical world and must undergo a "new birth" to reclaim its original divine image. He describes the soul as a laborer in God's vineyard, tasked with revealing the hidden wonders of nature and reuniting them with the heavenly realm.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the numbered points describing the soul’s journey. Having discussed the "new birth" and the soul's role in the "First Principle," the text now explains how the soul must cultivate its divine knowledge to become a productive laborer in God's kingdom.</meta>

<page-num>76</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

...it, it must trim and dress them so that they may shine into the divine and heavenly mystery; it must unite them with the Kingdom of God. This is to be understood in the following way:

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Instructions or admonitions.</margin>
49. Just as a plant or a <term>graft</term> <note>original: "grift"</note> that is planted works until it puts forth its branches and then its fruit, so must the twig <gloss>[graft or scion]</gloss> of the soul—which stands overshadowed in a dark valley—continually labor so that it might bring forth fruit. This fruit is the noble and precious knowledge of God. When this knowledge has grown within it, so that the soul knows God, it then yields its fair fruits, which are good ***teachings, works, and virtues***. It leads to the Kingdom of God; it helps to plant and build the Kingdom of God. Then, it is a true laborer in the Vineyard of Christ.

50. Therefore, what I teach, write, and speak about is nothing other than what has been worked within me. Otherwise, I could know nothing of it. I have not scraped it together out of historical accounts to form opinions, as the ***Babylonian*** School <note>A reference to "Babel" or the "City of Confusion," representing established, worldly churches and academic theology that Boehme felt lacked spiritual insight.</note> does, where men eagerly argue over words and opinions. By God's grace, I have obtained eyes of my own and am able to work in Christ’s vineyard myself.

51. I speak plainly and freely: whatever is patched together from conjecture and opinion (in which a person has no divine knowledge upon which to base their conclusions) is <gloss>[truly]</gloss> ***Babel***—a spiritual <term>prostitution</term> <note>original: "whoredome"; used here in a biblical sense to mean spiritual unfaithfulness or the corruption of truth.</note>. For personal conceit or mere thinking must not do it; indeed, it must not be any opinion or speculative understanding, but rather the knowledge of God in the Holy Spirit.

<margin>☞</margin>
52. The children of God have spoken as they were driven by the Holy Spirit. They have planted many diverse trees, but they all stand upon one root, which is the internal heaven. No one can find this root unless they also stand upon it; therefore, the <term>external heaven</term> <note>The physical, material universe or purely intellectual/scientific inquiry.</note> cannot discover them or explain them through human skill or "art."

53. The words of the holy children of God remain a hidden mystery to the earthly man. Although he thinks he understands them, he has nothing more than a dim, <gloss>[historical]</gloss> glimpse of them. We see this today in how men wrangle and argue about Christ’s doctrine and worship, and fight over God's will and how He must be served. Yet, God is not served or worshipped through any opinion, but ***in the spirit of Christ*** and in truth—this is how men truly serve God.

54. It does not depend on what ceremonies and customs we use. Everyone labors in their work and gifts according to their own ***constellation*** <note>In Boehme's thought, "constellation" refers to a person's unique spiritual and physical makeup, influenced by their inner nature.</note> and individual property, but all are driven and led by one and the same Spirit. Otherwise, God would be finite and measurable, if the gifts...

<meta>Catchword: were</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme argues that true divine knowledge comes from direct spiritual experience rather than academic study or "Babylonian" debate. He describes the soul as a graft that must grow to bear the fruit of virtue and emphasizes that while spiritual gifts vary, they all stem from a single divine source.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, inner knowledge, Babylonian School, Vineyard of Christ, spiritual vs. historical, internal heaven, spiritual gifts, Babel</keywords>
