<meta>This page continues the numbered points describing the soul’s journey and the spiritual labor required for new birth. The author emphasizes that the soul's struggle is a natural part of its purification and growth within the divine life.</meta>

<page-num>27</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

...were only one; but he is a significant wonder—whoever understands him walks in his wonders.

<margin>* Or, Take</margin>
55. I impart this to you in all sincerity, out of a true Christian zeal, from my own fountain, gifts, and knowledge; and I exhort you to <margin>* Or, Take</margin> understand it in a right sense, as it is intended. I do not extol or set myself up, but I speak brotherly to your mind to stir you up and to comfort you, so that you should not think the yoke of Christ to be heavy when the <term>external man</term> <note>The physical body and worldly mind, as opposed to the "internal man" or the spiritual soul.</note> often clouds the internal. The poor soul mourns for its <term>Image</term> <note>The divine likeness or spiritual form of the soul which is restored through Christ.</note>, which is nevertheless purified, truly begotten, and brought forth under tribulation [and the cross of Christ]. It is even so with me and other Christians as well; do not think it *strange*.

<margin>☞</margin>
56. It is very good when the poor soul is in combat; it is much better than when it is imprisoned and yet plays the hypocrite and puts on devout appearances <note>original: "shewes"</note>. It is written, *That all things shall work for the best for those who love God.*

57. Now, when the combat of the soul arises and proceeds—such that it would gladly see God and yet cannot at all times reach him—then know that it fights for the noble trophy of which the external man knows nothing. Indeed, the Spirit of God fights in the natural soul for that which is supernatural, so that he may lead the creature into God. He would always gladly crown the soul with the precious image if blind reason would only give him room and allow the understanding to cooperate.

<margin>☞</margin>
58. We must labor and strive against external reason, and also against flesh and blood, and wholly oppose the assaults and objections of the Devil. We must always break them and cast them away, and resist evil thoughts, impulses <note>original: "motions"</note>, and influences. With our whole soul, through prayer, supplication, or internal <term>resignation</term> <note>original: "resignation" (German: *Gelassenheit*), a central concept in Boehme's thought meaning the total yielding of the self-will to God's will.</note>, we must press into God's mercy.

59. Thus the precious grain of mustard seed is sown, which, if it is well preserved, becomes great like a tree. Upon that tree, the fruits of Paradise afterwards grow, on which the soul feeds when it wishes to *prophesy* and *speak of the Kingdom of God*. When it beholds the <term>*Divine Magia*</term> <note>original: "Magia," referring to the creative, supernatural power or wisdom of God, rather than common sorcery.</note>, it then *speaks of the wonders of God*.

60. For the being of God is undivided; it needs no room or place, but it arises [moves and flies] in the spirit of understanding like the splendor of the sun in the air. It shoots or glances into the image like lightning, by which the whole body is often ignited and enlightened.

<margin>☞</margin>
61. Moreover, know that we in this life are laborers and not idle persons, for the birth of life is a continual struggle and labor. The more we labor in God’s vineyard, the more fruit we shall obtain.

<summary>This page discusses the spiritual conflict between the "external man" (worldly reason) and the "internal man" (the soul), describing this struggle as a necessary labor to reach the "Divine Magia" and God's kingdom. Boehme encourages the reader to persevere through tribulation, as the soul is being purified and enlightened like a growing mustard seed.</summary>
<keywords>internal man, external man, spiritual combat, Divine Magia, resignation, mustard seed, Jacob Boehme, tribulation, new birth</keywords>

--- Page 96 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's explanation of the spiritual labor of the soul. It transitions from the necessity of inner struggle to the concept of the "Sabbath" or spiritual rest, debating whether such a state can truly exist within the physical, elemental world.</meta>

<page-num>78</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

...and eternally enjoy; and it contributes to our own edification, for our labor remains in our own *Mystery* <note>original: "Mystery"; here referring to the inner spiritual essence or the hidden "ledger" of a person's life and works</note> for God's deeds of wonder, and for our own eternal *crown and glory* before God; as in my other *writings* is set forth at length.

62. Concerning the *Sabbath* in *this world*, about which you have written and still hold the same opinion: the knowledge and understanding of it has not been given to me. Also, I do not know how there can be any perfect being in the "house of torment" <note>original: "Torment-house"</note> of the stars and elements. I cannot find such an understanding of it in the Mystery, because the first man [Adam] was not able to stand even when the heavenly Governor ruled in him, but was overcome by the kingdom of the stars and elements. Might it not, therefore, still be dangerous?

63. When we consider the possibility and the impossibility of it in the Mystery, it then appears as if there would be no Sabbath in the sad and sorrowful mirror of the Divine Being. For the Devil is a prince of this world, and even if he should be bound for a thousand years in the dark world, the fierce, wrathful stars—as well as heat and cold—still have their rule and dominance in this world. This world is nothing else but a *valley of misery*.

64. Now, if the dominion of the stars did not reach us or have any influence upon us, then we would not be in this world but in Paradise, where no wicked person could oppose us or see us; for in Paradise we are swallowed up or enclosed in God.

65. Just as we cannot see angels with our earthly eyes, a wicked person of this world will not see a new <gloss>[regenerate]</gloss> man in Christ. When we attain the state of the new man in Christ, then we are (as that new man) already in the Sabbath, and we only wait for the *redemption* from the wicked earthly life.

<margin>☞</margin>
66. For we are with Christ in God; we are planted together with him into his death. We are buried in him and arise as the new man out of the grave with him, living eternally in our own being or essence—understand this as being in his corporeality <note>original: "corporiety"</note>. We are with and in Christ, in God, and God is in us. Where should we then keep the Sabbath? Not in this world, but in the angelic world, in the world of light.

67. And if the wicked should be tormented for a thousand years in this world, then the place <gloss>[of this world]</gloss> must be moved <note>original: "translated"</note> or withdrawn into the dark world; for in this solar world, there is not yet any hellish torment. But if the sun were taken away, then it would be so indeed; and then the wicked would be even further separated from the judgment, and there would be a gulf of a <term>Principle</term> <note>Boehme uses "Principle" to describe distinct realms of existence or spiritual dimensions, such as the Dark World, the Light World, or the Outward World.</note> interposed and fixed between them.

<meta>Catchword: 68. Moreover</meta>

<summary>Boehme argues that a true spiritual Sabbath cannot fully exist in the physical world of stars and elements, which he calls a "valley of misery," but is instead found internally through union with Christ in the "angelic world." He suggests that the state of the regenerate person is hidden from the wicked, just as the spiritual world is hidden from earthly eyes.</summary>
<keywords>Sabbath, spiritual rest, regeneration, stars and elements, Paradise, New Man, corporeality, Principle, Jacob Boehme, mystical theology</keywords>

--- Page 97 ---
<meta>This page continues the author's argument regarding the nature of spiritual rest and the "Sabbath." It explores why God is not the source of human suffering and how the soul must wait for the final judgment to be fully separated from the "house of torment" of the physical world.</meta>

<page-num>79</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

<margin>☞</margin>
68. Furthermore, God is not a God of evil who desires revenge or torment, nor would he—out of his own vengeance—torment and plague the wicked for a thousand years before the Judgment. The wicked person torments himself within his own *life's birth* <term>original: "Lifes-birth"; the internal process by which a soul's nature and character are formed</term>; one form of life is an enemy to the other. That conflict shall indeed be his hellish torment, and God bears no blame for it.

69. He never desired the fall of man; rather, the *wrathful nature* gained the upper hand, and the spirit of man's will (which is free, as God himself is free) freely and willingly yielded itself up to the struggle, expecting to dominate and rule.

70. It was out of pride that the Devil fell, and man fell as well. If they had remained in humility, God would have continued to dwell in them; but they themselves departed from God. Yet, God loved the human image so exceedingly that he himself, out of love, re-entered the *image of man* <note>A reference to the Incarnation of Christ</note>. Why, then, would he desire man's torment?

<margin>☞</margin>
71. In God, there is no evil desire. However, *his wrath*—which is the *dark world* <term>Boehme’s term for the foundational, "fire-source" of nature that exists without the light of God's love</term>—is a desire for evil and destruction. It is this which caused the Devil and also man to fall. The dark world caused the Devil to fall, and the *external, wrathful nature* caused man to fall. Yet both of these are tied and bound to one another; we would clearly see and feel this if the sun were taken out of this world.

72. Therefore, I say again: the righteous person keeps the *Sabbath* <note>original: "Sabbath"; here meaning a state of spiritual rest and cessation from the "labor" of sin</note> in "Abraham’s bosom," in the rest of Christ. For Christ has destroyed for us the wrathful death that held us captive; he has opened life so that we, as a "new man," are able to spring forth, blossom, and rest in him.

73. But the *old man of the stars and elements* <term>The physical and psychological self governed by material nature and astrological influences</term> must remain in his own region—in his house of torment and misery—until he is returned to the earth. Then, everything passes back into its own *Mystery* <note>original: "Mystery"; the hidden spiritual source or "Mysterium Magnum" from which all things originate</note> again. The soul remains in its own *principle* <note>Its specific spiritual realm, either light or dark</note> until the judgment of God, when God shall again move and ignite the Mystery. Then, everything will separate into its own property; each world shall take in its own harvest, whether it be good or bad. It shall divide itself as light and darkness.

74. Therefore, I entreat you entirely as a brother and a Christian to take care that you grasp the Sabbath in the *rest of Christ*. Do not be so moved by the burning excitement of your spirit [or the fervent prompting of your mind], but search whether you are able to ground your ideas in the *light of nature* <term>The inherent human capacity for reason and understanding the physical world</term>. If you are able to ground and reach it in the light of the *eternal nature*, then indeed you may proceed; but show it to us plainly.

<summary>The author argues that human suffering and "hell" are self-inflicted consequences of the soul's own internal nature rather than God's vengeance. He emphasizes that while the "new man" finds rest in Christ, the physical self remains subject to the material world's misery until the final judgment.</summary>

<keywords>free will, the Fall, wrath of God, dark world, Mystery, Sabbath, light of nature, Jacob Boehme, spiritual rest, judgment day</keywords>

--- Page 98 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s explanation of spiritual understanding. He moves from discussing the state of the soul after death to explaining how the book of Revelation and divine mysteries can only be understood through a spiritual, "magical" lens rather than a literal or academic one.</meta>

<page-num>86</page-num>
# The Fifth Epistle.

...plainly so that we may see it; otherwise, our mind will be *unsatisfied* unless it finds the foundational ground.

75. This is not to be proved merely with *Scriptures*, which might be seemingly quoted to support it; people can just as easily use them to prove the opposite and apply them in other ways. If my mind had not turned itself into the love and rest of Christ, I would show it to you according to the fashion of this present *controversial world*.

76. The *Book of Revelation* is spiritual and lies or remains deep within the Mystery. It requires a highly illuminated mind and an understanding that has the *power* to enter into the Mystery of God. It speaks *magically* <term>original: "Magically"; here referring to a spiritual, symbolic, or supernatural mode of communication that transcends literal logic</term>. Therefore, a "magical" understanding is required to comprehend it. I do not find this magical understanding in a mere *historical* <note>original: "Hystoricall"; an understanding based only on literal facts or chronological events rather than spiritual truth</note> apprehension of the text.

<margin>☛</margin> 77. Anyone who intends to study the *Heavenly Magic* <note>original: "Heavenly Magia"</note> must know the heavenly figures of the form of the internal heaven: namely, the center or life’s circle, from which all beings arise and from which this world is produced. If he does not have the magical guide within him, then he should not meddle with these heavenly figures; otherwise, the *Great Turba* <term>original: "Turba Magna"; the "Great Turba" or "Great Confusion," a destructive force in Boehme's philosophy that breaks down or "spews out" anything not rooted in God's light</term> has the power to *spew* him out of the *Divine Magic*.

78. John the Evangelist—or whoever wrote the *Revelation*—knew the figures of the Divine *Magic*. Although he says he was carried away in the spirit and things were shown to him, these are figures that remain within the Divine *Magic*. Even though they are revealed, they require a master <note>original: "Magist"</note> who understands *Thesaurinella* <gloss>[ the little treasury of wisdom or the cabinet of the Divine Magic ]</gloss>.

79. He must understand all the "Three Principles" <note>A core concept in Boehme's work describing the dark fire-world, the light-world of love, and the visible physical world</note> along with their figures; only then does he have the power to open and reveal them. Otherwise, his labor <gloss>[ writings or expositions ]</gloss> will fall to the *Turba*. I speak sincerely: if you please, read my book *The Threefold Life* correctly, and there you shall find the root of this *Magic*. Although there are other much deeper works available, I wish for you to at least understand that one, for it has a sufficient foundation. Otherwise, you will not be able to comprehend the others. If it then pleases you to search further, you may certainly obtain them; however, there must be earnestness involved, otherwise they will remain silent <gloss>[ or seem to be only a bare, obscure history ]</gloss>.

80. For the foundation of it is deeply magical, as the illuminated mind shall indeed find if it will only dive into it. In that state, the *Revelation* is very easily understood, and in no other way.

<summary>The author argues that the Book of Revelation and divine mysteries cannot be understood through literal study alone; they require an enlightened, "magical" spiritual understanding. He directs the reader to his book, The Threefold Life, as a necessary foundation for grasping these deep spiritual truths.</summary>
<keywords>Revelation, Magic (Magia), Turba Magna, John the Evangelist, Three Principles, The Threefold Life, spiritual enlightenment, biblical interpretation</keywords>

--- Page 99 ---
<meta>This page continues the author’s explanation of spiritual understanding. He argues that true wisdom cannot be found through academic study or "conjecture" but only by allowing the Holy Spirit to guide the mind into the "Mystery of God."</meta>

<page-num>81</page-num>
<header>The Fifth Epistle.</header>

It shall not be understood at all except through the Mystery of God. Whoever is able to dive or sink himself into that *finds whatever he searches for*.

81. Accordingly, I would very much like you to test your spiritual zeal <note>original: "inflammation"; the inward "burning" or ardor of spiritual desire and inspiration</note>, so that you might know the *Guide* of the internal world, as well as the *Guide* of the external world. In this way, the spiritual school <note>original: "Magicall Schoole"; referring to "Magia," the divine wisdom or power through which God's will is manifested</note> of both worlds might be known to you. Then, the noble mind would be freed from mere opinion and conjecture, *for there is no perfection in conjecture*.

<margin>☛</margin>
82. The spirit must be receptive to and acceptable to the Mystery, so that God’s Spirit may be the guide in its vision; otherwise, it only sees into the outward mystery—that is, in the external heaven of the stars <note>original: "Constellation"; here referring to the physical and astrological influences that drive the natural mind</note>, which often intensely excites and drives the mind of man. Yet, such a person does not possess the divine spiritual school, which consists simply of a plain, childlike mind.

83. The external guide works and reflects only in a mirror <note>original: "Glasse"; meaning an indirect reflection or a superficial understanding</note>, but the internal guide reflects upon the very essence. However, the mind is not able to do this unless God’s Spirit guides it; therefore, God makes the choice.

84. Whomever the heavenly school accepts is made a spiritually gifted one <note>original: "Ma-gift"; a term likely combining "Magus" (a wise man or practitioner of divine Magia) and "gift," signifying one endowed with supernatural insight</note> without his own frantic efforts. And even though he must strive hard, he is chosen by God and *driven by the Holy Spirit*.

<margin>☛</margin>
85. Therefore, a person must test *which guide* has taken hold of him. If he finds that he has the divine light shining in his vision—that his guide brings him into the *heavenly school* on the path of truth, love, and righteousness—and that he is thereby assured and confirmed in his mind with divine certainty, then he may proceed in his work.

86. But if his drive is based on conjecture and doubt, and yet he feels a fiery urge, then the guide is from this world. This must be tested by its will and purpose: whether he seeks God’s glory or *his own honor and applause*; whether he willingly submits himself to the *Cross* and only desires to labor in Christ’s vineyard and seek the *good* of his neighbor; whether he seeks God or *bread* <note>original: "Bread"; here meaning material gain, salary, or worldly security</note>. His understanding must judge accordingly, and either give it permission or reject and restrain it as necessary.

87. As a brother, I would not conceal this from you in a Christian exhortation. I entreat you to accept it in no other way than as it is intended—well-meant toward you, as my duty requires. Because of your request, I am your debtor in Christ to your anxious, seeking mind, just as one *member* <note>original: "Member"; a part of the collective body of Christ/the Church</note> is bound to assist another.

88. Concerning your very Christian offer, I acknowledge and accept of it as an act of love, just as one member comes to help the...

<meta>catchword: other</meta>

<summary>Boehme distinguishes between the "external guide" of worldly reason and the "internal guide" of the Holy Spirit, advising the reader to test whether their spiritual drive seeks God's glory or personal gain. He emphasizes that true understanding of divine mysteries requires a "childlike mind" and divine selection rather than mere intellectual effort.</summary>

<meta>This page begins a new letter, the Sixth Epistle, following the conclusion of the Fifth Epistle on the previous page. The author, Jakob Böhme, addresses new recipients and introduces a deeply philosophical discussion on the nature of humanity and God.</meta>

<page-num>83</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

# THE
# SIXTH EPISTLE:
---
## BEING
---
### A very deep and philosophical <note>original: "Phyloſophick"</note> letter,
## WRITTEN
---
### To Doctor **Gottfried Freudenhammer <note>original: "Godfreet Freuden-Hammern"</note>**; and to Master
### **Johann Heuser <note>original: "John Heusern"</note>**.
---
### Concerning the Knowledge of God,
### and of all things.
## ALSO
---
### A Short Declaration of the
### False and True **LIGHT**.
---

> *May the salvation of our Lord Jesus Christ, through His entrance and manifestation in humanity, work with His love in us all.*

---

1. Much respected sirs and dearly beloved brothers in Christ: when God, by His grace, opens our right understanding so that we are able to know and properly understand the image of God (namely, man)—what he is in body, soul, and spirit—then we acknowledge that he is the visible and also the invisible spiritual world. Specifically, man is *an extract of all the three principles of the Divine Being* <note>In Böhme’s complex mystical system, the "Three Principles" refer to the "Dark World" (the Father's fire/power), the "Light World" (the Son's love), and the "Outer World" (the physical universe).</note>.

2. In whom the hidden God, through the expression and impression <note>The text breaks off here and continues on the next page.</note>...

<sig>M 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: impression</meta>

<summary>This page serves as the title and opening of Jakob Böhme's sixth letter, addressed to Gottfried Freudenhammer and Johann Heuser. Böhme begins by describing man as a "microcosm" or a spiritual extract of God's three primary principles of existence.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Gottfried Freudenhammer, Johann Heuser, Three Principles, Image of God, Mysticism, Anthropology</keywords>

--- Page 102 ---
<meta>This page continues the sentence from page 83: "In whom the hidden God, through the expression and impression..."</meta>

<page-num>84</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

...of his various *distinct* Power, and eternal *Wisdom* has set forth himself in a visible *Image*, through which he does conceive <note>original: "Idea"; here used as a verb meaning to form a mental image or archetype</note>, form, and shape within that same *Being* the wonders of the expressed *Word*; in this process, the *Word* of his powers makes itself *substantial* <note>original: "essentiall"</note>.

3. And so, in or by man, he has represented and set forth an *Image* of his speaking and outspoken *Substantial* word, in which the *Divine Knowledge* <note>original: "Science"; from the Latin *scientia*, meaning knowledge</note> with the various divisions of the *Eternal* speaking is contained.

4. And from this, man has the understanding and knowledge of all things, so that he is able to understand the union <note>original: "conjunction"</note> of Nature and also its separation; for no spirit has any higher rule or deeper reach than in its own Mother <note>In Böhme’s thought, the "Mother" refers to the source or womb of nature from which a being originates</note> from which it takes its *Origin*, and in that ground it stands or remains in its *Center*.

5. This we see in the *Creatures* of the *Stars* and *Elements*: their understanding and knowledge is no higher than that of their Mother in whom they live. Each leads its life according to the nature and condition of its Mother, wherein it is bounded in the distinct division of the expressed *Word*. No *Creature* in the four Elements whose *Origin* is not out of the *Eternal Knowledge* is able to attain the knowledge of the hidden spiritual World of Power, except *Man* alone. Man, with his soul and understanding spirit, is contained in the eternal *Breathing* <note>original: "Spiration"; the act of breathing or the "out-breathing" of the Divine spirit</note> (or generation) of the Divine power and the various distinct divisions of the eternal *Word* of God.

6. Therefore, *human knowledge* receives in the *Center* of its understanding both good and evil; it *forms an idea* of and shapes itself in both good and evil, making itself *Substantial* therein. And so, with and by that knowledge, it brings itself into will, desire, and essence.

7. In this way, the *Abyssal will* <note>original: "Abysfall"; referring to the "Ungrund," the bottomless or groundless source of all existence in Böhme's philosophy</note> from the eternal word of various distinct divisions brings itself into an *Essence* and *Being* <note>original: "Ens and Being"</note> within the *Creaturely Word* (namely, in the creaturely *knowledge* of the Soul). It does this in the same manner and form as the *Breathing* of God has brought itself—through the various divisions of the Eternal will in the visible world—into diverse properties: namely, into good and evil, into love and enmity. In such a *contrast* <note>original: "Contrary"</note>, the *Being* might be distinct, separate, formal, sensible, and perceivable, so that each thing might, in such a *contrast*, find and perceive itself.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. For in God, all Beings are but one *Being, namely, an eternal One*, or unity, the Eternal only *Good*. This Eternal One, without variety or separation <note>original: "severalty"</note>, would not be manifest to itself.

9. Therefore, the same One has breathed forth itself out of itself, so that a plurality and distinct variety might arise; this variety or separation has led itself into a peculiar *WILL* and properties.

<summary>Böhme explains that man is a unique image of God's "expressed Word," possessing a depth of knowledge that other creatures lack because his soul originates directly from the divine "Spiration." He argues that the diversity of the world—including the contrast between good and evil—exists so that the eternal Unity of God can become manifest and perceivable to itself.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Divine Science, The Word, Spiration, Abyssal Will, Microcosm, Unity and Variety, Nature, Soul</keywords>

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of how divine properties are transformed into desires and then into physical beings.</meta>

<page-num>85</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

properties; the properties into desires, and the desires into beings.

10. To such an extent that all visible things (both *animals, vegetables,* and *minerals*) arise from the diversity <note>original: "severality"</note> and comprehensive impression or formation of the expressed *Word*, out of the knowledge <note>original: "Science"; here meaning the divine systematic pattern or wisdom</note> of the great *Mystery*. Everything comes from the *experience of the distinct <note>original: "severalized"</note> Word*.

11. Everything has its own separation within itself. The center of everything is a *spirit* from the origin of the *Word*. The separation within a thing is a peculiar self-will of its own impression or formation, where each spirit brings itself into being according to its *substantial <note>original: "essentiall"</note> desire*.

12. The form and features of bodies arise from the experience of the will, where the center of everything (being a particle or spark from the expressed Word) again expresses or speaks itself forth. It brings itself into a various, distinct particularity, in the manner and form of the divine *speaking* [or the operation of the *eternal Word* in its *generation* and *manifestation*].

<margin>☛</margin> 13. Now, if there were no *free will* in such *speaking*, then the *speaking* would have a law and would remain confined and compelled. No *desire* or *free imagination* could arise, and then the speaking would be *finite* and *just beginning* <note>original: "inchoative"</note>, which it is not.

14. But it is a *breathing* <note>original: "Spiration"</note> of the *Abyss* <note>original: "Abysse"; in Boehme's philosophy, the bottomless void or "Ungrund" that exists before and beyond created nature</note> and a distinct separation of the *eternal stillness* [or immobility]. It is an outpouring or distribution of itself, where the particularity again stands in its own distinct separation in a peculiar *self-will*, and is again an expression of itself. From this, *nature* and *creaturely life* take their origin, and hence a self-will has arisen in everything.

15. Thus, everything brings itself from its own experience into form, feature, and shape, and likewise into life and operation, as it stands in its center in the *universal experience*—namely, in the *Great Mystery*, in the *Mother* of all beings.

16. We see this in the Earth, which in the beginning of its materials rose out of the separation of the *divine breathing* <note>original: "Spiration"</note> in a spiritual manner. There, the separation of the Word formed itself by its own proper will into an *entity* <note>original: "Ens"; a Latin term for a thing's essence or being</note> and being. Thus, by the formation or impression, it brought itself into the perception of the *essence*.

17. In this perception or sensibility, the *magnetic desire* <note>Boehme uses "magnetic" to describe the attractive force that pulls spiritual energy into material density</note> has arisen, so that the properties of the distinct, distinguishing, or separating will have brought themselves through that desire into bodies, according to and from the nature of the three principles of the *divine manifestation*.

18. From which origin the Earth has so many and diverse bodies

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that all physical matter—animals, plants, and minerals—originates from the "expressed Word" of God. He argues that creation requires free will and a "magnetic desire" to move from spiritual stillness into the diverse, living forms of the material world.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, the Word, creation, free will, Great Mystery, Spiration, magnetic desire, Ens, Abyss, nature</keywords>

--- Page 104 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of how divine principles manifest in the physical world, specifically regarding the three "Centers" of existence.</meta>

<page-num>26</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

...bodies, both good and evil, such as soils <note>original: "Earths"</note>, salts, stones, metals, etc. These bodies lie mixed in the Earth, for the three Principles exist mutually within one another as a single Being.

19. And they exist only in three differences or distinct degrees of Centers (which are the divine manifestation). In these, each Center makes and produces its own breathing <gloss>expression</gloss>, nature, and being out of itself; yet all originally arise from the Eternal One.

<margin>☛</margin>
20. The first Center is the breathing forth <note>original: "Spiration"</note> of the Abyss <note>original: "Abysse"; in Boehme’s philosophy, the "Ungrund" or the bottomless void before and beyond creation</note>—namely <note>original: "Viz."</note>, God’s Speaking, the comprehensive impression, and the divine perception of Himself. Here, God bears and begets Himself in Trinity and speaks Himself forth into powers.

21. The second Center, or breathing, is the *expressed essence of the divine power*, and it is called *God’s Wisdom*. Through this, the Eternal Word breathes itself forth into knowledge—that is, into an infinity of many things. It brings this plurality of knowledge into imagination, the imagination into desire, and the desire into nature and conflict <note>original: "strife"</note> until it reaches fire.

22. In that fire, the conflict of painful agony dies as it is consumed, losing its own natural right and property. Yet this is not to be understood as a literal death. Instead, the Power brings itself into awareness <note>original: "sensibility"</note>. By killing the self-centered desire of its properties through the death <note>original: "mortification"</note> of self-will, it brings itself through the fire and into the light.

23. In the light, another Principle—the Great Mystery <note>original: "Grand Mystery"</note> of the true divine manifestation—is understood. In the fire, the First Principle, which is the Eternal Nature, is understood. They are two in one, like fire and light.

24. The fire gives the soul, and the power of the light gives the spirit. In this power of the light of the divine breathing—through wisdom and through the manifestation of the fire (meaning the fire of the spirit)—the Mother of the eternal spirits (namely, of angels and the souls of men) is understood. So also is the spiritual, angelic world—that is, the hidden, internal world of power, which is the mother of the heavens, stars, and elements (the external world).

25. The third Center is the *Word of Creation* <note>original Latin: "Verbum Fiat," literally "Let the Word be done"</note> (namely, *the natural Word of God*) from the power of the first and second Principles. It is a <term>Separator</term> <note>an agent that distinguishes and divides things into their specific forms</note>, or <term>Creator</term>, and the maker of all creatures in the internal and external worlds, each according to its own property.

26. This <term>Separator</term> or <term>Breather</term> <note>original: "Spirator"</note> of the division <note>original: "severation"</note> of the divine powers has spoken itself forth from itself, out of the first and second Principles—namely, out of the worlds of fire and light, and also

<summary>This page describes the three Centers of divine manifestation: the first being God's self-perception, the second being God's Wisdom leading to the light-world, and the third being the "Verbum Fiat" or natural Word that creates all physical and spiritual things.</summary>
<keywords>Three Principles, Spiration, Divine Wisdom, Eternal Nature, Verbum Fiat, Separator, Fire and Light, Angelic World</keywords>

--- Page 105 ---
<meta>This page continues Böhme's explanation of how the "Spiration" (divine breathing) moves out of the darkness of the "Adumbration" (shadowing) to create the physical, sensible world. It explores the origins of the "Third Principle" (the material world) and the dual nature of will, light, and fire.</meta>

<page-num>87</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

...out of the *impression* and enclosure of the shadowing <note>original: "Adumbration"; the initial state of potentiality or "shading" before manifestation</note>; that is, out of the darkness. By the separation of the divine breathing <note>original: "Spiration"</note>, it has modeled and formed itself, becoming material, moving, and conscious <note>original: "sensible"</note>.

27. From this, the third Principle—the visible world with its existence and life—has arisen. The entire creation of the visible world, whose life and being emerged from the *Three* <note>the three Principles: darkness/fire, light/love, and the physical world</note>, has come into existence. This happened through the eternal nature, the great mystery—that is, out of darkness, fire, and light; namely, out of Love and Anger.

28. Fire is called Anger because it is a state of pain and conflict; Light signifies or is called Love because it is a sweet yielding and giving of itself. The darkness serves to separate knowledge and understanding, so that we may comprehend what Light and Life are, as well as what is evil and painful.

<margin>☞</margin>
29. We must understand that there is a twofold fire and a twofold light. According to the dark impression, there is a *cold fire* and a false light arising from the imagination of the harsh impression; this light originates only in the imagination and has no true foundation <note>original: "Ground"</note>.

30. The second fire is a *hot fire*. It has a fundamental, true light arising from the source <note>original: "Original"</note> of the *Divine Will*, which also brings itself forth in nature, passing through the fire into the light.

31. In this twofold fire and twofold light, two principles and a twofold will are understood.

32. For the false light from the imagination arises out of the self-will of nature—specifically from the interaction of its properties. When these properties test one another, selfish desire <note>original: "self-lust"</note> and a false imagination arise. In this state, nature models and fantasizes for itself, within its own desire, its own version of the *Abyss*. It desires to bring itself into power, ruling by its own self-will and authority without the will of God.

33. This occurs when this self-will refuses to be obedient and submissive to the unsearchable and abyssal <note>bottomless</note> will of God (which exists in itself, independent of nature or created beings, in the *Eternal One*). It refuses to resign and wholly give itself up to Him to be *one* will with Him. Instead, it makes itself its own separator and maker.

<margin>* Or, Wicked</margin>
34. It creates for itself a *knowledge* <note>original: "Science"; here meaning a specialized, self-centered understanding or skill</note> within itself and severs itself from God's will. We can see this in the Devil and also in *fallen humanity* <note>original: "false fallen Man"</note>. Because of this, they were cast out of the divine order <note>original: "Separation"</note>, so that the Devil must remain with his own will in the separation of the dark impression. In that state, the Word brings itself into nature and into the pain of consciousness <note>original: "sensibility"</note>;

<summary>This page describes the emergence of the physical world from the dual forces of "Love" and "Anger" (Light and Fire). Böhme distinguishes between a "true" divine light and a "false" light created by the self-will and imagination of fallen beings like the Devil and humanity.</summary>

<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Three Principles, Spiration, Adumbration, Cold fire, Hot fire, Divine Will, Abyss, Eternal One, Self-will, Fallen Man, consciousness</keywords>

--- Page 106 ---
<meta>This page continues Böhme's exploration of the "Separator" and the nature of light. It builds on the previous discussion of the two types of fire and light, distinguishing between the true light of God and the false light generated by self-will.</meta>

<page-num>88</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

...sensibility <note>conscious awareness</note>; namely, in the original of the fire-source, which notwithstanding cannot reach or obtain the true fire, wherein the will of God leads itself into the sensible life and nature; namely, into a shining light.

35. For the Separator of the natural self-hood has no true essence <note>original: "Ens"</note> wherein its light may remain steadfast, for it does not draw with its desire out of the eternal One (namely, out of the gentleness of God) but draws itself into its own existence; its light arises only in its own self-possession <note>original: "ownedhood"</note>.

31. <note>The original text numbers this paragraph 31, though it follows 35; it is likely a misprint for 36.</note> Therefore there is a difference between God’s light and the false light, for God’s light arises [or takes its original] in the Eternal One; namely, in the essence of the Divine Generation <note>the process of God's self-manifestation</note>, and brings itself through the Will of God into Nature and Being. It is formed and brought by the Divine Separator into a state of being <note>original: "Ens"</note>, and shines in that same Nature in the darkness, as in *John 1*.

37. For the formed or modeled knowledge <note>original: "Science"; here referring to the creative idea or potentiality</note> is (in respect to the impression) a darkness; but the Divine light illuminates it so that it becomes a fiery light, wherein the breathing <note>original: "Spiration"</note> or Speaking of God is made manifest in Nature and Creature, and stands in a sensible or perceivable life; of which Saint John speaks, *Chapter 1: The life of man was in him* [that is, in the Word] and *Christ* says in *John 8: That he is the light of the World, that gives life to the World.*

38. For without this Divine light proceeding from the Generation of the Divine Tri-unity <note>the Trinity</note>, there is no constant, true light, but only a light of the imagination from the natural impression of self-will.

<margin>☞</margin>
39. Therefore man (being the Image of God) must open and lift up the eyes of his understanding (wherein God’s light leans toward and offers itself to him, and desires to shine in him) and not be as a beast, which does not stand with its Separator in the internal realm in eternity, but only in an external figure of the expressed word.

40. Which has only a temporary life in a finite and beginning <note>original: "inchoative"</note> Separator, in which the Eternal Separator represents itself as if in a mirror <note>original: "Glasse"; Böhme often uses the mirror as a metaphor for the reflection of divine ideas</note> [or mirror of Ideas] and brings the Divine knowledge <note>original: "Science"</note> into forms and figures, like a pattern or imitation of the Great Mystery <note>original: "Grand Mystery"; referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*</note> of the spiritual world, where the Eternal Principles cooperate and play in a type or resemblance, both according to fire and light.

41. And yet man, in respect to his external, tangible, or finite body, stands only in such a fleeting, figurative shadow or resemblance; but with his spiritual body, he is the true, essential Word of the Divine property, in which God speaks and generates his Word,

<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>Böhme distinguishes between the true light of God, which comes from the Divine Tri-unity, and the false, temporary light of self-will. He argues that humans, as the image of God, possess a spiritual body that participates in the eternal Word, unlike animals which exist only in the temporary, external reflection of the Great Mystery.</summary>

<meta>This page continues Böhme’s explanation of the "Separator"—the divine differentiating power—and how the human "self-will" has corrupted man's original divine nature into a "monstrous" or beastly state.</meta>

<page-num>89</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

and there the Divine Wisdom <note>original: "Science"; here referring to the creative knowledge or potential of God</note> distributes, imparts, impresses, forms, and begets itself into an Image of God.

42. In which Image God is manifest (in a sensible and created being) and dwells and wills there himself; therefore, man must break his own will and entirely submit himself to God’s will.

<margin>Note.</margin>
43. But if man’s self-will will not do it, then he is more void of understanding and more harmful to himself than the wild earth, which yet stands still for its own Separator <note>the principle that divides and shapes matter into form</note> and lets it form and make out of it whatever it pleases.

44. For God has made all things in his Divine play or operation out of his Breathing <note>original: "Spiration"</note>, through and in his Separator, and all things hold or stand still for him; only the false light causes the Separator of the creature to bring itself into a self-will, so that the creature opposes the will of God.

<margin>☞</margin>
45. This false light in man has its foundation in the *will of the Devil*, who through the insinuation of his false desire has made man *monstrous* <note>distorted from his original natural order</note>, so that he also has assumed a false imagination, whereby (*through his own desire*) he has made the false, introduced desire of the Devil *substantial* <note>original: "Essentiall"; meaning it has become part of his actual essence</note> in him.

46. Whereupon in the human body (which was formed out of the *clay* <note>original: "Limus"; Latin for mud or slime, referring to Genesis 2:7</note> of the Earth in the *Divine command* <note>original: "Fiat"; Latin for "let it be"</note>) a beastly *Separator* has arisen; this has revealed or made manifest the properties of all beasts, from which such manifold and diverse lusts, imaginations, desires, and wills exist in man.

47. This false Separator has advanced itself to the height and gained the dominion, and attracted all the principles to itself, and has made a monster outside of God's order.

48. This monstrous image has, in or with its will and desire, wholly turned itself away from the will of God and from the *Divine light*; whereby the *Divine essence* <note>original: "Ens"</note> from the being or substance of the holy World vanished or disappeared in him. And he (*namely*, man) remained only as a *distortion of Heaven* <note>original: "Monster of Heaven"</note> and was made by his Separator a *Beast of all Beasts*, which even now rules in, over, and with all beasts; in whom the Spirit of the World with the stars and elements has obtained the rule and dominion.

<margin>☞</margin>
49. Therefore, man now runs and seeks again his first right home, or *native country*; for in this condition or property, he exists in mere restlessness. Sometimes he is seeking in one thing and soon after in another, and supposes he can bring himself to rest in this *monstrosity*; and yet he runs on in the false, awakened, beastly will, which cannot reach the will of God.

50. He runs now only in the false light of his selfhood, which is born in his imagination with a *monstrous Separator* which

<meta>This page continues Böhme’s argument that humans must abandon their self-will to receive divine grace. He compares the human soul to a rose among thorns and explains that true spiritual knowledge only comes from the "Divine Science" or creative wisdom of God.</meta>

<page-num>50</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

makes him an earthly mind, in which the constellations have their influence and operations; and he has the whole visible world as his enemy.

51. And he stands like a rose in a bush of thorns, which is continually rent, scratched, and torn by the thorns. Yet he could not be called a rose if divine grace had not come to assist and restore him, and once again inspired and given itself into his internal ground <note>original: "Ground"; referring to the deepest essence or "soul-center" of a person</note>, wherein the *love of God for the new regeneration* is offered to him.

52. Therefore, I say that it is extremely necessary for man to learn to *know himself* and what he is before he runs and seeks. Otherwise, his seeking will only prove to be a torment, by which he torments and perplexes himself in a false separator <note>the principle that divides and shapes one's perceived reality</note> and yet cannot obtain rest and true satisfaction.

53. For all these earthly wills [and ways] in which he thinks to bring himself into rest and quietness are only an adverse will, striving against God—namely <note>original: "Viz."; short for the Latin *videlicet*</note>, the Eternal One.

<margin>☛</margin>
54. For it does not lie in any man’s own willing, going, or running—as *Saint Paul says*—but in *God's mercy*, that is, in the *grace* which is inspired into him.

55. For without grace, man is dead and blind toward God, and he is unable to attain any true life unless grace is stirred up, awakened, and revealed in him.

56. Moreover, in these earthly wills, there can be no awakening, for they cannot reach or obtain grace, much less awaken it. Therefore, the whole man—in soul and mind—must only immerse himself into *grace* and be willing to become a "nothing" to himself. He should desire nothing but grace, so that grace might be living and working in him, and his own will be subdued <note>original: "Stupifyed"</note>, overcome, and mortified <note>killed or made dead to worldly desires</note>.

57. As the sun breaks forth in the night [or darkness] and changes the night into day, the same is to be understood concerning man. Of this, Christ says: unless you turn and become like children, you shall not see the kingdom of God—namely, the divine Separator, from which all things have arisen and originally spring.

58. For no knowledge is right or fundamental unless it comes from the divine science <note>original: "Science"; here meaning creative knowledge or wisdom</note>, out of the differentiation <note>original: "severation"</note> of the divine breathing <note>original: "Spiration"</note> from which all things have their origin.

59. Now, if such knowledge is to arise in man again, then the divine Separator must stand in an essence of his likeness—namely, in a divine *being* <note>original: "Ens"; Latin for "being" or "essence"</note>, in which the divine Word speaks, and the divine light shines in that same speaking or breathing.

60. And even then, human science (which in its beginning <note>original: "inchoatively"</note> is...

<summary>Böhme explains that man's earthly will is an enemy to God and that spiritual "regeneration" requires total submission to divine grace. He argues that true knowledge is not found in human effort but originates from the "Divine Spiration," or the creative breath of God.</summary>

--- Page 109 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme’s discussion on the "Divine Science" (spiritual knowledge). He explains that because the soul originated from God's creative word, it can understand the true nature of all things if it is enlightened by divine grace.</meta>

<page-num>91</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

(arisen from the breathing forth <note>original: "Spiration"; the act of breathing out, referring to God’s creative breath</note> of the Word) may in that same light see, not only itself, but likewise all other natural things according to the division <note>original: "severation"; the way the one Word is divided into many forms</note> of the Words. In a mystical <note>original: "Magicall"; in Boehme’s context, this refers to the supernatural power of the will and spirit rather than stage magic</note> manner, it may work in and with all things in a divine way, nature, and quality.

<margin>☛</margin>
61. For man is blind to all of God's works and has no true knowledge unless the divine <gloss>breathing</gloss> of [God's] speaking is revealed in his internal core <note>original: "ground"; the deepest part of the soul</note>, according to the nature and quality of that breathing from which all beings originally proceed.

62. All of man's searching and seeking by which he tries to find the foundation of a thing is blind; it is performed only on the shell or outside, which covers the essence of the tree.

63. If there is to be a true discovery, then human knowledge <note>original: "Science"; meaning a deep, systematic understanding or wisdom</note> must enter into the quality of the thing and be able to perceive the very Divider <note>original: "Separator"; the active principle or "archetype" that shapes an object’s form and properties</note>.

64. Therefore, it is the greatest and most laborious misery of mankind that they run and seek in total blindness, and begin to seek the shell in the literal word and its various expressions <gloss>or many forms of writings</gloss>. In reality, all things are outwardly marked <note>original: "signed"; referring to the "Signature of All Things," where outer form reveals inner spiritual nature</note> just as they are in their internal being <note>original: "Ens"; Latin for "being" or "existence"</note> and essence. The Divider of all things has shown and presented himself visibly and formally, so that the Creator *is known in and by the Creation.*

65. For all beings are but one single Being, which has breathed itself out of itself, and has divided and shaped itself. Yet it proceeds from that same impression or formation into a uniquely distinct center; that is, with every impression and shaping of the desire.

66. Where the separated, parted, and divided will impresses and forms itself into a specific individuality, a center arises. Within that center, a Divider or Creator of its own self <gloss>or Being</gloss> arises—namely, a shaper of the re-expressed or re-breathed will. We see this in the Earth, where every herb has its own Divider within itself, which creates and divides it into its specific form.

<margin>☛</margin>
67. Now, if man (being God’s image, in whom the divine speaking is manifest according to the divine knowledge) wishes to investigate the creatures and gain real understanding and knowledge of them—whether they be animals, vegetables, or metals—he must then obtain grace from God once again. This is so that the divine light may shine in his knowledge, enabling him to look through the natural light. Then, all things will be opened and revealed to his understanding.

68. Otherwise, he continues his seeking like a blind man who speaks of colors but neither sees nor knows any color; this all

<summary>Boehme argues that true understanding of the natural world requires an internal spiritual awakening, as the external "shell" of things only reveals its true essence to those enlightened by divine grace. He introduces the "Separator" as the internal principle that gives every living thing its unique form and nature.</summary>

<keywords>Spiration, Separator, Ens, internal ground, divine science, natural light, Signature of all things, Jacob Boehme, theosophy</keywords>

--- Page 110 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme’s explanation of how human reason operates in relation to divine wisdom. He argues that without divine illumination, human intellect is merely a reflection of the physical stars and constellations, rather than a source of true spiritual knowledge.</meta>

<page-num>92</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

68. All social ranks and orders of the world should consider that they all run blindfolded without the Divine Light, operating only in an astral, sidereal <note>original: "Sydereall"; relating to the stars or constellations</note>, and imaginary fantasy, according to how the constellations of the stars form and frame themselves within and upon human Reason.

69. For *Reason* is nothing else but a *human constellation*, which is a dim sketch or resemblance of *all the Principles* <note>Boehme’s "Three Principles" of the divine essence: the dark world, the light world, and the visible world</note>; it exists only as an imaginary figure and not in the Divine Science <note>original: "Science"; meaning certain, divine knowledge or wisdom</note>.

70. But if the Divine Light is made manifest and shines within it, then the Divine Word begins to speak [or work] there out of eternal knowledge [science or wisdom]. Then Reason is a true dwelling place or receptacle of divine knowledge and revelation, and only then may it be rightly and truly used; but being devoid of this, it is no more than an *Astrum* <note>original: Latin for "star"; here referring to the inner celestial influence of the visible world</note> of the visible world.

<margin>☞</margin> 71. It is therefore declared to all lovers of the arts (whose <term>Separator</term> is a craftsman of great subtlety) that they must first seek God's love and grace, and surrender themselves to it and become wholly one with it. Otherwise, all their seeking is but a delusion, or the chasing of a shadow, and serves no purpose; nothing of any fundamental worth is found unless one person entrusts another with something <note>Referring to the oral or spiritual transmission of wisdom from master to student</note>.

72. It is forbidden to the children of God, in whom grace is revealed, *to cast pearls before swine* <note>A reference to Matthew 7:6, meaning one should not share sacred or deep truths with those who will only despise or misuse them</note>, upon pain of eternal punishment.

73. It is only freely granted to them to declare the light and to *show the way to attain the Pearl*.

74. But to give the divine <term>Separator</term> into a bestial hand <note>Meaning a person driven by animal instincts rather than spiritual awareness</note> is prohibited, unless a man knows the path and the will of the person [who desires it].

75. Thus, my beloved brethren, upon such consideration and instruction, I will (through the permission of divine grace and the cooperation of this present time) briefly explain and describe for you the Divine Mystery: how God, through His word, has made Himself visible, sensible, and perceivable—moreover, taking on the nature of a creature and a form. Please consider this further, yet let it be done as mentioned above; otherwise, I shall be as if mute to you, and the blame for that should not be attributed to me.

76. God (as He is in Himself) is neither nature nor creature, neither this nor that, neither high nor deep; He is the <term>Abysse</term> and the <term>Bysse</term> of all beings, an eternal One where there is no foundation or place. To the creature, in its own limited strength [or capacity], He is a *Nothing*, and yet He is *through all things*.

77. Nature is *His Something*, with which He makes Himself visible, sensible, and perceivable, according to both eternity and time.

<summary>Boehme warns that human reason is merely a reflection of the stars unless enlightened by God, and he cautions against sharing divine secrets with the unworthy. He defines God as the "Abyss" (the groundless origin) who manifests Himself through the "Something" of Nature.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Reason, Divine Light, Separator, Abyss, Bysse, Nature, spiritual illumination, Three Principles</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the Sixth Epistle of Jakob Böhme, following his discussion on the limitations of human reason without divine illumination. Here, he introduces a summary table (likely on the following page) intended to illustrate the divine origins of the elements and the process of creation.</meta>

<page-num>93</page-num>
<header>The Sixth Epistle.</header>

# The Sixth Epistle.

78. All things have arisen through the Divine Imagination, and they still stand in such a birth, station, or government.

79. The four elements likewise have such a ground (origin or birth) from the Imagination of the Eternal One. Regarding this, I will set down a table here showing how one thing unfolds and <term>spirates</term> <note>original: "ſpirate"; from the Latin *spirare*, meaning to breathe or exhale. In Böhme's theology, this refers to the process by which the Divine Spirit emanates or "breathes forth" into creation.</note> itself forth out of another.

80. In this attached table, the ground of all the mysteries of the Divine Manifestation is portrayed for further consideration. The understanding and capacity for this is not within Nature's own ability without the light of God; but it is easily understood by those who are in the Light, and it is childlike (simple and plain).

81. As my writings sufficiently and extensively show, it is represented here only briefly in a figure or *scheme*.

82. And so, sirs, I commend and commit you to the greeting of the *Love of Jesus Christ*; who, by his presence and greeting, is the very key to understanding this table.

->Dated: November 11,<-
->1623.<-
->J.B.<-

# THE
# SEVENTH EPISTLE,
### TO
## One of the NOBILITY
### IN
# SILESIA <note>A historical region in Central Europe, now mostly located in southwestern Poland.</note>.

->*Our Salvation is in the life of Jesus Christ in us.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
**N**oble and right Honorable Sir: I wish you the Divine light and the internal Divine contemplation of the soul within itself, along with all bodily well-being, sincere wishes, and cooperating desires for fellowship and society as fellow members in our *Immanuel* <note>original: "IMMANUEL"; a Hebrew name meaning "God with us."</note>.

2. Seeing that I have observed you are a lover of Divine Wisdom, and also a growing branch in the Tree of Life <note>original: "Life-Tree"</note> of God in Christ—in whom all the children of God are joined as members—and having perceived how the "drawing of the Father" <note>A reference to the biblical concept (John 6:44) that God pulls the soul toward spiritual truth.</note> has brought you into a hungry desire for the true Sap <note>The vital fluid of a plant; here used metaphorically for the life-giving power or grace of God.</note> and Divine Power, and has likewise enriched you in some measure with the knowledge of that same Tree of Life; I have therefore taken this occasion (out of a Christian desire and duty as a fellow member of that same Tree of Life of Christ) to salute you once more. I wish for us to mutually encourage one another as laborers set in the Vineyard of Christ <note>A common biblical metaphor for the field of spiritual work and the growth of the Church.</note> and called to this work.

<margin>☞ 3.</margin>
3. Especially, let us look well to ourselves in this valley of darkness, and lift up our eyes and heads, for we see the darkness...

<meta>This page continues the Seventh Epistle. It follows the previous page where Böhme encourages the reader to lift their eyes and heads from the "valley of darkness."</meta>

# The Seventh Epistle.

---

...ness and its very workings before our eyes; and remind ourselves that Christ has taught us that **our redemption draws near**; and indeed, *go out from Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; refers to the biblical Babylon, symbolizing a state of spiritual confusion, false religious systems, or worldly bondage.</note>, which has held us captive and imprisoned for a long time.

<margin>* Externally</margin>
4. And do not pay attention to the loud cries and empty talk where they promise us golden mantles of grace and drape them around us—comforting, tickling, and flattering us with a strange pretense <gloss>or show of holiness</gloss>, as if we were received as children of grace * from the outside through some specific individual election <note>original: "Election"; refers to the theological doctrine of predestination or being chosen by God for salvation.</note>. Also, we should not look upon or rely on **our own merits** or abilities.

5. All of these things are of no use before God; instead, a **new creature in Christ**, born of God, is what matters before Him; for *Christ alone is the grace that counts with God*.

<margin>In the spirit of the world <note>original: "In Spiritu Mundi."</note>.</margin>
6. Now, whoever is born of Christ—living and walking in him, and putting him on (according to his inner self) in his *suffering, death, and resurrection*—is a member of his body. From him alone flow streams of living water through the powerful Word of Christ, which has become human within him in his internal foundation. This Word speaks itself out of him through the creaturely person in the <gloss>cosmic spirit of the external man</gloss>.

7. For God has revealed the "Grand Mystery" <note>original: "grand Myſtery" or "Mysterium Magnum"; Böhme's term for the primal chaos or the divine potential from which all things were created.</note> (within which the whole creation lay in an essential way without yet being formed) out of the power of his Word. Through this Grand Mystery, he expressed <gloss>the word of powers</gloss> into a separation or variety of spiritual forms. In these spiritual forms, the "knowledge" <note>original: "Science"; here meaning a distinct power, branch of knowledge, or specific quality within the divine nature.</note> of the powers existed within the "Desire"—that is, in the *Fiat* <note>A Latin word meaning "let it be," referring to the divine command of creation.</note>. Within this Desire for manifestation, every specific quality brought itself into a physical, bodily being. In the same way, that same Grand Mystery—**namely**, the essential Word of God’s power—lies within man (the image and likeness of God), pertaining to both eternity and time.

8. Through this Mystery, the living Word of God speaks and expresses itself either in **Love**, **Anger**, or in imagination, depending on how the human mystery stands in its shifting desire <gloss>or affection</gloss> toward evil or good. As it is written: *With the holy you are holy, and with the perverse you are perverse* <note>A paraphrase of Psalm 18:25-26.</note>; also, *just as the people are, such a God they also have*.

9. For whatever quality the Mystery within a person is stirred and awakened to, that is the kind of word that speaks itself from his powers—just as we plainly see that the wicked express nothing but vanity.

10. Now, how could there be a good expression or will where the Mystery that does the speaking <gloss>and willing</gloss> is based on a false foundation, poisoned by the Devil in the wrath of nature? This false mystery can

<summary>Böhme argues that true salvation comes not from external religious claims of "election" but from becoming a "new creature" through the indwelling of Christ. He explains that the "Grand Mystery" of creation resides within humans, and the nature of one's inner desire determines whether God's Word manifests as love or anger.</summary>
<keywords>Babel, redemption, election, new creature, Spiritu Mundi, Grand Mystery, Mysterium Magnum, Word of God, Fiat, human desire</keywords>

--- Page 114 ---
<meta>This page continues the Seventh Epistle, following Böhme’s argument that true spiritual transformation must happen within the internal foundation of a person, rather than through external merits or empty religious shows.</meta>

<page-num>96</page-num>

# The Seventh Epistle

...can neither will, nor do any good that may be acceptable to God, unless it is first ignited by God so that it obtains a godly will and desire; from this, a divine expression and the practice of good follows.

11. For Christ said, *An evil tree cannot bring forth good fruit.* How then will he bring forth good fruit where a false tree stands under a strange appearance <gloss>or glittering hypocrisy</gloss>? The purple mantle of Christ has its fruits within it; but what does that matter to a false beast that is full of poison and will cover himself with that mantle and take it for his own, yet still brings forth nothing but hellish fruit? Or what right has a titular Christian <note>original: "Titular Chriſtian"; someone who is a Christian in name only, without actual faith.</note> to boast and glory that he is a Christian, while he lives, walks, and exists without Christ?

<margin>☞</margin>
12. No one is a Christian unless he is <term>tinctured anew</term> <note>original: "Tin&tured anew"; Böhme uses this alchemical term to describe a fundamental spiritual transformation or "dyeing" of the soul with Christ’s nature.</note> with the spirit of Christ and has sprung forth out of God’s love, so that the grace of God in Christ is manifest in the mystery of his life regarding the soul, and cooperates and wills within the human life.

<margin>In the spirit of the world <note>original: "In Spiritu Mundi."</note>.</margin>
13. Now, if he wishes to become such a person, then he must turn from his *imagining in the cosmic spirit* <note>original: "Coſmick Spirit"; the mundane or planetary spirit that governs the physical world and external human mind.</note> with which the soul is covered and disguised, and which enters into earthly workings. He [must] become like a child that only leans with its whole affection toward the mother and draws into itself the mother's *milk* of grace. From this, a new <term>ens</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term for "essence" or "being."</note> grows, in which the life of grace arises. That is, the *imputed grace* must be born and become a person in him regarding the internal ground. Without this, no one is a *Christian*, no matter how many devout shows he makes; though he may dissemble, flatter, and do what he will, his sins must be forgiven him only through the <term>divine dialogue</term> <note>original: "Alloquy"; a speaking to or with God.</note>, or the <term>breathing of the spirit within himself</term> <note>original: "Spiration"; the act of spiritual breathing or the movement of the Holy Spirit.</note>.

<margin>* Or, breeding or hatching.</margin>
14. For when Christ is conceived in the inspired <gloss>or "inspoken"</gloss> word of grace—which the soul takes and impresses into itself from His promise—then the foundation is laid in the corrupt or decayed mystery for a child of God. Then the divine <term>impregnation</term> <note>original: "Impregnation"; here meaning the spiritual fertilization or "hatching" of the new life.</note> begins and proceeds, in which the humanity of Christ is conceived and born. This alone is the *Temple of the Holy Spirit*. From this new birth, the fiery soul eats God’s bread, which comes from heaven; and without this, man has no life in him (*John 6*). No hypocrite under the "purple mantle of Christ" can enjoy this, but only that man who is not born of flesh and blood, nor of the will of man, but is reborn of God. In him, the Word of God (from which the first man was created) speaks, rules, lives, and wills.

15. For the life of man was in the beginning in the Word (*John 1*) when that same Word was inspired or breathed into the created image <note>original: "Image"; referring to the biblical concept of man being created in the image of God.</note>. But when the soul turned itself from the speaking of the Word into a private self-willing and speaking in good and evil—that is, into its

<summary>Böhme argues that true Christianity requires an internal spiritual rebirth ("tincturing") rather than outward religious profession. He describes the process of "conceiving" Christ within the soul as the only way to escape the "cosmic spirit" and achieve genuine salvation.</summary>

its own lust and contrived imagination, then the first good will in the creature to express itself again perished; and now he must enter again into the *first Speaking Word* and speak with God, or he is eternally *without God*.

16. The present world cannot and will not understand this, for it has wholly and fully turned itself into a selfish way of speaking for the pleasure of the flesh. In its self-will, it speaks only of earthliness and transitory things, such as honor, might, power, and authority; moreover, it speaks of pride, covetousness, envy, and malice. It utters nothing else but the cunning, crafty serpent and its offspring. When these offspring cannot obtain or uphold what the self-will desires, then it speaks from cunning, mischievous malice and iniquity *with money*. Through selfish power and violence, it speaks forth *many thousand soldiers* who must maintain it by force, so that the self-will that has departed from God may be truly upheld. As we now see before our eyes, by this very expression, this self-will is also beaten down; it kills and destroys itself.

17. Therefore, beloved sir and fellow member in the Tree of Life of Christ, I would entreat you in a Christian way and stir you up (as one member is bound to do for another). In the present expression <gloss>[ways and courses]</gloss> of the world—where the *<term>Turba Magna</term>* <note>Latin: "Great Turmoil" or "Great Disturbance." In Böhme's philosophy, this refers to the awakened wrath of God that brings about destruction and judgment.</note> also plays and expresses itself, and where there shall be great contesting, pulling down, or degrading—I urge you to constantly and steadfastly keep yourself in the internal speaking of God's mercy. Continually enter into your internal foundation, and in no way be persuaded or misled by the serpent toward the false speaking of *brother-slaughter*. Instead, you (as a noble lord) should continually behold yourself in the mirror <note>original: "Looking-glasse"</note> of Christ’s life and doctrine.

<margin>\* Punishment, or severe execution. Note.</margin>
18. For this present speaking is spoken in the wrath of God through His awakened and ignited anger. It is very evil and dangerous to have a hand or voice in it, especially when the \* <term>Turba</term> must be spoken. It is altogether unfaithful; it devours its father and mother who brought it forth, and it is a broom <note>original: "Besom"; a broom made of twigs, often used metaphorically for a sweeping judgment or cleansing force.</note> of God's anger.

19. Great care must also be taken regarding accepting or joining any of the *supposed religions* for which men contend and fight. Do not assent with a conscience of faith to whichever party achieves victory; for there is no other true faith that saves except *CHRIST IN US*. He alone destroys sin in us and crushes the head of the serpent’s imagination within us. He arises in God’s righteousness (which He has fulfilled in us with His blood) from the *sleep of death*.

20. Christ must arise from death in our poor soul; namely, in a new humanity, which walks and dwells with, and in Christ in

<summary>Boehme warns against participating in the violent political and religious conflicts of his time, which he attributes to "self-will" and the "Turba Magna" (divine wrath). He emphasizes that true salvation is found only in the internal presence of Christ within the soul, rather than in external religious factions.</summary>

<page-num>98</page-num>
# The Seventh Epistle.

Heaven, where Heaven is in the new man; from which proceeds the work <note>original: "worker"</note> of Love, as is fitting and necessary for the children of God.

<margin>👉</margin>21. And though the external man lives in earthly weakness and infirmities, yet that does not take away the Temple of Jesus Christ; for Christ in the internal foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> continually bruises the head of the Serpent in the flesh; and Christ must be continually stung by the Serpent in the heel, until we are freed of this Beast.

22. Moreover, dear sir, I entreat you in a Christian, brotherly way to seriously take notice of the present time in the true fear of God. If you are pleased to let my good intentions take hold with you, you will never regret it <note>original: "it will never repent you"</note>; for I speak that which is made known to me from the *Most High* out of His *Grace*. Please be diligent to consider it, and let the spirit of God be your *meditation*.

<margin>👉</margin>23. For there shall shortly come a time when good friends shall be sifted and tested, so that we might stand steadfast in Christ; of which, in love, I would remind you. For the time of *Refreshment* comes soon after, where faithful people shall entirely love one another—a love for which I continually hunger and thirst. It is my sincere and constant wish that *Babel* <note>Babel: A term Boehme frequently uses to symbolize the "confusion" of fragmented, institutionalized, and warring religious sects.</note> may soon come to her end, and Christ may come into the *Valley of Jehoshaphat* <note>A biblical reference to the site of God's final judgment (Joel 3:2).</note>, so that all nations might see and praise him.

24. I entreat you to send my three treatises (namely <note>original: "Viz."</note>, 1. *Of Repentance*; 2. *Of the New Birth*; 3. *Of Resignation*—some of which I gave you myself, and the rest I sent by Mr. Rudolf) to Mr. Rudolphus of Gersdorf. For I have written to him that he should send them to me at Zagan, to Mr. Christianus Bernhard, from whom I shall receive them by some means or other. Or, if you yourself have any occasion to go toward Zagan, please send them to Mr. Christianus Bernhard, living at the Market Place. A year ago he was a <term>customs official</term> <note>original: "Customer"</note>; he is a young companion of the <term>Theosophic School</term> <note>Theosophic School: The informal network of students and seekers who studied Boehme's mystical writings.</note>; I have a convenient opportunity to reach him every week.

25. These treatises are very much desired by the seekers <note>original: "the Lovers"</note> and may do much good. I pray, send them at the first opportunity, for it is of great concern. And when you find convenient leisure to study, I will send you something deeper, for I have written this autumn and winter without ceasing. I commend you to the love of Jesus Christ and His gracious protection.

->Dated February 19, 1623.<-

<header>The Eighth Epistle.</header>

### THE
## EIGHTH EPISTLE:
### An Answer to four
# QUESTIONS.

1. *Regarding the Serpent, which after the Curse must eat earth and creep upon its belly.*
2. *Regarding Paradise and the Garden of Eden.*
3. *Whether the beasts (since they were in Paradise and yet also wholly earthly) also fed upon Paradisiacal fruit?*
4. *Whether the beasts before the Curse were as wild, hairy, and rugged as they are now?*

---

->Our Salvation is in the Life of Jesus Christ in us.<-

---

**W**ORTHY, much respected, very learned, and beloved friend and brother in Christ, our only life; I heartily wish you a happy New Year, that you may begin the same in a Divine will, through the drawing of the Father toward Christ <note>original: "drawing of the Father to Christ" — a reference to John 6:44, "No one can come to me unless the Father who sent me draws them."</note>. I likewise wish that you may finish this year, in this time, in an effectual working power of the spirit of Christ in His vineyard, and that many grapes may grow in the garden of Christ within you. I pray that God would be pleased to preserve you in the bundle of the living during this year, when the constellation of His wrath draws the sword <note>The "constellation of His wrath" and "the sword" likely refer to the onset of the Thirty Years' War (1618–1648), which devastated Germany during Boehme's life.</note>; as I do not doubt,

<sig>O 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: but</meta>
