<meta>This page continues the Eighth Epistle. Boehme is concluding a sentence from the previous page expressing his hope that his correspondent will continue to grow spiritually.</meta>

<page-num>100</page-num>
<header>The Eighth Epistle.</header>

...but that you (as a cooperating branch on the vine of Christ) will show yourself in good, new, and heavenly fruits.

2. For the gate of grace and knowledge likewise stands in a specific motion [and opens itself in a unique desire and affection] so that the children of Christ might also reap their harvest [and fruits of Christian love and piety, just as the children of Babel <note>original: "Children of Babel" — Boehme uses Babel to represent the confused, divided, and institutionalized churches of his day.</note> reap their harvest of envy, contention, and desolation], if they would only seriously labor and not lie so faint and sluggish in the sleep of Antichrist—as the grace of the Most High has given me to know and understand.

3. I intended to have spoken with you again when I last returned, but by the guidance of God, I was led another way; yet I shall still do so whenever I come that way, if the opportunity permits.

4. I also think of the discussion we had then at our *meeting*, where many things were questioned. Because of the many objections made then (which caused much misunderstanding and confusion), they could not be decided as well as they should have been, especially since I was in a hurry.

5. However (at the request of some high-ranking people with whom I spoke during the Christmas holidays after I left you—where some very learned men from *Jauer* and *Strieg* <note>Jauer (Jawor) and Strieg (Strzegom) are towns in Silesia, now in modern-day Poland.</note>, along with other fine gentlemen, were present), I have written a fairly large book concerning *Election* <note>original: "Election" — This refers to Boehme's work *De Electione Gratiae* (On the Election of Grace), written in 1623, which addresses the theological conflict over predestination.</note>. In that book, all those questions and more are set down in detail and resolved on the deepest foundation.

<margin>☛</margin>
6. And I hope that this work shall put an end to many contentions and controversies, especially concerning some points between the *Lutherans* and *Calvinists*, and other controversial *sects* as well. For there, the true foundation is set down in detail before their eyes, every person's opinion is satisfied, and the two opposing views are, as it were, united into one body—provided that anyone is able to see, know, and understand it despite the devil's poison and the "earthly imagination." I do not doubt that the time is at hand when strife and controversy shall be transformed into truth.

7. Yet among true Christians and the children of God in all nations, there has never been any controversy or division; for in Christ we are all but one tree spread out into many branches and twigs.

8. Controversy has arisen because the world has fallen into its own selfish lust and imagination. Through this, people have torn themselves away from Christ, their stem, in whom Christians should stand in unity; instead, they have turned themselves toward images [mental opinions and idols] and questions.

9. Out of these questions, such controversies and contentions...

<meta>This page continues the Eighth Epistle. Boehme is concluding a thought from the previous page regarding how false opinions and pride have corrupted the true meaning of Christianity.</meta>

<page-num>101</page-num>
<header>The Eighth Epistle.</header>

...are arisen; where the pride of the Devil has been involved in these questions, and has so imprinted and modeled itself upon the image of man, that people have sought for their own images and opinions, and have extolled and advanced themselves in them. Christ’s humility—in which we should die in Christ from our evil nature—is quite forgotten; so that for the present, we are more like a monster of an image than a living Christendom in spirit and in power.

<margin>☞</margin>
10. For a Christian must and ought to stand in the Tree of Christ with the increase and growth of Christ’s life, and also live in the spirit of Christ and bear fruit. Within such a person, Christ himself (as to the internal ground <note>original: "internall ground" — In Boehme's theology, the "ground" refers to the deepest, essential level of the soul or reality.</note>) lives and is all in all. He continually breaks the head of the serpent’s will in the flesh and brings to nothing the works of the Devil; he must know, will, and do from Christ. It must come to effective action (that is, into divine working); for without this, no one is a Christian.

11. Christ must wholly receive and take possession of the internal ground of the soul, so that the severe justice of God (which holds us captive in His anger) might be satisfied with Christ’s fullness [and effective merit]. Thus, Christ may fulfill the anger of God in us with love and mortify <note>original: "mortifie" — to put to death or subdue.</note> the will of the Devil, as well as the nature in the wrath of God, and wholly annihilate its will. In this way, the person may die in the love of Christ and bring forth a new will in the love-spirit of Christ through the nature of the soul, which lives and walks in God, as St. Paul says: *Our conversation <note>original: "conversation" — From the Latin "conversatio," meaning citizenship or conduct/way of life (Philippians 3:20).</note> is in the Lord.*

12. **Lip-labor** and the **pratings** of the mouth avail nothing; they do not make a Christian. A Christian must always be born (or regenerated) of Christ; otherwise, he is no Christian. No outward, imputed righteousness or grace helps at all by itself.

13. All the comforting, flattering, soothing, and dissembling is but in vain where the purple mantle of Christ is merely put upon the man of iniquity and malice [or the Cain-like hypocrite], who wants to be an adopted child of grace only from the outside.

<margin>* One who is pregnant.</margin>
14. For no prostitute or *one who is pregnant* <note>original: "one that is gotten with Childe."</note> can be a virgin, even if she puts on a maiden’s garland; indeed, no prince or potentate can bestow virginity upon her.

<margin>☞</margin>
15. Thus, the flattering hypocrisy and comforting [with Christ’s merits and promises] is to be understood: unless we are converted and become like children who hang on the breasts of their mother, and receive and conceive in us the <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens" — A Latin term meaning "being" or "essence." Boehme uses it to describe the essential spiritual substance or power of Christ.</note> [or essential power] of Christ, which destroys the harlot—so that a new spirit may be born in us out of Christ, which contains within it Christ’s sufferings and death. We must be born out of his resurrection and resign ourselves into the *whole process of Christ*, which is the *imputed grace in Christ.*

<summary>Boehme argues that true Christianity requires an internal spiritual rebirth and the actual presence of Christ within the soul, rather than just outward religious talk or a legalistic belief in "imputed grace." He emphasizes that one must participate in the "whole process of Christ"—his death and resurrection—to be truly transformed.</summary>

<margin>☞</margin>
16. A Christian must be begotten and regenerated by the same grace that makes him a Christian in and of Christ. Namely, he must be a true branch in the tree which is Christ, so that the engrafted word of life may spring forth and become essential—living and working in the soul. Only then do the merit of Christ and his imputed grace become effective: when the person is growing on the tree, rooted in the internal ground <note>original: "internall ground" — In Boehme’s thought, the "ground" is the deep, essential core of the soul where it meets the divine.</note>.

17. Beloved friend Mr. Frederick, your questions require a long explanation. In my treatise or *Commentary upon Genesis* <note>Boehme refers here to his massive work, *Mysterium Magnum*, an interpretation of the book of Genesis.</note> they are all settled at length. If you obtain the eyes and sight of Christ, then there is no need for such questions about such small things. These matters are indeed too high for <term>Reason</term> <note>Boehme often contrasts "Reason" (natural human intellect) with "Divine Vision" or "Faith."</note>; but in Christ, they are nothing but childlike play. However, I will answer you briefly as follows:

### The First Point.

->*Of the Serpent, which after the Curse must eat Earth and Creep upon its Belly.*<-

18. First, concerning the article of the serpent (which after the curse must feed upon the earth and creep upon its belly), I answer that while its *shape and form were as described*, its body and spirit were not as evil in the fiery <term>Science</term> <note>original: "Science" — from the Latin *scientia*, here meaning an active, formative knowledge or the essential inner workings of a creature's nature.</note> of the ground of nature as they became after the curse.

<margin>☞</margin>
19. For both <term>Tinctures</term> <note>original: "Tinctures" — a term from alchemy used by Boehme to describe the fundamental spiritual qualities or "dyes" that give a being its character (usually a balance of "light" and "fire" qualities).</note> of good and evil—of the origin of the first and second principle—were manifest in the serpent. Therefore, it was so exceedingly cunning; its nature was able to see into its own center and, through those tinctures, perceive the ground of the whole creation.

20. Before its origin as a creature (when it was placed into a distinct form within the <term>Grand Mystery</term> <note>original: "Grand Mystery" — or *Mysterium Magnum*, the chaos or "Great Mystery" from which God brought forth the manifested universe.</note>), the serpent was a fair and excellent <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens" — a Latin term meaning "being" or "essence"; the underlying substance of a thing.</note> of great power and virtue.

21. But the Devil’s imaginations poisoned this *Ens* when he sat like an enthroned prince in the ground of nature with very high power and dominion. In the process of <term>Severation</term> <note>original: "Severation" — the process of being separated or distinguished into a specific individual form.</note>, this essence formed itself into a serpent. Therefore, the Devil made use of the serpent as his instrument, using the same cunning and the same poison which held the mightiest power to make Eve "monstrous" <note>Meaning to pull her away from her original divine form into a corrupted, earthly state.</note>.

22. You <term>Physicians</term>! <note>Boehme often addresses doctors and alchemists, believing that physical medicine must be understood through spiritual principles.</note> It is necessary for you to know and understand the mystery of the serpent and what lies hidden under its poison. If you extract that poison and process it correctly, you may obtain a <term>Tincture</term> against poison, the like of which is not to be found elsewhere.

23. The serpent existed in the *Ens* of the <term>Grand Mystery</term> before it was a

<meta>The text cuts off here at the catchword "Creature," continuing on the next page.</meta>

<meta>This page continues the Eighth Epistle. Jacob Boehme is answering theological questions regarding the Fall of Man and the nature of the serpent and Paradise as described in the Book of Genesis.</meta>

<page-num>103</page-num>
<header>The Eighth Epistle.</header>

The creature was a Virgin; but after the Curse, she became a Whore; this is to be understood Magically [or Parabolically].

24. She saw in herself the ground of the inward and outward world; therefore, one must emerge from the inward world and kill the monster she has placed upon Eve. A whole book could be written about what the Devil's desire has accomplished by and through her.

25. But when she helped to deceive the Image of God, then God cursed her so that she became blind to the internal ground <note>In Boehme's theology, the "internal ground" is the spiritual center where the soul meets God. To be blind to it is to be trapped in purely physical or "wrathful" existence.</note>. She was also made completely manifest in the four elements; thus, she fell into the Earth from which the body was taken. Moreover, she fell only into the wrathful [or bad] part of the Earth. She can no longer obtain or reach the good part or quality of the Earth as other beasts do; therefore, she must eat Earth—namely, the quality of the curse within the Earth.

26. She was a flying worm <note>original: "Worme" — in Early Modern English, "worm" often referred to serpents, dragons, or any creeping creature.</note>; otherwise, nature would have provided her with legs, as other worms on Earth have. But her nimbleness, wily subtlety, and cunning made Eve long [after the forbidden fruit].

# The Second Point.

## *Of Paradise and the Garden of Eden.*

<margin>☞</margin>
27. Paradise was the <term>temperature</term> <note>original: "temperature" — here meaning a state of perfect balance, harmony, or "tempering" of all qualities.</note> in Man when he did not know what good and evil were. There, the Divine light shone through Nature and <term>Tinctured</term> <note>original: "Tinctured" — an alchemical term meaning to permeate or transform with a spiritual essence.</note> and balanced all things; for Paradise is revealed in us again in Christ, regarding the internal ground.

28. But when God saw and knew that man would fall, Paradise no longer sprang forth with fruit through the Earth in the whole world (even though it was manifest everywhere), but only in the Garden of Eden where Adam was tempted. For Eden is the place, but Paradise is the quality—namely, the life of God in the <term>similitude</term> <note>original: "similitude" — a likeness or image that reflects the divine Harmony of the universal Being.</note> [or Harmony of the universal Being].

# The Third Point.

## *Whether the beasts (since they were in Paradise and, moreover, entirely earthly) also fed upon Paradisiacal fruit?*

29. Beloved friend Mr. Frederick, every Spirit eats of its...

<summary>Boehme explains the spiritual degradation of the serpent after the Fall and defines Paradise not merely as a location, but as a state of internal spiritual harmony (temperature) that was lost to man but restored through Christ.</summary>
<keywords>serpent, the Fall, Eve, internal ground, four elements, Paradise, Garden of Eden, temperature, tincture, Adam, Frederick</keywords>

--- Page 122 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s Eighth Epistle, specifically answering questions about the nature of animals (beasts) before and after the Fall of Man and the Curse on the Earth.</meta>

<page-num>104</page-num>
<header>The Eighth Epistle.</header>

The beasts also ate from their mother, from which they were made. Specifically, they ate the <margin>The quintessence <term>the purest, "fifth" essence</term> in the Spirit of the World <note>original: *Spiritu Mundi*</note>.</margin> quintessence of the Earth within the cosmic spirit. The innermost nature of animals is not nearly as high a degree as that of Man. Thus, they fed upon their mother—that is, their spirit feeds upon the cosmic spirit, and their body feeds upon the four elements.

30. God knew very well that man would not remain upright but would fall. What use or profit would the food of Paradise then be to the animals? Indeed, a property of Paradise remains within the quintessence, and they feed upon that even to this day. For in every animal, there is a power that is incorruptible, which the cosmic spirit draws into itself until the separation of the last judgment.

## The Fourth Point.

->*Whether the animals were as wild and rough before the Curse as they are now?*<-

31. Beloved Mr. Frederick: the garment that Adam had before the Curse—when he was still naked <note>Boehme refers to a spiritual "garment of light" that Adam supposedly wore before the Fall, making physical clothing unnecessary.</note>—was exceedingly fair and suited him very well. Likewise, the hides of the animals were rough and hairy to them; but under the Curse, all things (both in the animals and in the plants of the Earth) were deformed and changed into a monstrous, strange form. They did indeed have such a covering before, but it was far more glorious in its colors, features, and ornamentation, coming from the pure <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tincture" — in Boehme’s alchemy, this is the vitalizing, spiritual power that gives color, life, and beauty to physical matter.</note>.

<margin>Or, the book called the *Grand Mystery* <note>original: *Mysterium Magnum*</note>; an exposition on Genesis.</margin>
32. Regarding these questions, I entreat you to look into the *Grand Mystery* with the eyes of Christ, in whom all the treasures of wisdom lie. Then you shall see them better through your own understanding than I can briefly set down in writing in this haste. I commit you to the love of Jesus Christ.

<summary>Boehme explains that animals derive their life from the "quintessence" of the world and that their physical appearance became "monstrous" and "rough" only after the Earth was cursed. He concludes by referring the reader to his larger work, the *Mysterium Magnum*, for a deeper understanding of these mysteries.</summary>

<meta>This page begins the Ninth Epistle of Jacob Boehme. In the previous epistle, he answered questions regarding the state of animals before and after the Fall; here, he shifts to addressing a "very learned" correspondent regarding the internal source of divine wisdom.</meta>

<page-num>105</page-num>
<header>The Ninth Epistle.</header>

# THE NINTH EPISTLE.

->*The open fountain of God in the heart of Jesus Christ be our refreshment and constant light.*<-

***

1. Worthy, much-respected, and very learned Sir, I heartily wish for you even that which my very soul desires from God; namely, the real, true *divine knowledge* in the love of *Christ*, that God would grant the opening of the *center* of the soul, whereby the paradisiacal lily-twig in *Christ’s* rose garden might spring forth, grow, blossom, and bear fruit; and that the streams from *Christ’s* fountain might flow from you; and that you might be taught by God, so that His Holy Spirit might drive and rule you: *As it is written: Those who are driven and moved by the Spirit of God are the children of God.*

2. I have received your letter, and thereby understand that you have read my writings and that you delight in them; and I wish from my very heart that the sense and right meaning of them may be apprehended and understood; and then there would be no need for any further asking and searching.

<margin>☛</margin>
3. For the book in which all mysteries lie is man himself; he himself is the book of the Being of all beings, seeing he is the likeness [or similitude] of God; the great *secret mystery* <note>original: *Arcanum*</note> lies in him, and the revealing of it belongs only to God’s Spirit.

4. But if the lily in the humanity of Christ springs forth in the new birth out of the soul, then from that same lily the Spirit of God proceeds, as if from its own origin and ground; and that same Spirit seeks and finds *all mysteries in the divine wisdom.*

5. For the lily-branch which springs forth in the new birth out of Christ’s humanity (understand the *newly born spirit* out of the soul’s essence and out of Christ’s power) is the true, real branch springing from and remaining in God’s tree.

<summary>Boehme addresses a learned correspondent, explaining that true spiritual wisdom is found within the human being—the "book of all beings"—through a spiritual rebirth he describes as the growth of a "lily" in the soul.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Ninth Epistle, divine knowledge, new birth, image of God, mysticism, lily-twig, arcanum</keywords>

--- Page 124 ---
<meta>This page continues the Ninth Epistle of Jacob Boehme. On the previous page, Boehme compared man to a book containing all mysteries and discussed the spiritual "lily" that grows in the soul through Christ. Here, he explains the necessity of the "new birth" and the internal struggle between the spiritual and earthly natures.</meta>

<page-num>106</page-num>
<header>The Ninth Epistle</header>

6. Just as a mother gives birth to a child, so the "new man" is born in and out of God. He is not God’s child and heir, a child of Heaven and Paradise, in any other way at all.

<margin>☛</margin>
7. No assigned <note>original: "imputed"</note> righteousness is enough—for a stranger cannot inherit God’s Kingdom. Instead, there must be an innate righteousness coming out of God's own essence <note>original: "Essentiality"</note>, out of the water and spirit of God. As Christ told us, *we must become like children* and be conceived in God's essence. Like new children in God, we must spring forth and be born anew, just as a beautiful flower grows out of the wild earth, or as precious gold grows in a rough stone or dull ore. Otherwise, we cannot see or inherit the Kingdom of God.

8. For whoever wishes to possess the internal spiritual world must be born from it; the earthly flesh made from the four elements cannot inherit the Kingdom of God.

9. But the *purest essence* <note>original: *Quint-essence*</note>—which is the "one element," namely *Paradise*—from which the four elements originate, must be dominant and rule over the four elements. In the same way that light contains darkness as if it were swallowed up (though the darkness is still really there), so it must be with humanity.

10. However, this cannot happen to the outward person during this earthly life, because the outward world rules over the outward person. This external focus was revealed in man, which is his fall.

11. Therefore, the external person must perish, just as the external world perishes and passes away. Because of this, man cannot attain perfection in this lifetime. Instead, the true man must continue in a struggle and conflict <note>original: "combate and strife"</note> against the corrupt earthly life, which is his own enemy, where eternity and time struggle against each other.

12. For through this conflict or the mutual struggle in Nature, the great *secret* <note>original: *Arcanum*</note> is opened, and the eternal wonders in God’s wisdom are revealed from the soul's essence.

<margin>☛</margin>
13. Just as the eternal God has revealed himself through time and brings his eternal wonders through time into conflict and contest—so that through the struggle, that which is hidden (and lies in the *Mysterious Nothing* <note>Boehme's term for the *Ungrund*, the absolute void or source before creation</note>) might reveal itself and be brought to light—so too must the great mystery be revealed in man through struggle and conflict, where God's anger and love are in conflict like fire and light.

14. For the soul arises out of the eternal fire, out of the Father's property—that is, out of the eternal, *beginningless* <note>original: "un-inchoative"</note> nature, out of the darkness. In the soul, that light (which went out and disappeared in *Adam*) must be renewed and born again through the coming of Christ. Then the Kingdom of Christ and of God is freely given to him out of grace.

<meta>catchword: 15. For</meta>

<summary>Boehme describes the "new birth" as a literal transformation of the soul into God's essence, contrasting it with merely "imputed" righteousness. He explains that spiritual growth requires a constant internal struggle between the eternal soul and the corruptible, earthly elements of the body.</summary>
<keywords>new birth, internal struggle, Quint-essence, Arcanum, Mysterious Nothing, essence of God, spiritual transformation, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 125 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s Ninth Epistle. Boehme continues to argue that true Christianity is not found in external rituals or intellectual concepts, but in the internal rebirth of the soul through "resignation" (total surrender) to God.</meta>

<page-num>107</page-num>
<header>The Ninth Epistle.</header>
<sig>P 2</sig>

15. For no one can take this for themselves unless the love of God again presses through grace into the center of the soul, bringing the divine will out of the soul's fire like a new sprout or a new image into the heavenly essence <note>original: "essentiality"</note>, just as light shines out of a fire.

16. Therefore, everything that Babel <note>original: "Babel" refers to the confused, divided institutional churches of Boehme's time, which he compared to the Tower of Babel.</note> teaches regarding external "imputed righteousness" <note>the theological idea that God legally credits Christ's righteousness to a person's account without necessarily changing their inner nature.</note> and external assumed adoption [and the specific election or rejection of souls from eternity] is without foundation or footing. Christ said: *You must be born again, otherwise you cannot see the Kingdom of God.*

17. The seemingly holy, flattering comfort found in Christ's death is worthless, unless one actually enters into Christ's death to spring up anew in Him—to arise in Him and with Him, and to become "Christ" [or an anointed child of God] in the "new man."

<margin>☞</margin>
18. Just as Christ has mortified, extinguished, and overcome the world—and also the Father’s anger (which is the center of the Eternal Nature) in the soul’s nature—with His love (that is, with the new fire of love introduced into the soul’s essence, where the Devil had previously placed his desire); even so must we, in and with Christ's spirit, quell and crush the "earthly Adam" in God’s anger. We must mortify it through God's love so that the new man may spring forth; otherwise, there is no forgiveness of sin, no adoption [or sonship <note>original: "filiation"</note>], and no righteousness.

19. The Kingdom of God must be inwardly innate and born within us; otherwise, we cannot see into the angelic world with the eyes of eternity.

20. All imaginations, inventions, and methods—all reading, studying, and teaching—are to no purpose [without this path of the new birth]. No skill or human reasoning can achieve it; we must enter only through the gate that God has opened to us in Christ. We must spring forth in God’s Kingdom and die to the earthly will so that it neither hinders us, nor clings to us, nor clogs us. The "seed of the woman" <note>A reference to Genesis 3:15, interpreted here as the Christ-spirit within the soul.</note> must continually crush the Serpent's head within us.

21. Self-reason [human logic] cannot make a child of God, for it does not depend on our willing, running, or working <note>original: "keeping a doe"</note>, as Saint Paul says, *but on God’s mercy and compassion.*

<margin>☜</margin>
22. My "selfhood" <note>The ego or the self-centered will.</note> cannot achieve it. My selfhood must die in Christ’s death and fall or resign itself into the "nothing" [total surrender]. Then my selfhood falls into God’s mercy and returns to the state of the first man <note>Adam before the Fall.</note>, standing again in the word *Fiat* <note>Latin: "Let it be done," referring to God's creative power.</note>, where God’s mercy—through Christ’s entrance into our humanity—makes [or recreates] the "new man" out of grace.

23. And therefore, the corrupt earthly will must die in a real, true, and upright repentance, and enter into "resignation" <note>Boehme’s concept of *Gelassenheit*, or the total yielding of the self-will to God.</note>; that is—

<meta>catchword: into</meta>

<summary>In this passage, Boehme rejects the "Babel" of institutional theology—specifically the idea of purely external righteousness—in favor of a radical, internal "new birth." He argues that the soul must undergo a process of "resignation" and "mortification" of the self-will to be recreated by God's grace.</summary>

<keywords>New Birth, Babel, Imputed Righteousness, Resignation, Selfhood, Mortification, Fiat, Jacob Boehme, Mysticism, Regeneration</keywords>

--- Page 126 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s Ninth Epistle. Boehme continues describing the internal process of "resignation" (surrender), whereby the seeker must die to their own self-will to be reborn in God's mercy.</meta>

<page-num>108</page-num>
<header>The Ninth Epistle.</header>

...into nothingness, and wholly surrender the will of reason unto death, neither willing nor knowing himself any more, but entering into the mercy and compassion of God.

24. And then this saying has its place and meaning, as God *speaks through the Prophet: "My heart breaks within me, so that I must take pity on him. Can a mother forget her child, so that she should not have compassion on the son of her womb? And even if she should forget, yet I will not forget you; behold, I have marked you in my hands."* <note>original: Jeremiah 31:20 and Isaiah 49:15-16. Boehme often paraphrases scripture to emphasize God's internal relationship with the soul.</note>

<margin>☛</margin>
25. In this (namely, in God's mercy) the new man arises and springs up in the Kingdom of Heaven and Paradise, though the earthly body remains in this world.

26. For Saint Paul says, *Our citizenship <note>original: "conversation," which in the 17th century meant one's way of life or citizenship; see Philippians 3:20</note> is in Heaven.* Thus the new man walks in Heaven, and the old man in this world; for the Heaven in which God dwells is within the new man.

27. Thus (beloved sir and brother), and in no other way or manner, have I found the Mystery. I have not studied or learned it [from books], but if you or any other thirsts after it, I am bound as a brother in my affection and love to show him the way I encountered it—as I have written at length in my works, chiefly in the book *The Threefold Life of Man* and in the book *The Three Principles of the Divine Essence.* <note>original: "Being"</note>

28. Indeed, I wrote it for myself as a spiritual exercise in the knowledge of God and in the mystery of His great wonders. Nevertheless, by God’s providence and guidance, it has reached the point of being published and read. I would gladly wish that everyone who earnestly desires to understand it might have it; and I wish from my heart that it may be truly manifest and made known to the reader *of this letter* and to everyone within themselves—then there would be no need for any further searching or seeking.

29. But seeing that God has promised through the prophets (especially in *Joel*) that He will pour out His Spirit in the last days upon all flesh, the time should be considered and noted.

<margin>Note.</margin>
30. I say what I have known: that whoever at present will die to himself, the Spirit of the Lord shall seize <note>original: "apprehend"</note> him according to *Joel’s* prophecy and manifest His wonders through him. Therefore, if anyone is in earnest, he shall find it through experience.

<margin>☛</margin>
31. Yet let everyone be faithfully warned: if God's light arises in him, he must remain steadfast in great humility and resignation <note>the total surrender of the self-will to God's will</note>—namely, in the death of Christ.

32. (For the heavens shall now at last pour out their long-accumulated *outflow* <note>original Latin: "egestum," referring to a discharge or result of a process</note> of the *constellations* <note>Boehme believed the "stars" or astral influences exerted pressure on human nature; he warns here against being led astray by these external spiritual forces</note> which they have worked into human nature), lest he also be seized by the starry heavens and go beyond the limit, out of the state of resignation.

<summary>Boehme describes the "new man" as a heavenly being living within the believer, born from total surrender to God's mercy. He claims his writings come from spiritual experience rather than academic study and warns readers to remain humble as God's Spirit is poured out in the "last days."</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Resignation, New Man, Holy Spirit, Prophecy of Joel, Threefold Life of Man, Three Principles of the Divine Being, Spiritual Rebirth, Mysticism</keywords>

--- Page 127 ---
<meta>This page concludes the Ninth Epistle of Jacob Boehme. In the preceding paragraphs, Boehme emphasized the necessity of total surrender to God's mercy as the only way for the "new man" to be born within.</meta>

<page-num>109</page-num>
<header>The Ninth Epistle.</header>

<margin>*The proselytes <note>original: "Prosolites"</note> or disciples of Isaiah Stiefel and Ezekiel Meth, who claimed they had reached perfection in this physical body.</margin>

33. As can be seen by the followers of Ezekiel Meth <note>original: "Metists," referring to a contemporary sectarian movement Boehme critiqued for their spiritual pride</note>, who came even to the gates of the Deep but were again taken captive by the starry heaven <note>referring to the influence of the astral or physical world</note>. They turned into themselves and exalted themselves, ceasing their struggle against the Serpent. They entered into a state of self-centeredness <note>original: "selfehood"</note> [or a unique, pride-filled <term>Luciferian</term> <note>referring to the pride of Lucifer</note> delusion of their own holiness], imagining they had been transformed into a Deity; thus, they have confused the external world with the internal spiritual world.

34. This is unfounded and without basis. We must take great care and ensure we remain steadfast in the deepest humility, so that the seed that is sown may grow into a tree and reach its blossoming, and so that the Spirit of God may take form within us.

35. For out of that blossom arises the Morning Star <note>a symbol of spiritual enlightenment or Christ</note>, so that a person may learn to know what they truly are, and what God and Time are.

36. <note>A manicule (pointing hand) in the margin highlights the importance of this paragraph's warning about the current era.</note> I inform you, Sir, out of sincere affection, that the present time must be taken into serious consideration; for the Seventh Angel in the <term>Book of Revelation</term> has prepared his trumpet. The powers of Heaven are in a state of particular motion. Furthermore, both gates [of light and darkness] stand open, and both light and darkness are in great desire; whatever a person chooses to embrace, they shall enter into it.

37. While some shall rejoice exceedingly at this, others shall mock it; after which follows the painful and severe judgment upon <term>Babel</term> <note>Boehme's term for the corrupt, divided, and "confused" institutional churches of his day</note>.

38. And so I commend you and your family to the pleasant and lovely love of Jesus Christ.

---

->Dated, Görlitz <note>original: "Gerlits"</note>, October 27, 1621.<-

<summary>Boehme warns against the spiritual pride of the "Metists" and cautions that the opening of spiritual gates signals an era of choice and imminent judgment on the corrupt institutional church.</summary>

<meta>This page begins the Tenth Epistle of Jacob Boehme. It marks a transition from general warnings about spiritual pride to a specific guide on how to overcome internal corruption and achieve a state of divine contemplation.</meta>

<page-num>110</page-num>
<header>The Tenth Epistle.</header>

# THE TENTH EPISTLE
### Concerning
## The Overcoming of **Antichrist** within Ourselves.
### AND ALSO
## How we may attain **Divine Contemplation.**

->*Our Salvation is in Christ Jesus.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin> **WORTHY** and much-respected Sir, I wish you the grace, knowledge, and blessing of God in **Christ Jesus**. After I was informed by *D. K.* <note>likely a reference to Dr. Kober, a frequent associate of Boehme</note> that you, as a Christian brother and fellow member in the Lord, stand in a sincere *desire*, being drawn by the Father toward **Christ Jesus**, and also labor in your mind to discover how you may reach **Divine Contemplation** and vision within yourself; therefore, at the Doctor's request, I did not want to miss the opportunity to visit and greet you with a short letter. I wish to briefly explain to you, through my **gifts** and out of Christian love, the way to **Divine** *Vision* and *Feeling*. In this way, I offer you in brotherly love the sap of my little *coral* <note>original: "Corall"; likely a metaphor for a small but precious growth of spiritual life</note> in the spirit and life of **Jesus Christ**, just as one branch or twig on the tree is bound to do for the other. I desire that I might be well understood, if perhaps I might further encourage your zeal.

<margin>2.</margin> Since you clearly perceive within yourself that the **Antichrist** in **Babel** <term>Babel</term> <note>meaning "confusion"; Boehme uses this to describe the corrupted, divided state of the established churches</note> holds the power and authority throughout **Christendom**, and acts—

<summary>In the opening of his tenth epistle, Jacob Boehme writes to a fellow believer about the path to spiritual vision and the necessity of recognizing the "Antichrist" or spiritual corruption present both in the world and within the self.</summary>
<keywords>Antichrist, Babel, Divine Contemplation, Christ Jesus, spiritual vision, Jacob Boehme, internal transformation</keywords>

--- Page 129 ---
<meta>This page continues the Tenth Epistle, focusing on the definition of the "Antichrist" not as an external figure, but as the internal spirit of selfishness and pride. It transitions into practical advice for achieving spiritual poverty and divine union.</meta>

<page-num>111</page-num>
<header>The Tenth Epistle.</header>

<margin>* Or, His seamless coat.</margin>
...promotes falsehood and the lust of the flesh. Our dear Immanuel <note>meaning "God with us," a title for Christ</note> has faithfully warned us of this and said, "Flesh and blood shall not inherit the Kingdom of Heaven" (John 6) <note>Boehme likely refers here to the spiritual teachings in John 6, though the specific quote is found in 1 Corinthians 15:50</note>. Yet the Antichrist seeks and desires nothing else but temporary honor, might, and power—to climb up and advance himself in the lust of the flesh. Moreover, this Antichrist has for a long time so politely and modestly decorated and adorned himself with Christ's * purple mantle <note>original: "Purple Mantle"; likely referring to the seamless robe of Christ, used here to mean a disguise of outward piety</note> that people have not recognized him. Instead, they have honored and adored him as a *saint*. This has been clearly revealed to me by the grace of the Most High; therefore, I wish to briefly explain to you what a Christian is, and also what the Antichrist within a person is, for your further consideration.

<margin>* Or, Employment.</margin>
3. Christ says, "*Whoever does not forsake houses, land, money, goods, wife, children, brothers and sisters, and deny himself and follow me, is not my disciple or servant*." He also says that you must turn and become like children, or be born anew of water and the spirit, or else you shall not see the Kingdom of God. This does not mean that one should run away from his vocation and *calling*—leaving his wife and children for a solitary desert and wilderness and forsaking everything—but only that he must forsake the Antichrist; that is, the **SELF** in everything [the *mine* and *thine* <note>original: "meum and tuum"; Latin for "mine and thine"</note>].

4. Whoever wishes to attain divine contemplation and feeling within himself must put to death the Antichrist in his soul. He must depart from all "self-ownership" <note>original: "ownehood"</note> of the will—indeed, from all created things—and become the poorest creature in the self-interest <note>original: "owne-hood"</note> of his mind. In this way, he holds or owns nothing as his own property <term>propriety</term> <note>in this context, "propriety" refers to private ownership or the sense of "this is mine," not "proper behavior"</note>, regardless of his social standing or condition.

5. Even if he is a **King**, his mind must forsake all self-ownership. He must regard himself in his position, dignity, and earthly goods as nothing other than a servant of God. He should use those things to serve God and his brothers. He must believe that he possesses everything he has not by natural right—as if it were his own to use according to his own will and pleasure—but that it belongs to his fellow brothers and members. God has appointed him as a steward and officer over it, and he must realize that he is serving his Lord, who will eventually require an account from him.

6. He must wholly and fully surrender his own will—which drives him toward the possession of self-ownership [or selfish affection and attachment to created things]—to the suffering and dying in the death of **Jesus Christ**. In earnest repentance and conversion, he must humbly beg God to put to death this evil will toward selfishness and worldly lust through the death of **Jesus Christ**. He must pray that God brings the will of his soul into true adoption as a child <term>filiation</term> <note>original: "filiation"; the state of being a child/son</note> of God, so that he no longer wills or desires for his own sake, but that...

<summary>Boehme identifies the internal "Antichrist" as the ego's desire for power and ownership. He argues that true Christianity requires a spiritual "poverty" where one views all possessions and status as a stewardship held for God rather than personal property.</summary>
<keywords>Antichrist, self-will, ownership, stewardship, spiritual rebirth, Immanuel, meum and tuum, filiation, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 130 ---
<meta>This page continues the Tenth Epistle, describing the process by which a person surrenders their own will to God. It follows the previous page's discussion on the "Antichrist" being the internal spirit of pride and selfishness within the individual.</meta>

<page-num>112</page-num>
<header>The Tenth Epistle.</header>

...God’s will might be his own will and desire in him; that he might be dead (as to the will of the soul) in and to his selfhood <note>original: "owne-hood"; the state of being focused on one's own ego or desires</note> and that God in Christ might be his life.

7. He must wholly immerse his will with the deepest humility into God's mercy and lay hold of a resolution in the Divine promise of grace. He must decide that this very hour he will depart from all self-centered attachment to the pleasures of this world and never enter into them again, even if he should become the fool of all the world because of it. He must wholly immerse himself into the deepest submissive lowliness and unworthiness before God with repentance. Yet, in his soul, he must apprehend and hold fast the promise of grace and stand firm in it, like a soldier before his enemy when his body and life are at stake.

<margin>☞</margin> 8. When this happens, his own will (which is the **Antichrist**) will be seized and put to death **in the death of Christ**. His soul will soon become like a young, simple child who has lost its natural understanding of self-centeredness; it begins to lament before God as a young child before its mother, trusting the mother to give it whatever she pleases.

<margin>☞</margin> 9. And this is what Christ said: "You must turn and become as children, and forsake all, and follow me." For **Adam** departed from God’s will into his own self-will. In his own self-desire, he brought the whispers of the Serpent and the will and desire of the Devil into himself. Thus, he brought himself and his life’s consort (which before stood in mutual harmony and agreement in one single will, which was God’s) into division and disunion. At that point, the properties of nature deviated from their balanced harmony and concordance; each property entered into its own selfhood (being its own or peculiar self-desire). Then the lust and imagination for good and evil arose in him, and heat and cold immediately fell upon him. He died to the holy life in that balanced and mutual harmony in which he had lived in one single pure **Element**, where the **four elements** were within him in equal weight or **proportion** <note>original: "Temperature"; here meaning a balanced state of physical or spiritual qualities</note>.

10. And God warned him of this, saying, "**Eat not of the Tree of the knowledge of good and evil, or else you shall die**." He meant by this the death to the Kingdom of Heaven—namely, [the fading away] of the beautiful Angelic Image, which died immediately in the false desire introduced by the Serpent. Therefore, this false will of the Serpent must first die in Christ’s death through true **Conversion**. Out of this death, Christ arises in his spirit again within us, in the heavenly Image which died in **Adam**, and the inward man is regenerated and born anew in Christ’s spirit.

11. This new Spirit reaches a state of Divine vision or contemplation in himself;

<meta>catchword: himself ;</meta>

<summary>Boehme describes the spiritual process of dying to the "selfhood" of the ego to be reborn in Christ's spirit, restoring the balanced "angelic image" that Adam lost during the Fall. He identifies the individual's self-will as the internal Antichrist that must be overcome through child-like humility and repentance.</summary>
<keywords>selfhood, Antichrist, Adam, Serpent, Conversion, regeneration, Angelic Image, Temperature, four elements, humility, spiritual rebirth</keywords>

--- Page 131 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s "Tenth Epistle," expanding on the internal transformation of the believer. It contrasts the "newborn spirit" with the "Antichristian" self-will that persists in the flesh.</meta>

<page-num>213</page-num>
<header>The Tenth Epistle.</header>

himself; it hears God’s Word and has divine understanding and inclination, and may behold the *Great Mystery* <note>original: "Grand Mystery"; likely a reference to the *Mysterium Magnum*, the hidden ground of all being</note> in divine and natural mysteries. And although the earthly flesh still clings to him in its inclination, yet it does not hurt or spoil the newborn spirit within him at all.

12. In this new birth, he is like solid, fine gold in a rough, drossy stone; the drossy casing <note>original: "foyle"</note> of the stone is not able to destroy or spoil the gold. For his true will is dead to earthly desire and continually desires to kill and mortify the lust of the flesh; he kills it without ceasing. For here the *Seed of the Woman*—namely, the new man born in Christ—bruises the *head of the Serpent’s will* <note>A reference to Genesis 3:15, interpreted here as the internal battle against sin</note> in the flesh, which is the spirit of *Antichrist*.

13. And beloved sir, I tell you as a Christian and brother in all faithfulness, sincerity, and uprightness: in our *supposed religion*—where people do nothing but contend, refute, and revile one another over *the letter* of scripture and the different opinions derived from it—we are still in the midst of *Babel* <note>Babel signifies a state of spiritual confusion and sectarian conflict</note>. It was never worse than it is now, even though people boast that they have left *Babel* and possess the true religion (the value of which I leave to be carefully considered).

14. But based on what has been revealed to me in the Lord my God, through the exceedingly precious *talent* <note>An allusion to the biblical parable of the talents (Matthew 25)</note> given to me by God, I say this: people have indeed dipped Christ's purple mantle in the blood of Christ and put it on as a covering. But they have only covered the Antichristian child of self-will, painting over the *Antichristian bastard* with a different color.

15. For people flatter it excessively and cover it with Christ's suffering, merit, and death. They comfort it by saying that Christ has paid for everything, and that it only needs to apply or comfort itself with the merit of Christ and receive it in faith as a *satisfaction*. In this way, they show us only an outward, credited righteousness.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. But true understanding follows a completely different *ABC* <note>alphabet or basic principles</note>. No amount of comforting, self-willed effort, or religious routines <note>original: "keeping a round"</note> is of any use. The suffering and death of Christ will not be given to the Antichristian beast within the *self*, but only to those who depart from and relinquish all self-centered ownership <note>original: "ownehood [ and propriety ]"</note> of created things. It is for those who wholly resign themselves to the suffering and death of Jesus Christ, dying to their own will in and with Christ, and are buried with him, only to rise in him to a new will and obedience. These are they who hate sin, who put on Christ in his suffering, reproach, and persecution, and who take up his cross and follow him under his *Red Banner* <note>The "Red Banner" refers to the blood and martyrdom of Christ</note>. To them, I say, it will be given. These people put on Christ by following his process, and in the inward spiritual man they become—

<sig>Q</sig>

<vocab>Great Mystery, Seed of the Woman, Antichrist, Supposed Religion, Babel, Talent, Antichristian Bastard, self-ownership, Red Banner, inward spiritual Man</vocab>

<summary>On this page, Boehme argues that true Christianity requires a literal death of the "self-will" rather than a mere intellectual or outward acceptance of Christ's merits. He criticizes the religious sectarianism of his day as "Babel" and emphasizes the internal struggle between the "newborn spirit" and the "Antichristian" nature of the flesh.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, New Birth, Antichrist, self-will, Babel, Seed of the Woman, mysticism, mortification, faith vs. works</keywords>

--- Page 132 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme’s exploration of the "new man" versus the "animal man." He argues that being a Christian is not a matter of outward profession but an internal reality where Christ's spirit replaces the fallen, "beastly" nature of the individual.</meta>

<page-num>114</page-num>
<header>The Tenth Epistle.</header>
<meta>catchword: of</meta>

Christ's members, and the Temple of God, who dwells in *us*.

<margin>☞</margin>
17. No one has the right to comfort himself with Christ's merits unless he desires to fully "put on" Christ <note>A biblical metaphor (from Galatians 3:27) meaning to adopt the character and spiritual nature of Christ</note> within himself. Furthermore, he is no Christian until he has put Him on through true repentance and conversion to Him, with an absolute resignation and sincere self-denial, so that Christ espouses and betroths Himself to him.

18. The beginning of this occurs in the Covenant of Baptism, where the child promises and swears under Christ’s Red Banner what must afterwards follow in actual deed or real practice. Or, if someone has turned away [from the practice of what he then promised], he must, through a conversion of his will, turn himself back into it again. I say with certainty that for many people, the "Mantle of Christ" will turn into hellish fire because they use it to cover their "Antichrist" <note>Here referring to personal hypocrisy or the internal spirit of opposition to God</note> and yet remain nothing but a beast.

19. For a Christian must be born of Christ and die to the Adamic will <note>The "Adamic will" refers to the fallen, selfish nature inherited from Adam</note>. He must have Christ within him and be a branch or member of His flesh and spirit—not according to the animal beast, but according to the spiritual man.

20. For the Spirit of God does not possess the "beast" [the outward, sensory, natural, or rational man], but rather the Temple of Christ—namely, *Christ's spiritual flesh and blood within us*. For Christ said, *Whoever does not eat the flesh of the Son of Man has no life in himself* <note>John 6:53</note>.

21. Now, there must be a mouth that is indeed proper and fit to eat it, for it will not be given to the "beast," much less to the <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical term referring to the core being or substance of a thing</note> of the Serpent. For every spirit eats from its "mother" (the source from which it arose). I leave this for every understanding person to consider; here I only mention what a Christian ought to be if he is to consider himself a Christian.

<margin>☞</margin>
22. For a "beast" is no Christian. Rather, a Christian is one who is baptized with the *Holy Spirit* into the *death of Christ*, who has "put on" Christ and lives in Christ's heavenly flesh and blood. He has tasted *Christ’s Supper* and sits with Christ at the table. He is a Christian who walks in Christ’s footsteps and continually mortifies <note>original: "mortyfieth"; to subdue, deaden, or kill off the power of sinful desires</note> the Antichristian, evil beast within his flesh and blood (which still clings to a Christian), binds it, deprives it of its strength, and patiently surrenders himself during temptations, which are offered to him in many hundreds of ways for his trial and purification.

23. A Christian must learn the alphabet <note>original: "A. B. C."</note> backwards and consider the wisdom of his own reason as foolishness, so that Christ may take form in him and he may be made capable of receiving heavenly wisdom.

24. For the wisdom of the outward world is blind in respect of

<summary>Boehme defines true Christianity as an internal, spiritual transformation where the believer "puts on Christ" and subdues their animalistic, fallen nature. He warns that religious rituals like baptism are meaningless without a sincere life of self-denial and spiritual "eating" of Christ's essence.</summary>

<keywords>New Birth, Baptism, Adamic Will, Self-Denial, Mortification, Holy Spirit, Inner Christ, Mysticism</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the tenth epistle, finishing a sentence from the previous page regarding how the Spirit of God interacts with the human soul and the surrender of the self-will.</meta>

<page-num>115</page-num>
<header>The Tenth Epistle.</header>

of God, and yet does not see Him. Even though all things live and move in God, and He Himself is through all things, yet He possesses nothing except that which dies to its own will. That He must possess, and He possesses it willingly, for it wills nothing without Him; it is in the *End* of the *Creation* as well as in the *Beginning*.

25. I could mention more of this to you if the occasion permitted. I have described and declared these things at length in my *Writings*, drawing from the center and the origin <note>original: "Originall"</note> of all *Essences* <note>Boehme uses "Essences" to refer to the fundamental spiritual qualities or substances of reality</note>. Here, I have only hinted briefly at what a *Christian's* state, being, and condition is. I hope you will consider this further and give yourself over to this process, as I trust you are already doing.

26. But for a more brotherly connection <note>original: "recreation"</note>, I thought it good to visit you with a short *Letter* <note>original: "Epistle"</note>. I wanted to comfort myself a little with you in the hope and faith that works and resides in *us*, until we are eventually freed from this *cottage* <note>A metaphor for the physical body or the mortal state</note> and are afterwards refreshed and made perfectly alive <note>original: "quickned"</note> with one another in *Divine* and brotherly union and *vision*.

27. I have written this at the request of the *Doctor* mentioned earlier, in all sincerity and uprightness. And so, I commend you to the tender love of *Jesus Christ*.

<summary>Boehme concludes his letter by explaining that God only possesses the soul that has surrendered its own self-will, and he expresses hope for a future spiritual reunion beyond the physical body.</summary>
<keywords>surrender of will, divine presence, essences, spiritual growth, mortality as a cottage, Jacob Boehme, Christian life</keywords>

<header>The Eleventh Epistle.</header>

# THE
# ELEVENTH EPISTLE:

->*Our Salvation is in the effectual working love of Jesus Christ in us.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Dear brother in the life of Jesus Christ, I wish from my heart that the divine working love may be with you, so that your noble lily-twig <note>A "lily-twig" is Boehme's metaphor for the growth of new, spiritual life or the "new man" within a person.</note> which has sprung forth in Christ may grow great in the power of Christ and bring forth much fruit in the paradise of God, to the heavenly joy of us all and our eternal brotherly fellowship.

2. I rejoice in my soul when I perceive that a good, virtuous, fair *branch* has sprung up in our tree of life, *Christ*; and I also hope to be a sharer of its good fruit.

3. Just as one branch on the tree enjoys the sap and power of the others, and they all grow and bear fruit together in one single *power*, so we are also in the tree of Christ, all truly *one*; and this tree is *Christ in us all*.

4. Since you freely and sincerely, with full acknowledgment of heart and voice <note>original: "mouth"</note>, commit yourself to this tree of life and, on the contrary, renounce and speak against the poison and delusion of Satan; therefore I wish for nothing more at present than to be able—through the power of this tree, which is *Christ*—to share the influence of the power I have received from him with you, so that we might grow together as members in one power.

<margin>☛</margin>
5. And I have no doubt that the Most High has fathered <note>original: "begotten"</note> his lily-twig in you. For without divine power, we have no *longing* or *hunger for God*, and we also cannot know him without his spirit within us; everything that we fundamentally <note>or in reality</note> understand and know of him comes from his revelation and working <note>original: "Operation"</note>.

6. For although the world prattles and talks much about God, it only does so out of custom, and receives its knowledge from the

<meta>catchword: History</meta>

<summary>In this opening of the eleventh epistle, Boehme uses the metaphor of a tree to describe the spiritual unity of believers in Christ, whom he describes as the "Tree of Life." He encourages the recipient, viewing their spiritual growth as a "lily-twig" nourished by divine power rather than mere human custom.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Lily-twig, Tree of Life, Christ in us, spiritual growth, divine power, revelation, unity of believers</keywords>

<header>The Eleventh Epistle.</header>

# The Eleventh Epistle.

The history of the literal word [grasped by the habitual faculties of its natural reason] is still void of true knowledge.
> For no one knows the Father except the Son, and he to whom the Son will reveal Him. <note>original: Matthew 11:27</note>

7. And therefore we have no true knowledge of God, unless it be given to us by the Son, who lives in us, if we are [living] branches on the vine.

8. For Christ said, "Whoever is of God hears God’s word"; and to the "reason-learned" <note>original: "Reason-learned," meaning those who rely solely on intellectual study and logic rather than spiritual experience</note> who were only skillful and taught in the letter, he said, "Therefore you do not hear, because you are not of God; also you are not my sheep; you are ravening wolves, and hirelings."

9. Therefore I say, that if we will speak rightly of God, and understand His will, then His words must abide in us, in a vital and living operation; for Christ said *without me you can do nothing*; also no one can call God Lord without the *Holy Spirit in him*; for his calling Him "Lord" must be born of God, and flow from the *Holy Spirit*.

<margin>☞</margin>
10. Nothing is pleasing to God, and also nothing is accepted by God, but that which He Himself does and works in and through the spirit of man; therefore Christ says, *all the plants which my Heavenly Father has not planted shall be rooted out and burnt with fire.*

11. Therefore, my beloved brother, you do well to hold and keep yourself to the Source <note>original: "Originall"</note> of life, and desire power and strength from it. You shall be well energized, refreshed, and strengthened; you are an acceptable guest to God and the members of Christ in this your purpose.

12. And if you persevere steadfastly and resist the devil, the World, and the earthly flesh and blood; and prepare yourself to fight like a true noble champion for victory against all these; and overcome in yourself the powerful and open enemy, self-love; and come rightly into our Universal Love <note>original: "Generall L O V E"</note>; then you shall certainly know and find by experience that the noble and exceedingly worthy Trophy, or Crown, of Christ’s victory—which He obtained in the overthrow of death and Hell—shall be set upon you, with the Heavenly Kingdom of joy.

13. And then all the children of Christ, together with the holy Angels, shall rejoice exceedingly more with you than for ninety-nine righteous ones who have already obtained it.

14. And the fair and noble *Sophia* <note>Divine Wisdom, often personified by Boehme as a bride or a guiding spiritual force</note> shall be given for a spouse unto your soul. She now stands at the door of your soul and earnestly entreats and calls you with her voice; and she knocks, bidding you to come forth and hold out rightly in battle against sin, death, the Devil, and hell. With your earnestness or fixed resolution, you must set the great <term>Petards</term> <note>A petard was a small bomb used to blow open gates or walls in a siege.</note> against the strong fortress of nature, and she will help you to blow up and demolish this strong fort.

<summary>Boehme explains that true spiritual knowledge comes through Christ's presence in the soul rather than through intellectual study, and he encourages the reader to fight a spiritual battle against self-love to achieve union with Divine Wisdom (Sophia).</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Reason-learned, Sophia, spiritual warfare, self-love, Holy Spirit, Petards, regeneration</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the Eleventh Epistle, where Jacob Boehme encourages the reader to persevere through spiritual trials. He describes the soul’s progression toward a mystical "marriage" with Divine Wisdom (Sophia) and warns against looking back at worldly desires.</meta>

<page-num>118</page-num>
<header>The Eleventh Epistle.</header>

<margin>Shepherd's Crook <note>original: "Shepheards Crooke"; likely a symbol of pastoral guidance or the authority of Christ as the Good Shepherd.</note></margin>
15. And then you shall see great wonders, and at the hour of conquest, the joyful nuptials or marriage of the Lamb shall be celebrated in heaven within you; and then the shepherd's staff shall be given by Christ into the hand of your soul.

16. But be sure, remember to remain truly and faithfully to the end, keeping what you have promised to this chaste *Sophia* <note>original: "Sophia," the Greek word for Wisdom, personified in Boehme's work as the divine bride of the soul.</note>; it must be in right earnest, and you must not look back toward Sodom like *Lot's wife*, who was turned into a pillar of salt.

<margin>* Or, Mark.</margin>
17. Instead, you must go out of Sodom with Lot according to God's command, and enter into the footsteps of Christ, not at all regarding the mocking, reproaches, and abusive <note>original: "opprobrious"</note> speeches of the world. Rather, love the *brand* <note>original: "Brand of Christ," referring to the "marks" or stigmas of Christ—the suffering and rejection associated with following Him.</note> of Christ more than the friendship, honor, and goods of the whole world, and then you may walk along with us upon the pilgrim's path of Christ.

18. But if you do not relish this, but instead mind and desire the pleasure and honor of the world, then you are not yet ready and prepared for the marriage, nor to come to your spouse, our loving and dear *Sophia*.

19. Therefore consider yourself well; behold and examine your whole heart. If you find a longing desire and drawing toward this (as I in part perceive and notice), then do not delay or put it off for an hour, but go forward. Enter with right earnestness <gloss>[ or a fixed mind ]</gloss> into repentance, and yield up your will wholly and fully to enter instantly into it, never to go out from it again—even if you should therefore *forsake your body, LIFE, honor, and goods*.

20. And if you do this, then you are rightly prepared, and the true *pioneer* <note>original: "Pioneer," meaning one who goes before to prepare the way; here, a spiritual force or the Holy Spirit.</note> will come into your soul and do in you what you cannot do without him.

21. And although afterwards <gloss>[ vain ]</gloss> inclinations and great strife and opposition in the flesh might stick and cling to you, and your reason might call you a fool—yes, even if God's anger should cover and cloud you in soul and body—yet all this will not hurt you. You shall spring forth from under such thorns with a new mind and walk with the spirit in *heaven*.

22. And although the earthly body must be busy <note>original: "conversant"</note> with created things, yet it shall be with the body as it is with a rough stone in which fine gold grows.

23. Do not be at all offended by my tribulation and persecution, nor be afraid; for it is the mark of Christ. Just look back into the Scriptures and see how it went with the children of God: how they were continually persecuted and killed by those who should have taught the ways of God.

24. For I am entrusted with a precious pearl, which God does
<meta>Catchword: does (fo...)</meta>

<summary>Boehme exhorts the reader to commit fully to the spiritual path, describing the mystical union with Divine Wisdom (Sophia) as a marriage that requires the abandonment of worldly honors. He explains that spiritual growth often occurs through suffering and persecution, much like gold growing within a rough stone.</summary>

Jacob Boehme reflects on the opposition he faces from "Reason" and those he deems "wicked," while encouraging his correspondent to remain humble and prayerful. <page-num>119</page-num>
<header>The Eleventh Epistle.</header>

[...If you] so cover <note>This sentence continues from the previous page, likely suggesting that God "covers" or hides divine mysteries from the prideful.</note> it, that the unworthy do not see it, but are blind to it and are offended by the simplicity of the person; so that they may remain blind to themselves in the wisdom of their own Reason. This they see, and yet they do not understand it, because they scorn and despise the simplicity of Christ.

25. But the time is very near at hand when they shall give a serious <note>original: "earnest"</note> account for it.

26. But since God has given you to understand what it is and where it comes from, be thankful to Him for it; it has happened to you out of grace. For you have humbled yourself before Him, and even greater grace may yet come to you if you persevere steadfastly in *humility* and earnest *Prayer*.

27. I will willingly share <note>original: "impart"</note> my love with you, as a fellow member of Christ, by praying and working together; for it is pure joy in my heart to do so. Although I must suffer bodily trouble and calamity because of it, yet I rejoice to see what God the Lord has done through this poor man <note>Boehme frequently refers to himself as a "poor man" or simple laborer to emphasize that his wisdom comes from God rather than academic study.</note> so far.

28. Satan cannot hinder the ways of God; and though it seems as if he hinders them by his murderous cries, yet they are instead made more widely known <note>original: "divulged"</note>, so that the children of God inquire after the true foundation <note>original: "ground"</note>.

29. But the wicked crowd <note>original: "crew"</note> is hardened and hindered by it, while others are called through it; and you shall see this before a year passes. And even if they *kill* me, yet it must move forward; for it is from the Lord.

30. And I commit you to the tender, effectively working love of Jesus Christ; and I commend myself to your brotherly love, favor, and affection.

<header>The Twelfth Epistle.</header>

# THE TWELFTH EPISTLE:

## TO Mr. JOHN BUDOFSKY.

### Our Salvation in the life of Jesus Christ.

---

->*Worthy and much-respected Sir,*<-

**All sincere wishes of Divine Salvation in holy power, and all temporal welfare, are offered first.**

1. I have received your letter, sent to me for Christian friendship and refreshment in divine knowledge, in divine desire, and affectionate, hearty Christian love; it is exceedingly welcome and acceptable to me.

2. I also rejoice that God still has His children and "little flock" here and there; otherwise, at present, the world is deeply drowned in wickedness and is seized by the fire of divine anger <note>original: "Anger"; in Boehme’s theology, this refers to the *Grimmigkeit* or the wrathful, dark quality of nature when separated from God's light.</note>, which shall shortly cause a great tear <note>original: "rent"</note> in Antichristian Christendom, as is well known and manifest.

<margin>☞ Or, Following,</margin>
3. A person does very well and *rightly* who learns to truly *know himself*—what he is. This cannot be achieved through Reason or sharp searching [or deep study and speculation in the natural arguments <note>original: "acruments"; likely meaning the logical tools or arguments produced by the intellect.</note> of Reason], but rather through the true *process of Christ* <note>The "process of Christ" refers to the spiritual journey of mimicking Christ's life, suffering, and resurrection within one’s own soul.</note> in a truly resigned <note>original: "resigned"; referring to *Gelassenheit*, the spiritual state of total surrender and yielding to God's will.</note> soul. Such a soul disclaims and forsakes its own Reason, its own wit, and human selfishness; with a true conversion from the earthly way, it enters into the highest simplicity of Christ and into the truest, deepest humility under the Cross of Christ—just as Christ faithfully taught us when He said, *Unless you turn and become as children,*

<meta>catchword: Children,</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme begins a letter to John Budofsky, expressing joy at finding a kindred spirit and warning that the world is currently gripped by divine wrath. He argues that true self-knowledge and salvation come not through intellectual study, but through a humble "resignation" of the self to the process of Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, John Budofsky, self-knowledge, Reason, resignation, Gelassenheit, process of Christ, divine anger, humility</keywords>

--- Page 139 ---
<meta>This page continues the Twelfth Epistle to John Budofsky. The previous page introduced the necessity of self-knowledge and the "process of Christ" through resignation (Gelassenheit). This page elaborates on the nature of this inner transformation and the limitations of human reason.</meta>

<page-num>121</page-num>
<header>The Twelfth Epistle.</header>

> *Children, unless you are born anew of the water and Holy Spirit, you cannot see the Kingdom of God.* <note>A modernization of John 3:3-5. Original: "Children, and be borne anew of the water, and holy spirit, you cannot see the Kingdome of God."</note>

4. To this state, a true and real resignation <note>original: "Resignation"; referring to *Gelassenheit*, the spiritual practice of total surrender and yielding to God's will</note> and renouncement of the human self belongs. In this, a person wholly commits himself to his inward ground <note>original: "Ground"; the deepest essence of the soul where it connects to the divine</note> and completely annihilates his selfhood within himself. He turns himself by earnest repentance, with an entire inward desire, away from this world’s *being* and toward God. He must wholly die in the death of Christ to his own strength and self-will, and fall down or sink into the mercy and compassion of God. In this way, he may be apprehended by the Holy Spirit in his own inward ground, so that the Spirit may see, will, and act through him what is pleasing to God. The Spirit alone is the true searcher of divine knowledge and the light of the soul; in this light, the soul beholds and knows God. In no other way may it attain to divine and natural knowledge, vision, and contemplation.

5. For the natural, rational person understands nothing of the mystery of God’s kingdom, for he is outside of it and not in God. This is plain and manifest among those learned in human reason, in that they contend and wrangle about the being and will of God and yet do not know it, for they do not hear God’s Word within themselves.

6. Everything is dead and void of understanding regarding God if it does not have the living voice and the *divine hearing of the new birth in the essence <note>original: "Ens"; a term referring to the fundamental spiritual substance or "being" of Christ as it takes shape in the believer</note> of Christ within it*. It is necessary that the Spirit of God gives testimony of his outward hearing and teaching within him. In this internal seeing, God alone is known and his being is understood. To this, the outward literal or written Word is only a form and a prepared *instrument*.

<margin>☛</margin>
7. But true understanding must flow from the inward ground, out of the living Word of God (which must first be opened and revealed in a person) and enter into the written Word. There must be one concordance and harmony; otherwise, all teaching of the divine being and will is nothing but a building upon the great *Babylon* <note>In Boehme's thought, "Babylon" represents the state of spiritual confusion and the "tower" of human intellectual pride and false religious systems</note> of earthly reason and wonders.

8. In this internal ground, all my knowledge concerning the divine and natural ground has its rise, beginning, and understanding. For I was not born of the schools of this world [or educated in the scholastic arts <note>original: "Scholastick Arts"; the formal academic theology and philosophy taught in universities</note>] and am a plain, simple man. But by God’s Spirit and will, I have been brought—without my own purpose or desire—into divine knowledge through deep natural searchings.

9. This knowledge and free gift of grace I shall heartily and willingly share with my dear brothers and fellow members in the tree of life of Jesus Christ. I daily offer supplications to God that their hearts might be opened in divine hearing and understanding, so that such knowledge might be manifested and made known also in

<sig>R</sig>
<meta>catchword: them</meta>

<summary>Boehme describes the process of "resignation" or spiritual surrender as the only way to attain true divine knowledge, contrasting this inner revelation with the empty intellectual "wrangling" of university-educated theologians. He emphasizes that his own profound insights come not from formal schooling but from the Holy Spirit working within his "inward ground."</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Resignation, Gelassenheit, Inward Ground, New Birth, Ens of Christ, Babylon, Scholastic Arts, Divine Knowledge, Internal Revelation</keywords>

--- Page 140 ---
<meta>This page concludes the Twelfth Epistle to John Budofsky. It transitions from a critique of rationalist theology to a personal note of friendship and a promise of a future visit once the weather improves and peace allows.</meta>

<page-num>122</page-num>
<header>The Twelfth Epistle.</header>

...them <note>referring to the "divine and natural knowledge" mentioned on the previous page</note>, and so we might be delivered out of the contentious, disputing Babylon <note>original: "contentious Disputing Babylon"; a reference to the confusion of tongues at the Tower of Babel, used here to describe the sectarian fighting and theological arguments of the time</note> and be brought into one brotherly love, bearing within us what God’s will and being is.

10. And Sir, I want you to know that your acknowledgment and offered friendship are very welcome to me. I also wish to discuss <term>Divine matters</term> with you and find refreshment in them. Since we are so far from one another, this cannot be done easily right now; yet it may happen soon. I fully intend (when the days are a little longer and we are more certain of stable weather—if God grants us enough favor and a time of peace) to speak with you and other good brothers and friends in those parts in person. At that time, I will answer the points you raised and speak with you in depth <note>original: "fundamentally"</note> about them, which cannot be done now in haste. I commend you and your family to the gentle <note>original: "meek"</note> love of Jesus Christ.

---

->Dated, December 13, 1622.<-
