# THE
## THIRTEENTH
### EPISTLE.

---

### A LETTER Written to one in TEMPTATION and trouble of mind, showing from where it arises.

---

->*Our salvation is in the life of Jesus Christ in us.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Dear Sir, my fellow member and brother in Christ our Savior: after first offering my heartfelt wish and shared desire for **divine love** and **grace**, I wish to let you know in Christian love that I have considered your condition with Christian sympathy and fellow-feeling. I have brought your situation before the gracious compassion of the Most High <note>A traditional title for God.</note> to see what he would be pleased to reveal to me about it.

2. Therefore, Sir, I must tell you that through that same gracious compassion, I obtained such an insight and vision into your condition and temptation that its basis and cause have been made known to me. I will set it down briefly as a <term>memorandum</term> <note>original: "Memorandum"; a note or record intended to be remembered and reflected upon.</note> so that you may consider and ponder it seriously by yourself.

3. The first cause of such strong, active temptation is the supernatural, superabundant, and unspeakable love of God—that is, the

<vocab>Epistle, Temptation, Divine Love, Grace, Sympathy, Vision, Memorandum, Salvation, Jesus Christ</vocab>

<summary>In this opening of the Thirteenth Epistle, Jacob Böhme addresses a correspondent suffering from spiritual distress, claiming to have received a divine insight into the causes of the person's temptations.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, spiritual temptation, mental distress, divine grace, pastoral care, 17th-century theology</keywords>

--- Page 142 ---
<meta>This page continues the explanation of spiritual temptation as a conflict between the divine will and the human "creaturely" will. Note: The previous page was numbered 123; although the OCR indicates 114, this page is sequentially 124.</meta>

<page-num>114</page-num>
<header>The Thirteenth Epistle.</header>

divine good will, and then the creaturely <note>The "creaturely will" refers to the natural, self-centered will of a human being as a created thing, as opposed to the selfless will of the Creator.</note> will of man struggling against one another. The human will refuses to fully resign and give itself up with total confidence to such great grace of God, which is offered to it out of pure love. Instead, it seeks itself and its own love of transitory things; it loves itself and the <margin>* Or, things.</margin> * worldly existence more than God.

4. Therefore, man’s own nature—which in its own center, without the love of God, exists in mere anguish, strife, enmity, and restless conflict—tempts him. Into this nature, the Devil also shoots his false desires to lead man astray from such high grace and love of God.

5. This temptation is the greatest of all. It is the very combat which Christ maintains with his love (poured out into the nature of man) against such self-centeredness <note>original: "selfeness"; the state of being focused entirely on one's own ego.</note>, as well as against God’s anger, sin, death, the Devil, and hell. In this combat, the "human Dragon" <note>A metaphor for the fierce, prideful, and fallen nature of the human ego.</note> must be devoured by the love of Christ and transformed into an angelic image.

<margin>☞</margin> 6. And if the love of God in Christ *did not have its influence in you*, you would have none of this combat. Instead, the Dragon (namely, the false, devilish will) would maintain his natural right and possession without any such conflict or disturbance.

7. Now, therefore, this perplexing and distressing temptation is felt very strongly in one's nature by the Dragon. He is in the labor pains <note>original: "travelling [travailing] anguish"; the intense pain of childbirth, used here to describe the soul's agony during spiritual rebirth.</note> of his own nature when such great love of God enters him and seeks to change his natural rights into a divine, self-denying will.

8. For here Christ, the Serpent-destroyer <note>A title for Christ referencing Genesis 3:15, where the offspring of the woman is prophesied to crush the head of the serpent.</note>, stands within the human being—even in the midst of "hell"—and storms or assaults the strong fortress of the Devil. From this arises such a struggle, where Christ and Lucifer fight for the soul, as God has allowed you to see and know through personal experience in your first temptation.

<margin>☞</margin> 9. *Thus Christ bruises the head of the Serpent, and the Serpent stings Christ in the heel* <note>original: Genesis 3:15. Böhme interprets this as the struggle between the divine spirit and the lower nature.</note>. The poor soul stands in the middle in great trembling and sadness, able to do nothing but stand still in hope. It is not even able to lift up its face before God and pour out effective prayers, for the Dragon turns its face toward the vanity of this world. He shows the soul the beauty and glory of this world and mocks it for wanting to become another kind of creature. He presents to the soul the kingdom in which it lives and its own natural foundations.

10. And here the soul stands with Christ in the wilderness, during the *forty days of temptation*, where the power, glory, riches, and plea-

<summary>Böhme explains that spiritual distress is a sign of Christ's love struggling against the selfish "Dragon" of human nature. He compares this internal conflict to Christ's forty days of temptation in the wilderness and the biblical prophecy of the serpent's head being bruised.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Böhme, spiritual warfare, creaturely will, the Dragon, Christ vs Lucifer, Genesis 3:15, temptation in the wilderness, self-denial, spiritual rebirth</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of spiritual temptations. While the previous page (numbered 114 in the sequence) described the initial struggle of the soul, this page (numbered 225 in the original text) details the "Second Temptation," where the human ego attempts to possess and control the divine light for itself.</meta>

<page-num>225</page-num>
<header>The Thirteenth Epistle.</header>

[The plea]sure of this world is offered to it; alluring it to elevate itself and enter into its own self-will.

<margin>☞</margin>
11. The *Second Temptation of Lucifer*, and the selfish Dragon of nature, is this: when the soul has tasted the Divine love and has been once illuminated, the soul then wants to keep that light as its own property <note>original: "propriety"; meaning exclusive ownership or possession.</note>. It wants to work in that light through its own power and ability, as if it were its own personal possession. Understand that the nature of the soul—which is a "Dragon" like Lucifer when it is without God’s light—wants that light for itself. But this Dragon will not surrender its natural rights; it wants to be the maker and disposer of the Divine power and live within it with great joy in its own fiery, <insert>selfish</insert> nature. But this cannot be.

12. This Dragon (namely, the fiery nature) must be changed by its own will into a fire of Love and give up its natural rights. However, it is unwilling to do so. In such a change or transformation <note>original: "transmutation"</note>, it looks for its own independent power but finds none. Therefore, it begins to doubt God's grace because it sees that in such a process it must forsake its own natural desires and will. Because of this, it is constantly afraid and refuses to *die to its own natural rights within the Divine light*. It always thinks that the light of *Grace* (which works without such harshness and fiery force) is a false light.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. This is why outward Reason (which is blind <insert>and sees nothing correctly by itself</insert>) continually thinks: **Oh!** Who knows how it really is with you? Who knows if it is true that God has illuminated you, or that He is within you? It might likely proceed from such a *fantasy* <note>original: "Fansie"; here meaning an illusion or a trick of the imagination.</note>. You do not see the same thing in other people, yet they expect to be saved just as much as you do. You are only making a fool of yourself before the world, standing in fear and trembling at God’s anger more than those who comfort themselves only with the *promise* of grace regarding the future Revelation.

14. Thus it happens that the internal ground <note>The "ground" refers to the deepest, essential foundation of the soul.</note> sighs and longs for the ignition and movement of the light, and desperately wants to have it. But nature is unable to do anything; it feels as if it were completely rejected by God. This is actually true regarding the "Self-will"; for God has planted a new will *into it*. The soul must die to its own will and be changed into God’s will.

15. And because the natural will must die here and surrender its own rights to the will of God, there are such painful temptations within it. The Devil does not want his *Prey-Fort* <note>original: "Prey-Fort"; a metaphorical stronghold or fortress where the Devil holds the soul captive.</note> to fall or be demolished. For if Christ shall live in Man, then the spirit of

<meta>The text cuts off here, continuing with the catchword: self-lust</meta>

<summary>This page describes the 'Second Temptation,' where the soul's natural ego (the 'Dragon') tries to claim divine enlightenment as its own personal power rather than surrendering to God's will. It highlights the internal conflict between the old 'self-will' and the 'new will' planted by God, which causes doubt and spiritual distress.</summary>

--- Page 144 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of spiritual temptations. Having previously discussed how the ego attempts to possess divine light for itself, the author now addresses the "Third Temptation," which concerns the deep-seated "strongholds" of the human will and the need for total resignation to Christ's example.</meta>

<page-num>126</page-num>
<header>The Thirteenth Epistle.</header>

Self-centered desire <note>original: "selfe-lust"</note> and imagination must die, though they do not die completely during this life because of the flesh. They die daily and yet continue to live; therefore, there is a conflict that no wicked man feels—only those who have "put on Christ," in whom Christ fights against *Lucifer*.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. The Third Temptation takes place in the strongholds of the Devil—namely, in the will and the mind, as well as in flesh and blood. This is where the "false centers" reside in a person, such as a specific self-will devoted to a proud, worldly life, the lust of the flesh, and earthly things. It also includes the many curses of men that have been wished upon his body and soul through his temptation. All these sins have taken root and formed a center, standing in the *astral* spirit <term>In this context, the "astral spirit" refers to the worldly or natural mind, which Boehme believed was influenced by the stars and the physical elements.</term> like a powerful fortress. Christ now fights against this and will destroy it. The human will still values and clings to this stronghold of power, pleasure, and worldly beauty as its own property <note>original: "propriety"</note> and best treasure; it will not surrender or be obedient to Christ.

17. Therefore, beloved sir and Christian brother, I am telling you what our loving Lord Jesus Christ has shown me in my reflections. Examine yourself to see what your temptation is. Our dear Lord said, *“We must forsake all and follow him,”* so that we may be truly *spiritually poor* <margin>Or, Truly spiritually poor.</margin>.

18. Now, if your mind is still stuck in self-centered desire, imagination, and the love of earthly things, then you are experiencing temptation within those "centers" which are still active in you.

<margin>☞</margin>
19. If you will follow my simple, child-like advice, I tell you this: when such a temptation arises, you must imagine nothing else but the bitter suffering and death of our Lord. Consider the insults and scorn he faced, the contempt, and the poverty he endured in this world for us poor people. Resign your desire and your entire will into that, wishing with all your heart to be shaped into *his image*. With sincere willingness, follow him in his *way of life* <margin>Or, Way of life.</margin> and patiently endure whatever you are called to suffer, doing so willingly for his sake. Desire only to be like him. For the sake of his love, be content to be lowly, despised, and afflicted, so long as you can maintain and keep *his love in you*. Seek no longer your own will, but only what Christ wills through you.

20. Dear sir, I am concerned that there is still something in you that displeases Christ, which is why there is such a struggle within you. Christ wants you to die to your own will in his death, and to rise in his will and live with him. *Christ is currently in your soul, fighting for your soul.*

<summary>This page defines the "Third Temptation" as the human will's attachment to worldly power and self-interest, located in the "astral spirit." Boehme advises the reader to overcome this by surrendering their will and contemplating Christ’s suffering and poverty.</summary>
<keywords>Third Temptation, astral spirit, self-will, resignation, imitation of Christ, spiritual poverty, Lucifer, soul's conflict</keywords>

<header>The Thirteenth Epistle.</header>

<margin>☞</margin>
21. Let all earthly will go and resign yourself wholly and fully. Let joy and sadness, comfort and conflict, be all the same to you; in this way, you shall be a conqueror with Christ over the world, the Devil, death, and hell. At last, you will find through experience what Christ has been in you and why this has happened to you—which has been the path <note>original: "processe"</note> of all the children of Christ. I speak this out of Christian affection.

<page-num>128</page-num>
<header>The Fourteenth Epistle.</header>

# THE
# FOURTEENTH
# EPISTLE.
## TO
## Frederick Crausen,
### AT
### GOLTBERG.

---

->*May the open fountain in the heart of Jesus Christ be our refreshment and constant light.*<-

<margin>1.</margin> **W**orthy, learned, and kind sir, my friend and beloved brother in Christ: after offering all hearty wishes for the love, illumination, and blessing of God, I want to say that your diligent study of *Divine Wisdom* is very acceptable and joyous to me.

2. This is all the more so because I perceive in your letter that *God* has opened your heart and spirit to a right understanding. I wish from my heart (as indeed I do not at all doubt) that the precious *Coral* <note>original: "Corall"; in Boehme’s mystical chemistry, coral often represents the spiritual "tincture" or the life-force of the new humanity in Christ.</note> in the humanity of Christ might again spring forth from the inward man in your paradisiacal plant—which withered in *Adam*—and bring forth true fruits for the table of God. This happens in the spirit of Christ and his tender humanity *within us*.

3. I pray that this noble and precious branch may be firmly engrafted into the vine of Christ and spring forth anew from it. May it blossom with us amidst this present, wicked, thorny world, and help to foretell the *summer of Christ* in his time of the *Lily* <note>Boehme used the "Time of the Lily" to describe a prophesied future age of spiritual purity and peace.</note>;

<meta>Catchword: indeed</meta>

<summary>In the opening of this letter to Frederick Crausen, Jacob Boehme expresses joy at Crausen's spiritual progress and prays for the growth of Christ's "paradisiacal plant" within him. He frames this internal spiritual growth as part of a larger movement toward a future age of peace and divine wisdom.</summary>
<keywords>Frederick Crausen, Divine Wisdom, Coral, Adam, Summer of Christ, Time of the Lily, spiritual growth</keywords>

--- Page 147 ---
<meta>This page continues the Fourteenth Epistle from Jacob Boehme to Frederick Crausen. It follows the introductory greetings where Boehme expressed joy at Crausen’s spiritual awakening.</meta>

<page-num>139</page-num>
<header>The Fourteenth Epistle.</header>

## The Fourteenth Epistle.

Indeed, some branches out of *Christ's Rose Garden* <note>original: "Christs Rose-garden"; a metaphor for the blossoming of spiritual life and the community of true believers.</note> do here and there appear; and they spring forth as a wonder of *God* in the midst of the fire of tribulation in *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; Boehme’s term for the confusion and corruption he saw in institutional churches and worldly systems.</note>.

<margin>☞</margin>
4. Regarding your statement that my writings have given you some direction: so be it. Be thankful to *God* for it, who thus reveals his wonders and deep, hidden wisdom through humble and untrained people. He sets them as a light to the children who, in the cradle of the world, work in their *Babel* and fables; they are convinced by simple humility that their works, will, and life are only a carved image and a forged, selfish invention that has no foundation or root in him.

5. At present, the Most High has given us many ways to understand how, in a short time, his wonders in his hidden wisdom are [and shall] be declared to the world in writings for its enlightenment. In these, our posterity—and all those who obtain understanding from *God*—shall not only wonder but exceedingly rejoice.

6. I understood from *Mr. Walter* that you have received some of my writings, which delight you very much. Yet I could wish that you also had the latest pieces, which are more plain and clear, and have a sweeter foundation; in these, the revealed *God* may be known very clearly in all his wonders and works.

7. In many places, they will reveal more light to you in your practice <note>Given Crausen's interests, "practice" likely refers to his spiritual discipline or perhaps his work in medicine or alchemy.</note>; for the foundation of nature is very clearly discovered in them, as is our very fair and pleasant garden of *Christ* and the new birth.

8. Beloved *Frederick*, it will give you much encouragement in your temporal and eternal exercise. I hope that you (being a grafted scion <note>original: "engrafted cyon"; a "scion" is a young shoot or twig cut for grafting. Boehme uses this to describe a person joined to the spiritual life of Christ.</note>) shall not break off from the tree of Divine Wisdom. Shortly, a time will come when it shall be of necessary use, and you shall rejoice among the first ones who go out from *Babel*.

9. I thank you very much for your *gift* <note>original: "Present"</note> which I have received. In return, I shall carry it in my will into the Mystery of the Most High, and it shall be received as a treasure for you; I acknowledge by this your true and open heart.

10. However, the *Pearl* <note>original: "Pearl"; referring to the "Pearl of Great Price" from the Gospel of Matthew, representing the kingdom of heaven or ultimate spiritual truth.</note> will not be given for that reason, but for nothing, just as *God* has done for us in *Christ*. So, one member is bound to the other. I commend you to the sweet and pleasant love of *Jesus Christ* and exhort you further to seek after the *Pearl*.

->Dated, July 17, 1622.<-
->*J. B.* <note>Jacob Boehme</note><-

<summary>In this conclusion to the letter, Boehme credits God for the wisdom in his writings and encourages Frederick Crausen to continue his spiritual "practice." He promises that his later works will provide even clearer insights into the foundation of nature and the spiritual rebirth needed to escape the confusion of "Babel."</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Frederick Crausen, Babel, Rose Garden, Divine Wisdom, spiritual growth, Pearl of Great Price, new birth, Mystery</keywords>

<meta>This page begins the Fifteenth Epistle from Jacob Boehme. It transitions from the previous letter's themes of spiritual awakening to a specific focus on the believer as a "plant" in the garden of God, emphasizing how a person's inner spiritual state affects the world around them.</meta>

<page-num>110</page-num>
<header>The Fifteenth Epistle.</header>

---

# THE FIFTEENTH EPISTLE.

---

> May the salvation and light of God in the life of Jesus Christ enlighten you and grant you a deeper understanding of his will.

---

1. **C**ourteous Sir and good friend; I cannot but rejoice with you, seeing that you have given up your life to be a plant of God. Thus, it springs and buds forth in the body of Jesus Christ, the Son of God, who has begotten us again as living beings in himself. He has set us before his Father as a lovely plant in his pleasant, paradisiacal garden, for his joy and for his works of wonder.

<margin>* Or, things.</margin>
2. And I find (if I consider it rightly) that you are not only a plant of God for yourself. Just as a pleasant herb and flower does not keep its strength and virtue <note>original: "vertue"; here meaning the inherent power, essence, or medicinal efficacy of a plant.</note> within itself, but casts forth and diffuses its virtue as a sweet savor unto all living essences <note>original: "Essences"; in Boehme’s thought, this refers to the fundamental spiritual qualities or entities that make up reality.</note>, and freely presents itself to all creatures regardless of what becomes of it—and so it does not spare itself, but continually produces its power and scent—

3. So I find it to be with the soul of man. It continually grows and freely yields its power or virtue for anyone to taste who desires it and is capable of receiving its power—whether it be for love or anger, for the life of God in Christ, or for a life of pride leading to the furthest depth of misery, which in the end befalls those who have not grown in God.

<margin><note>A manicule (pointing hand) is printed here.</note></margin>
4. But praise, glory, and honor are due to those who are reborn <note>original: "regenerate"; a theological term for being spiritually made new through Christ.</note> in Christ. Though they lose their lives here and appear before the "sting of the thorny plant" as a poor, useless herb that is trampled underfoot—like an herb that is cut down and no longer seen or noticed, so that reason says it is quite gone—yet for all that, it has its root in the Earth and springs up [again];

<meta>Catchword: thus</meta>

<summary>Boehme uses the metaphor of a plant in God's garden to describe the spiritual life of a believer, explaining how the soul's inner "virtue" naturally influences others. He offers encouragement to those who are "reborn" in Christ, noting that even if they are trampled by the world, their spiritual roots remain firm.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, regeneration, spiritual growth, Christ as life, virtue, metaphor of the plant, soul's influence</keywords>

--- Page 149 ---
<meta>This page continues the Fifteenth Epistle. Boehme uses the metaphor of the soul as a plant grafted into Christ to explain the relationship between the spiritual soul (which lives in God) and the physical body (which remains subject to the material world/stars).</meta>

<page-num>131</page-num>
<header>The Fifteenth Epistle.</header>

---

...thus likewise the soul of the Saints is grafted into the *holy life* of *Jesus Christ*. It stands in God his Father and springs forth again through *Death*.

5. Having known this through experience, we rejoice; therefore, we value the life of this world—which consists of the influence of the stars and elements—as the *least* important thing, and we rejoice that we are the Children of God.

6. Since we know that God is *truly* in *us*, yet hidden from our earthly life, we know that our soul is in God and grows in God. However, the body remains under the dominion of the stars and elements, according to the nature of this world.

7. Thus we are God's image and likeness, as he himself is everything. Should we not, therefore, rejoice? *Who will separate us from God, if the soul is in God, where no death or destruction exists?*

☞ 8. Therefore, my dear faithful friend and brother in Christ, I consider it a great joy that I have found a precious plant of God in you. My soul sensed this and was strengthened by it, even when the Oppressor <note>original: "Oppressour"; likely a reference to religious or political persecutors who opposed Boehme’s mystical teachings.</note> tried to tear it out of the land of the living; when it lay under those who oppress, and the *thorny plants of Antichrist* intended to devour us.

9. But just as God comes with his power to help the branches that stand in him so they might not perish—even though the Devil and Death storm against them—so they must spring forth again through death, and through the wrath or fierceness of the anger and *sting of death*. Although God bestows the most noble and precious herb of his garden upon it, his will must stand: what is sown in him must grow in him.

10. This we know because he has given his heart (*namely*, the most precious plant within himself) to become Man for us. This serves as a powerful, sweet fragrance of regeneration <note>original: "regeneration"; the process of spiritual rebirth or being made new in Christ.</note> in him. So, even when we were in death, we might be able to grow with and through him out of death and into God his Father, bringing forth the fruits of Paradise.

11. Since we know that we are the plants [fruits or *nursery* <note>original: "Nurserie"; here meaning a place where young plants are cultivated, symbolizing the gathering of believers.</note>] of God, let us fear nothing. Let us continually grow in *the life of God* and bring forth fruit for God’s honor and for deeds of wonder; we shall enjoy them eternally.

<margin>* Sin, iniquity</margin>
☞ 12. And since we know that our precious life stands in great danger (between the Kingdom of Heaven and the Kingdom of Hell, lying captive to both during this lifetime), we must walk warily and carefully so that our "Pearl" <note>The "Pearl" is Boehme's frequent metaphor for the divine spark or the kingdom of heaven within the soul.</note> may not be broken. We must not let the *savor of sin* into us to corrupt and spoil us; otherwise, the growth of the precious fruit is hindered, and God would complain of us—that he is like a *vine-dresser who gleans* and would gladly enjoy the *precious grapes*.

---

<summary>Boehme describes the believer as a plant grafted into Christ, explaining that while the body is subject to the physical world, the soul lives in God. He warns his friend to guard their spiritual "Pearl" against the corrupting influence of sin and the "Antichrist."</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, grafting, stars and elements, image of God, Antichrist, regeneration, Pearl of great price, spiritual growth, vine-dresser</keywords>

--- Page 150 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s Fifteenth Epistle. Having previously discussed the soul being grafted into Christ like a plant, Boehme now encourages his correspondent to remain spiritually vigilant and explains how his own "spiritual sleep"—a period of silence following the persecution of his first book—has ended.</meta>

<page-num>132</page-num>
<header>The Fifteenth Epistle.</header>

## The Fifteenth Epistle.

13. Therefore, let us be watchful to fight against the Prince of Wrath <note>original: "Prince of wrath"; a common reference to Satan or the spiritual force of anger and darkness.</note>, so that the precious grapes and fruits of God may grow in us. In these, God may find a pleasant smell and taste, so that we may be a pleasant, sweet fragrance to him in Christ.

14. We shall fully enjoy it when we are freed from the vanity of this life. Then we shall live and flourish in God and eat of the pure life of God without any lack. He will be our food, and we will be his, so that there may be a pleasant, mutual Paradisiacal growth in one another—we in God, and God in us—in the eternal source of the holy life in God’s nursery, where there is absolute perfection in pure love.

15. For this cause we labor so earnestly and suffer the scorn and contempt of the world: so that while our earthly life grows toward death, our heavenly life may sprout forth through death. Thus, the earthly life appears as something to be scorned compared to the heavenly; indeed, the earthly is *not worthy* to be called a life when compared with the heavenly one.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. And therefore we suffer patiently in the earthly life and rejoice in the heavenly, in the hope that we shall be freed from vanity. Then we shall be well refreshed with consolation; whatever we have been compelled <note>original: "faine"; meaning obliged or required by circumstances.</note> to sow here in tribulation and trouble, we shall reap in great joy.

<margin>
* That is: by his writing to him to employ his talent, for Boehme intended to write no more after his first book (Aurora) was forcibly taken from him by the authorities.
</margin>
17. Therefore, my very dear brother in the life of God in which you stand: you are more acceptable to me because you have awakened me out of *sleep*, so that I might go on to bring forth fruit in the life of God and afterward rejoice in it with the children of God.

18. And I want you to understand that after I was awakened again, a very strong fragrance was given to me in the life of God. I hope to bring forth fruit in it and to awaken the *sleepy*, just as God has awakened me out of the sleep in which I lay.

19. And I entreat you, for the sake of the holy life of God in Christ, that in the future you would not be faint or weary. Instead, animate and stir up your life in Christ so that our spirits may be perceived and understood, which cannot happen without the *Divine Power*.

<margin>
Note. ☞
† Proceeding from God.
</margin>
20. For everyone speaks from their own essence <note>original: "Essences"; here referring to the fundamental spiritual nature or inner qualities of a person.</note> regarding the wonders of God, according to how their life is ignited in God. No man can bring us to an understanding except the only spirit <margin>†</margin> of God. *On the day of Pentecost, this spirit changed the tongues of all nations into one in the mouths of the Apostles, so that the languages of all nations could understand the tongues of the Apostles. Although they spoke from only one tongue, the hearts and spirits of the hearers were opened in God, so that they all understood them, every person in their own language, as if the Apostles had spoken in their specific native tongues.*

<meta>The text continues with paragraph 21 on the following page.</meta>

<summary>Boehme discusses the necessity of suffering in the earthly life to achieve heavenly growth and thanks his correspondent for encouraging him to resume his writing after a period of forced silence. He concludes by explaining that true spiritual understanding comes only through the Holy Spirit, citing the Miracle of Pentecost.</summary>

<keywords>Prince of Wrath, spiritual growth, Jacob Boehme, Aurora, Pentecost, Holy Spirit, Essences, spiritual awakening</keywords>

--- Page 151 ---
<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s Fifteenth Epistle. Following his discussion on spiritual growth and the value of heavenly life over earthly suffering, Boehme addresses the difficulty readers may have with his mystical writings and mentions the personal circumstances currently occupying his time.</meta>

<page-num>133</page-num>
<header>The Fifteenth Epistle.</header>

## The Fifteenth Epistle.

21. Thus, it is only possible in God for one spirit to comprehend and understand another. I fear that in many places in my writings, I shall be difficult to understand; but in God, I am very easy and clear to the reader, if their soul is grounded in God—the source from which I write.

22. For I have very little from the scholarly learning <note>original: "hiſtoricall Art"; referring to traditional academic or theological education</note> of this world, and I do not write for the sake of the pride and honor of their learning. I am not born of their scholarship, but out of the life of God, so that I might bear fruit in the Paradisiacal rose garden of God.

23. And that is not for myself alone, but for my brothers and sisters, so that we might be one holy body in Christ to God our Father, who has loved and chosen us before the foundation of the world was laid.

<margin>☞</margin>
24. Therefore, just as Christ did not spare his life—and neither did his true disciples—but freely preached the kingdom of God, even though they suffered scorn and death in this world for it, and only for the sake of the heavenly kingdom; so we must not fear temporal <note>meaning: relating to worldly time or physical existence, as opposed to eternal</note> scorn and death for the sake of heavenly life. We must pray that God would deliver us from all evil and give us unity of mind.

25. But I am sorry that I am so difficult for you to understand in some points of my writings, and I wish that I could impart my soul to you so that you might comprehend my meaning.

26. For I understand that it concerns the deepest points on which the main argument depends, where I have used some Latin words. But my meaning rests in truth, not merely in the Latin language, but much more in the *Language of Nature* <note>original: "Language of Nature"; Boehme's belief that the sounds and structures of words reflect the inner essence of the things they describe</note>.

27. For it has been revealed to me in some measure how to sound out the *spirits of the letters* from their very origin. I would very gladly give you the meaning and interpretation of those words I have used and which you have misunderstood; but since it will take up some space, and because I am currently in a hurry, it cannot be done now. I am very willing to offer a very clear interpretation of them very soon.

28. For I have been so busy with traveling back and forth and other affairs that I could not satisfy you in this; I pray you have a little patience to wait for it.

29. For I still have much to do because of my brother's daughter (who recently died), so that I must travel every week into the country. I was also obliged <note>original: "faine"</note> to make two previous journeys, which took up my time.

<margin>* Or, withers.</margin>
30. If it pleases God that my traveling eventually ends, I hope that my work shall do many a poor soul good service in its hunger. Yet, whatever God wills, let it be done; just as many a blade of grass <margin>*</margin> perishes,

<summary>Boehme explains that his writings come from divine inspiration rather than academic study and acknowledges that his use of the "Language of Nature" may be difficult for readers. He also excuses his delay in clarifying these points due to personal family matters and travel.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual understanding, historical art, Language of Nature, spirits of the letters, mysticism, personal biography</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Boehme’s personal correspondence. He discusses the tension between his spiritual calling and his earthly duties to his family, expresses concerns about the security of his manuscripts, and comments on the political and prophetic significance of the coronation of Frederick V in Prague.</meta>

<page-num>134</page-num>
<header>The Fifteenth Epistle.</header>

Just as the Heavens do not give rain, so do worldly affairs hinder God's Kingdom.

31. Yet I know at present *no other* remedy or means to support my earthly body, along with my wife and children; therefore, I will use all diligence and prioritize heavenly things above all earthly things as much as lies in my power. If you have a desire to read any of my writings, they shall be faithfully shared with you; though I would gladly learn from God's children and also *refresh myself through their writings.*

<margin>☞</margin>
32. For I consider myself to be the most humble among them; I have written only a little for my own remembrance and spiritual practice. But since it pleases you to read it, I have no reason to hide it from you.

33. For I acknowledge the great efforts you devote to this, and I thank God that He has sent me a person in this world with whom I may openly discuss God's Kingdom; otherwise, everything is so full of blindness and madness that I barely dare to open my mouth.

34. I hear the mockers who come along, but I care little for their insults; I know what kind of spirits they are. I wish they had my knowledge, for then they would stop their jeering.

35. Regarding the copying <term>original: "Transcribing"</term> of my writings which I am to send, I cannot say whether they can be safely done by *N.*, for he cannot keep a secret. I often hear foolish, mocking men speak of my writings, which I assume comes from him and cannot believe otherwise; for he is only a man of the world, born entirely from the school of this world. We would have little loyalty or security with him.

36. We should not at first cast pearls (since they are precious) in the way, but wait for another time until they are more common, lest the *Oppressor* <note>likely referring to religious authorities or the Catholic Church, whom Boehme often viewed as suppressors of spiritual truth</note> destroy them.

37. It may well be given to him to transcribe, but not the *first* time. Only after it has already been copied once should he have it, so that the *Oppressor* may not be able to destroy the original.

<margin>
* *Frederick V, Elector Palatine.* <note>The "Winter King" of Bohemia whose brief reign was a pivotal moment in the early Thirty Years' War.</note>
</margin>
38. Regarding your desire to know about the events in *Prague*, where I was present for the arrival of the new * *King* (you have understood that the news has already reached *Sagan* <note>a town in Silesia</note>): he arrived at the fortress at *Hradčany* <note>original: "Retshin"; referring to the Hradschin district/Prague Castle</note> from *Slaný* <note>original: "Shlan"</note> and was received by all *three estates* <note>the nobles, knights, and representatives of the royal cities</note> with great ceremony, as has been the custom for all kings in the past.

<margin>
† *Confused Christendom.* <note>Boehme frequently uses "Babel" to symbolize the confusion and corruption of the institutional Church.</note>
* *Gabriel Bethlen.* <note>original: "Bethlem Ga-bor"; the Prince of Transylvania and a leader of anti-Habsburg forces.</note>
</margin>
39. I urge you to pay close attention to what the prophet *Ezekiel* has written in chapters 38 and 39: whether the time of the great battle is not at hand upon the *mountains of Israel* in † *Babylon*, especially regarding the * *Transylvanian* <note>original: "Sevenberger"; referring to Siebenbürgen or Transylvania</note> who is expected to get help from

<meta>This page continues Jacob Boehme’s correspondence regarding his prophetic visions and the logistical challenges of sharing his prohibited writings during a time of great European political upheaval.</meta>

<page-num>135</page-num>
<header>The Fifteenth Epistle.</header>

...the Turks and very easily reach the River Rhine.

40. At that time, the great slaughter of the children of Babel <note>Babel: Boehme’s term for the corrupt and divided state of institutional Christianity and society.</note> may come to pass. Two great rods of God shall appear: one by war and the other by mortality <note>original: "Mortality"; likely referring to the plague or widespread disease</note>, through which Babel shall be ruined. This is shown by the Spirit of the Lord in all those who have prophesied before us.

41. I believe the election of a true German Emperor must be deferred for a little while longer. In the meantime, great war and contention, as well as the desolation of many cities, strongholds, and powerful countries, shall follow. Even now is the exact time of which the Spirit prophesies, though we do not understand it with perfect precision.

<gloss>The Astral Spirit, or the apprehension of reason.</gloss>
42. For a thousand years before God is as a single day. The Spirit sees all things as if they were close at hand, and then the sidereal man <term>original: "sidereall man"; the part of human nature governed by the stars or worldly reason</term> supposes that it will happen instantly; yet it remains within God’s own counsel.

<margin>☞</margin>
43. However, we know for certain that the ruin of the City of Babel is very near, and it appears to us as if the time were even now at hand. Yet we cannot fully grasp the counsel of God—just as a traveler who stays only a day in a country cannot learn everything about it, so it is with us.

44. For God keeps the specific time and hour to himself, and yet he shows the wonders that are to come through his Spirit.

<gloss>The *Aurora* being then in the hands of the Common Council. <note>original: "The Aurora"; Boehme’s first book, which had been confiscated by the city council of Görlitz.</note></gloss>
45. I want you to know that H. N. has sought to copy out the book I desired, and he seeks to get the correct original of the first one. As I understand it, this will be accomplished; it may be most conveniently delivered by N. N.

<gloss>
* The Worldly.
† The Spiritual.
* Confused Christendom.
</gloss>
46. However it may be, the new Antichrist <note>original: "new Antichrist"; likely referring to contemporary religious/political figures Boehme saw as corrupt</note> triumphs nightly in the growth of the old, and burns like a fire in juniper wood <note>Juniper wood was known for burning with intense heat and long-lasting embers.</note>. He assumes it is a time of joy and a "golden age," but it is actually a time of misery, and Babel is caught in a flaming fire.

47. Because I am in a hurry at present, I have no time to write to you more extensively. Nothing more has been started yet, but I hope to begin soon, as the driving impulse of my mind continually shows me. I shall faithfully send it to you at the appointed place.

48. And I faithfully commend you to the meekness of Jesus Christ.

->Dated, Thursday after St. Martin’s Day,<-
->**1 6 1 9.**<-

<page-num>136</page-num>
<header>The Sixteenth Epistle.</header>

# THE
# SIXTEENTH
# EPISTLE.

->*The open Fountain in the heart of Jesus*<-
->*Christ be our refreshment.*<-

<margin>* Text: The Fountain of Wisdom <note>original: "Fons Sapientiæ"</note></margin>

1. Noble, honored, and much-respected Sir: With the salutation and kind wish of Divine love and the fullness of joy in our *Immanuel* <note>Immanuel: Hebrew for "God with us," a title for Jesus Christ.</note>, in his wonderfully sweet power, and with all temporal <term>temporal: relating to worldly life and physical health as opposed to spiritual or eternal matters</term> prosperity of body being offered as my preliminary wish; I desire to let you know that I have been informed how you are a well-wisher to the *Fountain of Wisdom* and do make use of some of my writings. I also understand that you bear a great desire for the well-spring of Christ and that noble *Wisdom*, which has moved me to write to you, seeing that you have perused some of my works.

2. However, there are some to be found who—out of envy, misapprehension, and misunderstanding—babble and storm against them. This can be seen in the attached pamphlet, where that poor, proud, foolish man vapors <note>vapors: to talk boastfully or emit empty, nonsensical words</note> and storms, and yet has not the least understanding of the source from which my writings flow.

3. Indeed, he puts a false and most strange sense and meaning upon them only so that he might confirm his own miserable opinion. For he has spread abroad some writings concerning *God’s election* <note>election: the theological doctrine regarding how God chooses individuals for salvation.</note> of us, and by doing so, he thinks he can entangle and trap <note>original: "snarl"; to twist or entangle</note> us in despair, thereby opening a gate to all lewdness and wantonness.

4. For this reason, he does not like the taste of the open fountain of Christ in my writings. Consequently, I have written a short declaration regarding his attached pamphlet and have given it only briefly for the consideration of those who read my book, since the foundation of these ideas is found sufficiently and satisfactorily elsewhere in my writings. I have done this so that people may see how this carping pamphleteer <term>carping pamphleteer: a writer of short tracts who complains or finds fault in a petty or malicious way</term> thinks he can beguile and rob us of the chiefest treasure, upon which our eternal salvation—

<meta>Catchword: and</meta>

<summary>Boehme addresses a noble supporter, expressing gratitude for his interest in the "Fountain of Wisdom" while defending his work against a critic's pamphlet. He argues that his opponent misunderstands his writings and misuses the doctrine of election to cause despair.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Immanuel, Divine Wisdom, Election, Predestination, religious controversy, polemics, spiritual fountain</keywords>

--- Page 155 ---
<meta>On this page, Jacob Boehme continues his defense against a critic’s attack on his doctrine of divine election. He explains that his insights come from a direct spiritual experience rather than academic study, and he encourages his supporter to remain firm in this "new" understanding.</meta>

<page-num>137</page-num>
<header>The Sixteenth Epistle.</header>
<sig>T</sig>

and happiness depends, and that with cunning words by alleging and quoting Scripture. Just as a toad sucks poison out of honey, he <margin>* Text: Pulleth it about by the hair <note>original: "puls it by the ears"</note></margin> *perverts* the Scripture. This is seen in his description of the Virgin Mary and the promised seed of the woman, and how he falsifies and embitters the Scripture upon which he builds his doctrine of *Election* <note>Election: the theological doctrine regarding how God chooses individuals for salvation</note>.

5. Because of this, I am deeply grieved in my heart that the man is so burdened and possessed by such an opinion. This burden is very heavy, and he cannot get rid of it unless he learns to understand the *Center* of all *Beings*—the source from which good and evil arise, what God's love and anger are—and learns to understand the *Three Principles* <note>Three Principles: Boehme’s foundational philosophy describing the three "worlds" or states of being: the dark fire-world, the light world of God's love, and the visible world of nature</note>. Otherwise, he will not be freed from such opinions.

6. Although I am not surprised that my writings seem strange to him; for there is something new in them that surpasses the reach of reason. They have a different sense and a different understanding than his, springing from another root. I have not gathered them together from the *Letter* <note>the Letter: referring to literal or academic study of scripture without spiritual inspiration</note>, nor did I learn them from other men’s writings. I was an ignorant child in that respect, as laypeople usually are; I knew nothing of such things. I did not even seek them in that way. I sought only the heart and open well-spring of *Jesus Christ*, to hide myself from the *Tempest* of God’s anger and from the opposition of the Devil, so that I might find a guide and leader to rule and direct me in my life.

7. But when this pressed so hard upon me—and my mind struggled so strongly in the combat against sin and death and toward the mercy of God—I was resolved to part with my life rather than give in or stop. At that moment, a *Garland* was set upon me, which I hope to enjoy and rejoice in forever. I have no pen sufficient to describe it, much less can I express it with my mouth. From that experience my knowledge came, as well as the desire to write it down, solely for my own memory. I intended to keep it by me until the end of my days; how it happened <insert>[that it was published]</insert>, you know very well, Sir, through Mr. N. But God's providence and permission in this were such that you and your brother were called as the *Firstlings* <note>Firstlings: the first ones to receive or support the work</note> to it, and by your means, it was spread abroad.

<note>A pointing hand (manicule) appears in the margin here for emphasis.</note>
8. Therefore, I exhort and entreat you, for the sake of your eternal salvation, to heed and mind well the *Pearl* with which God favors us. For a time will come when it shall be sought after and greatly accepted. Let no stormy gusts of opinion drive you to and fro, but look upon it correctly. Pray to God the Most High that He would be pleased to open the door of knowledge; without this, no one will understand my writings. For they surpass and transcend *Astral Reason* <term>Astral Reason: Boehme's term for natural human intellect influenced by the physical stars and the material world, as opposed to divine revelation</term>; they grasp and comprehend the *Divine Birth*. Therefore...

<meta>catchword: fore</meta>

<summary>Boehme claims his spiritual knowledge comes from a direct "garland" of divine revelation rather than books, and he warns that human reason alone cannot understand his works. He credits his correspondent and his brother as the first supporters responsible for publishing and spreading these insights.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, divine election, Three Principles, spiritual experience, revelation vs. reason, Astral Reason, Firstlings, the Pearl</keywords>

--- Page 156 ---
<meta>On this page, Jacob Boehme concludes his letter by emphasizing that spiritual truth cannot be reached through human reason alone, but only through divine illumination and a humble, childlike heart. He also reflects on the early, unrefined nature of his first book, *Aurora*.</meta>

<page-num>138</page-num>
<header>The Sixteenth Epistle.</header>

Therefore, there must also be the same Spirit to understand them correctly; no speculation [or sharp intellect or concept of reason] reaches them unless the mind is illuminated by God. The way to find this is faithfully shown to the seeking reader.

9. I speak in good truth and sincerity before God and man, and I also appeal to God's judgment and declare that there is no good at all in any *disputing* without God's light and Spirit. Furthermore, nothing that is permanent, constant, undoubtedly grounded, or pleasing to God can arise from it.

<margin>☛</margin>
10. Therefore, whoever wishes to learn the right and true way to God fundamentally, let him depart from and forsake his own reason and enter into a penitent, humble life, resigned to God like a child <note>original: "filiall life"</note>. In this way, he shall obtain heavenly power and skill and shall put on Christ’s childlike spirit, which shall lead him into all truth. Otherwise, there is no true way to God but this one alone.

<margin>*The Pearl of Sophia <note>Sophia: The Greek word for Wisdom, personified by Boehme as the Divine Bride of the soul</note>. The Divine illumination.</margin>
11. If it progresses so far that the *Virgin's Garland* is placed upon him, he will no longer need to say, "*Teach me,*" and so on. For it is written, *They shall all be taught by God*. Otherwise, I have no knowledge, skill, or understanding. In my writings, I have been like a young scholar going to school, or like a passing rain shower—whatever it lands on, it hits. This is how my understanding has been, even to this day.

12. The book **Aurora** was my first "childish" beginning. I also wrote it contrary to the expectations of human reason, according only to the appearance of light. I understood it very well in a magical [symbolic, mystical, or metaphorical] manner, but it was not sufficiently explained. It needed a more detailed description and exposition. I intended to have kept it for myself, but it was taken from me against my will and published, as you, Sir, know. I commend myself to your favor, and all of us into the gentle love of Jesus Christ.

->Dated the third of July, in the Year of our Lord<-
->1621.<-

<page-num>139</page-num>
<header>The Seventeenth Epistle.</header>
<sig>T 2</sig>

# THE
# SEVENTEENTH
## EPISTLE.

***Worthy and much-respected Noble Sir,***

1. After my hearty greetings and my wish for Divine love and the fullness of joy in our **IMMANUEL** <note>original: Hebrew for "God with us"</note>, in His wondrously sweet power, along with all bodily and temporal welfare: I want to kindly let you know that I have fully considered the discussion we recently held. Since I perceive you to be a *zealous lover of the truth and Divine Mysteries*, I did not want to miss the chance to visit you with this *Epistle* <note>original: Greek *epistolē*, meaning a letter</note>. An opportunity has been given to me to provide an answer regarding the article <note>original: "Article," referring here to a specific point of doctrine</note> of a person who opposed me on the topic of God’s *Free Grace* and *Election* <term>Election: The theological concept of God choosing specific individuals for salvation</term>. I have sent this, my answer, for you to read through.

2. However, since the mind cannot rest satisfied with so little, I am ready and willing—if it is desired—to write a whole book to enlarge and unfold this topic from its very Center. I would do this so that the heart might rest *satisfied* with it; though I should suppose that a Christian might find enough in this little writing to be satisfied regarding this and other articles of faith.

<margin>P</margin>
3. But seeing as this article of "Election" has nevertheless perplexed many men—and because of it, such opinions are stated and concluded which open a gate for all iniquity <note>original: "iniquity," meaning sin or wickedness</note> into the world—I am therefore grieved by it. It has been given to me to know from the Most High that this article has *not as yet* been understood from its very foundation. I wish from my heart that it might be *understood*, so that we might not look so strangely at one another like "Men-Devils," but rather as loving brothers and the innate, dearly purchased children of Christ. We should walk in a right, true love toward one another; this can never be done while holding the notion and conjecture that God *chooses one person and not another*.

4. But if I look upon my brother as my [ own ] flesh and spirit...

<summary>Boehme begins a letter to a nobleman regarding the controversial doctrine of "Election," arguing that the current misunderstanding of how God chooses people for salvation leads to social discord and "iniquity." He offers to write a more detailed explanation of how God's grace truly functions from a spiritual "Center."</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Election, Free Grace, Immanuel, Divine Mysteries, brotherly love, theology, predestination</keywords>

--- Page 158 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's explanation of why he is writing about the doctrine of election. He argues that true spiritual understanding leads to love for one's brother and the discovery of God within oneself, rather than contentious debating.</meta>

<page-num>140</page-num>
<header>The Seventeenth Epistle.</header>

spirit, then it may be truly accomplished. Both the Scripture and the origin of mankind powerfully testify to this and convince us of it. Even more, my conscience in the spirit of the Lord convinces me that I ought to love my brother as my own life, or as my God. Would God command me to love someone who is a damned devil? No, but someone who is a member of my own body.

<insert>☞</insert> <note>This pointing hand, or manicule, was a common printer's mark used to draw the reader's attention to an important passage.</note>
5. Therefore, for this reason, I have taken the opportunity to write to you and Christianly entreat and advise you to better consider this article of faith. In that consideration, do not allow anything at all to possess your mind or take root in you except the pleasant Name of *Jesus*. He has come into this world and manifested Himself in our humanity to seek and save us poor lost people who were dead to the Kingdom of God, and to restore again what was lost in *Adam*.

6. I do not write this to act as a master over you, but in a brotherly manner for a mutual exploration and shared refreshment with you. I hope that our faith and confidence toward God may be strengthened in the Lord, for we are all only human. We should rightly behave toward one another as fellow members of one body, both in doctrine and in life; for he who finds his brother in the spirit of Christ finds *himself*.

<margin>
* Or, Understanding.
</margin>
7. Much arguing is not at all profitable; it only creates confusion. Follow me in my writings to the *Center of all Beings*, and you shall see the * origin <note>original: "originall"</note> of good and evil and be freed from all this error. You will find so much in my writings that will give real satisfaction to the mind. As soon as the center of all beings is understood, such joy arises in the mind that it surpasses all the joy of this world. 

<margin>
* Or, The Philosophers' Stone.
</margin>
For the noble and precious *stone of the wise men* <note>original: "stone of the wise men," referring to the Philosophers' Stone, a legendary alchemical substance capable of turning base metals into gold, used here as a metaphor for spiritual perfection and the knowledge of God</note> lies within it. He who finds it considers it of higher excellence than the outward world with all its glory. Should it not be a joy to find and *know God*, so that a person is able to see and find *all things* within themselves—even things scarcely explained in a thousand books—and to know them truly in everything? Why should I contend and wrangle about religion if it is manifest in my heart, so that I am able to truly behold all things in their root and origin?

8. I do not say this to boast of myself—for I am as *nothing*, and God is *all in me*—but so that if anyone had a mind to seek, they might also seek and obtain. Although I did not seek it in that way, nor did I understand it—indeed, I knew nothing of it—I only sought the pleasant, loving heart of *Jesus Christ* to hide myself there from the wrathful anger of God and the enemy, the Devil. But then, more was revealed to me than I understood or sought after. From that revelation, I have written, never thinking I would become known among such high-ranking people.

<meta>catchword: 9. For</meta>

<summary>Boehme encourages his reader to seek the "Center of all Beings" within themselves rather than engaging in religious disputes, claiming that finding the spirit of Christ leads to universal knowledge and joy.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, mysticism, Philosophers' Stone, Center of all Beings, brotherly love, spiritual revelation, internal knowledge</keywords>

--- Page 159 ---
<meta>This page concludes Boehme's introductory remarks in the Seventeenth Epistle. He discusses the humble origins of his writings, the necessity of divine illumination to understand them, and the importance of active spiritual seeking over passive waiting for divine "election."</meta>

<page-num>141</page-num>
<header>The Seventeenth Epistle.</header>

## The Seventeenth Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin>9. For I thought that I wrote only for myself, and intended to have kept it by me even until my end. Yet now it is revealed and has come into many men's hands without my knowledge or effort. Therefore, I am urged to ask you and others, and to remind you not to look at the simplicity or lowliness <note>original: "meannesse"</note> of the author, or to be offended by my person. For it pleases the Most High to reveal His counsel through humble people <note>original: "foolish people," a reference to 1 Corinthians 1:27 where God chooses the "foolish things of the world" to shame the wise</note> who are considered as nothing by the world, so that it may be known and acknowledged that it comes from His hand.

10. Therefore, if my writings come into your hands, I would like you to look upon them as the work of a child in whom the Most High has carried out His work. For there are things hidden <note>original: "couched"</note> within them that no human reason may understand or grasp. Instead, they are childlike, plain, and very easy for the enlightened <term>illuminate</term> to understand. These things will not be grasped by human reason unless that reason is ignited with God's light; without that, there is no finding. This is what I wish to remind you of, and all those who read them.

<margin>☞</margin>11. Christ said, *Seek and you shall find; knock and it shall be opened to you: my Father will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask Him for it.* Within this lies the hidden pearl <note>A reference to the "pearl of great price" from Matthew 13:45–46, representing spiritual truth</note>; whoever wishes to have it must obtain it in this way, otherwise there is no finding it—except for a partial, flawed, and blind knowledge, like a deceptive shadow of the imagination <note>original: "fanfie" (fancy)</note> or a hollow sound <note>original: "painted sound"</note>. In the pearl, there is a living knowledge, where a person never needs to ask, "Is it true?" For it is written, *They shall be taught of God.* Also, *We will come to you and make our home in you.* Also, *He who does not have the Spirit of Christ is none of His.* And therefore Christ says, *Seek first the Kingdom of God and His righteousness, and then all other things shall be added to you.* He commands us to strive and seek after it—not to sit still and wait for election <term>Election</term>—but to come to Him, as in Matthew 10, and to labor in His vineyard; we should not wait and expect to be pushed <note>original: "driving"</note>, but should come willingly.

12. Since I have found a wise heart in you, I am more emboldened to write to you, hoping that you will judge wisely. Now, if anything in my writings should seem dark, obscure, or difficult to understand, please write it down and send it to me as the opportunity arises; I will make it more childlike, plain, and clear. I commend you, your family, and myself with them, in brotherly union, into the gentle and tender love of Jesus Christ.

---

->**Dated July 3, 1621.**<-

He invites the recipient to ask for clarification on any obscure points in his mystical writings.</summary>

<page-num>148</page-num>
<header>The Eighteenth Epistle.</header>

# THE
---
## EIGHTEENTH
### EPISTLE.
---

<margin>1.</margin> **NOBLE** Sir, after wishing you the Divine love and fullness of joy in our *Immanuel* <note>original: "Immanuel"; a Hebrew name meaning "God with us," referring to Christ</note>, in his wondrous sweet power, and all bodily and temporal prosperity; I kindly certify to you, as opportunity now allows me, that I have considered the *conference* <note>original: "Conference"; a meeting or discussion</note> we had lately. Since I have observed yourself and others there present in high Divine *zeal*, as lovers of God and his truth who desire to search with earnestness the mystery and ground of all beings and to come into the light, I would not omit writing to you to remind you all of it. In doing so, I hope to give further *encouragement* <note>original: "advantage"</note> to such *zealous* seeking and declare how the Pearl <note>The "pearl of great price" (Matthew 13:46), symbolizing spiritual wisdom or the kingdom of heaven</note> is to be sought and found.

2. For I am also among the *seekers*, and it is my chief responsibility not to hide or bury that which God has entrusted to me, but to set it forth so that God's will might be known in us. His kingdom might then come and be manifest in our *seeking* and *desire*, and we might be found as children of the Most High toward one another, acknowledging one another as *members* and brothers, and not as strangers and *outcasts*. We should not act as "devil-men" toward one another, as the article of *election* <term>Election</term> <note>The theological doctrine of predestination—the idea that God chooses specific individuals for salvation while others are damned</note> (as it has been handled by some until now) implies and suggests.

3. And although we are caught and captured in the heavy fall of *Adam* in the *Anger*—so that his Anger has indeed *chosen* us to be children of *damnation*—yet God has bestowed his beloved heart, *namely, the center of the Deity*, upon it. He has manifested it in humanity so that he might again regenerate us in him and manifest the life again *in us*.

<margin>☞ 4.</margin> And just as the heavy fall came from one man upon all, and passed [or pressed] upon all, so likewise the grace came from one man and passed upon all. And the Apostle <note>Referring to St. Paul in Romans 5:18</note> says that *Jesus Christ came into the world* to... <meta>The sentence continues on the next page with the catchword "to".</meta>

<summary>In this opening to the Eighteenth Epistle, Boehme addresses a noble acquaintance to encourage his spiritual journey, arguing that God's grace is offered to all humanity as a remedy for the fall of Adam, contrary to stricter interpretations of predestination.</summary>
<keywords>Immanuel, spiritual seeking, the Pearl, Election, Predestination, Adam's Fall, Regeneration, Center of the Deity</keywords>

--- Page 161 ---
<meta>This page continues the Eighteenth Epistle. Boehme is discussing the nature of divine election and the accessibility of God's grace to those who feel "lost" or "damned," arguing that God's calls to repentance imply a genuine possibility for the sinner to turn back.</meta>

<page-num>243</page-num>
<header>The Eighteenth Epistle.</header>

...to seek and save that which is lost—that is, the poor, lost, damned sinner, seized by the wrath of God and seemingly chosen for damnation. He did not come for the righteous, who, like Abel, Seth, Enoch, Noah, Shem, Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob, are already encompassed by His Love. Instead, He came for the poor sinful person held captive by God’s anger, such as Cain, Ishmael, Esau, and those like them. [He came] to seek and call these people to return and be converted. As God said to Cain, "Rule over sin; do not let it have its power over you." If Cain had been unable to do this, God would not have commanded it of him. Likewise, if it had not been possible for Adam to remain firm, God would never have forbidden him the tree.

5. However, since humanity cannot completely conclude, demonstrate, or satisfy the mind in this way—for the mind searches even deeper for God’s Omnipotence—another **study** is required. A person must learn to truly know the center of all essences, both love and anger: what the eternal love of God is, and what the eternal anger of God is (which hardens and devours man and makes him a child of eternal death). One must also learn how a person is able, during this life, to be freed from this prison and captivity [of God’s anger].

6. Since I have declared and explained this so extensively in my books, I thought the mind would be satisfied—especially in the *Book of the Threefold Life* and in the *Three Books of the Incarnation of Jesus Christ*, and even more deeply in the *Book of the Six Points concerning the Grand Mystery* <note>original: "Grand Mystery"; referring to the *Mysterium Magnum*, a central concept in Boehme's work regarding the manifestation of the universe from God</note>. There, I discuss the eternal birth of the Deity and the three principles of the three worlds: how they exist within one another as one, how there is an eternal peace and agreement between them, how one gives birth to the other and desires the other, and how any one of them without the others would be nothing. I assumed the mind would find satisfaction there, since these truths can be demonstrated in every being and every thing.

7. Since Master *N.* has some of these writings in his possession (though not all), please inquire about them. If you have an interest in them, you will find not only the foundation of this topic concerning *Election* <term>Election</term> <note>The theological doctrine of predestination—the idea that God chooses certain individuals for salvation while others are left to damnation.</note>, but the foundation of all topics—even everything the human mind turns toward—if the foundation revealed there is followed and reached.

<margin>10</margin> 8. My noble heart, I pray that you do not take lightly what God reveals to us out of His Love. Do not look down on the simplicity of the people through whom He does this; it is His good pleasure to manifest His power through the weak and those the world considers foolish. This is done to instruct the world, for all people live in conflict and strife and will not allow His Spirit to *draw them* toward Him, so that they might know and acknowledge God’s Kingdom to be within **US**. Therefore the

<meta>catchword: Centre</meta>

<summary>In this section, Boehme argues that God's call to biblical "outcasts" like Cain proves that salvation is possible for all. He points the reader toward his more detailed philosophical works to understand the underlying "Grand Mystery" of God's love and anger.</summary>

<keywords>Election, Predestination, Cain and Abel, Jacob Boehme, Threefold Life, Incarnation, Grand Mystery, Divine Wrath, Free Will, Spiritual Knowledge</keywords>

--- Page 162 ---
<meta>This page continues the Eighteenth Epistle. Boehme continues his argument from the previous page, shifting from the theological necessity of studying God’s love and anger to the personal, spiritual disposition required to receive divine revelation.</meta>

<page-num>144</page-num>
<header>The Eighteenth Epistle.</header>

The center of His Being, and of all beings, is revealed to them. This is done entirely out of His love toward us, so that we might yet turn away from miserable strife, conflict, and arguing, and enter into a brotherly and childlike love.

9. Sir, since I have found a longing mind in you, I would not hide from you that this will be a time of serious gravity. I say, blessed and happy are those who are included under the *sound* of the *trumpet* that has already sounded. For after this comes such a serious severity that *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; here referring to the confusion of conflicting religious sects and worldly corruption</note> and conflict—along with all pride, ambition, falsehood, and unrighteousness—shall drink a bitter draught, even the very cup she has filled. I urge you, for the sake of *eternal salvation*, to consider this further; it is known.

<margin>☛</margin>
10. I am ready—if your mind does not find enough ground in my writings for its rest and satisfaction (provided you note down your questions and send them to me)—to explain and expand upon it from the *center* of all beings, so that I hope your mind would be satisfied. However, this does not depend on intellectual searching, for no searching obtains or reaches the *pearl* <note>original: "Pearle"; a reference to the "Pearl of Great Price" mentioned in Matthew 13:45–46, representing divine wisdom or salvation</note> without God's light. A penitent, humble mind is required for this—one that completely surrenders itself to the grace of God and does not search for or desire anything except God’s love and mercy. In that state, the *bright morning star* <note>original: "bright morning Star"; a symbol for Christ and spiritual illumination</note> finally rises, and the mind finds that *pearl* in which soul and body rejoice. Once this is found, there is no need for further searching or teaching, for it is written: *They shall be taught by God.* The sound of the *seventh trumpet* <note>original: "seventh Trumpet"; in the Book of Revelation, the sounding of the seventh trumpet signals the completion of God's mystery and His ultimate reign</note> opens such a *pearl* in the hearts of many who seek it with earnestness in a humble will surrendered to God.

11. Therefore, my noble friend, I would not hide this from you: much disputing and rumination <note>original: "grubling"; to grope, dig, or search obsessively</note> in *self-reason* <note>original: "Selfe-Reason"; human intellect and logic operating independently of divine grace</note> does not find the pearl. Instead, an earnest, fixed, penitent will finds it—which is more precious and valuable than the world. He who finds it would not trade it for the riches of the whole world, for it provides him with *temporal* and *eternal* joy. It enables him to be joyful and happy even in the midst of a dungeon of darkness, and he considers the goods of this world as *dirt* and *dung* in comparison to it. *Christ* said: *Seek, and you shall find; knock, and it shall be opened unto you.* Also: *My Father will give the Holy Spirit to those who ask Him for it.* THIS IS THE ESSENTIAL FOUNDATION.

12. Let no one say, "My heart is shut up; I cannot pray." Even if my heart says "no" flatly, I will still throw myself into the suffering and

<summary>Boehme advises his correspondent that true spiritual wisdom (the "Pearl") is attained through humility and surrender to God rather than through intellectual searching or religious debate. He warns of a coming divine judgment on pride and religious confusion (Babel) while offering to personally answer further questions.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual illumination, humility, Pearl of Great Price, self-reason, Seventh Trumpet, Babel, repentance, divine grace</keywords>

--- Page 163 ---
<meta>This page concludes the Eighteenth Epistle. Boehme emphasizes total surrender to Christ and refers the reader to his book, *The Threefold Life*, for further instruction on finding divine wisdom.</meta>

<page-num>145</page-num>
<header>The Eighteenth Epistle.</header>

death of *Christ*; let him cast me into Heaven or Hell, yet I will be in his death; he is made an *eternal life* to me. And then, it is said, "*None can pluck my sheep out of my hands*" <note>original: "None can pluck my Sheep out of my hands"; a reference to John 10:28, signifying the spiritual security of the believer.</note>.

13. The way to seek and know the precious *pearl* <note>original: "Pearle"; referring to the "Pearl of Great Price" from Matthew 13:45–46, a central metaphor in Boehme's work for divine wisdom and the inner light of God.</note> is quite sufficiently explained in the book *The Threefold Life* <note>original: "Threefold life"; referring to Boehme’s treatise *The Threefold Life of Man* (1620), which describes the three realms of existence: the dark fire-world, the light holy-world, and the outer visible world.</note>; otherwise, I would have mentioned something about it here. I commend myself to your favor, and all of us into the meek love of *Jesus Christ*.

---

->**Dated, July 2, 1621.**<-
