<meta>This page begins the Nineteenth Epistle. Jacob Boehme writes to a noble recipient, offering New Year's greetings and expressing joy over the recipient's spiritual progress and hunger for divine wisdom.</meta>

<page-num>146</page-num>
<header>The Nineteenth Epistle.</header>

---

## THE
# NINETEENTH
## EPISTLE.

---

<margin>1.</margin>
**NOBLE**, worthy, and honored sir: I offer you all sincere wishes for a happy and joyful new year in God, along with all bodily well-being, through the love of Christ with which he loves us in himself by his *incarnation within us*.

2. I am glad for your physical health, and yet I am much more glad to observe how the *drawing of the Father* <note>original: "drawing of the Father"; a reference to John 6:44, "No one can come to me unless the Father who sent me draws them."</note> in the spirit of Christ continually stirs up and works in you a constant hunger for the precious pearl of divine knowledge. Because this happens in the same tree and growth in which I also sprout and grow, it brings to me—as from a fellow branch in our angelic, paradisiacal choir <note>original: "Corall"; likely meaning "choir" (Latin *chorale*) or a communal gathering of the blessed.</note>—pure desire and a loving, receptive will. It rejoices me in my meditation that the spirit of Christ still has his *church* and *temple* in the midst of the thorns, as it now appears. I wish from my heart, with panting desires, that it might flourish and grow even stronger, so that *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; referring to the biblical Tower of Babel, used by Boehme to represent the confusion, conflict, and "verbal wrestling" of institutionalized religion.</note> and the kingdom of contention and strife might be abolished and taken away, that we might converse and walk together in love and union as the children of Christ.

3. Seeing that you read some of my writings, I would be glad with all my heart if they were understood according to my own comprehension and intention—not for any temporary praise or glory for me (which belongs to Christ alone and is not *mine*), but for the sake of our eternal fellowship and brotherhood which we shall have together after this life.

4. I would very heartily share my *pearl* <note>original: "Pearl"; his spiritual insight or "The Pearl of Great Price" from Matthew 13:45–46.</note> which God has given me with my loving brothers, so that they, along with me, might bring their fruits of divine knowledge and love to the table of God. This work and labor is more acceptable to me than all the temporary praise, honor, and goods of the world.

5. And though I am like a child void of understanding compared to you, yet my Savior has been pleased out of his love and grace to bestow *his sense, mind, and understanding* upon me and

<meta>catchword: to</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme greets a noble friend for the New Year, expressing joy that the recipient is seeking divine wisdom. He emphasizes his desire for spiritual unity and the abolition of religious "Babel" (confusion and strife) in favor of a shared "heavenly brotherhood."</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Nineteenth Epistle, New Year, spiritual growth, Pearl of Great Price, Babel, divine knowledge, brotherhood of Christ</keywords>

--- Page 165 ---
<meta>This page continues the Nineteenth Epistle (labeled "Eighteenth" in this edition's header, likely a printer's error as it follows the Nineteenth's introduction). Boehme is explaining his spiritual insights regarding the difficult theological topic of predestination.</meta>

<page-num>147</page-num>
<header>The Eighteenth Epistle.</header>

to open it through himself, so that I effectively know him and his will.

6. Which [although it seems like folly to human reason] is as clear as sunshine to me. It affords me joy and the desire that, in all temptations and afflictions from the Devil and his confederates, I am able boldly and confidently to hide myself within it. My hope is stirred up and ignited there with God’s love-fire, and I have, as it were, a beautiful *Garden of Roses* within it. I do not only wish for my brothers to partake of this, but I also desire and wish from my heart that the golden roses might also blossom in them.

7. I have understood that you are still worried and troubled regarding the *Article* <note>original: "Article"; a formal point of religious doctrine.</note> about the will of God and his *Election* <term>Election</term> <note>The theological doctrine that God chooses certain individuals for salvation before they are born.</note> of mankind. You are still caught in a deep notion regarding the *Decree* concerning man—as if God chose some according to his purpose, and some he did not choose, excluding them from his decree and purpose; and therefore he does not draw them in the spirit of Christ to the Father, or the Father does not draw them in Christ. For my part, this has very often perplexed me, and I wish unfeignedly that it might be understood how the fundamental ground of this is in its own nature.

8. For the words of the *Scripture* are right and true concerning *Election*, but they are not understood correctly. From this misunderstanding comes great evil and mischief, with contending and eager contests. When I go into the "center" <note>Boehme refers to the inner spiritual essence or the heart of divine reality.</note>, then I find the whole ground; there is nothing so subtle or profound, nor is there anything that can be asked about the will of God, that is not made as clear as the sun within that center.

<margin>☞</margin>
9. For I find the whole understanding of both good and evil, of God’s love and anger, and of both desires [namely, of the darkness and of the light]. These I place within the humanity of Christ—how God became man. I consider how the forms of human properties in the humanity of Christ were wholly and universally (without any exclusion) imbued <note>original: "tinctured"; an alchemical term meaning to infuse or transform the inner nature of a substance.</note> with the love of God in Christ, with the eternal word or voice of the deity (that is, with the *Divine Mercury* <term>Divine Mercury</term> <note>In alchemy, Mercury represents the mediating spirit or the "Word" that connects the physical and spiritual; Boehme uses it to describe the creative power of God's voice.</note>). This was done with Divine essence (namely, in the blood of Christ), and the wrath which was manifest in <term>Adam</term> <note>Representing the fallen state of humanity.</note> within the human property was wholly drowned and shut up in eternal death; of which the Scripture now declares: *Death, where is your victory? Grave, where is your sting?* <note>original: "Hell where is thy Victory? Death where is thy sting?"; a reference to 1 Corinthians 15:55.</note>

10. Just as the artist or philosopher <note>Meaning an alchemist or "natural philosopher."</note> changes *Saturn* and *Mars* into *Mercury*—which in *Saturn* and *Mars*, in their own fierce and wrathful <margin>* Or, Strength</margin> power, is an evil, poisonous source or quality—into a *Panacea* <term>Panacea</term> <note>A legendary universal medicine or "cure-all" sought by alchemists; here used as a metaphor for Christ's healing power.</note>, which is a paradisiacal source and property, where neither *Saturn*, *Mars*, nor *Mercury* are perceived in their wrathful properties,

<sig>V 2</sig>

<summary>Boehme addresses the recipient's confusion over the doctrine of Election (predestination), arguing that while Scripture is true, it is often misunderstood. He uses alchemical metaphors—like the transformation of base metals into a Panacea—to explain how Christ's love transforms human wrath and "drowns" the fallen state of Adam.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the Nineteenth Epistle, in which Jacob Boehme discusses the theological problem of predestination. He argues that God’s grace is available to all, emphasizing that the potential for divine light remains within every human soul, even the most corrupt.</meta>

<meta>catchword: in</meta>

<margin>☞</margin> ...properties; but out of their fierce, wrathful, and malignant property, there is an ascension of love and great joy. This is also how it is now with an evil person: when he turns away from his wrathful, malicious will and, in resignation, gives himself completely through the death of *Christ* into the *Panacea* <note>original: "Panacéa"; a universal remedy or cure-all, here used as a title for Christ as the healer of the soul.</note> (*Christ*).

11. And just as the sun in the sky shines upon both the good and the evil, so likewise the desire of the *Panacea Christ* (being the Divine sun that shines within) presents itself to all people. If they would only open their will, depart from their selfishness, and set their desire upon that light, *Christ* would be born within them.

12. For the soul (as it is purely in itself) was spoken or breathed into the human body out of the eternal *speaking* word of the Father. It came out of the world of fire and light as part of God’s own Being, and it has two free wills: one from the fire (that is, from the Father’s anger, which is the eternal nature in which the soul is a creature, composed of spiritual *Sulfur, Mercury, and Salt* <note>In Boehme's alchemy-influenced theology, these "Three Principles" represent the fundamental forces of existence.</note>), and one from the light of the Divine power in the Divine sound, in which the soul is an angel and an image of God. <note>The word "found" in the original "Divine found" is likely a printer's error for "sound" (Schall), common in Early Modern translations of Boehme.</note>

<margin>☞</margin> 13. And though the soul lost the light with *Adam*, yet *Christ* has regained it. He has again moved or awakened the center of love, so that the life of the light (if it stirs up its desire) may again ignite itself within the humanity of *Christ*. This grace passes from one person to all, just as anger passed from one man to all.

14. And though it might be said that he ignites whomever he pleases, I declare it as a precious truth worthy of acceptance: the Divine light is not "ingressive" <margin>☞</margin> [that is, a light coming into a person from the outside], but it is hidden even in the center of a wicked man. Just as God is hidden within time, the light is "arisive" [that is, a light springing up or opening itself from within], just as the light of a candle arises out of the candle itself.

15. Man is not so completely corrupt [lost or decayed] that there is no possibility at all left in him. Though he may be corrupt and spoiled, yet God (when he received and took pity on man) again stirred up and awakened the Center of his Love in human nature. This love is the true Deity which had hidden itself [or disappeared] during the sin [or fall of *Adam*].

<margin>☞</margin> 16. And as the sin and wrath of *Adam* (though he was but one) pressed upon and into everyone; so likewise the movement [or affection] of God’s love in *Christ's* humanity passed and pressed out from *Christ’s* humanity and through the collective humanity of all people.

17. *Christ* has again become the heart in the *Human Tree*. The Divine Sound [voice or word], which has effectively revealed itself in *Christ’s* humanity, now sounds through *Christ’s* humanity...

<summary>Boehme explains that the "Divine light" or "Panacea" of Christ is an internal potential within every person, arising from within rather than being imposed from without. He argues that Christ’s sacrifice restored the "center of love" to all humanity, counteracting the universal corruption inherited from Adam.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Christology, Universal Grace, Panacea, Three Principles, Alchemy, The Fall, Human Nature, Inner Light</keywords>

--- Page 167 ---
<meta>This page continues the Nineteenth Epistle, exploring Jacob Boehme’s theosophical view of the soul's will. Boehme uses alchemical and astrological symbolism—Mars, Mercury, and Saturn—to describe the internal spiritual struggle between divine love and natural wrath.</meta>

<page-num>149</page-num>
<header>The Nineteenth Epistle.</header>
<meta>catchword: rience</meta>

within the Universal Human Tree; and nothing is lacking, nor is anything in the way, except that the twig which is on the Tree refuses to draw the Sap of the Tree into itself.

18. It happens often that a person's nature is too much in love with *Mars* <note>In Boehme's system, *Mars* represents the fiery, aggressive, or wrathful property of the soul.</note> and draws that property into itself, stirring up a heat that withers the twig. Thus, the *Mars* of the soul draws wrath and falsehood into itself, by which its *Mercury* <note>*Mercury* represents the vital spirit, the power of movement and "expression" within the soul.</note> becomes poisonous. Then *Saturn* (which is the "impression" or the contractive force of the life's property) becomes altogether obscure and dark. As long as the *Mercury* of the life dwells in such a state, it cannot be drawn by the love of God, but only by the anger of God; it is chosen for damnation so long as it lives or continues in that "free evil" <term>free evil: the deliberate choice of a wicked path</term> wicked will.

19. The love of God offers itself to him, but he refuses to accept it. God desires him, but the wrath holds him back, as Christ said:
> *O Jerusalem, Jerusalem! How often would I have gathered your children together, as a hen gathers her chicks under her wings, but you were not willing!*

20. This unwillingness stands in the way; that man, in his life, allows himself to be kept or held by God's anger. This anger is the wrath within the out-spoken (or expressed) *Mercury*, according to the fiery property of the Father.

21. Dear Brother, here lies the "wicked child"; learn only to know what God Almighty is in both Love and Anger, and how Man is that very same Being—an image created out of the Eternal Spirit.

22. Do not say, "God wills the evil." He cannot will or desire anything that is evil according to the property in which He is called *God*. But if I were to call this property (meaning the property of Anger) "God," then I would be calling Hell "Heaven," darkness "light," and the Devil an Angel.

23. It is true that all things belong to God (or all is God's), yet God is only understood as "God" within the source (or working property) of the light's love. In His light, the anger is actually a cause of the "love-desire" and of the Kingdom of Joy.

24. When the soul brings its "fire-desire" out of its own self-will and into the love-desire of God—when it departs from its own selfishness <note>original: "self-nesse"; the state of being centered only on one's own ego or desires.</note> and sinks into the mercy and compassion of God, casting itself into the death of Christ—then it no longer wills the "fire-source" <note>original: "fire-source"; the root of natural energy which, when separated from God's light, becomes the fire of anger.</note>. Instead, it desires for its fire-life to be dead in the death of Christ. In that moment, the poison of the *Mercurial* life dies in the will of iniquity, and there arises a new twig and a budding of love-desire.

25. Loving Sir and Brother, know that I do not write as one who is blind or silent, without knowledge; I have found this myself through expe-
<meta>The text continues on the next page with the word "experience."</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains that while all people are branches of a "Universal Human Tree," individuals often wither by choosing the "fire" of anger (Mars) over divine love. He emphasizes that "God" properly refers to the light and love, while the "wrath" is a secondary property that can be overcome by surrendering the self-will to Christ.</summary>

<keywords>Universal Human Tree, Mars, Mercury, Saturn, free will, self-will, wrath vs. love, alchemical theology, Jacob Boehme, Jerusalem metaphor</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the Nineteenth Epistle, where Jacob Boehme expresses his desire for spiritual unity with the recipient. He invites the recipient to voice their objections in writing and hints at a "precious jewel" of knowledge—a spiritual or alchemical insight—that could provide healing and clarity in a world clouded by the confusion of "Babel."</meta>

<page-num>150</page-num>
<header>The Nineteenth Epistle.</header>

experience: I have been as deep in your opinion as you yourself; yet my Savior has opened my eyes, so that I see. I do not see by my own ability or power, but in *His*; as He knows me in *Himself*, and He will see; and I wish with all my heart that you might have an insight into my seeing, and that you might see with me out of my seeing. I would willingly impart my heart and love to you as your own property, and look through this glass out of you.

26. But I perceive that I am as yet unheard by you, and that I am not as yet known to you in the knowledge which is given to me; and I wish from my heart that it may yet [once] be so.

27. I entreat and exhort you as a Christian, in all humility, that you would only gather your *objections* together and send them to me in *writing*. I will deal according to my gifts, as a Christian ought, and I will declare and explain them in such a manner that I hope you shall acknowledge me as a brother in it. <margin>☜</margin>

28. <margin>☞</margin> Not that I presume to do it from myself, but my desire, which burns in me like fire, requires it of you; and I (as I am I) hope to God that we shall both so prosper in it that God will unite us in His love-desire and knowledge. It shall not prejudice <note>original: "prejudice" — here meaning to cause harm, embarrassment, or legal disadvantage</note> you at all with scorn or disgrace, for I have a heart that can keep a secret.

29. I exhort you in love to a filial humility in the true *Resignation of Christ*; <term>Resignation of Christ: The spiritual practice of "Gelassenheit," or surrendering one's own ego and will entirely to God's will.</term> therein only may you be able to obtain and comprehend it. Otherwise, my well-wishing, good intent, and beginning is to no purpose, for I can give you nothing but my good affection and charitable will. If you will accept of it, it is well! If not, I protest and testify before you and the face of God that I have begun my Christian duty <note>original: "devoir"</note> aright towards you and in you; I have done my part, as I am bound in *conscience* to do.

30. I may come to see you myself, if my affairs will permit, provided that it may lead to God's honor and man's salvation; for I know many thirsty souls thereabouts, with whom I might refresh myself, and they in me.

31. I have at present found a very precious *Jewel*, <note>In Boehme's writing, a "Jewel" or "Pearl" often refers to a profound spiritual revelation or the "Philosopher's Stone" interpreted as divine wisdom.</note> which might be profitable not only for the soul but for the body, and good for your *patients*.

32. If men would labor in Christ's vineyard, God might even now give us such a sunshine which might warm the apothecary's shop, <note>Boehme uses the "apothecary’s shop" as a metaphor for a place of healing; he suggests that spiritual wisdom acts as a medicine for the world's ills.</note> for which many honest people have been a long time desirous. This sunshine would boldly dispel the smoke in *Babel* <note>Babel: A term Boehme uses to describe the confused, divided, and corrupt state of the institutional churches and scholarly world.</note> and be a refreshment to the children of Christ in their oppression, misery, and tribulation.

33. But in truth, *because men will be so wicked and godless*,

<summary>Jacob Boehme invites his correspondent to engage in a humble written dialogue about their spiritual disagreements, offering his insights as a "precious jewel" that could benefit both soul and body. He laments the confusion of 'Babel' but hopes for a divine 'sunshine' to bring healing and unity to Christ's followers.</summary>

<page-num>151</page-num>
<header>The Nineteenth Epistle.</header>

there shall be a horrible storm of hail and rain, at which the Earth shall quake, and many thousand souls will be drowned in the water.

34. I would gladly mention something to you here, but at this time it cannot be; please take notice of the storm towards the *East*; the one towards the *North* is not far from it. In the *South*, there is a great smoke that causes the eyes of those in the *West* to smart.

35. Let no man say, when the storm passes by, that this man or that man is righteous before God, or that "it will go well with him because of his *religion*." The anger of God is kindled against *all*, and they are *all* alike to him regardless of their *religion*, as long as one lives just like the other.

<margin>☛</margin>
36. The Most High sweeps out one broom <note>original: "besom"</note> with another; but there springs up a *Lily* for all nations; happy are those who grasp it.

37. The thirsty soul must not say, "The Lord has forsaken me, he has forgotten me." God can no more forget than a mother can forget her child—and even if she should forget, yet the Lord has not forgotten his poor, exceedingly distressed, and afflicted *Christendom*. He has noted her in his wounds pierced with nails; *his light shall shine from the East to the West for a testimony to all people*.

<margin>Note</margin>
38. From the *South*, a *Lily* arises toward the *North*; he who obtains it as his own possession <note>original: "propriety"</note> shall sing the song of God’s mercy. At that time, the Word of the Lord will flourish like grass upon the Earth, and the nations will sing the song of *Babel* in one tune; for the beginning has found the end.

39. Think upon my dark <note>original: "darke" — meaning obscure, enigmatic, or prophetic</note> sayings, for at this time I could not be more plain; seeing that men have only sought after pride and covetousness and have despised the mirror of Anger. They have not repented but have worked iniquity upon iniquity, until iniquity devours itself and the wrath of God is fully satisfied.

40. *Human Reason* will do little to help here with its consultations; instead, it will blow up the fire and provide further cause for trouble.

41. God was good before the distress; but because men forsake God, scorn and misery follow.

<margin>☛</margin>
42. Let everyone take care of himself; but he who does not seek and preserve himself shall be sought and preserved. And so I commit you into the love of *Jesus Christ*.

<summary>Boehme uses prophetic symbolism to describe a coming "storm" of divine judgment upon all nations regardless of their outward religion, followed by a spiritual renewal symbolized by the "Lily." He warns that human reason cannot prevent this judgment, which results from man's own pride and abandonment of God.</summary>

<warning>Left margin is darkened and slightly obscured due to the binding of the volume; some text at the left edge of paragraphs is slightly clipped.</warning>

# THE
## TWENTIETH
### EPISTLE.

---

->Our salvation in the life of **Jesus Christ** in **us.**<-

---

<margin>1.</margin> Noble Sir, etc. All heartfelt <note>original: "Cordial"</note> wishes of Divine light in effective Divine working power, from the fountain of Love, *Jesus Christ*, and all temporal prosperity are sent to you first. I thought it good to visit you with a letter <note>original: "Epistle"</note> and, out of a faithful Christian intent and affection, to remind you of the *conference* <note>meaning: a formal discussion or debate</note> between *H. N.* and *D. S.* regarding the *Divine* purpose and will concerning *Man*.

<margin>2.</margin> *D. S.* was not sufficiently answered at that time regarding his question. This was because I was not practiced in their *scholastic* <note>original: "Scholastick"; refers to the formal, academic style of university theologians</note> way of discussing this *article* <note>meaning: a specific point of doctrine</note> when contemplating the internal ground through the outward ground. Also, such *meetings*, because of the *alcohol* <note>original: "Drink"</note> (to which I am unaccustomed), tend to darken and cloud subtle and pure understanding.

<margin>3. ☞</margin> Also, because of their *Latin* tongue (or scholastic expressions), I was hindered from comprehending the matter in its foundation <note>original: "ground"</note>. As a result, he began to triumph with his accepted *opinion*. Yet, he did so without a sufficient understanding of the quoted passages of *Scripture*, and also without a sufficient basis for *logical* conclusions in *reason*—in which, truth be told, he is excellently well-trained in their *scholastic* way.

<margin>4.</margin> After this conversation <note>original: "discourse"</note>, I turned my focus in *Divine* grace to the internal ground of *Divine Vision* <note>original: "Vision"</note> to test these ideas. I prayed to the Lord that he would open the understanding of the entire foundation to me, so that I might know it in its own specific *nature* <note>original: "Species"</note>.

<margin>5.</margin> Thereupon it appeared to me that I (through a *Divine* introduction into the wonderful works of God) have sufficient cause and

<summary>Jacob Boehme reflects on a theological debate where he felt disadvantaged by the scholars' use of Latin and academic jargon. He recounts how he later turned to inward prayer and received a divine revelation that clarified the truth of the matter.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, scholasticism, academic debate, Latin, Divine Vision, spiritual understanding, H. N., D. S., internal ground</keywords>

Following the discussion of his debate with academic scholars on the topic of the Divine will, Boehme here explains his motivation for writing his book on Predestination and outlines its deep philosophical and theological contents.</meta>

<page-num>157</page-num>
<header>The Twentieth Epistle.</header>

and grounded in it. Because of this, I felt a great desire to write down this **Foundation** <note>original: "Ground"</note> of the Divine will and eternal purposes regarding **Predestination**, and to put it into a book. This was later requested by Mr. **B. T.** and others, and I took the opportunity to do so.

6. My intent was not to despise anyone's opinion or to harm them with unchristian disgrace or biting taunts. Instead, I wrote out of a faithful, sincere Christian affection, sharing the **Talent** lent to me by God as a brother.

<margin>
Note:
What is contained in the Book of Election.
</margin>
7. This work is so deeply and profoundly founded that one may understand not only the basis of this question concerning God’s will, but also the hidden God as he is revealed in all visible things. It provides a very clear explanation of how the foundation of the Grand Mystery (which is the eternal, expressed Word of God, in which Wisdom has worked from eternity, and in which all things have been seen in a <term>magical</term> <note>In Boehme's terminology, "magical" refers to the spiritual power of the divine will and imagination to bring forth form from the void</note> manner or Idea, before they had any created existence) is to be understood.

8. It also explains how the same Grand Mystery brought itself—through the expression or speaking out of divine <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Science"; in this context, it refers to systematic divine knowledge or the Latin *scientia*</note>—through the Word of God (in the realm of this world) into a state of separation and comprehensibility for the Creation. It describes how the origin of good and evil is to be understood within the separation of Divine knowledge in the Grand Mystery and the Eternal Principles, leading to Divine manifestation and action.

9. In this work, the hidden God may be understood not only in his Being and will, but also through the entire foundation of his manifestation through his expressed Word, arising from the eternal powers of the Grand Mystery (the essence of eternity). It explains how this manifestation has entered a visible, understandable, created, and external existence, and what the *foundation of all mysteries is*, as well as how they are sufficiently revealed and made manifest.

<margin>
* The *Spirit of the World* <note>original Latin: *Spiritus Mundi*</note>
</margin>
10. Furthermore, it contains a broad explanation of the foundation of the * <term>cosmic</term> spirit <note>original: "Cosmick Spirit," referring to the animating force of the physical universe</note> in which the creation of this world lives. It also provides a very clear foundation of the internal, spiritual, angelic, and soulful life, as well as the rise, fall, and restoration of man. It covers the symbols <note>original: "Typifications"</note> of Scripture in the Old and New Testaments concerning the Kingdom of Nature and the Kingdom of Grace, what God’s righteousness and his election or purpose are, and how these are to be understood.

11. There is also a clear demonstration of the lineage within the Kingdom of Nature, from *Adam* through his children, and of the Kingdom of the manifestation of grace in the inspired "voice of grace" of the embodied Divine <term>knowledge</term> <note>original: "Science"</note>, found in the **Word of Love** within the womb of grace.

<page-num>154</page-num>

## The Twentieth Epistle.

<margin>* Or, perceptible or experiential.</margin>
12. And then I provide a clear explanation of the passages of Scripture, especially chapters 9, 10, and 11 of the **Epistle of Paul to the Romans**. On these, reason supports itself, and a full **perceptible foundation** <note>original: "Sensall ground"</note> is demonstrated through the quotation and examination of the Scriptures.

13. Yet I do not do this in a logical way, as it is treated in the **university schools** <note>original: "Schooles"; refers to the academic institutions of the time where scholastic theology was taught</note>. There, they only raise objections and contradictions against one another, contriving knotty arguments and dilemmas. One side will not prove or examine the foundation and meaning of the other in a perceptible way within the understanding; instead, they attack, pressure, judge, and condemn one another as heretics and revile each other. This is indeed nothing else but **Babel** <note>A biblical reference to the Tower of Babel, used here to represent religious confusion and pride</note>, a mother of proud, haughty, grand whoredom, of errors, and of confusion. In such a place, the Name of God is blasphemed, and the Holy Spirit is reviled and judged by human **Reason** based only on the literal Word.

14. I wholly refused to follow that path in using my **gift** <note>original: "Talent"</note>; for not one jot or tittle of the **Law** in the **Scripture** shall perish until all is fulfilled. The sayings of the Scripture, together with their symbolic types, must remain true and are not contradictory, as reason supposes.

15. Therefore, I have taken those sayings of Scripture which seem **contrary** to one another—such as when it is written, *“God wills that all men shall be saved,”* and then again, *“God hardens their hearts, so that they do not understand although they see it”*—and I have cleared and reconciled them together. I hope to God and his children that they will see the divine manifestation of grace and understand it, and depart from such strife and controversy about the **will of God** and the person of Christ, and learn to understand the **justification** of a poor sinner before God.

16. I have faithfully and diligently done this toward everyone according to my gifts, out of a Christian, brotherly heart. I further offer that if anyone should still be stuck in such a concept or opinion and were not satisfied in his thoughts, let him set down his opinion and his conclusions in writing in a Christian and friendly way and send them to me. He shall receive such an answer to his questions and objections that **he shall see that I meant well as a Christian, and that it came from a divine gift.**

17. Seeing, Sir, that you, along with your brother **Mr. H. S.** and likewise the deeply learned doctors **J. S.** and **J. D. K.**, are my very much respected friends and my eternal fellow members and brothers in the **Life-Tree of Christ**; and I, as a fellow member with a religious heart, also rejoice with them, seeing that God has adorned and endowed them with understanding and

<meta>catchword: wisdom,</meta>

<summary>Boehme explains his method for reconciling seemingly contradictory biblical passages regarding predestination and grace, specifically in the Book of Romans. He critiques the argumentative "Babel" of academic theology and invites a friendly, Christian dialogue on these difficult spiritual topics.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Predestination, Epistle to the Romans, Babel, Scholasticism, Grace, Justification, Free Will, Divine Gift</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the Twentieth Epistle, where Boehme discusses the distribution of his treatise, his role as an unlearned messenger of God, and his willingness to answer theological questions.</meta>

<lang>English</lang>
<page-num>155</page-num>
<header>The Twentieth Epistle.</header>

wisdom and other Christian virtues; whom I have also always acknowledged as my favorable, charitable, and gracious **masters** <note>original: "Masters"; likely referring to his patrons or supporters among the nobility or educated classes</note>. Therefore, I have arranged for them to receive a copy of this treatise among them, desiring and asking them to deal with one another in a Christian, brotherly way, and share it with each other so it may be transcribed. This is necessary because of the greater duties required of my **gift** <note>original: "Talent"; referring to his divine inspiration and the writing work he feels called to do</note>, and copying it myself would be a hindrance to me; otherwise, I would have sent a copy to each person individually.

**18.** However, if this treatise should be intercepted and not reach their hands, then I will send them my own **manuscript**. I ask them to be pleased to read it over and consider it without prejudice or boredom; they shall find such rich sense and meaning in it that it will be profitable to them in many things, but especially in the Christian practice of the **new birth** <note>A central theme in Boehme’s theology, referring to the spiritual transformation and regeneration of the soul</note>.

**19.** And in whatever way I can be of further service to them with my small gifts, I shall always be mindful to faithfully perform it as an obligated Christian, in thankful gratitude for their good intentions, integrity, sincerity, and favors toward me.

**20.** And although I am a man of no social standing compared to their greatness or high rank, and also in comparison to the **Doctors of Divinity** <note>original: "D. D."; referring to university-trained theologians with whom Boehme was often in conflict</note>, let them for a while set aside human **reason** and consider that it pleases the Most High to manifest His wonders through **simple people** and those who are considered foolish in the world's eyes. This has happened from the beginning of the world until now, whenever great **upheavals** <note>original: "Alterations"; referring to major historical or religious changes</note> were about to occur.

**21.** Sirs, know for certain that I will not fail to provide an answer to any man's high or profound questions, provided they are virtuous, appropriate, and Christian; for this knowledge is entrusted to me by the Most High and given as a free gift of grace.

**22.** I mention this with Christian intent and affection so that if anyone still had any doubts about that opinion, I might, in love, help him free himself from it and bring him into a **balanced state of mind** <note>original: "Temperature of the minde"; a state of spiritual and mental harmony</note>. I should not consider any effort too great to share my gifts and understanding with him. And so I commend you and yours into the love of **Jesus Christ**, and myself into your favor.

---

->**Dated, February 20, 1623.**<-

# A POSTSCRIPT.

---

23. The tribulation and destruction of **Babel** <note>In Boehme’s writings, "Babel" symbolizes the confusion and corruption of the institutionalized, sectarian churches</note> approaches with exceeding haste; the storm arises on all coasts. It shall be a sore tempest; vain hope deceives, for the breaking of the tree is at hand, which is known by the wonders.

<margin>Internal/civil.</margin>
24. Internal, home-bred fire hurts its own native country; righteousness and truth are trodden under foot; great heaviness, trouble, and calamity grow on apace.

<margin>Pride, greed, envy, wrath, and all falsehood, under a holy appearance of religion.</margin>
25. Men shall mourn for an old, empty, ruinous cottage on which salvation does not rely; they will be enraged for the nest where Satan has hatched his young.

26. The Tower of Babel is without foundation; men suppose they can prop it up, but a wind from the Lord overthrows it.

<margin>Or, Proof.</margin>
27. The hearts and thoughts of men shall be revealed; for there comes a **trial** <note>original: "Proba"; a Latin term for a test or examination of quality</note> from the Lord whereby the **titular Christian** <note>A Christian in name only, lacking true spiritual transformation</note> shall be exposed in his false heart and soul, like a reed that is tossed to and fro by the wind. Because his heart is wavering—now this way, now that way—his false foundation becomes manifest.

<margin>The Turk shall turn into a true Christian.</margin>
28. Many shall betray themselves and ruin both body and goods through hypocrisy; the hypocrites and titular Christians shall falter for fear when their false foundation is revealed.

<margin>☛</margin>
29. The **Oriental Beast** <note>Often interpreted in this period as the Ottoman Empire</note> gets a human heart; and before this comes to pass, he helps to tear down the Tower of Babel with his claws.

<margin>The Emperor of Germany.</margin>
30. In the darkness of the North, there arises a Sun which takes its light from the sensory properties of the nature of all beings—from the formed, expressed, and re-expressing Word—and this is a wonder in which all nations rejoice.

31. An Eagle has hatched young Lions in his nest and brought them prey so long until they have grown great. He hoped that they would likewise bring their prey back to him; but they have forgotten that. They take the Eagle and pluck off his feathers and bite off his claws for unfaithfulness, so that he can fetch no more prey, even if he should starve from hunger.

32. But they fall out over the Eagle's nest and tear one another in anger, until their anger becomes a fire which burns up the nest; and this is from the Lord of all beings.

<meta>Catchword: 33. If</meta>

<summary>In this prophetic postscript, Boehme describes the imminent collapse of the corrupt "Babel" church system and uses animal allegories (the Eagle and Lions) to predict political rebellion and civil war within the Holy Roman Empire.</summary>

33. If the rich man knew upon what foundation he stood, he would examine himself and look toward his final end.

34. The sun gives life to many things, and likewise brings death to many things.

35. But he who lies still in self-will <note>The act of surrendering one's ego and personal desires to God's will</note> and allows his internal ground (from which man is originally created) to lead and guide him, is the noblest and richest person on the Earth.

36. The **Postilion** <note>original: "Postilion"; literally a messenger or herald. In Boehme's prophetic language, this figure represents a spiritual or political herald of change or judgment</note> arising out of the ground of nature comes and carries the sword over the Earth. He has six winds as his assistants, which for a long time have ruled upon the Earth. These break the Postilion’s sword through the revelation of the seventh wind, which they always kept hidden within them; but by reason of the Postilion’s power, they must call upon and manifest him.

<margin>☞</margin>
37. This seventh wind is revealed by a new fire, and at this time the fountain of grace shall flow with sweet water, and the afflicted and oppressed shall be refreshed.

# THE TWENTY-FIRST EPISTLE.
## TO FREDERICK CRAUSSEN,
### DOCTOR OF MEDICINE <note>original: "Physick"; in the 17th century, a "Doctor of Physick" was a physician or medical doctor</note>.

---

->*Our Salvation in the life of Jesus Christ in us.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Dearest sir, Christian, and faithful friend, I wish to you and yours, and to all those who desire and love *Jesus* (in all faithful, real, cooperating love-desire <note>Boehme's term for a yearning for God that actively works in harmony with Divine grace</note>), the light and effective working power of God in our Life-Tree <note>The "Life-Tree" is a recurring metaphor in Boehme's work, representing the spiritual growth of Christ within the human soul</note>, *Jesus Christ*, together with all bodily well-being.

<margin>2.</margin>
At the request of yourself and Mr. *N.*, I have considered those passages of Scripture which Mr. *N.* set down in his letter, which you delivered to me. In it, I was encouraged to explain <note>original: "expound"</note> them in Christian love, according to my gifts and understanding, but especially the ninth and eleventh chapters of the Epistle of Paul to the *Romans*, at which human reason often falters <note>original: "stumbleth"; these chapters discuss the complex and often controversial themes of divine election and predestination</note>. I have not only done this willingly and readily in...

<summary>Jacob Boehme begins a letter to Doctor Frederick Craussen, offering a spiritual blessing and agreeing to interpret difficult biblical passages from the Book of Romans regarding divine grace and human reason.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Frederick Craussen, Epistle 21, Book of Romans, Life-Tree, theology, divine grace, biblical interpretation</keywords>

--- Page 177 ---
<meta>This page continues Boehme's explanation of his theological method, emphasizing the internal harmony of Scripture over superficial contradictions and warning against interpreting through the "spirit of the world."</meta>

<page-num>159</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-First Epistle.</header>

Christian obligation and good affection by explaining <note>original: "expounding"</note> those cited <note>original: "alleadged"</note> texts; but I have also set down and described the true **FOUNDATION** <note>original: "GROUND"</note> of the Divine Manifestation in such a manner that I hope men will see the truth.

3. But if there is a mind divinely inclined and devoted that can give the honor to God, I hope it will be understood and taken according to my meaning and apprehension, and not otherwise interpreted as was done formerly; which I pass over. Instead, I prefer Christian love, as we are *obliged in Christ to instruct one another in a friendly way according to our various gifts*, and therein give God the honor and despise no one in his divine gifts; for he who does so *blasphemes the Holy Spirit*, and against such a person the Scripture pronounces a harsh sentence.

4. Now although this treatise is somewhat long, let the reader not consider it tedious and tiresome; for I thought it of little importance for me to attempt to prove and clarify such a writing without a sufficient foundation. Therefore, I have placed the cited passages of Scripture upon the deepest foundation <note>original: "innermost ground"</note> and shown how they *originally* arise out of *their center*, and what their sense and meaning is.

<margin>☞ <note>A manicule, or pointing hand, used by the author or a reader to mark a significant passage.</note></margin>
5. For it is not enough that I should gather together a great heap of scriptural passages to oppose and contradict those cited; no, no; this avails nothing before God and the truth. For the least stroke or letter of this *Law* shall not pass away until all is fulfilled, says <note>original: "faith," an OCR error for "saith"</note> Christ. The sayings of the Scripture must remain true and not conflict with one another; and though they seem to be contrary and contradict one another, yet it is only to those to whom the understanding of them is not given, and who are not gifted or made capable of explaining and interpreting them.

<margin>* Spirit of the World <note>original: "Spiritus Mundi"</note>.</margin>
6. But he who will undertake to clearly interpret them correctly must have the understanding of the *Harmony* <note>original: "Accordance"</note>, so that he may know how to reconcile those places which seem contradictory to human reason. He must not transfer or place them upon a *conjecture* or opinion—wondering whether it is so or not—if he wishes to teach thoroughly and with certainty about them. For from opinion and conjecture arise only strife and controversy, upon which great *Babylon* is built: namely, spiritual pride and unfaithfulness <note>original: "Whoredome"; often used biblically to describe spiritual infidelity or idolatry</note>. There, one wishes to be an *Apostle* and yet is not sent or acknowledged by God; instead, he runs in opinion and in the driving [or instigation] of the *Spirit of the World* <note>original: "Cosmick Spirit"</note>.

7. And although many run in the *Drawing of the Father* <note>A reference to John 6:44, "No man can come to me, except the Father which hath sent me draw him"</note>, yet if the true light of the eternal life in the Word of the Divine Essence does not appear to them (being an expression or breathing <note>original: "spiration"</note> of the holy and also of the natural word in its differentiation <note>original: "severation"; Boehme's term for the process of the Divine Word distinguishing itself into the variety of creation</note>, from which the creation has arisen, and from which good and evil have their origin), and...

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that his lengthy treatise is necessary to provide a solid spiritual foundation for reconciling seemingly contradictory scriptures. He warns that true interpretation requires divine insight rather than human conjecture or "spiritual pride," which leads to the confusion of "Babylon."</summary>

<lang>en</lang>

## The Twenty-First Epistle.

...illuminate him; he will be far from being able to unite the supposed contradictions <note>original: "contraries"</note> of the Scripture sayings and to speak from **ONE center** in such a way that not the least detail <note>original: "tittle"</note> is diminished in their harmony [or reconciliation].

8. I set this down not to displease *N.* or anyone else, but only because of the long-continued disagreement of human reason, in which the world goes astray and truth lies veiled. Because of this, men judge this article about the *will of God* and carry on with their reasonings *without a foundation*. But where Christ is born in a person, there strife ceases, and God the Father speaks His Word in Christ through the soul of that person. For such conclusions and reasonings, there must be an inward divine light which provides certainty; otherwise, there is no foundation for the reason to stand upon.

<margin>☛ <note>A manicule, or pointing hand, used to draw the reader's attention to this specific paragraph.</note></margin>
9. You may obtain this treatise from Mr. *Michael of Ender*, who has now received it. It contains about forty-two sheets in my own handwriting. If you are pleased to share it with *N.* as your good friend and kinsman, I am content, provided that you tell him he should not understand it as if I had written anything there passionately against him or anyone else. Such passions or emotional biases <note>original: "affections"</note> do not sit close to my soul without an urgent and great cause. Although I am not without failings and lapses, my Savior Christ has shown me such grace that all insults <note>original: "invectives"</note> and opposing objections against me—when compared to one word which proceeds out of divine love toward me, where I perceive a divine earnestness—fall away. They are rejected like a weed, which I would not willingly plant in my garden, for from such a thing nothing but a wicked <note>original: "naughty"</note> weed grows again.

10. Furthermore, it is requested of *N.* out of Christian love that, since I have explained <note>original: "expounded"</note> his cited <note>original: "alleadged"</note> passages of Scripture according to my small gifts (which are known to God), he would be pleased to do me a favor. If my explanation displeases him and is not, in his opinion, sufficiently grounded and fundamental, he should explain those cited texts himself—*especially the 9th and 10th chapters of the Epistle of St. Paul to the Romans* <note>These chapters are famous for discussing God's sovereignty and the election of Israel.</note> and even those very Scriptures which I have explained. He should explain them together with the entire foundation of the Divine Will regarding good and evil, how its origin is in man or outside of man, and unfold and declare them in a sensible <note>original: "sensall"</note> manner.

11. And then I desire that he would declare and explain to me the *inspired word of grace in the seed of the woman in Paradise* <note>A reference to the promise in Genesis 3:15 that the offspring of Eve would crush the serpent's head.</note>, and then the two lines: namely, the Kingdom of the corrupt human nature and the Kingdom of grace in the in-spoken voice of grace.

12. Or if my explanation regarding *Abraham, Ishmael, Isaac*, and also *Jacob and Esau* did not please him, let him out of Christian love...

<meta>catchword: would</meta>

<summary>Boehme argues that spiritual truths can only be understood through an inward divine light rather than human reason. He challenges his opponent to provide a better explanation of the "Divine Will" and the biblical stories of election if he finds Boehme's own treatise unsatisfactory.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Michael of Ender, Divine Will, human reason, Romans 9, Romans 10, election, grace, spiritual illumination, interpretation of Scripture</keywords>

<page-num>161</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-First Epistle.</header>

<margin>☞</margin>
...would show his gifts and explain the basis <note>original: "expound the ground"</note> of them, so that I might find his gifts and understanding in those places. And then, if I can see that God has gifted *him* with a larger measure of understanding in these high mysteries <note>The deep spiritual secrets of the faith.</note> than me, I will accept it with great gladness. I will love him in his gifts and give thanks to our God for them; I will rejoice with *him* as a fellow member <note>A fellow member of the Body of Christ.</note> in our gifts, in the spirit of Christ. All of this would lead more to the profit and benefit of our brethren and Christian fellow-members; it would be more godly, commendable, and praiseworthy than a raw contradiction, born out of emotional biases <note>original: "affections"</note>, for the sake of man’s willful ego <note>original: "selfe sake"</note>.

13. But I pray to my God in Christ that He would be pleased to open his heart, so that his soul may see into the basis <note>original: "ground"</note> of my gifts. For truly, I am a *simple* man; I never studied or learned this high mystery; neither did I seek <note>original: "fought" (likely a printer's error for "sought")</note> for it in such a way, or know anything of it. I sought only the heart of love in Christ Jesus. But when I had obtained that, with exceeding great joy of my soul, then this *treasure* of divine and natural knowledge was opened and given to me. I have not boasted of this so far, but have heartily desired and begged of God to know whether the time had yet come for this knowledge to be *revealed* in the hearts of many. Regarding this, I obtained an *effectual* answer; so I know very well what I have hinted at and mentioned <margin>☞</margin> [ in my writings ].

14. And although I am therefore hated by many in the world, yet men will *shortly see* why God has opened and revealed to a lay, humble <note>original: "meane [ abject ]"</note> man the *grand mystery*—*namely* <note>original: "Viz."</note>, the basis of all secrets and mysteries. And yet, *I may not reveal* all that is made known to me. Nevertheless, it might be shared with worthy people if I found that it were God’s will and profitable to men, just as a while ago a very precious *pearl* was revealed to me, which has its time for effective practical use and *benefit*. But it is always a very profitable benefit in my soul. Do not wonder and marvel so much at the simplicity of what God does; for the time of the proud has come to an end.

15. Further, I desire and entreat *Mr. N.* to deal Christianly and candidly, in love, with his gifts, and not to taunt and slander <note>original: "scandalize"</note> my name as he did before, by which the gifts of the *Holy Spirit* are spoken of as evil. If he does this, he shall be answered with similar modesty, mildness, and respect. But if it should happen, contrary to my good intent and hope, that I am *further* slandered by people or in writings, let him know (if I either see or hear of it with certain proof) that he shall not lack a meaningful answer <note>original: "answere to purpose"</note> through a *divine gift*; and he shall gain no advantage or praise from it.

16. And I mean this sincerely; I exhort him out of Christian love and obligation to answer, if he will not declare and explain

<summary>Boehme defends his spiritual insights as divine gifts granted to him as a simple layman and warns his critic, Mr. N., to engage with him in Christian love rather than slander. He asserts that while he has discovered the "grand mystery," he only reveals what is profitable and permitted by God.</summary>

...those places in a sensible <note>original: "sensall"; meaning full of sense or rational.</note> and comprehensive answer. Then, let him reconcile the contradictions which seem to oppose one another, so that we may mutually exchange our gifts and bring them into one foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> for the love and benefit of our brethren.

<margin>☛</margin>
17. And I commend you and your family, and all those who seek and desire the child *Jesus*, to the effective, working love of *Jesus Christ*. I desire that He may be conceived, become incarnate, and be born in everyone; then, strife and contention will have an end. When the "seed of the woman" <note>A reference to the prophecy in Genesis 3:15, where the offspring of Eve defeats the serpent.</note> breaks the head of the serpent, we return again to the *state of balance* <term>original: "Temperature"; in Boehme’s philosophy, this refers to the "Temperamentum," the perfect harmony of all qualities or forces within the soul.</term> and are in *Christ* as only one—like a single tree with many boughs and branches.

<header>The Twenty-Second Epistle.</header>

### THE
### TWENTY-SEC-
### OND EPISTLE.

---

<margin>1.</margin> **M**uch respected Sir, and dear Doctor, please give Mr. *N.* my letter to read over, but *not* this note; and urge him toward Christian humility. See if perhaps the eyes of his soul might be opened, which I heartily and freely wish for him. It will be no disgrace to him to love and embrace the truth, for I perceive very well what lies in the way and holds him back: nothing else but *self-love*. This is because he has so far laid out and published his own foundation <note>original: "ground"; Boehme uses this to mean a fundamental spiritual principle or philosophical basis.</note> and gained great reputation and respect among many, and this foundation of mine does not entirely agree with his. Consequently, self-love drives him to the opposite view, even though he has not yet understood my foundation and is still like an infant regarding it.

<margin>2.</margin> 2. But if the honor of God and brotherly <note>original: "memberlike"; referring to the concept of believers being members of one another in the body of Christ.</note> love take root in his heart, he truly has nothing against me or my writings. Indeed, they might even better him and help him grow as a Christian. But without a willing heart, he will remain blind to them; for no human reason understands this foundation *without* the eternal love of God, in which all the treasures of wisdom are hidden.

<margin>3.</margin> 3. Please send word back to me in a letter regarding what his opinion is; *his* hidden foundation is revealed by this. I hope that he too will gain sight, since he otherwise possesses a sharp mind <note>original: "reason"</note> and has thoroughly studied *Logic*. Perhaps he will search further, but if he will not, his opinion does not cancel out the gifts of God. He cannot overturn this foundation of mine—especially my explanation of those biblical texts—using any Scripture. I mean this sincerely toward him.

<margin>4.</margin> 4. Furthermore, I would ask you to do me the favor of sending the *Treatise on Election* to Mr. *N.* for him to read, since he is a courteous gentleman, and also because the debate over this *Article* <note>Refers to the "Article of Election," a theological doctrine concerning God's choice of who is saved (predestination).</note> ended with him in such a way that it should not be viewed as if a man were left completely in the dark <note>original: "struck dead in ignorance."</note>.

<sig>Y 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: 5. But</meta>

<summary>In this letter, Boehme asks a doctor to share his writings with a scholar ("Mr. N.") while warning that the man's pride and reputation (self-love) are currently blinding him to deeper spiritual truths. Boehme hopes that a humble study of his "Treatise on Election" will help the man understand the divine mysteries that human logic alone cannot grasp.</summary>

<page-num>164</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Second Epistle.</header>

## The Twenty-Second Epistle.

5. But if necessity requires it, I shall so explain myself that they shall see the ground from which I write. Let them give me whatever *questions* they please; whether they concern *Nature* or what is beyond *Nature*, whether they are about *Time* or *Eternity*. Through *Divine Grace*, I will not be found lacking, but will give a sufficient answer—provided that it is done in a *Christian* way, and not out of personal bias, passion, hair-splitting, or verbal abuse. I shall deal with them in the same manner.

6. <margin>☞</margin> In our recent meeting, I was poorly disposed to such a debate; for wine and rich food hide the foundation of the *pearl* <note>original: "Pearl’s ground"; Boehme frequently uses the "Pearl" as a metaphor for the divine spirit or the highest wisdom within the soul.</note>, especially because I am not accustomed to them. At home, I eat very simply and soberly. Thus, *Mr. N.* was not sufficiently answered. However, I offer to answer *him and all others* who have *Christian* intentions. Let them only give me their *questions* in writing and explain their own opinions on the matter so that I may see what they conclude. I will give them a fundamental, detailed, and explanatory answer. I will not defend myself using any *sect* or *sectarian name*, nor will I use such titles to patronize my position. Regarding the basis of truth, I am not a *Flaccinian* <note>original: "Flaccinian"; a follower of Matthias Flacius Illyricus, a strict Lutheran theologian who emphasized the total corruption of human nature. Boehme is distancing himself from specific academic theological labels.</note> as *N.* supposes; rather, I shall stand upon the true foundation.

7. <margin>☞</margin> For I teach that there is no self-ability without *Christ* to attain *adoption* <note>In a theological sense, "adoption" refers to the process by which a believer becomes a child of God.</note> as *N.* thinks; only, I am not satisfied with his opinion, much less with that of *Mr. N. N.*, which completely clashes against the *Scripture*. For I am dead to all personal opinions within me and have nothing but what is given to me by *God* to know. I leave it to all of you to judge how I know what I know. Consider that I, as a *lay, illiterate*, and untrained man, must deal with you who are educated in the universities and must set myself against *academic learning*. In my own reason, I do not know how to achieve this without *God’s* knowledge, but I look upon what *God* does. Within the basis of my gifts, I know well enough what I am doing <margin>☞</margin> in this purpose and intention. And yet, it is not an intention originating in me; rather, this is how the time brings it forth, and how *He* who rules all things drives and orders it.

8. <margin>☞</margin> Concerning our secret discourse (*as you know*), you must still be patient and continue in that known process for a good while. In this beginning, no other way will be allowed. It may well be accomplished in this process in the seventh year; for it must be opened through all the six properties of the *spiritual foundation*. Although it is already opened through the *Sun*, the *key* has scarcely reached the first or second degree of the *center* of nature. For each property among the six forms of the *spiritual* life has its own unique *Sun* within it, arising from the strength, influence, and origin of the light of nature—that is, from the *Essential Sun*. These are to be opened in order, according to their birth and origin.

9. First, *Saturn’s Sun* is opened through the *key* of the outward *Sun*, so that the *separation* <note>original: "Severation"; a term referring to the chemical or spiritual process of distinguishing and separating elements.</note> of nature is discerned. Secondly,

<summary>Boehme defends his spiritual authority as a "layman" against university-educated critics and begins to explain his esoteric system of the "six properties" of spiritual life.</summary>

<header>The Twenty-Second Epistle.</header>

# The Twenty-Second Epistle.

Jupiter's Sun is opened, and then the powers are discerned, like a blossoming tree, and you have come this far.

10. Thirdly, *Mars*—that is, the fire-soul—is opened, and then *Virgin Venus* appears in her white robes and plays with the soul to see if she might move it to the desire of Love. She goes out and in, up and down with the soul, and lovingly embraces <note>original: "accolates"; from the Latin *accollare*, meaning to embrace or hug around the neck.</note> it to see whether it would bring the fleeting properties of self-will (where the soul departed from its balanced state <note>original: "temperature"; referring to the *temperamentum*, the ideal internal balance of qualities.</note> into the fleeting life of the body's divided properties) back into her. This is so that *Virgin Venus* might be again animated <gloss>or soul-filled</gloss> and regain the fire's *tincture* <note>original: "Tincture"; in alchemy and Boehme’s thought, the essential spiritual essence or transformative power.</note>, in which its joy and its life consist.

<margin>☞</margin>
11. For *Virgin Venus* is the splendor of the white within the *Sun*, as understood in this place; but the ability <note>original: "sability"; likely a typo for "ability" or "stability."</note> to shine is not her own. The spiritual water is her own property, which arises out of the fire where the separation begins in the *saltpeter* <note>original: "Salniter"; an archaic term for saltpeter or nitre, representing a volatile spiritual force.</note> in *Mars’s Sun*. Then *Virgin Venus* separates within herself and covers herself with a copper garment <note>original: "Copper Vesture"; in alchemy, copper is the metal associated with the planet Venus.</note>; for *Mars* wants her for his own property. But he defiles her exceedingly in his wickedness and spatters her with earth and rust; for he cannot have her as his spouse unless he gives her his own fiery will as a property, and that he will not do. Therefore, they struggle for a long time; they are married, but they are unfaithful to each other.

12. And even then the *Sun* comes and opens the *Sun of Mercury*, which is the fourth key. There you shall see great wonders: how God created the heaven and the earth, and furthermore the foundation of the four elements. If you then observe correctly, you shall see your own personal *spirit* <note>original: "Genius"; referring to an innate guiding spirit or the soul's unique nature.</note> unfolded before you and see how the Word has become man—that is, the expressed Word re-expressing <gloss>or speaking itself forth</gloss> into the separation of the powers. You will see how *Virgin Venus* is separated, and how the forms of nature take her into themselves and treat her pitifully. They take her into their own domination and change themselves within her into a purple color. They wish to murder, but she is <margin>☞</margin> their baptism into the new life in this state.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. The fifth key is *Virgin Venus* herself, with which she opens her <margin>* Otherwise, God.</margin> * gold—that is, the *Sun*—so that she gives her will and fair garland to the murderers. Thus she stands as one pregnant <gloss>or humbled</gloss>. Then the alchemist <note>original: "Artist"; in this context, the practitioner of the "Great Work" or spiritual alchemy.</note> assumes that he has the new child, but he is far enough from it until its *birth*.

14. The sixth key is *Luna* <note>original: "Luna"; the Moon, representing the physical or reflective principle.</note>. When the Sun opens this, then *Mars*, *Jupiter*, and *Saturn* must all forsake their own will and let their fleeting, aspiring *pomp* fall; for the Sun in *Luna* takes them into the incarnation. Then the alchemist begins to be sad and thinks—

<summary>Boehme describes a series of spiritual and alchemical "keys" involving the planets, detailing the struggle between the fiery will of Mars and the loving, transformative nature of Venus. He explains how these cosmic principles interact to bring about spiritual rebirth and the "incarnation" of the Word within the soul.</summary>

he has lost, but his hope shall not be ashamed; for the *Moon* in its opened *Sun* is so hungry for the true Sun that she attracts it with force and effect into them. Whereupon *Mars* fails <note>original: "quaileth"</note> in his wrath and dies away in his own right, and then Virgin *Venus* receives him and flows <note>original: "insinuates"; here meaning to enter gradually or weave into.</note> with her love into him. Thereby, *Mars* in *Jupiter* and *Saturn* is made alive <note>original: "quick"</note> in this love of a joyful life, and all the six properties give their will over to *Venus*; and she gives her will to the *Sun*, and then the life is born that stands in the *Balance* <note>original: "Temperature"; referring to the *temperamentum*, the perfect state of harmony between opposing qualities.</note>.

<margin>☞</margin> 15. Dear Mr. *Doctor*, the pen is not to be trusted <note>Boehme often cautions that the written word is insufficient to convey these deep spiritual mysteries, or that it is risky to put them on paper.</note>; yet take care of the work. It will be so and no other way; do not move it, lest *Mercury* be enraged before his opening. For outwardly he is evil, but inwardly he is good and the true life, yet *Mars* is the cause of life. Also, they do not proceed so plainly and precisely in order with their opening, although the opening is done in *Order*; but the wheel of the senses <note>original: "sensall wheel"</note> turns itself about and winds inward until *Saturn* comes with his will into the internal ground. Then he stands in the *Balance*, and no longer produces unstable inclinations. But all that you now see are the revolting, fugitive spirits; they boast with Virgin *Venus*, but they all live in impurity <note>original: "Whoredome"; often used by Boehme to describe spiritual unfaithfulness or the mixing of holy and unholy desires.</note>. They must be converted and turn into the inner ground so that they may be made stable <note>original: "fixed"; an alchemical term for making a volatile substance solid or permanent.</note>. This continues until Virgin *Venus* loses her material, gross [ <gloss>impure</gloss> ] water in which the adulterers behave wantonly with her in false-will, so that she may become holy and spiritual. Then the Sun shines in her, which changes the nature into love.

<margin>☞</margin> 16. Loving Mr. *Doctor*, the *Philosophical Body* is the spiritual water from the fire and light; namely <note>original: "Viz."; an abbreviation for the Latin *videlicet*.</note>, the power of the fire and of the light. <margin>☞</margin> When it is severed from its coarseness through the opening of all the properties of nature, then it is truly spiritual. Then the *Solar* spirit receives no other property into itself at all, except only that which is able to reach its sensory Sun in the opened state. For the Sun takes nothing into itself but its own likeness; it takes its Heaven out of the earth (if you will understand me correctly), for that is its food, from which it generates a young Sun in itself, which is also called *Sol* <note>original: "Sol"; Latin for Sun.</note>. But it is a body; therefore, I say to you, keep yourself diligently and precisely to it. You shall truly rejoice if God lets you live long enough, if only you have the right *Father*—whom I have sought <note>original: "founded for"; likely meaning searched for or discovered.</note> and am greatly in love with.

<margin>☞</margin> 17. This is well known to me, for I have lately seen it, at which I do not only wonder but rejoice. Much is revealed to me therein, and although I might write somewhat more extensively, it is not necessary for this process. Also, the *pen* is not to be trusted; it may be done another time. I pray you to keep this letter secret and in faithfulness; if I come to you, I may entrust you with something which

...which I have lately seen and received; yet I shall travel as far as I dare if the opportunity arises and the nearby troubles do not hinder me. I will come to *Breslau* <note>modern-day Wrocław, Poland</note> around *Shrovetide* <note>The three days preceding Ash Wednesday, marked by preparation for Lent</note>, and so I may visit you on my return.

18. Dear Mr. *Doctor*, now that you have gained insight <note>original: "become seeing"</note>, read the treatise on *Election* <note>Refers to Boehme’s work *On the Election of Grace*, which deals with the theological concept of predestination</note> with deep internal reflection. It contains more in its internal foundation than can be outwardly expressed in reference to the sayings of the Scripture. This internal foundation I dare not provide or reveal to those who are *spiritually unwise*.

<margin>* In the Secrets <note>original Latin: *In Arcanis*</note>.</margin>
19. Be faithful in these * *Mysteries* and consider the wicked world, in its greed, unworthy of them. Regarding what you cannot understand allegorically <note>original: "Parabolically"</note>, questions are necessary, and somewhat more shall be revealed to you. Yet, I am prohibited by the *Prince of the Heavens* from revealing things in a specific order; I am only permitted to do so in the manner and nature of the blossoming earth <note>Boehme suggests his revelations follow an organic, natural growth rather than a rigid, systematic structure</note>.

20. Therefore, imitate the *bees* that gather honey from many flowers. Frequent writing might be of service to you, yet do as you please; "God takes God, need takes need" <note>An aphorism implying that the divine spirit in man recognizes the Divine, while the worldly nature seeks the world</note> [each thing receives its like].

<header>The Twenty-Third Epistle.</header>

# THE TWENTY-THIRD EPISTLE.
## TO CHRISTIAN STEENBERGER
### DOCTOR OF MEDICINE. <note>original: "Physick," the archaic term for the practice of medicine</note>

---

-> Our salvation is in the life of Jesus Christ in us. <-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
**W**orthy, learned, dear Christian friend; I send you all hearty wishes of Divine Love and Grace. I desire that the fountain of Divine Love might be opened to you through the Sun of Life, from which the *divine water springs*; as I do not doubt that the Bridegroom has called His bride—*namely, your soul*—to this wellspring, seeing I understand that God has placed you under the Cross and tribulation.

<margin>2.</margin>
2. This is the first mark and sign of the noble *Sophia* <note>original: "Sophia," referring to Divine Wisdom personified as a feminine figure in Boehme’s mystical theology</note>, with which she marks her children. For she is accustomed to manifest herself through the thorns of God's anger, like a beautiful rose on a thorny bush, provided that the soul keeps its vow and faithfulness; for there must be a faithful and firm bond and covenant between the soul and this fire-burning love of God.

<margin>☞</margin>
3. A person must set themselves to the purpose of entering into Christ's bitter passion and death, and die daily therein to their sins and evil vanities, praying earnestly to God for the *renewing of their mind* and understanding. They must be anointed and illuminated <note>The text breaks here at the catchword "nated," completing the word "illuminated" on the following page</note>
<meta>catchword: nated</meta>

<summary>In this opening to a letter to Dr. Christian Steenberger, Boehme encourages the physician to see his current hardships as a sign of spiritual favor from "Sophia" (Divine Wisdom). He emphasizes that salvation comes from the internal life of Christ and requires a daily commitment to dying to one's sins.</summary>

<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Christian Steenberger, Sophia, Divine Wisdom, spiritual suffering, the Cross, Christ's passion, inner renewal</keywords>

--- Page 187 ---
<meta>This page continues the text from the previous page, completing the word "illuminated" from the sentence describing the process of becoming a true Christian.</meta>

<page-num>369</page-num>
<header>The Threefold Life.</header>

illuminated by the Holy Spirit; and put on Christ, with His suffering, death, and resurrection, so that he may be a true branch on the vine of Christ, in whom Christ Himself works and rules, according to the internal foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> of His Spirit.

<margin>\* *Or, Make the iron red hot.*</margin>
4. This *Mystery* is understood <note>original: "comprehended"</note> through faith, where the deity and humanity are then joined together according to that same internal foundation, in the same way that fire \* heats the iron through and through, yet the iron retains its substance; but as long as the fire burns within it, it is changed into pure <note>original: "meer"</note> fire.

5. It is not that the creature grasps it by its own power; rather, it is grasped when the will completely resigns itself to God, and the Spirit of God rules in this resigned will. The will is the true *Temple of the Holy Spirit* <note>original: "Ghost"</note> in which Christ dwells essentially; not in an imaginary, thought-like, or creaturely way, but like the fire in the iron, or the sun in a plant <note>original: "hearb"</note>, where the influential power of the sun forms and makes itself *essential* within the *tincture* <note>original: "tincture," a term in alchemy and Boehme's theology referring to the inner spiritual essence or life-force</note> of the plant.

6. This is also to be understood in the spirit of man: when the holy power of God forms and images itself in man's spirit and faith, it becomes a spiritual being [or essence] which only the soul's "mouth of faith" can lay hold of—not the earthly man of flesh and blood, who is mortal. It is an *Immortal Being* in which *Christ* dwells within man; it is the Heaven of God stamped upon the "little world" <note>original: "little World," referring to the microcosm of the human being</note>, and it is a revelation of the throne or seat of God, where paradise springs forth again and bears fruit.

7. Therefore, the *Dragon* <note>original: "Dragon," representing the fallen, selfish nature or the devil</note> must first be slain; and although he still clings to the earthly flesh like the rind and bark on a tree, the spirit lives in God, as Saint *Paul* says: "*our citizenship <note>original: "conversation," which in Early Modern English meant conduct or citizenship</note> is in Heaven*"; and as *Christ* also said, "*he who eats my flesh and drinks my blood abides in me, and I in him; also, without me you can do nothing.*"

<margin>☞</margin>
8. Therefore I say, if anyone is a true Christian, he is so in *Christ*; he is born into <note>original: "begotten and innate in"</note> the life and spirit of Christ and puts on the Resurrection of Christ, for in this way the satisfaction of Christ is shared with <note>original: "imparted to"</note> him. Thus also, Christ overcomes sin, death, the Devil, and Hell within him; and thus he is reconciled and united with God, in Christ.

9. For the *new birth* is not a grace assigned <note>original: "imputed"</note> from the outside, as if we only needed to comfort ourselves with Christ's merits while continuing in the hypocrisy of sin. No, it is a childlike, innate grace: God has placed Christ, along with justification, upon the convert, so that Christ also redeems him within himself by the power of His resurrection from God's anger. Otherwise, he is no Christian, no matter how much he may flatter himself or make devout shows of hypocrisy.

<sig>Z</sig>
<meta>catchword: 10. Concerning</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme explains that true Christianity is an internal transformation where Christ dwells within the believer like fire in heated iron. He emphasizes that the "new birth" is a living, internal reality rather than a mere external legal status.</summary>
