<meta>This page continues from a letter where the author (Jakob Böhme) is discussing the nature of spiritual illumination and the internal presence of Christ in the believer.</meta>

<lang>English</lang>
<page-num>170</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Third Epistle</header>

10. Concerning the interpretation of some words (and also that which you requested of me) which are specified in my book called *Aurora* <note>original: "Aurora," Böhme’s first book, which explores the birth of nature and the spiritual world</note>—which have very hidden meanings, the knowledge of which was given to me by the Most High—I give you to understand that at present it is not appropriate to write at length and explicitly about them in letters, seeing as the times are dangerous and the Enemy of Christ <note>original: "the Enemy of Christ," likely referring to religious or political persecutors of the time</note> does horribly rage and rave. Until a little time has passed, however, I will give you a short hint to consider further.

11. First, there is a twofold meaning of the *Northern Crown*: The first points to the *Crown of Life*, namely the Spirit of Christ, which shall be manifested in the midst of the great darkness—that is, in the contrition [or distress] of the sensible nature of the conscience. Where a specific motion is present, then comes the Bridegroom: the power of Christ in the midst of such a motion.

<margin>☞</margin>
12. The other meaning is a *symbol of the outward kingdom*, representing the great confusions, entanglements, and contentions that shall occur when the nations are in controversy. There also is the symbol—namely, the *Victory*—indicated as it stands in the spiritual figure. It shows how things shall go, what people shall at last conquer, and how in the meanwhile, during such a lamentable time of tribulation, Christ shall be made manifest and known. After and during that miserable time, the great mysteries shall be revealed, so that men shall be able to know even in *Nature* the hidden God in Trinity; in which knowledge the foreign nations shall be converted and become Christians. Therein is signified how the sectarian contentions in religion shall be destroyed in such a manifestation, for all gates will be set open. Then all unprofitable praters <note>original: "Praters," meaning those who talk idly or excessively about religious matters without true understanding</note>, who at present lie like so many bars before the *Truth*, shall be done away with. Everyone shall acknowledge and know Christ, and this manifestation shall be the last. Then the Sun of Life shall shine upon all nations, and even then the Beast of iniquity with the *Whore* <note>original: "the Whore," a reference to the "Whore of Babylon" from the Book of Revelation, symbolizing corrupt religious or political systems</note> shall end their days, which is signified under the characters *Ra. Ra. Ra. P. in R.P.*, as is to be seen in the *Revelation*.

13. We dare not at present make this lengthy interpretation more clear; everything will reveal itself. Then men shall see what it was, for there is yet *a completely different time* <note>original: "clean another time"</note> to come.

14. Concerning the *language of nature* <note>original: "language of nature" (Lat. *lingua Adamica*), the mystical idea that there is a language where the sound of a word expresses the true essence of the thing named</note>, I assure you that it is real; but what I understand in it, I cannot teach or give to another. Indeed, I can give a description of how it is to be understood, but it requires much space, and there must be a personal conference and interaction regarding it; it is *not* to be set down in *writing*.

15. Also concerning the *philosophical* work of the *Tincture* <note>original: "Tincture," in alchemy and Böhme's thought, the spiritual essence or life-force that can transform and perfect matter</note>, its progress is not to be described so bluntly and plainly. Although I do not have it in the actual practice <note>original: "Praxis"</note>, the seal of God lies before it to conceal it.

<summary>Böhme discusses the symbolic meanings of the "Northern Crown" and the "Language of Nature," hinting at a future era of spiritual peace where sectarianism ends and the mysteries of God are revealed through nature.</summary>
<keywords>Aurora, Northern Crown, Crown of Life, Tribulation, Manifestation, Language of Nature, Tincture, Sectarianism, Revelation, Alchemy</keywords>

<meta>This page continues Jakob Böhme’s response to an inquiry about spiritual mysteries and the process of internal transformation, often described through alchemical metaphors.</meta>

<page-num>171</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Third Epistle</header>
<sig>Z 2</sig>

## The Twenty-Third Epistle

<margin>* Enter into the New Birth</margin>
...the true foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> of the same, under penalty of eternal punishment, unless a man knew for certain that it might not be misused. There is also no power to attain it unless a man first becomes *that* himself which he seeks within it; no skill or Art is of use unless one gives the <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tincture"; in alchemy, this refers to a potent substance or spiritual essence capable of transforming or healing</note> into the hands of another. He cannot prepare it unless he is certainly in the new birth.

16. Two <term>Central fires</term> <note>original: "Centrall fires"; likely referring to the inward spiritual fire and the outward natural fire in Böhme’s cosmology</note> belong to it, in which the power of all things consists; these may easily be attained if a person is rightly prepared. Therefore, Sir, do not trouble and toil yourself in the manner and way you mentioned—with any Gold or Minerals—for that is all false. The best things in Heaven and in the world, from above and below, must be ingredients in it; it is both far off and close at hand. The place is *everywhere* it may be found, but not everyone is fit and prepared for it. Neither does it cost any money, except what is spent on time and physical upkeep; otherwise, it might be prepared for two † florins <note>original: "Florens"; a type of gold or silver coin used in Europe</note> or less.
<margin>† Four shillings</margin>

17. The World must be made Heaven, and Heaven the World. It is not made of earth, stones, or metals, and yet it is of the *foundation* of all metals. It is a *spiritual Being* surrounded by the four elements, which also changes the four elements into one: a <term>double Mercury</term> <note>original: "doubled Mercury"; in alchemy, Mercury represents the spirit or volatile principle, here doubled to suggest a union of opposites</note>, yet it is not quicksilver or any other mineral or metal.

<margin>* A book titled *The Water-Stone of the Wise Men*.</margin>
18. Read the <term>*Water-Stone of the Wise Men*</term> <note>original: "Wasser Stein der Weisen"; a famous Rosicrucian/alchemical text published in 1619, often attributed to Johann Ambrosius Siebmacher</note>, which is in print. There is much truth in it, and it is also clear. The work is easy and the Art is simple—a boy of ten years might do it—but the wisdom within it is great and the greatest *Mystery*. Everyone must *seek it for themselves*. We must not break the *Seal of God*, for a fiery *mountain* lies before it, which amazes even me. I must wait to see if it is God's will. How should I teach others explicitly about it? I cannot yet make it myself, although I know something of it. Let no man seek more from me than I have already clearly enough indicated. I commend you, along with all the children of God, into the Love of *Jesus Christ*.

---

->**JACOB BÖHME.**<- <note>original: "JACOB BEEM"</note>

---

<summary>Jakob Böhme explains that spiritual transformation (the "New Birth") is the true foundation for attaining the "Tincture" or spiritual wisdom. He warns against seeking these truths through physical alchemy with gold and minerals, directing the reader instead toward an internal spiritual process.</summary>

<page-num>173</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Fourth Epistle</header>

# THE TWENTY-FOURTH EPISTLE
## In Answer to a QUESTION:
### WHY
### Did the Statue of a Deceased Person Weep or Shed Tears?

---

1. Concerning the question, it is obscure <note>original: "dark"</note> to the understanding, and there is a need for a *Joseph* <note>A reference to the biblical Joseph, who was famous for interpreting difficult dreams and mysteries.</note> to explain it; for it is a *magical* thing and very wonderful. Therefore, it is hard to answer, for it proceeds from the *Magia* <note>original: "the Magia"; in Böhme’s terminology, this refers to the creative, spiritual power of the will that underlies all reality.</note>.

<margin>* Or, resolve it.</margin>
2. Yet I will open and show you very briefly what my opinion is on the matter; not that I wish to conclude and pass an absolute, final judgment concerning it, but rather leave you and other enlightened men of God to your own opinion and understanding of it. Whether God has given me the ability to *prove it, I leave to your judgment, as you know the condition of the mentioned person better than I; for all things proceed according to the time, measure, and limit of that thing.

<margin>† Or, statue of stone: carved by a stone-cutter, or an image set up as a monument.</margin>
3. A hard, rough † stone wall <note>original: "Murall Stone"</note> has no life that is movable or active, for the elemental, plant-like <note>original: "vegetable"</note> life stands mute and still within it; and it is shut up within the first <term>Impression</term> <note>original: "Impression"; Böhme's term for the spiritual force that "compacts" or "presses" spiritual essence into material form.</note>. Yet, it is not as if it were *nothing*; there is nothing in this world where the elemental as well as the <term>sidereal dominion</term> <note>original: "Sydereall Dominion"; referring to the influence of the stars and celestial forces upon the physical world.</note> does not reside. However, in one thing it is more movable, active, and working than in another; and we cannot say that the four elements, together with the stars, do not have their *daily operation* in all things.

<meta>Catchword: 4. But</meta>

<summary>In this letter, Böhme begins to address a report of a statue weeping, attributing the phenomenon to "Magia" (spiritual magic) and the hidden celestial influences within material objects.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, weeping statue, Magia, sidereal dominion, elements, spiritual interpretation, Joseph (biblical), Impression</keywords>

--- Page 191 ---
<meta>This page continues Jakob Böhme’s explanation of a supernatural phenomenon involving a weeping statue, moving from the impossibility of a natural cause to a deeper exploration of his "Tria Prima" (the three primary substances) and the relationship between the microcosm of man and the macrocosm of the universe.</meta>

<page-num>173</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Fourth Epistle</header>

### The Twenty-Fourth Epistle

4. But since this is a *hard stone*, the *miracle* is above the accustomed and ordinary course of *Nature*. Therefore, we can in no way say that it has a natural cause within the *stone*, as if the stone’s own functioning could produce or initiate this. Rather, it is a *magical* motion originating from the *Spirit*, whose *Image* is carved and portrayed in the *stone*.

5. For a *stone* consists of three things, just as all *Beings* consist of these three things, though they are enclosed in a twofold property: namely, in a spiritual and a corporeal property. Those three things—of which everything in this *world* consists—are *Sulphur*, *Mercury*, and *Salt* <note>In Böhme’s alchemy, these are not literal chemicals but spiritual principles: Sulphur (the soul/fire), Mercury (the word/motion), and Salt (the physical substance/container).</note> in two properties: the one *Heavenly*, the other *Earthly*. Just as *God* dwells in *time*, and *time* dwells in *God*, yet *time* is not *God* but is outside of *God*, acting as a portrayed *image* of *Eternity*.

6. Likewise, *Man* exists both out of *time* and out of *eternity*, and consists of three things: namely, *Sulphur*, *Mercury*, and *Salt*, in two parts. One part is out of time—specifically, the outward body—and the other part stands in *eternity*—namely, the *soul*. Seeing then that *man* and *time*, as well as *eternity*, exist within one *dominion* in *man*, we must consider the *question* from this perspective.

<margin>The Microcosm out of the Macrocosm <note>original: "Microcosmus ex Macrocosmo"</note></margin>

7. For man is a *little world* <term>Microcosm</term> drawn out of the *great world* <term>Macrocosm</term>, and he possesses the property of the entire *great world* within himself. For *God* said to him after the Fall: *you are earth and to earth you shall return*. That is, *Sulphur*, *Mercury*, and *Salt*. All things in this *world* consist of these, whether they are *spiritual* or *corporeal*, except for the *soul*. The soul exists in such a property according to the law of *eternal nature*, as I have sufficiently demonstrated in my *writings*.

<margin>☞</margin>

8. Now when a person dies, the outward light in the outward *Sulphur* is extinguished; it goes out along with its outward fire, in which the life burned. Then the body falls to dust and enters again into that from which it came. But the *soul*, which is brought forth out of *eternal nature* and infused into *Adam* by the spirit of *God*, cannot die; for it is not from time, but from the *Eternal Generation*.

<margin>† Or, set its mind upon.</margin>

9. Now, if the *soul* has † set its desire upon any temporal thing and has imprinted itself with it <insert>[or strongly set its *imagination* upon it]</insert>, then it has impressed the property of that thing into its desire. It holds onto that thing magically, as if it possessed it bodily. Indeed, it cannot hold the physical body—meaning the elemental body—but it holds the *sidereal body* <term>sidereal body</term> <note>The "star-body" or astral body; in Böhme’s view, a subtle body between the physical flesh and the eternal soul.</note> until the *stars* also consume it. It often happens that *people* appear after their death in *houses* in their own bodies, but the body is cold, dead, and numb; the spirit of the *soul* only "puts it on" by means of the *astral spirit* for as long as it takes for the body to putrefy.

<summary>Böhme explains that miracles like a weeping statue are "magical" rather than natural, rooted in the connection between the soul and the physical world. He describes the human composition of Sulphur, Mercury, and Salt, explaining how the soul can remain attached to the material world after death.</summary>

<keywords>Jakob Böhme, alchemy, Tria Prima, Sulphur, Mercury, Salt, Microcosm, Macrocosm, sidereal body, astral spirit, afterlife, supernatural phenomena</keywords>

--- Page 192 ---
<meta>This page continues Jakob Böhme’s explanation of a supernatural phenomenon—a weeping gravestone—by exploring the relationship between the human soul, its lingering desires, and the "sidereal" (starry or astral) spirit that connects the physical world to the spiritual realm.</meta>

<page-num>174</page-num>
<header>The twenty-fourth Epistle.</header>

## The twenty-fourth Epistle.

10. Also, many people are so strongly possessed by the Astral (or starry) Spirit through the *Desire* of the soul that the body takes a long time to decay. For the soul's desire brings the sidereal spirit <note>original: "sydereall"; in Böhme's thought, this refers to the celestial or "starry" influence that shapes the physical body and personality.</note> into the body so that the elements are, as it were, impressed with an astral life. This is especially true if the soul has not yet attained rest, and if, during its life in the body, it had strongly set its imagination upon something (and taken it to heart), but the body died before the soul had released its desire and detached itself from that thing. Therefore, the will continues to run in that same impression. It would like to resolve its cause but cannot; consequently, it seeks the cause or reason for its detention and wishes to rest in *Eternity*. However, the impressed thing continues its effective working and driving until the stars consume it. Previously, in the *Catholic Church* <note>original: "Popish Religion"; Böhme, a Lutheran, is likely referring to the doctrine of Purgatory or the practice of praying for the dead.</note>, there was some mention of this, but without *sufficient* understanding.

11. <margin>*Or, Shed tears.*</margin> Now you may easily consider how it happened that the *engraved gravestone shed water or wept*. This is not done by the power or virtue of the stone, but by the strength and might of the *Spirit*—the person to whom the stone belongs and whose *image* it bears. Also, it is not done from the soul’s own essence, but *magically* through the astral *Spirit*. The constellations in the *Spirit* of the soul have impressed themselves into the sidereal spirit in the stone, all according to the soul’s desire. By this, the soul has signaled that there was something weighing heavily on its mind when it lived; this sadness (or anxiety of spirit) remained in the sidereal spirit. For *Christ* said, "*Where your heart is, there is also your treasure*"; also, in the *Revelation of Jesus Christ*, it is written, "*Our WORKS shall follow us.*"

12. Dear Sir, it is not my place to judge further in this matter. Consider whether the person mentioned did not have something weighing heavily upon her before her end: whether anyone had done her wrong, or if she had done wrong to anyone, or whether concern for her husband and children (insofar as she was a holy person) troubled her. Perhaps she saw any of them following an evil course and, through the power of the sidereal spirit acting through the stone, she might give such an admonition for their improvement. Noble Sir, consider this yourself; I may likely hit upon the truth among all these mentioned things. But since I never knew the person and know nothing of her, I leave the judgment to your *kindness* <note>original: "Favour"</note>. You know better than I what her condition was. I write only of the *possibility* of how it may be and pass no further judgment or determination.

13. But as for the fact that this might be laughed at and accounted ridiculous, I do not care. I am not deceived; I understand (I bless God) this foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> very well. For I have not learned such knowledge...

<summary>Böhme explains that a "weeping" gravestone is caused by the lingering astral spirit of a deceased person whose soul remains attached to earthly concerns or unresolved grief. He suggests this phenomenon is a "magical" sign intended to admonish the living, though he leaves the specific interpretation to the recipient of the letter.</summary>
<keywords>astral spirit, sidereal spirit, soul's desire, weeping gravestone, afterlife, Catholic Church, Jakob Böhme, spiritual impressions, grief, admonition</keywords>

He refers the reader to his other major works for a deeper foundation of these concepts and cautions against sharing these mystical truths with those who lack spiritual depth.</meta>

<page-num>175</page-num>
<header>The twenty-fourth Epistle.</header>

[lear]ned <note>The word "ned" completes the word "learned" from the previous page.</note> from or by man, but it has been given to me. I would sufficiently support it with a further and larger exposition if I were to write about the *Human Property* <note>original: "Humane Property"; Böhme's term for the essential qualities or characteristics that define a human being’s nature and spiritual makeup.</note>: how man exists in *Life* and how he exists in *Death*.

<margin>* On the Signature of All Things <note>original: "De Signatura Rerum"; the Latin title of one of Böhme's major works, exploring how the internal essence of things is revealed through their external form.</note></margin>
14. I am sending you the book titled the *Forty Questions*. There you may see further *evidence* <note>original: "ground"; foundational reasoning or spiritual basis.</note>, which is nevertheless even more firmly established in the "Center of All Beings" within the book titled the *Threefold Life*. It is established even more so in the book *The Signature of All Things*. Furthermore, I entreat you not to *mention* my judgment and explanation of this question *very much among frivolous people* <note>original: "light people"; those who lack spiritual depth or the seriousness required to understand mystical concepts.</note>. For fodder belongs to a cow, and understanding belongs to the intelligent. The wicked man judges wickedly, but the man of understanding tests all things. I speak from good intentions and affection.

->January 1, 1622.<-

->J. B.<- <note>Jacob Boehme.</note>

<summary>In this concluding segment of the letter, Böhme asserts that his spiritual knowledge is divinely inspired and directs the recipient to his other writings for more profound study. He also warns the reader to keep these deep insights away from the "wicked" or superficial who might judge them poorly.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Forty Questions, Threefold Life, Signature of All Things, mysticism, divine inspiration, human nature, spiritual discernment</keywords>

<meta>This page begins the twenty-fifth Epistle, a letter from Jakob Böhme to the nobleman Charles of Endern. Böhme continues his recurring theme of humility, explaining how his private spiritual insights were never originally intended for a wide or high-ranking audience.</meta>

<page-num>176</page-num>
<header>The twenty-fifth Epistle.</header>
<meta>catchword: pleased</meta>

# THE
# TWENTY-FIFTH 
# EPISTLE.
---
-> TO <-

<margin>* Carel von Endern.</margin>
# * CHARLES of ENDERN.
---

1. Noble, Right Honorable Sir, I wish and desire for you—from the most holy omnipresent God, who is the fullness of all things and the power of all beings—a happy, joyful new year, and all prosperous *well-being*.

2. Although I, being a simple man, never in all my life intended to interact or converse with such *high-ranking people* through the gift which God has bestowed upon me out of His mercy and love, or to become known and acquainted with them by it; yet when the *high light* was kindled in me and the *fiery impulse* <note>original: "instigation"; in Böhme's vocabulary, this refers to a divine prompting or a sudden flash of spiritual insight.</note> fell upon me, my will was only to write what I saw in a profound and particular manner, and knew in the Spirit. I intended to keep my writings to myself.

3. I saw well enough what would happen, but it never yet entered my mind that I should think so highly of myself as to have my writings known; for I considered myself too simple. I intended to write down the *Pearl-like Garland* <note>original: "Pearle-like Garland"; Böhme often used the "Pearl" as a metaphor for divine wisdom or the "Pearl of Great Price" mentioned in the Gospels.</note> only for myself, and to imprint it on my heart.

<margin>☞</margin>
4. But seeing that I (as a very simple man) did not understand or notice this, and yet I now see plainly that God's intention was far different than what ever entered my mind; I have learned this first consideration: *That God shows no favoritism based on status* <note>original: "no respect of Persons with God"; a reference to Romans 2:11, meaning God does not judge based on worldly rank or title.</note>; *but he who depends on Him is accepted and beloved by Him, and He carries out His work through him; for He alone is high, and is pleased*— <note>The word "pleased" is completed by the catchword from the following page.</note>

<summary>Jakob Böhme writes to the nobleman Charles of Endern, expressing his humility and his realization that God intends for his private spiritual insights to be shared with others, regardless of social rank.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Charles of Endern, humility, divine revelation, Pearl-like Garland, spiritual status, social hierarchy</keywords>

--- Page 195 ---
<meta>This page continues Jakob Böhme’s twenty-fifth Epistle to the nobleman Charles of Endern. Böhme is explaining his mystical theology regarding the fall of man and the nature of the "Three Principles"—his unique cosmological framework describing the relationship between God’s wrath (darkness/fire), God’s love (light), and the physical world.</meta>

<page-num>177</page-num>
<header>The twenty-fifth Epistle.</header>

...pleased to manifest himself in the weak, so that it may be known *that the Kingdom and the Power are his alone*.

5. And also that it does not lie in human searching and reason, or depend on the heavens and their powers; for they do not comprehend him. Instead, he is well pleased to reveal himself in the low and humble, so that he may be known in all things. For the powers of the heavens also continually work themselves out in shapes, forms, plants, and colors to manifest and reveal the holy God, so that he may be known in all things.

6. God's manifestation can be made much higher and clearer in man, since man is not only a Being <gloss>[ or Being ]</gloss> out of the created world, but his power, matter, and unique Being—which he himself is—stand and align with all three principles of the <term>Divine Being</term> <note>In Böhme’s system, the "Three Principles" are the dark world (fire/wrath), the light world (love/spirit), and the physical world.</note>.

7. And nothing was lost from the essence of the divine creature <note>referring to humanity</note> in its fall except for the divine light, in which it should live, walk, and exist in God in perfect love, humility, meekness, and holiness. In that state, man was meant to eat the <gloss>[ *Manna* ]</gloss> or heavenly bread of the Word and divine power, and live in perfection like the angels.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. This light, which shines eternally in God within the second principle <note>the realm of light and love</note> (which is the only cause of joy, love, humility, meekness, and mercy), is withdrawn and hidden from man in his fall. This happened because the first man—when he was formed in his "Mother of the Great World" <note>Nature or the macrocosm</note>—set his *imagination*, *lust*, and *longing* upon the "Mother of Nature." He desired the food of the first principle <note>the source of nature’s raw, fiery power and wrath</note>, in which the origin and birth of nature, the source of anger, and the most *anxious birth* <note>Böhme's term for the painful, laboring struggle of nature coming into existence</note> (from which all the physical things of this world are made) consist. Consequently, he became capable of the same, because he stood upon the *same root*.

9. Thus, according to the body and also according to the spirit, he has become a child of this created world, which rules, acts upon, and leads him. It also gives him his food and drink, and has conceived within him corruptibility and pain. He has acquired a *beastly* <note>original: "Beastiall"</note> body, which must eventually *decay* <note>original: "Corrupt"</note> back into its "Mother" <note>the earth/physical nature</note>.

10. For he was not meant to have this monstrous form; the "constellation" <note>the astrological and elemental influences</note> of the great world was not meant to dominate him. Instead, he had his own "constellation" within himself, which aligned with the holy heaven of the *second principle* of the Divine Being—that is, with the *emergence* and birth of the <term>Divine Nature</term>.

11. Now, man is not so far broken and decayed that he is no longer the same man whom God created; he has only acquired this "monstrous form," which is subject to decay and has its beginning only and merely from the most outward and third principle <note>the visible, physical world</note>,

<sig>A a</sig>
<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that God reveals Himself through the humble rather than the scholarly, and details how the Fall caused man to lose his divine light and internal spiritual alignment, resulting in a mortal, "beastly" physical body dominated by the stars and elements.</summary>
<keywords>three principles, the Fall, divine light, macrocosm, anxious birth, beastly body, constellation, divine nature</keywords>

<page-num>178</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-fifth Epistle.</header>

### The Twenty-fifth Epistle.

and has awakened and opened in him the gate of the first principle <note>In Böhme’s system, the "First Principle" is the dark, fiery source of God’s power and wrath.</note> (which is the severe, earnest source). This source burns in the great created world and is *wholly* ignited in the *damned*.

12. But the true man, whom God created, who alone is the true, right man, is still hidden in this *corrupt* man. If he *denies himself in his animal* <note>original: "beastiall"</note> *form and does not live according to its actions, drives, and will, but surrenders himself to God with his whole mind, thoughts, and senses, then this man lives in God. And God works the will and the deed in him, for all is in God*.

<margin>☞</margin>
13. The truly holy and heavenly man, who is hidden in the monstrous human <note>Böhme often refers to the fallen physical body as "monstrous" because it is a distorted version of the original divine image.</note>, is in heaven just as God is. Heaven is in him, and the *heart* or light of God is conceived and born in him—that is, God in him and he in God. God is *nearer* to him than his *animal* body.

14. The animal body is not his own native country where he is at home; instead, he is *outside* Paradise with it. But the true man, regenerated and born anew in Christ, is *not* in this world, but in the *Paradise of God*; and although he is in the body, he is nonetheless in God.

15. And though the animal body dies, nothing happens to the new man; he simply comes forth directly out of the house of torment and opposing will into his native country. There is no need for traveling any great distance to where he thinks he must go to be better off; instead, God is manifest in him. <margin>☞</margin>

16. The soul of man comes out of the first principle of God, but in that state, it is *not a holy being*. However, in the second principle <note>The "Second Principle" is the realm of light, love, and the son of God.</note>, it is manifest in God and is a *divine creature*, for it is there that the divine light is born. Therefore, if the divine light is not born <gloss>begotten or brought forth</gloss> in it <gloss>namely, the soul</gloss>, then God is not in it, and it lives in the most original, earnest source, where there is an *eternal conflict* <gloss>enmity or opposing will</gloss> within itself.

17. But if the light is born, then there is joy, love, and pleasant delight in the creature, and the new man—which is the soul—is in God. How could there not be *knowledge* where God is present in the creature?

<margin>☞</margin>
18. Now, it does not depend on the willing, running, and toiling of the creature to know the depths of the deity; for the soul does not know the *divine center* or how the *divine essence* is generated. Instead, it depends on God’s will, how he chooses to manifest it.

19. Now then, if God manifests himself in the soul, what...

<summary>This page describes the dual nature of humanity: the hidden "new man" who lives in God's light and Paradise, and the "animal" body that belongs to the fallen world. Böhme emphasizes that spiritual knowledge and rebirth depend entirely on God's will and the soul's surrender.</summary>

### The Twenty-fifth Epistle.

19. [What] has the soul done to achieve this? Nothing. It has only the *longing*—the labor <note>original: "travelling", referring to the "travail" or pangs of childbirth</note> toward the birth—and it looks toward God, in whom it lives. From Him, the divine light becomes visible <note>original: "apparent"</note> and shines within the soul. Then the first fierce [earnest] Principle <note>In Böhme’s system, the "First Principle" is the dark, fiery source of God’s power and wrath.</note>, from which movement originally arises, is changed into triumphant joy.

20. Therefore, it is a very unjust thing that the world rages and raves so much—tyrannizing, reproaching, contemning, and reviling—when the gifts of God show themselves differently in every person, and not everyone has the same knowledge.

21. What can a person claim for themselves if it is not born within them? Yet this is not a matter of human choice—it does not happen just because a person desires or likes it—but rather, as his internal Heaven is, so too is God revealed within him.

<margin>☞</margin>
22. For God is not a God of destruction in the process of birth [or universal generation], but an illuminator, enkindler, and nourisher. Every creature has its own center within itself, whether it lives in the holiness of God or in the anger of God; *nevertheless, God will be revealed in all creatures.*

23. If the world were not so blind, it might recognize the wonderful Being of God in all creatures. But since it rages and raves as it does, it acts entirely against itself and against the Holy Spirit of God. One day, they shall be *astonished* by that light. They will not be able to stop the Son whom the laboring <note>original: "travelling"</note> Mother brings forth in her old age, for the heavens declare this.

24. God shall enlighten him despite all the raging and raving of the Devil, and his light [splendor or glory] shall reach from the East to the West. I do not write this of my own accord, but I only foresee that this is near and shall come to pass.

<margin>* Dr. Balthasar Walter. *Forty Questions*.</margin>
25. I would have gladly pleased you at this time with what I promised, in which everything touched upon and hinted at here might be clearly explained—including what that good and well-known *Doctor <note>Balthasar Walter was a chemist and physician who was a key supporter of Böhme; he famously traveled the world seeking wisdom before finding it in Böhme’s writings.</note> requested concerning the origin, being, life, and purpose <note>original: "drift"</note> of man and his soul, as well as his final end. However, it is not yet finished.

26. For the gifts that were once given to me by God are not dead and gone; although they were hidden by the Devil and the world, they now often appear and show themselves to be even deeper and more wonderful.

27. And very shortly (God willing), you shall receive something of this; for a higher beginning has been made toward it, especially concerning the ***THREE PRINCIPLES*** of the

<header>The twenty-sixth Epistle.</header>

# THE TWENTY-SIXTH EPISTLE.
### TO
<margin>* Carel von Endern <note>original: "Carel von Endern," the German name of the nobleman to whom Böhme is writing.</note></margin>
## *CHARLES OF ENDERN.

---

<margin>1.</margin>
The love in the heart of God the Father, and the light of His power in the life of Jesus Christ, be our refreshment. May it help us toward the new birth, so that the true Image <note>original: "Image," referring to the divine likeness or "new man" within the believer</note> may appear for God’s honor and His wondrous deeds. May it cause the fair sprout of His Lily <note>original: "Lilly," a recurring symbol in Böhme’s work representing the spiritual blossoming of the soul and the coming of a peaceful, divine age</note> to grow in us, in the Paradisiacal Garden of Jesus Christ.

2. Noble and Right Honorable Sir: with my humble and most willing service, and hearty wishes for all your temporal and eternal prosperity. I have received by your messenger a measure of grain <note>original: "Corne," which in 17th-century English referred to any cereal crop like rye or wheat</note> that you sent to me, and I thank you most kindly. I pray that God Almighty blesses you in abundance for it.

<margin>☛</margin>
3. Noble Sir, because you are so *humble* and do not look upon or regard what the world does—and because you do not value the subtleness (or clever pride) <note>original: "pert prudency," meaning an arrogant or self-important worldly wisdom</note> of the high-ranking, but instead seek and labor after what the Lord of Heaven builds (even though it appears as foolishness to this world)—it seems good in His sight to carry out His work through humble, low, plain, and *childlike* people. He does this so that He alone might be exalted, and no man might have anything to boast about. You, likewise, may surely hope for this same knowledge, which is more beautiful and excellent than all the pomp and riches of the world. For everything that is temporal leaves and forsakes a person, but the honorable garland (or trophy) of Christ does not forsake a person in death. Instead, it brings them to the heavenly, triumphant *host of angels*, into their true, eternal, native country.

4. Now, since we know and understand exceedingly well that we are only pilgrims in this world, staying in a strange lodging in very great

<summary>Jakob Böhme writes to the nobleman Charles of Endern, thanking him for a gift of grain and praising his humility. He emphasizes that spiritual knowledge is granted to the humble and childlike rather than the prideful, leading them to their true eternal home.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Charles of Endern, new birth, Lily of Christ, humility, spiritual wisdom, pilgrims, heavenly host</keywords>

--- Page 200 ---
<meta>This page continues the twenty-sixth epistle from Jakob Böhme to the nobleman Charles of Endern. Böhme is explaining why worldly wisdom fails to grasp spiritual truth and encourages the nobleman to continue his spiritual pursuit.</meta>

<page-num>182</page-num>
<header>The twenty-sixth Epistle.</header>

danger, lying captive in a sore and hard prison, and must continually fear and expect death; therefore, Noble *Sir*, you do very well and deal more wisely than the prudent of the world, in that you look about you and endeavor after your *eternal native country*, and not after the pomp, power, and riches of this world, as generally the high and great ones do.

5. I have no doubt that you shall herein obtain a very fair garland from the *Virgin* of the eternal wisdom of God; which, if it comes to pass, will be of more value and acceptance to you than all temporal riches and this whole world with all its being and splendor. If I had no effective knowledge of this <gloss>Crown or Trophy of Sophia</gloss> <note>original: "Sophia," the Greek word for wisdom, personified by Böhme as a divine feminine figure representing God's wisdom.</note>, I would not write; for from the "wise" of this world (who only out of self-pride, without God’s spirit, claim and attribute all knowledge and understanding to themselves), I have received not only poor thanks, but also nothing else but derision and scorn. At this I do rejoice, rather in that I bear reproach for the sake of the *name* and knowledge of *God*.

<margin>☛</margin>
6. For if my knowledge had been gotten in *their school*, then they would embrace and love their own; but because it is from another school, they do not know it. Moreover, they despise it, as they have done unto all the Prophets, *Christ*, and his *Apostles*. This shall not trouble or turn me aside; but as I have begun, I will depend on my God and Creator with the greater earnestness and fixed resolution; I will commit myself unto Him—let Him do with me what He pleases.

7. I attribute no wisdom unto myself; moreover, I rely not upon any purpose or intention of reason; for I see most plainly, and find it as clear as the sun, that God works in a completely different way.

8. Therefore, if we deal in a childlike manner and not in our reason (but only depend on Him with true desire and right earnestness, and put all our confidence in Him), then we obtain the noble <term>Virgin of his wisdom</term> <note>Böhme uses the "Virgin of Wisdom" to describe a state of spiritual purity and divine insight that is granted to the humble rather than the intellectually arrogant.</note> sooner than through our sharp inventions and acute reasonings. For when she comes, she brings true wisdom and heavenly understanding along with her, and without her, I know nothing.

9. But seeing you have a great delight to read such writings concerning the highest being (and as I hope it is God’s providence), therefore I will not withhold from you what the highest good has entrusted to me. Shortly, I will send you something that is more complete; for a very wonderful and excellent book has been begun concerning *the life of man*; which, if the Lord pleases to continue His hand over me, shall clearly reveal what man is, and what he has to do to obtain the highest good.

<meta>catchword: 10. For</meta>

<summary>Böhme encourages Charles of Endern to prioritize his "eternal native country" over worldly power, explaining that true wisdom (Sophia) is found through childlike faith rather than academic or "rational" pride. He also mentions he is currently writing a new book about the nature and purpose of human life.</summary>

<page-num>183</page-num>
<header>The twenty-sixth Epistle.</header>

## The twenty-sixth Epistle.

10. For it proceeds very clearly through the three *Principles* <note>original: "Principles," referring to Böhme's cosmological concept of the three worlds: the dark world of God's fire/wrath, the light world of God's love, and the outer physical world.</note>; it shows the entire foundation so that a person (unless they willfully choose to be blind) may *know God, the Kingdom of Heaven, and also themselves*. It also addresses our tragic fall and our restoration in the life of *Jesus Christ*. It will especially discuss the beautiful *Lilies* <note>original: "Lillies," Böhme's symbol for the spiritual flowering and renewal of the world in the final age.</note> which God will bestow upon the *latter world*, which will be very pleasant to read.

11. And though I do not yet fully understand the entire foundation of what it shall be, I see *it* in a great depth. I hope, if God grants enough grace, not to give up until it is finished. I also hope to finish the promised writings for the future, which are now *held back* by the Oppressor, as well as [ <insert>my commentary</insert> ] on *Moses*. In these, the great wonders of God shall be clearly revealed to the light of day—gifts which God will freely bestow on the latter world.

12. However, at present everything is in *Babel* <note>original: "Babel," Böhme's term for the confused, divided, and corrupt institutional church and state.</note>, and there shall be a great tearing apart. Yet, let no one despair; for just as God helped the children of *Israel* with consolation during the *Babylonian* captivity and sent them *Prophets*, even so shall *Lilies* now grow up in the midst of thorns—and this is wonderful.

13. Also, no one should think that the total destruction of the city *Babel* is happening right now. There will truly be an incredibly great *split*—one that people today do not believe possible. For the *Antichrist* is not *completely* revealed, though certainly in part.

14. People will assume they have completely rooted him out, and after some sorrow, great joy will follow. They will make laws and covenants, along with severe, strict articles of religion—yet for the *most part*, these will be for the advancement of their own honor and power. People will assume that the Holy Spirit of heaven is speaking and that there is now a *golden age*. Yet it is full of God's anger and is still in *Babel*, and the true essence of the right life in Christ is not yet within it.

15. Also, the *rider* on the *pale horse* <note>original: "Pale Horse," a reference to Revelation 6:8, symbolizing death and judgment.</note> shall follow and cut down *many* with the sickle. Yet, in the meantime, the *Lily* springs up among the wonders, against which the last *Antichrist* shall raise *persecution*. It is then that *his end* comes, for the appearance of the Lord terrifies him.

<margin>Authority or permission.</margin>
16. And then *Babel* burns up in the zeal and anger of God, and the outcome is wonderful. I have no <margin>authority or permission</margin> to write more plainly about this. Yet, at that time, my writings shall be very useful; for a time is coming from the LORD which does not originate from the *starry heavens* <note>original: "Starry Heaven," referring to the influence of astral/sidereal cycles or natural astrology, as opposed to direct divine intervention.</note>.

17. Blessed is the person who seeks the Lord with total earnestness, for He shall not be found in historical records <note>original: "History," meaning a mere intellectual or literal understanding of scripture and history without spiritual experience.</note> but in true trust <note>original: "affiance," meaning faith or reliance.</note>, and in the right

<summary>Böhme predicts a time of great upheaval and religious strife, warning that while people may think they have defeated the "Antichrist," true spiritual renewal (the "Lily") will only come after a period of divine judgment on "Babel."</summary>

<page-num>184</page-num>
<header>The twenty-sixth Epistle.</header>

True resignation <note>original: "resignation," meaning a total spiritual surrender or yielding of the self-will to God's will—a central theme in Böhme’s theology.</note> into the life and into the doctrine of Christ: there the Holy Spirit shall appear with wonders and powers which **Babel** <note>original: "Babel," Böhme's term for the confused and corrupt institutional church structures of his day.</note> currently does not believe in its own inventions [forged hypocrisies]. Nevertheless, it is certainly coming and is already underway, though still hidden from the world.

18. I have sent the entire work of the *Second Book* <note>Referring to his second major treatise, *The Three Principles of the Divine Essence* (1619).</note> to you by **Mr. Fabian**, but I do not know whether you have received it; since that time, because of my journey, I have not spoken with **Mr. Fabian**. If you have not received it, you may ask him for it. And so, I entrust you to the gentle love in the life of *Jesus Christ, the Son of God*.

->*Written in haste: Görlitz, Friday before Advent, 1619.*<-

# THE TWENTY-SEVENTH LETTER.

->TO<-

<margin>* Karl von Endern.</margin>
## **CHARLES von ENDERN.**

---

1. **N**OBLE, Right Honorable Sir, I offer my humble, ready, and willing service, with all cordial wishes for God's love and grace for your "new creature" <note>original: "new creature," a biblical reference to 2 Corinthians 5:17, meaning one who is spiritually reborn.</note> in the "new man" within the body of *Jesus Christ*. I also wish you all temporal prosperity and blessing for your earthly body.

2. <margin>Pointing hand symbol</margin> I have considered your noble heart and mind, which is kindled and inflamed with love not only toward God, but likewise toward His children. This has greatly rejoiced me in *Christ*, and I must especially acknowledge your true earnestness and zeal. For you have put much effort into my few writings of the first part and disregarded the murderous (or confusing) cries of the Oppressor <note>original: "the Oppressor," likely referring to Gregorius Richter, the primary clergyman in Görlitz who persecuted Böhme and called for his books to be suppressed.</note>, seeking them out yourself *to read them* and *to copy them out with your own hand*.

3. This persuades me that God has opened a little gate [of His wisdom] to you, which was firmly shut to the Oppressor. He sought for it through <term>Art</term> <note>original: "Art," referring here to academic learning or intellectual study without spiritual inspiration.</note> and pride, and therefore it was hidden from him. He was offended by the "hand of the writer" <note>Referring to Böhme’s lack of formal education and his status as a simple shoemaker.</note> and did not consider what is written in the Holy Scripture: *My power is made perfect in weakness*; and how *Christ* thanked His Father that He had hidden these things from the prudent and wise and revealed them to babes. And He said further, *Yes, Father! For so it has pleased You*.

4. I am also truly persuaded and convinced in my spirit that you have not done this out of any curiosity, but as becomes the children of God who seek the precious pearl <note>The "pearl of great price" from Matthew 13:46, representing divine wisdom or the Kingdom of God.</note> and are desirous 

<summary>Böhme begins a letter to Charles von Endern, praising the nobleman's dedication to studying his early manuscripts despite the opposition and mockery from institutional religious authorities.</summary>
<keywords>Karl von Endern, new man, Oppressor, Gregorius Richter, weakness, wisdom, precious pearl, copying manuscripts</keywords>

--- Page 204 ---
<meta>This page continues the twenty-seventh epistle from Jakob Böhme to the nobleman Charles von Endern. Böhme is describing the nature of Divine Wisdom (the "Pearl") and explaining his spiritual obligation to write, despite his lack of formal education and the opposition he faces from local authorities.</meta>

<page-num>180</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Seventh Letter.</header>

...of it; which if it be found, is the most precious *Jewel*, which man loves far beyond his earthly life. For it is greater than the world, and more fair, beautiful, and excellent than the **Sun**. It rejoices man in tribulation and brings him out of darkness into the light; it gives him a *certain* spirit of hope in **God**, and leads him upon the *right* path. It goes along with him *into death*, and brings him forth to life out of death; it quells the anguish of **Hell**, and it is everywhere *his light*. It is **God’s** friend in His love; it affords him *reason* and *discretion* to govern his *earthly body*; it leads him from the false and evil way; and whoever does obtain and keep it, it crowns him with its garland.

<margin>☛</margin>
5. Therefore, noble Sir, I have no other cause to write to you but from a desire of sincere love toward the children of **God**, so that I might refresh and recreate myself with them. I do it not for temporal goods or gifts (although you have offered your kind and loving favors to me, even though I am a *stranger*, in a courteous and friendly manner), but for the hope of *Israel*, so that I may greatly rejoice with the children of **God** in *the life to come*. My labor, which I undergo here in love toward them, shall then be well rewarded when I shall so rejoice with my brethren, and *everyone’s work shall follow them* <note>A reference to Revelation 14:13: "their works do follow them."</note>.

<margin>☛</margin>
6. Therefore, I am in right earnest, since a *sparkle* of the precious *Pearl* <note>The "Pearl" is Böhme's frequent metaphor for Divine Wisdom or the inner light of Christ.</note> is given to me, and **Christ** faithfully warns us *not to put it under the table, or to bury it in the Earth* <note>References to the parables of the bushel (Matthew 5:15) and the talents (Matthew 25:25).</note>. Thereupon, we ought not *to fear man so much, who can only kill the body and then is able to do no more; rather, we ought to fear Him who can destroy both body and soul and cast them into* **Hell.**

7. And though in my time I shall receive but *little thanks* from some who love their belly more than the Kingdom of **Heaven**, yet my writings have their gifts and stand for their time. For they have a very precious, worthy, and earnest birth and descent; and when I consider myself in my poor, low, unlearned, and *simple person*, I then *wonder more* than my very adversary does.

8. But seeing I know <term>experientially</term> <note>original: "Experimentally," meaning through direct spiritual experience and trial rather than theoretical study.</note> in *power* and *light* that it is a mere gift of **God**, who also gives me a driving will for it—that I must write what I *know and see*—therefore I will obey **God** rather than man. I do this lest my office and stewardship be taken *away* from me again and given to another, which would eternally grieve me.

9. But seeing, Sir, that you have obtained a longing and delight to read the same—and that (as I truly hope) from the providence and appointment of **God**—therefore I shall not conceal it from you. For **God** the Most High has called you, through His wonderful counsel, to publish the first work, when I thought the
->Oppressor<-

<summary>Böhme explains that his spiritual writings are a gift from God which he is duty-bound to share, regardless of his own lack of education or the threats of his "adversary." He praises the nobleman for his role in publishing Böhme's first work through divine providence.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the twenty-seventh epistle from Jakob Böhme to the nobleman Charles von Endern. Böhme is describing his spiritual obligation to write, despite the opposition he faces from local authorities and his own feelings of inadequacy.</meta>

<page-num>187</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Seventh Letter.</header>

the oppressor had devoured it; but even then it sprang forth as a green twig, completely unknown to me.

10. And though I know nothing of myself regarding what God is about to do—and his counsel and path, which he will take, are hidden from me, and I can say nothing of my own accord—the oppressor might also accuse me of *pedantic pride* <note>original: "Pedentick Pride"</note>, suggesting that I would only arrogantly boast of my small gifts (which were given to me by grace) and thereby present the thoughts and imaginations of my heart for my own vainglory and boasting.

<margin>p</margin>
11. Yet I declare in the presence of God and testify before his judgment (where all things shall appear, and everyone shall give an account of his deeds) that I myself *know* not what has happened to me, or how it goes with me, except only that I have a compelling will <note>original: "driving will"; Böhme refers to a spiritual impulse or inner necessity to write.</note>.

12. Also, I do not know what I shall write; for when I write, the *Spirit dictates the same to me* in great, wonderful knowledge, so that I often cannot tell whether I (as to my spirit) *am in this world or not*. At this, I do exceedingly rejoice; and therein, sure and certain knowledge is imparted to me.

13. And the more I seek, the more I find, and always deeper, so that I many times consider my sinful person too weak and unworthy to set upon such high *mysteries* <note>original: "Mysteries"; Böhme's term for the deep spiritual truths regarding God and creation.</note>. Then the spirit sets up my banner and says, "*Arise! You shall live therein eternally and be crowned with it; why are you amazed?*"

14. Therefore, noble Sir, I give you to understand in a few words the ground and cause both of the will and the investigation in my writings. If you have a mind to read anything in them, I leave it to your choice. I send you now what was written formerly, when I was with you—namely, from the beginning of the twenty-second chapter to the end—where in very deed the noble coral <note>original: "noble Corall"; Böhme often uses gemstones like coral and pearl to symbolize hidden spiritual value.</note> is opened, and my mind shows me that you shall take liking and delight in it. Herein also you shall obtain *a little pearl* <note>original: "little Pearle"; a recurring Böhmean metaphor for Divine Wisdom or the kingdom of heaven within.</note>, if your mind is approved and directed toward God.

15. The rest (which are about thirty sheets <note>original: "Sheets"; Böhme refers to manuscript pages or gatherings of paper.</note>) are with our known and good friend; he shall give them to you. Very high and deep things are contained in them, and they are very worthy of *preservation*. You shall likewise have them if you please to read them.

16. And although I set myself to keep these things somewhat quiet and secret, yet it is manifest, for I hear that haughty people desire them. By these people, the Enemy (being a destroyer) might make them disappear; for I know well what manner of enemy the Devil is, who gainsays and opposes me. Therefore, I entreat you to deal wisely and carefully; I shall quickly (if need require) call for them.

<summary>Jakob Böhme explains that his writings are dictated by the Spirit rather than his own intellect and shares specific manuscripts with Charles von Endern. He warns the nobleman to handle these "mysteries" with care to protect them from "haughty people" and the Devil's opposition.</summary>

17. Shortly, if God permits, more and deeper things shall be written concerning *Moses* and the *Prophets*; and then, finally, regarding the whole Tree of Life in the *Being of all beings* <note>original: "Being of all Beings"; Böhme uses this phrase to refer to the divine essence or the fundamental reality of God from which all creation originates.</note>, showing how all things begin and end, and into what state of being each thing appears and comes to light in this world.

18. I hope that the beautiful and excellent lily <note>The "Lily" is a recurring symbol in Böhme’s writing, representing a future age of spiritual peace, purity, and the blossoming of divine wisdom within humanity.</note> promised by God shall spring forth in the *North* <note>Böhme frequently predicted a spiritual reformation or "blossoming" beginning in the North, which many of his followers interpreted as a reference to Northern Europe or Germany.</note> in his own spirit among the children of God's love in *Christ*. For we find a very precious pearl still springing forth—not known to me now in the body, but blooming in the mind—and so, I commend you to the pleasant love of God.

<header>The Twenty-Eighth Epistle.</header>

# THE
# TWENTY-EIGHTH EPISTLE.

## TO
<margin>* Carol von Endern.</margin>
## *CHARLES of ENDERN.

->*The Light, Salvation, and eternal power, flowing from the heart of* **Jesus Christ**, *be our refreshment.*<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
Noble, right Honorable Sir: I offer first all cordial wishes for your divine salvation and, along with that, all prosperity. I cannot neglect to salute you with this short epistle, seeing that your courteous and generous heart has bestowed a measure of corn <note>original: "Measure of Corne"; in the 17th century, "corn" referred to any cereal grain (like wheat or rye). Such gifts were vital support for Böhme, who often faced financial hardship due to his controversial writings.</note> upon me. I gladly received it and return many thanks for it; and I shall pray to God—the Creator and Preserver of all things, in whose power all things are—to bless you in abundance for it.

2. And although I have *not* deserved it from you, and am but a stranger to you, I acknowledge your kind heart in this toward the children of God. Because you are so very humble for the sake of God and His Kingdom, and because from your high status in this world you cast yourself, with your favor and love, into the simple humility of God's children, I recognize this to be *the fear of God*. It shows a desire for communion with the children of God; in which [communion and fellowship] we are all one body in God through *Christ*, consisting of many members <note>original: "members"; Böhme is referencing the New Testament concept (1 Corinthians 12) where individual believers are described as different parts or "members" of the single Body of Christ.</note> and creatures.

<summary>Jakob Böhme writes to the nobleman Charles von Endern to thank him for a gift of grain, praising von Endern for his humility and his willingness to treat a social inferior as a brother in Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Charles von Endern, gratitude, humility, Body of Christ, charity, spiritual fellowship</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the twenty-eighth epistle from Jakob Böhme to the nobleman Charles von Endern. Having thanked von Endern for his charity on the previous page, Böhme now explains the spiritual connection created by such acts of kindness.</meta>

<page-num>190</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Eighth Epistle.</header>

## The Twenty-Eighth Epistle.

3. At this we should not only greatly rejoice, but resign ourselves up to him in one unified love, so that his power may be effective and abound in us, and his Kingdom may be conceived and brought forth in us, and that we might be made mutual partakers of his essence in one shared knowledge.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Or, Draw upon the kind heart.</margin>
4. And it is not only plainly manifest to us in the Holy Scripture, but also in the light of nature <note>original: "light of nature"; in 17th-century philosophy, this refers to the innate human capacity for reason and understanding of the natural world, separate from divine revelation.</note>, that if a man does good to another—especially if it proceeds from a kind, charitable goodwill, heart, and inclination—the heart, spirit, and mind of the one who has received the good is in turn inclined in favor and love toward his kind friend, and wishes all blessings and good things for him. 

As he presents his own needs and condition before God, he likewise brings the will, mind, and love of his faithful friend’s condition before and into God. In the power of God, this *provides* the *kind heart* with rich and abundant blessing, not only for this earthly life. Beyond this, a path and way is made into the Kingdom of God, so that if he turns himself to God and desires His love and grace, then his *friend’s love* (which has already brought him before God) helps him to press and *force his way* to God. It enables him to wrestle with the *<term>Turba</term>* <note>original: "Turba"; a Latin term used by Böhme to describe the spirit of "turmoil" or "confusion," specifically the destructive power of divine wrath or the chaotic element in nature that holds the soul captive.</note>—the corrupt infection [or poisonous desire] in which the Devil holds us captive—and so assists him to destroy the boundary or vessel of the *Anger* <note>original: "Anger"; here referring to the "Wrath of God," a central concept in Böhme’s theology representing a primal, consuming fire that must be transformed into Love.</note>. For my part, I am not only obligated, but also completely desirous and willing to do this.

5. Now, because God has given to me out of his kind grace a deep and high knowledge of his will and being, I am ready and willing to *serve you* both with my prayers *for you* toward God, and also with what I shall set down in writing. So far as you may recognize that it is from God (as I am truly persuaded it is) and that you have a sincere desire to read it, I shall not conceal it from you; I will also be eager to have mutual and personal conversation with you.

6. And if you should find any misunderstanding in those writings, I would sufficiently clarify and inform you about it. Or, if anything should seem too difficult to be understood, I would willingly put it into a *simpler explanation*. Or, if you are pleased to put any question that is higher or more profound to me, I hope to God (to whose will I commit all things) that it will be granted to me to reveal it. I shall endeavor carefully in all ways, with prayers to God, that I may again show myself in acceptable services of love toward you. I hope in God that he will not deny me this, as my heart is wholly given up to it, and does endeavor that it

<header>The Twenty-Ninth Epistle.</header>

# THE TWENTY-NINTH EPISTLE.

## TO 
<margin>* Carol van Endern.</margin>
## * CHARLES OF ENDERN.

1. **R**ight Honorable and beloved in *Christ*; I wish to you the rich and effective blessing of God, in His power, so that the Pearl's ground <note>original: "Pearls ground"; in Böhme’s theology, "ground" refers to the underlying foundation or essence of the soul where divine wisdom (the Pearl) is realized</note> in the life of *Jesus Christ* may be revealed <note>original: "manifest"</note> in the divine, shining light, within your own light of life; and that it may bring forth an abundance of fruit for divine contemplation and eternal joy.

2. Just as I have always known you to be a well-wisher to the study of wisdom, my whole desire at present (as an expression of thankfulness for the many courtesies and favors I have received) is that I might be able to share with you what the Most High has given me in a short time out of His wellspring of grace.

3. And although I have no ability to do it whenever I wish, yet my mind is so completely ignited in its center that I would very heartily and readily share it with my brothers in *Christ*; and I continually entreat the *Lord* that He would be pleased to open the hearts of men so that they might understand it, and that it may come to a truly living <note>original: "quick [essentiall]"</note> and active power <note>original: "operation"</note> within them.

4. And from a sincere intention, I would not conceal from you that (since the New Year), at the request of some learned men and people of high standing, I have written a treatise on *Election* <term>Election: The theological doctrine concerning God's choice of those who are to be saved</term> or of God's will concerning humanity. I have explained and expanded upon it from such a foundation <note>original: "ground"</note> that all *mysteries* of both the outward, visible, elemental world and also of the hidden, spiritual world may be discerned within it; and for that purpose, I have in a special 
<margin>† Or, texts.</margin>
manner cited the † passages of Holy Scripture which speak of...

<meta>This page continues the twenty-ninth epistle from Jakob Böhme to Charles of Endern. Böhme is describing his recently completed treatise on the doctrine of Election, explaining how he has reconciled the seemingly contradictory aspects of God's will regarding the "hardening" of hearts.</meta>

<page-num>193</page-num>
<header>The Twenty-Ninth Epistle.</header>

...of God's will to harden [the heart], and then of His unwillingness to harden, and so tuned or harmonized them together that the right understanding and meaning of the same may be seen.

<margin>❦</margin> 5. And it is so proved and demonstrated that I hope in God it shall provide the means and occasion to take away that strife and controversy in the *churches*. It is known and manifest that the time is near at hand when the contentions about *religion* shall enter into the *Temperature* <note>original: "Temperature"; in Böhme’s philosophy, this refers to a state of perfect balance or harmony between opposing spiritual qualities, where conflict ceases</note>, though with the great ruination of the false kingdom in *Babel* <note>original: "Babel"; Böhme uses this term to represent the state of religious confusion, sectarianism, and the institutionalized church that he believed had replaced true spiritual life with hollow ritual and dogma</note>, which has set itself up in *Christ's* stead, along with *other great alterations*. Concerning these things, although men will hardly believe me now, they shall in a short time really appear and show themselves. And this, beloved sir, I would not omit to hint to you for your further consideration and Christian meditation, etc.

<page-num>394</page-num>
<header>The Thirtieth Epistle</header>

# THE
# THIRTIETH EPISTLE.
# IMMANUEL.
<note>original: "Immanuel"; Hebrew for "God is with us." This was a common greeting and motto used by Böhme to signify the presence of the divine within the believer.</note>

1. **M**y very Christian brother and friend: I offer all heartfelt wishes of *Divine Love* and further spiritual illumination, along with true perseverance, constancy, and patience—patiently to endure the *Cross of Christ*—as my opening greeting.

2. *God*, in the very beginning of your spiritual understanding, has *sealed* you with the mark of *Christ* for a further confirmation; He has *crowned* you to be a *champion*, so that you should work as a true steward <note>original: "owner"; likely referring to the biblical concept of a faithful householder or one who takes responsibility for God's work</note> in His *service*.

3. And I urge you as a *Christian* that you would, with *prayer* and supplication, commit your cause to *God* in patience and stand quietly under the *Cross of Christ*. Be diligent with the *talent* <note>original: "Talent"; referring to the spiritual gifts or abilities entrusted to a person, as in the biblical parable</note> entrusted to you; you shall see great wonders, and your *talent* will become more and more pleasant and beloved to you. For *Christ* has also taught us that we should *forsake* everything for His name's sake and cling only to Him; for He requires a pure, *resigned soul* <note>original: "resigned soul"; this refers to Böhme’s core concept of *Gelassenheit*, the total surrender of the individual self-will to God's will</note>, in which He will dwell.

4. <margin>☛</margin> You have no reason to be amazed or terrified; *God* knows well for what purpose He employs you. Commit yourself only to Him in *patience*, and strive against *reason* <note>original: "reason"; Böhme often contrasts human "reason"—which relies on worldly logic—with "faith" or "divine wisdom"</note> which denies and contradicts spiritual truths. In doing so, you will die to the *world* and live for *Christ*; then you will carry out your warfare correctly for the sake of His love, and from that gain the noble *crown* of *eternal life*, where we shall rejoice and enjoy one another’s company forever.

5. *God* has planted a *rose garden* for Himself in your *young heart*; take care that the *Devil* does not sow thorns and thistles there. A time will soon come when your rosebud shall bring forth its fruit. Do not strive to hold an *office* <note>original: "Office"; a position of authority or status</note>, but remain still before the Most High, doing whatever He desires of you. Let the *smoke* of the *Devil* pass away; rejoice instead at this *reproach* <note>original: "reproach"; the social shame or criticism faced for one's faith</note> that comes with the mark of *Christ*, etc.

<summary>In this letter, Böhme offers spiritual counsel to a "young heart," urging patience, perseverance under the "Cross of Christ," and the "resignation" of the self-will to God's purposes.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Immanuel, Cross of Christ, resignation, Gelassenheit, spiritual warfare, rose garden, talent, religious persecution</keywords>

<meta>This page begins the thirty-first epistle. Jakob Böhme addresses a "Dear Sir and Christian Brother," responding to a letter he received. He uses organic metaphors, such as the vine and the "Coral" (or Tree of Pearl), to describe the shared spiritual life and the growth of the "New Birth" within believers.</meta>

# THE
# ONE AND THIR-
# TIETH EPISTLE.

->**Our Salvation [is] in the life of Jesus**<-
->**Christ.**<-

---

<margin>1.</margin>
1. Dear Sir and Christian Brother: I offer all faithful, heartfelt, and sincere desires of my spirit for true Divine light, power, and knowledge, along with intimate joy in Divine contemplation and in our eternal fraternity in the life of Christ.

2. I have received the letter which you sent, and I rejoice in the Lord my God, who so richly and abundantly imparts His grace unto us and so opens our hearts that we, in the union <note>original: "conjecture"; here meaning the joining together or meeting of their respective spiritual gifts</note> of our gifts, desire to search into His wisdom and wonders.

<margin>☞ * Or, Tree of Pearl.</margin>
3. And your offered friendship is very pleasing and acceptable to me. According to what this letter implies, I acknowledge you as a springing and very eager branch on the Vine, Christ, and also as my fellow member and twig on this <term>Coral</term> <note>original: "Corall"; also called the "Tree of Pearl." Böhme uses this as a metaphor for the growth of the spiritual body or the "new man" within the believer.</note>. I wish, in the power of my knowledge, that it may be a true, constant, and immovable earnestness, as I have no doubt that the precious coralline branch of the New Birth is begotten in you out of Christ’s spirit and wisdom.

<margin>☞</margin>
4. Because of this, I would also gladly, with all my heart, impart my little sap, strength, and influence from God's gifts unto my fellow branches and twigs. I wish to help quicken and refresh them in my weak power and also receive enjoyment from their gifts, as we are obliged and bound to do for each other—both by the command of God and also by the right of Nature. I am driven toward this in an especial manner by my gifts, and for this cause I have spent much time and effort, yet with great desire, longing, and delight. It has been continually my earnest hope, and the very...

<summary>In this opening to his thirty-first epistle, Jakob Böhme welcomes a new correspondent into spiritual fellowship, using the metaphors of the Vine and the "Coral" to describe their shared growth in Christ. He expresses a deep desire to share his spiritual insights ("sap") with others for their mutual benefit and the glory of God.</summary>

<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Epistle 31, New Birth, spiritual growth, Vine of Christ, Tree of Pearl, Coral, mutual edification, Divine wisdom</keywords>

--- Page 214 ---
<meta>This page continues the thirty-first epistle. Jakob Böhme is explaining his background and the origins of his spiritual writings, emphasizing that his knowledge comes from divine illumination rather than academic training.</meta>

...drift and aim of my desire to serve my brothers in the Lord, in the *Vineyard of Christ*.

<margin>☞ |</margin>
5. And although I am a simple man [unskilled and inexperienced in *scholastic learning* and the arts; nor have I ever been trained to apply myself to the works of high masters or to comprehend great *mysteries* through my *reason*, but in my outward occupation I have been a *tradesman* <note>Böhme was a master shoemaker by trade.</note>, by which I have honestly supported myself for a long time]; yet my inward occupation and practice has, with very earnest and strong desire, entered into the *mortification of my sinful nature* <note>original: "mortification of my sinfull man"; the spiritual practice of "killing off" the selfish desires of the flesh.</note> inherited from the corrupt *Adam*. I sought how I might die to my self-centeredness and self-will in the death of *Christ*, and arise in His will to a new spirit and a will of *Divine* renovation of mind and understanding.

<margin>* Presumed.</margin>
6. Therefore, I once so strongly and fixedly *resolved rather to forgo my earthly life than to desist from this purpose and these struggles*; and what I have suffered in them and because of them, the Lord knows, who led me through His judgment of my sins. But afterward, He crowned me with the fairest, triumphant view of His Divine Kingdom of joy. To express this, no pen is sufficient; but I willingly grant <note>original: "beteeme"</note> and heartily wish it for the *reader of this epistle* and for all the children of God.

7. And from this triumphant light [or joyful, convincing illumination from God] that has been given to me, I have written for many years. For I obtained so much grace through it as to see and know my own book—which I myself am, *namely* <note>original: "Viz."; an abbreviation for the Latin *videlicet*, meaning "namely" or "that is to say."</note> the image of God. Moreover, I was able to behold and truly contemplate the center of all beings and to understand the *Formed Word of God*. I also came to understand the origin and meaning of the compacted, framed, or formed language of the senses <note>original: "Sensuall tongue"; referring to the "language of nature" or the way physical reality expresses divine properties.</note> and all its properties, as well as the mental, unformed, *holy language*. In this, I have written many profound books, which will be partly incomprehensible to *reason* without God's light.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. Although I, as a weak earthly instrument in my external self, could only with great difficulty at first (being an untrained and unlearned man) comprehend and bring this high work to the understanding [or fitly express it for the understanding]. This can be seen in the *Aurora* <note>Böhme’s first major work, *Aurora: The Root of Day-Spring*, written in 1612.</note>, which is the first part of my writings. Also, I did not intend to make it known to any man; instead, I wrote it as a personal record for myself of that very wonderful knowledge, contemplation, and felt illumination. And though the spirit signaled for what purpose it was intended, yet reason—*namely*, the external man—could not comprehend it, but only saw its own unworthiness and lowliness. I kept these writings (*namely*, the *Aurora*) by me, until at last I spoke of them to someone, by whose means they came before the *learned*, who immediately...

<summary>Jakob Böhme describes himself as an unlearned tradesman who received divine illumination after struggling to "die" to his sinful nature. He explains that his writings, including his first book *Aurora*, arose from this spiritual experience rather than academic study.</summary>
<keywords>Jakob Böhme, Aurora, tradesman, divine illumination, mortification, self-will, Image of God, Formed Word, holy tongue, scholastic learning</keywords>
