<meta>This page continues the thirty-first epistle from Jakob Böhme. He is discussing the persecution he faced regarding his early writings and how his spiritual journey has evolved.</meta>

<page-num>147</page-num>
# The first and third Epistles

<warning>The top portion of the page is heavily shadowed and dark, making the first few lines difficult to read.</warning>

...manifestly studied and endeavored that it might be taken from me. There, Satan thought to make a bonfire of it, and thereby to scandalize and cast a hatred <note>original: "Odium"</note> upon my person. Therefore, I have suffered much for the sake of Christ my Lord, so that I might follow Him correctly in His process.

<margin>☞</margin>
9. But just as it happened to the Devil regarding Christ, so it also happened to him regarding my writing. For he who desired and intended to persecute them actually published them, and brought me into a greater, stronger, and more serious spiritual exercise. Through this, I was more practiced in judgment and stood stronger in opposing the Devil and his violent assaults in the *serpentine <term>essence</term> <note>original: "Ens"; a philosophical and alchemical term referring to the fundamental nature, being, or substance of a thing.</note> of the earthly Adam*. I did more and more disclose and break open the *gates of the deep* (or hidden depths of eternity) and have come into the clear light. Consequently, my writings are read with delight both far and near, and copied by many *very learned doctors* and other persons of high, noble, and low descent—and this happened entirely without my own promotion or effort, but through God's providence.

<margin>* In a better understanding.</margin>
10. I would have very willingly shared some of them with you now, but I do not have them at hand and cannot get them so quickly at present. It is true that some treatises are written, so I hope many a hungry soul shall be refreshed; for the later writings are much clearer and better *understood than the first ones you mentioned to me. But if you would take the trouble you spoke of and come to me yourself, and in the fear of God converse with me in Divine wisdom, I shall be glad. You may take your opportunity with me as you please, for I am continually occupied in writing. Therefore, I have laid aside my trade <note>Böhme was a master shoemaker but eventually left his craft to focus entirely on his theological and mystical writing.</note> to serve God and my brother in this *calling*, and to receive my reward in heaven—even though I shall incur displeasure and ungrateful hatred at the hands of *Babel* <note>Böhme used "Babel" to refer to the corrupt, worldly religious institutions and the confusion of competing dogmas in his time.</note> and the *Antichrist*.

<margin>† In the imitation of Christ.</margin>
11. I return many thanks for Mr. *Nagel’s* greetings and those of other Christian fellow-members who are in the † *pilgrimage of Christ*, as I am informed. When your circumstances allow, greet them from me again most kindly. Mr. *Elias Teikman* has not come to me yet, nor do I know where he is. Mr. *Balthasar Walter* has often mentioned him with love, but I do not know him, except in the spirit, for I have only heard of him from others.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Note.</margin>
12. Concerning my condition, I assure you at your request that I am (blessed be God) very well at present. But I see in the Spirit a great persecution and change approaching the country and people. It is very near at hand, as is mentioned and foretold in my writings, and it is high time to go out and flee from *Babel*. Therefore, I cannot speak of any rest or settlement; * only all will be full of misery, robberies, murders, and unheard-of devilishness in Christen-

<meta>catchword: Christen-</meta>

<summary>Böhme describes how attempts to suppress his writings only served to spread them further and strengthen his spiritual resolve. He also predicts a coming period of great social and religious upheaval, urging readers to distance themselves from corrupt institutions.</summary>

# THE THIRTY-SECOND EPISTLE.

---
->Our salvation consists in the effective, working love of Jesus Christ within us.<-
---

<margin>1.</margin> **M**y very loving and Christian friend; I wish you the highest peace with the hearty love of a fellow member of Christ. I work in the desire that the true sun of the effective love of Jesus Christ may continually arise and shine in your *spirit, soul, and body*.

<margin>2.</margin> Your letter dated the 24th of January, I received fourteen days after Easter; I rejoice to see in it that you are a thirsty, fervent, and eager seeker and lover of the true foundation [of the knowledge of divine mysteries] which I perceive you have sought and searched for with diligence.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Or, living spark.</margin>
<margin>† Or, constellation.</margin>
<margin>3.</margin> That my writings have come into your hands and please you is certainly caused by the divine appointment of God; who brings lovers to [that which they love] and often uses strange means by which He satisfies the desire of those who love a thing. He feeds them with His gifts [and graces] and puts an essence <note>original: "Ens"; a Latin term for 'being' or 'substance'. Böhme uses it to describe a spiritual seed or living spark.</note> of the true fire into their love so that it may burn correctly. You may rest assured that if you continue your constancy in love to the *truth*, it will open, reveal, and manifest itself to you in its flaming love and make itself *certainly known*. However, the searching for it must be begun correctly; for we do not attain the true foundation of divine knowledge by the sharp searching and intellectual speculation of our reason from the outside. Instead, the searching must begin *from within*, in the hunger of the soul; for reason penetrates no further than its own constellation <note>original: "Astrum"; referring to the governing influences or laws of the physical world.</note> of the outward world, from which reason has its origin.

<margin>4.</margin> But the soul searches its own constellation <note>original: "Astrum"</note>, namely, in the in-
<meta>catchword: ward</meta>

<summary>In this opening of his 32nd epistle, Jakob Böhme encourages a new reader, explaining that true divine knowledge is found through internal spiritual hunger rather than external intellectual reasoning.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the thirty-second epistle, where Böhme explains the relationship between the visible world and the spiritual reality behind it. He provides guidance on how a soul can achieve divine enlightenment through humility and resignation of the self-will.</meta>

<page-num>200</page-num>
<header>The Thirty-Second Epistle.</header>

...inward spiritual world from which this visible world has its origin, outflow, and production, and where its ground [and foundation] stands.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum"</note></margin>
5. But if the soul wishes to search its own <term>Astrum</term> <note>original: "Astrum"; Latin for "star," here referring to a person's spiritual constellation or inner destiny.</note> [or spiritual constellation]—namely, the *Great Mystery* [or the eternal divine nature]—it must first completely yield all its power and its will to divine love and grace. It must become like a child and turn toward its center through repentance, desiring to do only what the Spirit of God is pleased to explore through it [or employ it for].

6. When it has yielded and resigned itself in this way—seeking nothing but God and its own salvation, as well as how it may serve and love its neighbor—and then finds within itself a desire for both divine and natural knowledge, it may be certain that it is being *drawn* [and inclined] *to it by God*. Then it may safely search and find the "deep ground" mentioned in my writings.

7. For the Spirit of God searches through that soul and eventually brings it into the depths of the deity, as Saint Paul says: *The Spirit searches all things, even the deep things of God.*

<margin>☞</margin>
8. Dear sir, it is a simple, childlike way that leads to the highest wisdom, though the world does not know it. You do not need to seek wisdom in remote places or travel into foreign countries for it; she stands at the door of your soul and knocks. If she finds an empty, resigned, and free place in the soul, she will truly reveal herself there, rejoicing more than the sun does in the elements. If the soul yields itself up to be fully possessed by wisdom, she penetrates it with her flaming fire of love and unlocks all mysteries to the soul.

9. Sir, you may perhaps wonder how a plain layman could come to understand such high mysteries, having never read them nor heard them from any man. But, dear sir, I tell you that what you have seen in my writings is *only a glimpse of the mysteries*, for a person cannot write them down. If God considers you worthy to have the light kindled in your soul, you would see, taste, smell, feel, and hear unspeakable words of God concerning this knowledge. There is the true <term>Theosophical School of Pentecost</term> <note>Pentecost refers to the biblical descent of the Holy Spirit; Böhme uses this term to describe direct, divine inspiration and teaching from God.</note> where the soul is taught † by God.

<margin>† From or by.</margin>
10. After this, there is no longer any need for searching or painful toiling [about it], for all gates stand open. Even a very simple, humble person may attain it if they do not hinder themselves with their own self-will [imaginations] and efforts; for it already lies within a person and only needs to be awakened [stirred up or brought to life] by the Spirit of God.

11. In my talent [or writings] (as I was able in my simplicity...

He describes the "School of Pentecost" as an internal state where God directly reveals the mysteries of the universe to the humble seeker.</summary>

## The Thirty-Second Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>* That is, the book titled *The Way to Christ*, containing a treatise on Repentance, Resignation (or Self-Denial), and Regeneration.</margin>
...able to describe it), you shall easily find the way to it; especially in this annexed *book*, which also comes from my own efforts <note>original: "Talent"; here referring to his divinely-inspired work or contribution</note>. It was published in print only a few weeks ago. Sir, I present it to you in love as to my Christian fellow-member, and I exhort you to read it often; for its virtue is such that the more it is read, the better it is liked (or, the longer it is known, the better it is loved). In this book, you will see a true, brief foundation, and it is a sure foundation; for the author has found it so through experience in this practice.

<margin>☞</margin>
12. But regarding the foundation of the high *natural mysteries*, which you, Mr. Walter, and Mr. Leonhart Elvern desire a further and clearer explanation of: please ask Mr. Walter for it. I have sent an explanation and other new writings to both you and him; if you like them, you may have them copied out. You will find very great knowledge in them. I wish that all of you might truly understand it. I would have gladly made it plainer, but because of its great depth and out of regard for those who are unworthy, it may not be done.

<margin>☞</margin>
-> Christ says in Matthew 7:7: <-
> Seek, and you shall find; knock, and it shall be opened unto you. No one can give this to another; everyone must obtain it for himself from God. One may certainly give a <term>manuduction</term> <note>original: "manuduction"; literally a "leading by the hand," meaning a simple introduction or guidance</note> or direction to another, but he cannot give him the understanding of it.

13. Yet know that *a lily is blossoming for you in the Northern countries*. <note>Boehme often used the "Lily" as a symbol for a coming age of spiritual purity and the renewal of true Christianity.</note> If you do not destroy it with the sectarian contention of the learned, then it will become a *great tree among you*. But if you choose to argue rather than to know the true God, then the ray (or beam of light) will pass by and strike only a few; and afterward you will be forced to draw water for your souls' thirst among foreign nations.

14. If you consider this rightly, then my writings shall give you great assistance and direction in this; and the <term>Signate-star</term> <note>original: "Signate-star"; likely referring to a prophetic or astrological sign marking a new spiritual era or the "signature" of the times</note> above your pole shall help you, for its time has arrived (or is born).

15. I will freely give you what the Lord has given me; only take care to use it rightly. It will be a witness for you against the mocker. Let no one look at my person; this is a mere gift of God, bestowed not only for my sake, but also for yours, and for all those who happen to read them.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. Let no one look any longer for the *time* to come; it is already born (begun or arrived). Whomever it shines upon, it strikes; whoever is awake sees it, and he who sleeps does not see it. The time has appeared and will soon appear fully; he who watches sees it. Many have already felt it, but a great tribulation must first pass before it is wholly manifest. The cause of this is the contention of the learned, who trample the cup of Christ underfoot and argue about a child—things have never been worse since humanity...

<summary>In this letter, Jacob Boehme presents his book *The Way to Christ* to his associates, emphasizing that spiritual understanding must be granted by God rather than taught by men. He warns that a new spiritual era (the "Lily") is beginning in the North, but risks being suppressed by the petty arguments of scholars.</summary>

# THE

# THIRD EPISTLE.

## Regarding the Persecution against
## ***JACOB BOEHME***, <note>original: "Beem"</note>
### **BY**
## ***GREGORY RICHTER***, <note>original: "Rickter"</note>
### **Primate, or Superintendent of**
### **GÖRLITZ.** <note>original: "Gerlitz"</note>

->*Our Salvation and Peace in the life of*<-
->*Jesus Christ.*<-

---

**Mr. *Marcus Mansern*,**

1. Much <note>The OCR "UCH" follows a decorative drop cap "M" in the original text.</note> respected and beloved Sir; all cordial wishes for the steadfast, working love of our Lord *Jesus Christ* in *soul*, *spirit*, and *body* are offered first: I have received both of your letters and have observed your *Christian* heart, which is sincerely inclined in a fellow member-like love toward me and the pure truth. I desire that God would ratify, strengthen, and preserve you in such a purpose, so that you may grow in the *Tree of Life*, *Jesus Christ*, and bring forth an abundance of good fruit.

2. Your own reason will let you know that the hand of the Lord, according to His will, has guided me so far and brought me

<meta>This page continues the thirty-third epistle from Jacob Boehme to Marcus Mansern. In the previous page, Boehme described how God’s hand led him to his current state of understanding despite his lack of formal education.</meta>

<page-num>204</page-num>

# The Thirty-Third Epistle.

---

<margin>* Or, Taken from.</margin>
2. ...to such knowledge. With this knowledge, I have again willingly and readily served many people, seeing that I have not learned my knowledge in the schools or from books, but *extracted it* <note>original: "Cut of"</note> from the great Book of all Beings, which the hand of the Lord has opened within me.

3. Since there is in that Book *a Cross* of true understanding, God therefore marks His children (to whom He gives this Book to read) with that same Cross—the Cross on which *human death is slain and eternal life is restored.*

4. As a brother, I want you to know that this same mark is stamped on my forehead as a *triumphant trophy*, upon which the Resurrection of *Jesus Christ* is imprinted.

<margin>☞</margin>
5. This mark is dearer to me than all the honor and goods of the world—the fact that God has so highly esteemed me, an unworthy man, as to mark me with the *conquering sign* of His beloved son, *Jesus Christ.*

6. At this sign of conquest, the Devil is so terrified that he is ready to burst with pure anger. Consequently, he has raised a great tempest against me out of his sea of death and has cast his horrible floods upon me, thinking he could overwhelm and drown me.

7. But his violent streams have until now been completely ineffective; for the conquering trophy of *Jesus Christ* has defended me and struck his poisonous, darting rays down to the earth. Through this, Satan’s hypocritical <note>original: "pharisaicall"; in this context, it refers to the legalistic and self-righteous spirit of his religious persecutors</note> murderous poison has been revealed in many hundreds of men who have since turned themselves toward the mark of *Christ*. Thus, I see with joy that all things must work together for the best for those who love God.

8. The report or outcry that reached you was nothing more than hypocritical revilement and scorn by means of a scandalous, reproachful, and lying pamphlet of a single sheet written in *Latin* <note>original: "Latin tongue"</note>. In this pamphlet, Satan has plainly set forth and exposed a hypocritical heart. By God's permission, this has happened so that the people might learn to recognize and flee from the poison of such a hypocritical heart.

9. I confidently believe that the crudest devil dictated that pamphlet, for his claws are clearly and plainly discovered within it. It can be clearly seen that he is a liar and a murderer; it shall serve as a *warning* to many to take better care of their souls, just as this pamphlet is ascribed to Satan by almost all the learned men here.

10. The cause of this rage and fury was the printed book concerning *repentance and true resignation* <note>referring to Boehme's book "The Way to Christ," specifically the treatises on Repentance and Resignation</note>, a book that has been very profitable to many.

11. This severely vexed the hypocritical spirit, seeing that such a...

<meta>catchword: ground</meta>

<summary>Boehme describes his spiritual enlightenment as a "conquering sign" from God and explains that the public attacks against him, specifically a Latin pamphlet, are the desperate reactions of a "pharisaical" or hypocritical spirit provoked by his writings on repentance.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, spiritual enlightenment, Book of All Beings, Cross of understanding, Gregory Richter, pharisaical spirit, The Way to Christ, religious persecution</keywords>

--- Page 223 ---
<meta>This page continues the thirty-third epistle of Jacob Boehme. In the previous section, Boehme discussed the spiritual marks or "trophies" of Christ that he bears, which provoke the anger of the Devil but offer him divine protection.</meta>

<page-num>205</page-num>
<header>The Thirty-Third Epistle.</header>

the foundation should be revealed; thinking that such a doctrine and life should be expected and required of him as well: which is not well-received, so long as men live in the lust of the flesh and serve their own appetites <note>original: "belly-God"; a reference to those who prioritize physical pleasure and greed over spiritual matters</note> in carnal pleasure and delight.

12. Know this for your guidance: his insults and lies only serve to publicize and reveal my book. This has reached the point that everyone—whether of high, learned, or low status—desires to read it and holds it in high regard.

<margin>† Or, spread</margin>
13. This book has spread through Europe in a short time and is highly valued, even in the *Court of the Prince-Elector of Saxony*; where I am invited to a conference with high-ranking people. I have agreed to attend this at the end of the *Leipzig Fair*. Who knows what may be accomplished there? Perhaps the mouth of the bold slanderer may be stopped, and the truth made known and revealed.

<margin>☛</margin>
14. I exhort and entreat you, therefore, to wait with patience, in *Christian* love and joy, for the coming and revelation of *Jesus Christ* and His appearance, which shall soon break forth and destroy the works of the Devil.

15. I do not fear the Devil; if God will use me any longer as His instrument, He will no doubt defend me well enough. For the truth needs no defense; her defense is this, as *Christ* speaks: *“When you are persecuted for my name’s sake, and when they speak all manner of evil against you falsely for my sake, rejoice, for your reward is in Heaven.” Also, “let him who is righteous continue to be righteous, and let him who is wicked continue to be wicked; every one shall reap what he has sown.”* <note>Boehme is paraphrasing passages from Matthew 5:11-12, Revelation 22:11, and Galatians 6:7.</note>

16. Since my citizenship <note>original: "conversation"; in the 17th century, this word referred to one's conduct, community, or citizenship, specifically referencing Philippians 3:20</note> is in Heaven, and I—in both body and soul—run toward it, where my spirit already dwells in *Christ*; what advantage then is worldly dignity and honor to me? I only suffer persecution in the body, and not in the soul.

<margin>* That is, completely stripped of this corrupt flesh, which cannot inherit the Kingdom of God.</margin>
17. Why should I fear the shell <note>The physical body</note> which covers the Spirit? When the shell is gone, then I am completely in Heaven with an unveiled face; who will deprive and rob me of this? No one. Why then should I fear the world in a heavenly cause?

18. If the cause and matter are evil, why do I suffer disgrace and reproach, and stand in trouble, misery, and fear? Why do I not get rid of it? But if it is good, why then should I despair, seeing I know whom I serve? Namely *Jesus Christ*, who makes me conformed to His image.

<margin>*Note.* ☛</margin>
19. Since He died and rose again, why should I not also be willing to suffer, die, and rise with Him? Indeed, His *Cross* is my daily dying, and His *Ascension* into heaven is daily accomplished in me.

20. Yet I wait for the crown of victory <note>original: "conquest"</note> which *Jesus Christ* has laid up, and I am still in combat and wrestling as a warrior.

<meta>catchword: and</meta>

<summary>Boehme discusses how the persecution and slander against him have ironically helped spread his writings throughout Europe, even reaching the electoral court in Saxony. He expresses his lack of fear regarding worldly suffering, viewing his physical life as a mere "shell" and his true existence as already dwelling in Heaven with Christ.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Elector of Saxony, Leipzig Fair, persecution, spiritual warfare, citizenship in heaven, crown of victory, religious slander</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the thirty-third epistle of Jacob Boehme. Jacob Boehme encourages his correspondent to remain steadfast despite persecution and expresses gratitude for a gift of sweets. He also notes that while his works are rejected in his homeland, they are finding an audience elsewhere.</meta>

<page-num>206</page-num>
<header>The Thirty-Third Epistle.</header>

...and I exhort you as my fellow-wrestler to fight a good fight in faith, and wait in patience for the revelation of *Jesus Christ*; and still stand fast.

21. For this smoking firebrand <note>Boehme often used "firebrand" to refer to his primary persecutor, the pastor Gregorius Richter, who died shortly after this letter was written.</note> which now smokes shall shortly be consumed in the fire; and then the remainder shall rejoice. Then it will be manifest what I could have written to you, which for the present is slandered and reviled, yet only by the *ignorant*.

22. But the wise will observe, mark, and take notice of it; for they observe the time and see the darkness and also the dawning of the day.

23. Beloved Mr. *Marcus*; that you and your father are in good health rejoices and gladdens me. I have received the box of comfits <term>original: "comfits"; small seeds, nuts, or pieces of fruit preserved in sugar, similar to modern candy</term> and return you thanks for them.

24. I would gladly write again to the *apothecary* <note>original: "Apothecary"; a pharmacist or medical professional</note> at *Wildan* in *Livonia* <note>Wildan (Viljandi) in modern-day Estonia, which was then part of the territory of Livonia</note>, if I only had an opportunity to send mail that way; I pray, let me know when you have one.

25. That which my native country casts away, other nations shall take up.

26. I send to you and your father a copy of my books for each of you, and some for your good friends; for I heard that formerly you had obtained some of them for yourselves. <note>Paragraph 26 is unnumbered in the source text.</note>

27. I have none of my other writings at hand; I would gladly have sent some of them to you, but I hope to come to your region shortly. Then, God willing, I will bring some along with me. I commend you to the kind and gracious love of *Jesus Christ*.

---

->Dated, March 5, 1624.<-

->J. B.<- <note>Jacob Boehme</note>

->THE<-

---

<summary>In this final section of the thirty-third epistle, Boehme encourages his friend Marcus to remain patient through religious persecution and predicts that his slandered works will eventually be vindicated. He expresses gratitude for a gift of sweets and notes that his writings are finding acceptance in foreign lands even as they are rejected at home.</summary>

<header>The Thirty-Fourth Epistle.</header>

# THE THIRTY-FOURTH EPISTLE.
## TO FREDERICK CRAUSSEN.

---

->Our salvation is in the life of *Jesus Christ* in *us*.<-

---

1. **DEARLY** respected Sir: I send you all hearty wishes for Divine Love, and all promised bodily health and prosperity. May it please God, according to His will, to ease and turn away your frail illness <term>original: "crasie distemper"; in this period, "crazy" meant physically broken or fragile, and a "distemper" was a bodily illness or an imbalance of the "humors."</term>.

2. The state of my body is still tolerable, for which I thank God; but it is wholly besmeared with the Pharisaical mud of slander <term>original: "aspersion"; the act of spreading false or damaging charges</term>, so much so that the common people can scarcely discern or acknowledge me to be a human being.

3. So completely and grievously is Satan enraged against me and my printed book <note>This refers to *The Way to Christ*, the only work of Boehme's published during his lifetime, which caused a massive uproar among the local clergy.</note>; and he does so exceedingly rage and rave in the chief Pharisee <note>This refers to Gregorius Richter, the primary pastor of Görlitz and Boehme's most vocal persecutor.</note> as if he would devour me. But yet, for these past three weeks, the great fire has begun to be somewhat calmed, in that he perceives that many hundreds of people speak against him—people whom he has cursed, reviled, and condemned as well as me. He has so horribly smeared me with lies that I can scarcely be recognized.

4. Thus *Christ* covers His bride and the precious pearl because of the unworthiness of men; for anger is kindled in them, and vengeance is at hand.

<margin>* Trial or proof.</margin>
5. I pray for them and they curse me; I bless, and they revile me; and I stand in the * trial <note>original: "Proba"; Latin for a test, proof, or trial.</note> and bear the mark of *Christ* on my forehead.

<summary>Jacob Boehme writes to Frederick Craussen, offering prayers for Craussen's health while describing the intense religious persecution and slander he is suffering from the local clergy in Görlitz.</summary>
<keywords>Jacob Boehme, Frederick Craussen, Gregorius Richter, persecution, spiritual trial, "The Way to Christ", Pharisee</keywords>

--- Page 226 ---
<meta>This page continues the thirty-fourth epistle of Jacob Boehme to Frederick Craussen. Boehme is discussing his ongoing persecution by the religious authorities (whom he compares to the Pharisees) and framing his suffering as a necessary part of the "process of Christ."</meta>

<page-num>208</page-num>
<header>The Thirty-Fourth Epistle.</header>

6. Yet my soul is not dismayed by it; instead, it considers this to be the triumphant, victorious sign of Christ. For a person must be set correctly on the path <note>original: "processe"; in Boehme's theology, this refers to the spiritual progression or journey of inner transformation</note> of Christ so that he may be made in the likeness of His image.

7. For Christ must be continually persecuted, reviled, and slain; He is a sign that is spoken against <note>A reference to Luke 2:34</note>. But upon whomever He falls, He crushes them and destroys the inbred malice and iniquity of the Serpent.

8. Have I taught others this way? (Indeed, I must teach it.) Why then should it be grievous or irksome to me to walk in that same way? Nothing is happening to me other than what I have told others would happen; it is all going exactly right, for this is the pilgrim's path of Christ.

9. For true Christians are only guests and strangers here; they must travel through the Devil's kingdom to their own home, through the thistles and thorns of God's curse. Here we must wrestle steadfastly and overcome until we get through; *and then we shall be well rewarded.*

10. For those who judge us now shall, on the day of God's Judgment, be set before our eyes, where we—along with Christ—shall pass sentence upon them. What will they say to us then? Will they not be speechless <note>original: "dumb"</note> and judge themselves, even as they judge us now? Oh, that they could only consider it here, and now desist and return from their ways.

<margin>☞</margin>
11. I desire that God would have mercy upon them, for they know not what they do. They are in greater misery than those whom they persecute; they are poor imprisoned captives and slaves of the Devil, who so infects, torments, and poisons them that poison has become their very life.

12. And therefore they rejoice in bringing forth the thorns and thistles of the Devil—namely, fruits of God's anger. The children of Christ (whom they persecute and drive toward their native country) ought to consider this well: they should pray to God for redemption, not only for themselves but also for these, their miserable, poor, captive, and imprisoned fellow-members; that God would likewise illuminate them and bring them home.

13. Dear brother, it is now a time of great earnestness; let us not sleep, for the Bridegroom <note>A reference to the Parable of the Ten Virgins in Matthew 25:1-13</note> passes by and invites His wedding guests. He who hears Him goes along with Him to the wedding.

14. But he who refuses and desires only to sleep in the lusts of the flesh shall be exceedingly sorry and grieved that he has slept away such a time of grace.

15. It seems strange to the world that such people should be acceptable to God—those whom the Scribes [the learned priests and Pharisee-

<meta>catchword: see]</meta>

<summary>Jacob Boehme describes his persecution as a participation in the "process of Christ," arguing that true Christians are merely pilgrims traveling through the Devil's kingdom. He calls for prayer for his persecutors, viewing them as miserable captives of spiritual poison, and urges his readers to remain spiritually awake during this "time of grace."</summary>

<meta>This page continues the thirty-fourth epistle from Jacob Boehme to Frederick Craussen. Boehme is critiquing the state of institutional Christianity, which he argues has become a hollow shell of "opinions" and "images" rather than a lived experience of divine power.</meta>

<page-num>209</page-num>
<header>The Thirty-Fourth Epistle.</header>
<sig>E e</sig>

## The Thirty-Fourth Epistle.

[Pharise]es <note>original: "es"; this fragment completes the word "Pharisees" from the previous page</note> do despise and persecute; and it does not look back to consider what the learned did to the Prophets, Christ, His Apostles, and their successors.

<margin>☞</margin>
16. But the present Christendom is merely titular <term>titular: existing in name only, without real power or substance</term> and verbal—formal and historical only, sticking to lip-service <note>original: "lip-labour"</note> and the devout hypocrisy of the mouth—while *the heart is worse than when they were heathens*. Let us seriously consider this and not occupy ourselves with them, so that a seed may remain upon the earth and that Christ may not always be used only as a covering for the cunning hypocrisy of iniquity.

17. Let us encourage and comfort one another so that we may endure with patience (for a great storm of affliction and tribulation is still coming), so that we may persevere and stand steadfast.

18. For Christianity consists not only in words and knowledge, but in *power*. What are the present arguments about but merely speculative <note>original: "conjecturall"</note> knowledge, mental idols, and the fantastical images and opinions of men? They wrangle and bicker over notions and images which they have blindly persuaded their minds and consciences to adore, but *they deny the power* of Christianity.

<margin>Text, Images [or mental idols]</margin>
19. But there is a time of trial coming when it will be seen what their opinions have been, and what their formal church ways of worship and blind Cain-like <note>A reference to the biblical Cain, whose superficial sacrifice was rejected by God</note> hypocrisy have been, and how they have clung to them; when they shall fall from one opinion to another and yet have no rest or certain stability.

20. Ah, their opinions are nothing but *heathenish idols*, just as they were before they had the name of Christ.

<margin>☞</margin>
<margin>Note</margin>
<margin>† Carefully crafted opinions</margin>
21. The learned and the rulers seek nothing but their own profit, ambition, and honor in these things; they have set these up in Christ's place. But they are only opinion-peddlers—sellers of images and mental idols, the internal superstitious relics of their own reason and Babylonian <term>Babylonish: in Boehme's writing, this refers to the "Tower of Babel" or a state of spiritual confusion and false authority</term> imaginations—which they sell (or offer) for money. He who gives them much shall have devout commendations and praise; to him they will sell plausible † images. They do not care for people's souls, as long as they may gain and enjoy their worldly goods.

<margin>* Or, Faithless Whore.</margin>
22. O, dark night! Where is Christianity? Has she not turned into a faith-breaking adulteress? Where is her love? Has it not been wholly turned into copper, steel, and iron? By what may the present Christendom be known? What difference is there between her and the Turks <note>original: "Turcks"; a general term used at the time for Muslims or the Ottoman Empire</note> and other heathens? Are they not just as good?

<margin>☞</margin>
23. Where is her Christian life? Where is the communion of saints, where we are but one in *Christ*, and where *Christ* is only one in us all? There is not yet one branch on the tree of Christian life—

<summary>Boehme delivers a scathing critique of institutional Christianity, labeling it "titular" and "Babylonian," and accusing religious leaders of being "opinion-peddlers" who prioritize profit and status over spiritual power.</summary>
<keywords>titular Christianity, hypocrisy, mental idols, opinion-peddlers, Babylonian imaginations, communion of saints, spiritual corruption</keywords>

<meta>This page concludes the thirty-fourth epistle from Jacob Boehme to Frederick Craussen. Boehme continues his critique of the "Babel" of conflicting religious sects, viewing his own persecution as a necessary part of spiritual growth and a precursor to a divine reformation.</meta>

<page-num>210</page-num>
<header>The Thirty-Fourth Epistle.</header>

like one another, and there have grown purely adverse twigs in opposition to one another.

<margin>* The confused entanglements of sects and opinions.</margin>
24. Oh Brother, we who stand firm, let us be wary and watchful, and get out of * Babel <term>Babel: A biblical reference to the Tower of Babel, used here to symbolize the confusion and discord of competing religious denominations</term>; it is high time. Although they scorn and flay <note>original: "flay"; here meaning to criticize or punish with extreme severity</note> us, yet we will not worship the Dragon nor his Image <note>References to the false idols and powers described in the Book of Revelation</note>; for those who do so shall be eternally punished.

<margin>† The New Man.</margin>
25. Do not be offended by my persecution; and even if the same happens to you, remember that there is another life, and that they only persecute our own enemy <note>The "Old Man" or the sinful, earthly nature</note> which we ourselves hate. They can take no more from us than the shell [or bark] in which the † Tree is grown.

26. But the tree stands in Heaven, and in Paradise, in the ground of eternity; no devil can root it out. Let the devil's stormy gusts pass over it: their oppression and tormenting is our growth.

27. I am again moved by my adversaries, through the movement of God's anger, so that I may grow and increase; for now my talent <term>talent: referring to Boehme's spiritual insights and writings, echoing the Parable of the Talents in the New Testament</term> is published in my native country.

28. The enemy's intention is evil, yet through it he publishes and spreads my talent abroad. It is greatly desired here, and many a hungry soul is refreshed by it; although the ignorant sort cast their reproach and scorn against it, you shall yet hear wonderful things.

<margin>* Text, Born <note>original: "Borne"; meaning the time has arrived or been brought forth</note>.</margin>
29. For the time has *come, of which I was told three years ago by a vision: namely, THE TIME OF REFORMATION. The end [the outcome or the time when it shall come to pass] I commit to God; I do not know it perfectly yet.

30. And with this, I commend you to the love of Jesus Christ.

->Dated May 8, 1624.<-

<page-num>111</page-num>
<header>The thirty-fifth Epistle.</header>
<meta>Catchword: that</meta>

## The thirty-fifth Epistle.

<margin>☞</margin>
...like a raging lion; and sets himself mightily against Jesus and his children; so that we may, like Paul, fight a good fight and at length obtain the prize, *our salvation*, thereby. We must not, for the sake of temporary honor and pleasure, deny Christ in the Truth we have once acknowledged; for if we did, it would be very hard to recover it again.

2. For my part, I thank God in *Christ Jesus* that he has signed me with his mark and daily makes me like his image. I pray to him that I may continue steadfast, and also that he would deliver my enemies from the abominable death of eternal horror in which they currently lie captive without knowing it. May he bring them also into the light, so that they may know this way and enter into our eternal fraternity <note>original: "Eternall Fraternity"; meaning the spiritual brotherhood of believers</note>.

<margin>☞</margin>
3. Yet to speak wisely of these things, we clearly see before our eyes that this way is completely opposed to the *Devil*, because he rages so much against it without cause. It is indeed a very great wonder that he should make such a stir against such a small work of devotion <note>Boehme is likely referring to his book "The Way to Christ," which sparked significant controversy</note>; surely it must not "relish," but must taste bitter to him. Meanwhile, there are many larger books to be found, full of foolishness, nonsense, and even idolatry, which he does not oppose; but his spite is only against the Way to Christ, so that no one might walk in that way.

<margin>
Note: This followed in the seventh year after the author's death.

* *Gregory Richter*, the Primate of *Görlitz*. ☞ <note>Richter was a primary antagonist of Boehme, frequently denouncing him from the pulpit and seeking his exile.</note>
</margin>
4. For especially at this time, he thinks to set up his kingdom powerfully, and yet he sees currently that it has breaches in it everywhere. For the assailant breaks in from many directions, especially from the *North*, and we can do no better right now than to ignore this enemy and overcome him with patience under the Cross of Christ and with earnest repentance. Then, at length, he will become feeble and faint, and this firebrand will reach its end.

5. For he is but a fiery flash of God's anger, which must be quenched with divine love and humility. We must in no way add fuel to it to make it burn, but kill it with Christ's victory; just as Christ with his love overcame the wrath of God and hell, crushed death, and ruled mightily over all his enemies. Then such enemies within the anger of God shall not long prevail; as Christ has taught us, we should feed our enemies with meat and drink and rejoice when they afflict us for his sake; our reward is in heaven.

<margin>
The temporal sword, or making war.

* Or, reproaches.
</margin>
6. It is not good, out of our own personal ambition, to use a <gloss>†</gloss> *worldly sword* or weapon against such an enemy of Christ; for by doing so, he would only become stronger. Instead, with patience and prayer, we shall surely overcome him. I do not care about his unjust <gloss>*</gloss> *revilings*; I have a clear conscience regarding him. The time is coming quickly, that...

<meta>This page continues the thirty-fifth epistle from Jacob Boehme to his friend and supporter, Dr. Kober. Boehme is discussing the persecution he and his family are facing in his home city of Görlitz, orchestrated by the local religious authorities.</meta>

<page-num>213</page-num>
<header>The thirty-fifth Epistle.</header>

# The thirty-fifth Epistle.

...that such an enemy will be cut in pieces by the sword of God’s power.

7. It is appropriate that we should consider the current time; for Babel <note>original: "Babel"; Boehme used this biblical term to refer to the corrupted and confused state of the institutional churches of his day.</note> burns throughout the whole world, and there is woe in every street; only men do not see it yet, but are blind to it.

<margin>☞</margin>
8. I give you very great thanks for your brotherly faithfulness and Christian care in taking some of my wife’s belongings into your keeping. If the High Priest <note>original: "High Priest"; Boehme's sarcastic title for Gregory Richter, the primary pastor in Görlitz who led the persecution against Boehme.</note> insists on assaulting my house, let him do it, so that it may be known in all countries what a maker of uproars he is. It will lead much to the honor of him and his followers; he will earn a great deal of credit in the Electoral Council <note>The governing body of the Elector of Saxony, who had jurisdiction over Görlitz.</note> for having stormed my house with his "trusty" officers and broken the windows. If others should do or cause such a thing, the Council would not tolerate them in the city.

<margin>* Or, an evil report concerning the city.</margin>
<margin>† The day of Pentecost.</margin>
9. Therefore, I much marvel that they make such an uproar in Görlitz and, without cause, provoke such a scandal <margin>*</margin> abroad against the city. If the reason for it were investigated, it would sound very strange; nevertheless, it must be so, for the time is born <note>meaning: the destined time has arrived.</note>. Something else will suddenly come; this is but a sign, type, or figure. Since they have so solemnly celebrated the Feast of the Holy Spirit <margin>†</margin> (Pentecost), he [the Spirit] must surely assist them powerfully! By this, it may be very well discerned what spirit they are children of, and which spirit they serve; and this will give us even more reason to avoid them. I suppose it is loathsome enough; it stinks of Pharisaic pitch and hellish soot. O, that God would once have pity on the simple people, who are so very blind and pull down the judgment of God upon themselves, which will shortly be poured out.

10. Their stability is very well seen; that which they approve of at one time, they disallow at another. Oh, if some Jesuits should come and reclaim the Church from Luther again, what good Papists they would make!

<margin>☜</margin>
11. But let it go as it does; silence is best. They hunt after a gnat and think they have caught venison; but there lies hidden within it a small grain of mustard seed of the Cross <note>original: "Graine of Mustard seed of the Crosse"; a reference to the transformative power of suffering and faith.</note>, which put Christ to death; that shall burst their guts and grow to be a great tree, and this none can resist.

12. My wife does not need to have any shutters <term>original: "Window-shuts"</term> made; if they want to break the windows, they may, and then the fruits of the High Priest will be seen. Let her have a little patience. If she cannot find a place in Görlitz, I will find a place for her somewhere else where she shall have enough peace; but let her stay inside at home and not go out, except when necessary, and let the enemy rage; he will not eat her up.

<summary>Boehme discusses the escalating persecution in Görlitz, specifically the threat of his house being stormed by local religious authorities, while expressing faith that such trials are signs of a coming spiritual transformation.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the thirty-fifth epistle from Jacob Boehme to Dr. Kober. Boehme is currently away from his home in Görlitz, seeking favor and protection from high-ranking officials in the court of the Elector of Saxony while his family remains behind, facing harassment from the local pastor, Gregory Richter.</meta>

## The thirty-fifth Epistle.

<margin>* The Prince Elector of Saxony.</margin>
13. I must wait here a while longer and see what God intends to do; for I have only recently made a few acquaintances among the influential people here, which happens daily. I am still well, thanks be to God, but I have not yet had any opportunity to ask for *protection* from the lords, as the *Prince Elector* <note>The ruler of Saxony, one of the most powerful princes in the Holy Roman Empire.</note> has gone on a journey, along with some of the chief lords.

14. Though I do not rely upon any worldly protection, I will venture out in God’s name and trust him alone, from whom I have received my talent <note>A reference to the biblical Parable of the Talents; Boehme refers to his spiritual calling and writings.</note>.

<margin>† Doctor Aegidius Strauch.</margin>
15. Next Sunday, a conference is scheduled here at my landlord’s house, which the † *Superintendent* <note>A high-ranking Lutheran official who oversaw several churches; here referring to Aegidius Strauch.</note> himself desires to have with me. I am also invited to supper, where some of the Prince Elector’s *Counsellors of State* will be present. Whatever happens there, I shall let you know at the first opportunity.

16. For he loves my *Treatise of Repentance*; only he would gladly ask me—and hear from my own mouth—concerning some points which are too deep for him, to understand from what *source* <note>original: "Ground"</note> they flow. I am well pleased with this and shall observe how it goes.

17. Similarly, I also expect the decision of *Privy Counselor Loss*, whom I shall visit as soon as possible. Whatever happens there, I will let you know at the first opportunity. I hope all will go well; as God wills, so I will. Who knows what God wants done, or what he will do with me? I greatly marvel at how I am led so wonderfully *without any intent or purpose* of my own.

<margin>☞ * Belonging to a table or cabinet.</margin>
18. Through this messenger, I am sending two rixdollars <note>original: "Rixdollers"; large silver coins used throughout the Holy Roman Empire.</note> to my wife for her needs. If she lacks anything, she knows well where she may get it. The key to the * drawer lies in the parlor by the warming pan <note>A long-handled metal pan filled with hot coals, used to warm a bed before sleeping.</note> on the shelf. *Your treatise* lies in the drawer; you may ask for it. Your letter which you sent to *Zittau* <note>original: "Zitta"; a town in Saxony.</note> has not yet reached me. If anything happens, let me know; and if there is no reliable messenger at hand, just send it to Mr. Melcher Berntten at Zittau. He has a way to send things here every week; let him know so that he may send it away, as we have agreed.

<margin>❦</margin>
19. Greet my wife and two sons for me, and urge them to have Christian patience and prayer, and to seek *no personal revenge*, so that the enemy may gain no advantage.

20. The business with Mr. *Fürstenau’s* family is indeed not good; nevertheless, it will turn out to be no great matter. For it is the High Priest’s <note>Boehme's sarcastic nickname for Gregory Richter, the pastor of Görlitz who persecuted him.</note> own reproach; and it would even turn to his great disgrace if it were properly answered. I wish his Prince only knew the truth of it; he would protect him with a vengeance. These are the "good fruits" of the High Priest.

21. <margin>☛</margin><margin>* Or, a peal of bells.</margin> I hope to visit you very soon. Even if I should return here again, it will not cost me my neck; it is <unclear>but</unclear> wild clamor, for there is nothing in it but the *Priest's* <note>Referring to Gregory Richter, the primary antagonist of Boehme in Görlitz.</note> bell-clapper which sounds so "sweetly." It is easy to judge whether it is *Christ’s* voice or the *Devil's*. You do not need to be so deathly afraid of this *quarrel*; it is not a *business* you need to be ashamed of. It is nothing but *Babel’s* <note>Babel, referencing the Tower of Babel, was Boehme’s term for the confused, divided, and corrupt institutional churches of his day.</note> bell, which is rung to give warning [or to sound the alarm].

22. Just stand to it stoutly in the Spirit of *Christ*, and then *Christ’s* bell will also be rung. God give them and us all a good mind.

23. <margin>|</margin> Please greet Mr. *Frederick Renischen* for me; I could do nothing for him here yet, for things here depend very much on *favor*, and there are many attendants whenever there is anything to be gained. I would very readily serve him in *love* if I were only able; I cannot yet advise myself until God helps me. My *son* *Jacob* shall stay in *Görlitz* so that his *mother* may have some comfort there, until I can arrange things otherwise. This would have happened already if I did not have to stay here; let her only have *patience*. And so I commend you into the *love of Jesus Christ*.

<summary>Boehme reassures Dr. Kober that the religious opposition they face is merely a "mad clamor" from the corrupt church and expresses his hope to return home soon. He also provides updates on his son Jacob's stay in Görlitz to support his mother while Boehme continues to seek favor in Dresden.</summary>
<keywords>Babel's bell, religious persecution, Görlitz, Gregory Richter, spiritual courage, family matters, Jacob Boehme</keywords>
